## III A Point of Interrogation / 三、问号

===Original===
The man from Scotland Yard had just surrendered hat, coat, and umbrella to the vestiaire and was turning through swinging doors to the dining-room. Again, embracing Lanyard, his glance seemed devoid of any sort of intelligible expression; and if its object needed all his self-possession in that moment, it was to dissemble relief rather than dismay. An accent of the fortuitous distinguished this second encounter too persuasively to excuse further misgivings. What the adventurer himself hadn’t known till within the last ten minutes, that he was coming to Troyon’s, Roddy couldn’t possibly have anticipated; ergo, whatever the detective’s business, it had nothing to do with Lanyard.
Furthermore, before quitting the lobby, Roddy paused long enough to instruct the vestiaire to have a fire laid in his room.
So he was stopping at Troyon’s﻿—and didn’t care who knew it!
His doubts altogether dissipated by this incident, Lanyard followed his natural enemy into the dining-room with an air as devil-may-care as one could wish and so impressive that the maitre-d’hôtel abandoned the detective to the mercies of one of his captains and himself hastened to seat Lanyard and take his order.

===Chinese===
苏格兰场来客刚把帽子、大衣和雨伞交给衣帽间（vestiaire），正转身穿过弹簧门往餐厅去。他的目光又一次把兰亚德整个罩住，却似乎不带一丝可供索解的表情；这一刻，被看的人若需要拿出全部的镇定，与其说是掩饰惊慌，毋宁说是掩饰宽慰。这第二回相逢也带着一股「纯属偶然」的腔调，浓得叫人再要多心都说不过去。冒险家本人直到十分钟前才晓得自己要来特鲁瓦旅馆，罗迪绝无可能未卜先知；因此，这位侦探无论办的是什么案子，都与兰亚德不相干。
再者，离开门厅之前，罗迪还特为驻足，吩咐衣帽间的人在他房里生起火来。
这么说，他就在特鲁瓦旅馆下榻——而且满不在乎谁晓得这件事！
这桩小事把他的疑虑一扫而空。兰亚德随他的天敌走进餐厅，那副吊儿郎当、满不在乎的神气恰到好处，又派头十足，竟害得餐厅总管（maitre-d’hôtel）把侦探撂给手下一名领班听凭发落，亲自赶过来为兰亚德安座、听他点菜。

===Original===
This last disposed of; Lanyard surrendered himself to new impressions﻿—of which the first proved a bit disheartening.
However impulsively, he hadn’t resought Troyon’s without definite intent, to wit, to gain some clue, however slender, to the mystery of that wretched child, Marcel. But now it appeared he had procrastinated fatally: Time and Change had left little other than the shell of the Troyon’s he remembered. Papa Troyon was gone; Madame no longer occupied the desk of the caisse; enquiries, so discreetly worded as to be uncompromising, elicited from the maitre-d’hôtel the information that the house had been under new management these eighteen months; the old proprietor was dead, and his widow had sold out lock, stock and barrel, and retired to the country﻿—it was not known exactly where. And with the new administration had come fresh decorations and furnishings as well as a complete change of personnel: not even one of the old waiters remained.
“ ‘All, all are gone, the old familiar faces,’ ” Lanyard quoted in vindictive melancholy﻿—“damn ’em!”
Happily, it was soon demonstrated that the cuisine was being maintained on its erstwhile plane of excellence: one still had that comfort.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
点菜已毕，兰亚德便任由新的印象涌上心头——头一个却有点叫人泄气。
尽管来得多半出于心血来潮，他重寻特鲁瓦旅馆却并非没有明确的目的：说穿了，便是想为那个苦命孩子马塞尔之谜寻一点线索，哪怕细若游丝也好。可如今看来，他是把时机耽搁到了无可救药的地步：光阴与变迁把记忆中的特鲁瓦旅馆剥得只剩一副空壳。特鲁瓦老板没了；老板娘也不再坐镇账台（caisse）；他措辞婉转得叫人无从回避的打听，从餐厅总管嘴里套出的消息是：这家旅馆十八个月前便换了新东家；老老板去世，遗孀把产业连锅端卖了个精光，退隐乡间——确切去处无人知晓。新东家一来，粉刷装潢焕然一新，人手也整个儿换了血：连一个旧日的侍者都不剩。
「『全都走了，全走了，旧日那些熟悉的面孔。』」兰亚德怀着怨毒的忧郁引了一句旧诗——「见他们的鬼！」
所幸不久便见分晓：菜式的水准依旧维持着旧日的高妙——这一点安慰总还留着。……

===Original===
Other impressions, less ultimate, proved puzzling, disconcerting, and paradoxically reassuring.
Lanyard commanded a fair view of Roddy across the waist of the room. The detective had ordered a meal that matched his aspect well﻿—both of true British simplicity. He was a square-set man with a square jaw, cold blue eyes, a fat nose, a thin-lipped trap of a mouth, a face as red as rare beefsteak. His dinner comprised a cut from the joint, boiled potatoes, brussels sprouts, a bit of cheese, a bottle of Bass. He ate slowly, chewing with the doggedness of a strong character hampered by a weak digestion, and all the while kept eyes fixed to an issue of the Paris edition of the *London Daily Mail*, with an effect of concentration quite too convincing.
Now one doesn’t read the Paris edition of the *London Daily Mail* with tense excitement. Humanly speaking, it can’t be done.
Where, then, was the object of this so sedulously dissembled interest?
Lanyard wasn’t slow to read this riddle to his satisfaction﻿—in as far, that is, as it was satisfactory to feel still more certain that Roddy’s quarry was another than himself.

===Chinese===
另一些印象没那么切身，却叫人迷惑、叫人不安，而说来矛盾，偏偏还叫人安心。
隔着餐厅的中腰，兰亚德正好将罗迪尽收眼底。这位侦探点的一餐与他的仪表相称得很——两样都是不折不扣的英国式简朴。他生得四四方方：方方的身架，方方的下巴，冷淡的蓝眼睛，肥厚的鼻子，一张捕兽夹似的薄嘴唇，脸红得像生牛排。他的晚餐计有：一片刀切烤肉、煮土豆、抱子甘蓝、一小块干酪、一瓶巴斯（Bass）啤酒。他吃得慢条斯理，咀嚼间透着一股倔劲儿——强悍的性子偏叫孱弱的肠胃拖了后腿——而且自始至终把眼睛钉在一期《伦敦每日邮报》（*London Daily Mail*）巴黎版上，那份专注未免演得太逼真了。
要知道，谁也不会屏气凝神地读一份《伦敦每日邮报》巴黎版。就人情之常而论，这是办不到的。
那么，这份苦心遮掩的兴趣，对象究竟何在？
这个哑谜，兰亚德不多时便解到了自己满意的地步——满意在于：他由此越发确信，罗迪追猎的目标另有其人。

===Original===
Despite the lateness of the hour, which had by now turned ten o’clock, the restaurant had a dozen tables or so in the service of guests pleasantly engaged in lengthening out an agreeable evening with dessert, coffee, liqueurs and cigarettes. The majority of these were in couples, but at a table one removed from Roddy’s sat a party of three; and Lanyard noticed, or fancied, that the man from Scotland Yard turned his newspaper only during lulls in the conversation in this quarter.
Of the three, one might pass for an American of position and wealth: a man of something more than sixty years, with an execrable accent, a racking cough, and a thin, patrician cast of features clouded darkly by the expression of a soul in torment, furrowed, seamed, twisted﻿—a mask of mortal anguish. And once, when this one looked up and casually encountered Lanyard’s gaze, the adventurer was shocked to find himself staring into eyes like those of a dead man: eyes of a grey so light that at a little distance the colour of the irises blended indistinguishably with their whites, leaving visible only the round black points of pupils abnormally distended and staring, blank, fixed, passionless, beneath lashless lids.
For the instant they seemed to explore Lanyard’s very soul with a look of remote and impersonal curiosity; then they fell away; and when next the adventurer looked, the man had turned to attend to some observation of one of his companions.
On his right sat a girl who might be his daughter; for not only was she, too, hall-marked American, but she was far too young to be the other’s wife. A demure, old-fashioned type; well-poised but unassuming; fetchingly gowned and with sufficient individuality of taste but not conspicuously; a girl with soft brown hair and soft brown eyes; pretty, not extravagantly so when her face was in repose, but with a slow smile that rendered her little less than beautiful: in all (Lanyard thought) the kind of woman that is predestined to comfort mankind, whose strongest instinct is the maternal.

===Chinese===
时辰虽晚——此刻已过十点——餐厅里仍有十来张桌子招待着客人，他们正用甜点、咖啡、利口酒和香烟，惬意地把一个愉快的夜晚往长里拖。这些客人多半成双成对，唯独与罗迪相隔一张桌子的地方坐着三位一席；兰亚德注意到——或者只是自以为注意到——苏格兰场来客偏偏只在这一席谈话的间隙里，才翻动他的报纸。
三人之中，有一位一望便知是有身份、有家底的美国人：六十开外，一口糟得没法听的口音，一阵撕心裂肺的咳嗽，一张清瘦的贵胄面孔，却被备受煎熬的灵魂的神情罩上浓重的阴霾——沟壑纵横，褶痕遍布，扭曲变形——俨然一副熬尽人间苦痛的面具。有一回，这人抬起眼来，恰与兰亚德的目光不期而遇，冒险家骇然发觉，自己正望进一双死人的眼睛：那双灰眼淡到极处，稍离远些，虹彩的颜色便与眼白浑然莫辨，只剩一对异样放大的瞳孔，圆圆的黑点，直勾勾、空落落地瞪着，呆滞，无欲无求，藏在光秃秃的眼睑底下。
有一瞬，那双眼睛仿佛带着一种疏远而不涉私情的好奇，直探进兰亚德的灵魂深处；随即敛了下去；待冒险家再望过去时，那人已侧转身，去理会同伴的一句什么话了。
他右手边坐着一位姑娘，多半是他的女儿；因为不单她也是那种一眼可辨的美国闺秀，而且她太年轻，不可能是老人的妻子。娴静的老派典型；仪态端庄而不张扬；衣着动人，品味自有主见而不惹眼；一头柔软的棕发，一双温柔的棕色眼睛；论标致，容颜静止时并不惊艳，可她那徐徐绽开的微笑，几乎使她称得上美丽：总而言之（兰亚德想），这就是天生注定要抚慰人世的女人，最强的天性便是母性。

===Original===
She took little part in the conversation, seldom interrupting what was practically a duologue between her putative father and the third of their party.
This last was one, whom Lanyard was sure he knew, though he could see no more than the back of Monsieur le Comte Remy de Morbihan.
And he wondered with a thrill of amusement if it were possible that Roddy was on the trail of that tremendous buck. If so, it would be a chase worth following﻿—a diversion rendered the more exquisite to Lanyard by the spice of novelty, since for once he would figure as a dispassionate bystander.
The name of Comte Remy de Morbihan, although unrecorded in the *Almanach de Gotha*, was one to conjure with in the Paris of his day and generation. He claimed the distinction of being at once the homeliest, one of the wealthiest, and the most-liked man in France.
As to his looks, good or bad, they were said to prove infallibly fatal with women, while not a few men, perhaps for that reason, did their possessor the honour to imitate them. The revues burlesqued him; Sem caricatured him; Forain counterfeited him extensively in that inimitable series of Monday morning cartoons for *Le Figaro*: one said “De Morbihan” instinctively at sight of that stocky figure, short and broad, topped by a chubby, moon-like mask with waxed moustaches, womanish eyes, and never-failing grin.
A creature of proverbial good-nature and exhaustless vitality, his extraordinary popularity was due to the equally extraordinary extravagance with which he supported that latest Gallic fad, *le Sport*. The Parisian Rugby team was his pampered protégé, he was an active member of the Tennis Club, maintained not only a flock of automobiles but a famous racing stable, rode to hounds, was a good field gun, patronized aviation and motorboat racing, risked as many maximums during the Monte Carlo season as the Grand Duke Michael himself, and was always ready to whet rapiers or burn a little harmless powder of an early morning in the Parc aux Princes.

===Chinese===
她在席间的谈话里很少插嘴；那场谈话实际上是她那位推定的父亲与第三位同伴的二重奏，她难得打岔。
至于这第三位，兰亚德确信自己认得，虽然他望见的无非是雷米·德·莫尔比昂伯爵（Monsieur le Comte Remy de Morbihan）的背影。
他心头掠过一阵兴味盎然的悸动：莫非罗迪追的竟是这头了不得的大人物？果真如此，这场追逐倒值得跟上一跟——对兰亚德来说，这出消遣因添了新奇这味佐料而愈见妙不可言，因为这一回，他破天荒要扮一个事不关己的看客了。
雷米·德·莫尔比昂伯爵的名号，虽说《哥达贵族谱系年鉴》（*Almanach de Gotha*）里查无此人，在他这一代人的巴黎，却是念一声便能起风唤雨的。他自诩一身兼有三重名头：法国最其貌不扬的男人、最有钱的男人之一，外加最得人缘的男人。
至于那副相貌，无论说美说丑，据说对女人向来百发百中；不少男人——恐怕正因如此——还以仿学他的尊容为荣。滑稽时事剧拿他打诨；塞姆（Sem）为他作漫画；福兰（Forain）在《费加罗报》（*Le Figaro*）每周一晨间那组无人能仿的漫画里，把他摹了个淋漓尽致：一望见那副又矮又壮的敦实身量，顶上一张胖乎乎、月亮似的圆脸——上蜡的髭须，女人气的眼睛，再加一抹永不缺席的嬉笑——人们便脱口而出：「德·莫尔比昂！」
此公出了名的好脾气，精力用之不竭；他那异乎寻常的人缘，全拜他以同样异乎寻常的豪阔、去捧那门最新的高卢风尚——*le Sport*（体育运动）——所赐。巴黎的英式橄榄球队是他娇宠的得意门生；他是网球俱乐部的活跃会员；不但豢养一群汽车，还开着一家赫赫有名的赛马房；他纵马围猎，是猎场上一等一的好枪；他赞助航空与摩托艇竞速；蒙特卡洛赛季里押的满注，不输米哈伊尔大公（Grand Duke Michael）本人；而在王子公园（Parc aux Princes），他也随时乐意于清晨磨一磨决斗的细剑，或是焚上一撮无伤大雅的火药。

===Original===
But there were ugly whispers current with respect to the sources of his fabulous wealth. Lanyard, for one, wouldn’t have thought him the properest company or the best Parisian cicerone for an ailing American gentleman blessed with independent means and an attractive daughter.
Paris, on the other hand﻿—Paris who forgives everything to him who contributes to her amusement﻿—adored Comte Remy de Morbihan﻿ ﻿…
But perhaps Lanyard was prejudiced by his partiality for Americans, a sentiment the outgrowth of the years spent in New York with Bourke. He even fancied that between his spirit and theirs existed some subtle bond of sympathy. For all he knew he might himself be American﻿ ﻿…
For some time Lanyard strained to catch something of the conversation that seemed to hold so much of interest for Roddy, but without success because of the hum of voices that filled the room. In time, however, the gathering began to thin out, until at length there remained only this party of three, Lanyard enjoying a most delectable salad, and Roddy puffing a cigar (with such a show of enjoyment that Lanyard suspected him of the sin of smuggling) and slowly gulping down a second bottle of Bass.
Under these conditions the talk between De Morbihan and the Americans became public property.

===Chinese===
可是，关于他那神话般家财的来路，坊间也有些难听的窃窃私语。至少就兰亚德而论，他不认为此公是一位久病缠身、坐拥家产、又带着一位可人女儿的美国绅士最合适的旅伴、最高明的巴黎向导。
另一方面，巴黎——这座谁给她添了乐子、便什么都可以宽恕的都市——对雷米·德·莫尔比昂伯爵宠爱有加……
不过，兰亚德对美国人素有好感——这是他随伯克在纽约度过那几年的副产品——他的偏见说不定正由此而来。他甚至觉得，自己的性灵与美国人之间存着某种微妙的相通。说不定他自己就是美国人，谁能说得准呢……
有一阵子，兰亚德竖起耳朵，想从那席似乎令罗迪兴味无穷的谈话里捕捉些什么，却因满屋子嗡嗡的人声而一无所得。不过渐渐地，人客开始稀落，到末了只剩这一席三人，外加正受用一盘精美绝伦的沙拉的兰亚德，还有罗迪——他叼着雪茄吞云吐雾（那副受用的样子，叫兰亚德疑心他犯了私运烟草之罪），慢慢灌着第二瓶巴斯啤酒。
在这样的情势下，德·莫尔比昂与美国客人之间的谈话便成了公产。

===Original===
The first remark overheard by Lanyard came from the elderly American, following a pause and a consultation of his watch.
“Quarter to eleven,” he announced.
“Plenty of time,” said De Morbihan cheerfully. “That is,” he amended, “if mademoiselle isn’t bored﻿ ﻿…”
The girl’s reply, accompanied by a pretty inclination of her head toward the Frenchman, was lost in the accents of the first speaker﻿—a strong and sonorous voice, in strange contrast with his ravaged appearance and distressing cough.
“Don’t let that worry you,” he advised cheerfully. “Lucia’s accustomed to keeping late hours with me; and who ever heard of a young and pretty woman being bored on the third day of her first visit to Paris?”
He pronounced the name with the hard C of the Italian tongue, as though it were spelled Luchia.

===Chinese===
兰亚德听清的第一句话，出自那位年长的美国人之口，之前是一阵沉默，加上对怀表的一番请教。
「差一刻十一点。」他宣告。
「时间宽裕得很。」德·莫尔比昂欣然道。「也就是说，」他找补一句，「只要小姐不觉得无聊……」
姑娘的回答，伴着朝那法国人优雅的一颔首，淹没在了头一位说话人的嗓音里——那嗓音浑厚洪亮，与他残败的形容、揪心的咳嗽恰成古怪的对照。
「这个您别挂在心上。」他快活地劝道，「露西娅跟我熬惯了夜；再说，年轻漂亮的姑娘头一回逛巴黎，才到第三天，谁听说过就腻烦了？」
他念这个名字时，用的是意大利语的硬音 C，听去仿佛拼作 Luchia。

===Original===
“To be sure,” laughed the Frenchman; “one suspects it will be long before mademoiselle loses interest in the rue de la Paix.”
“You may well, when such beautiful things come from it,” said the girl. “See what we found there today.”
She slipped a ring from her hand and passed it to De Morbihan.
There followed silence for an instant, then an exclamation from the Frenchman:
“But it is superb! Accept, mademoiselle, my compliments. It is worthy even of you.”
She flushed prettily as she nodded smiling acknowledgement.

===Chinese===
「那是自然，」法国人笑道，「只怕小姐一时半会儿还看不腻和平街（rue de la Paix）呢。」
「您说得是，那样美的物件都出自那条街。」姑娘道，「瞧瞧我们今天在那儿寻着的宝贝。」
她从手上褪下一枚戒指，递给德·莫尔比昂。
片刻的静默，随后是法国人一声赞叹：
「这才叫精美绝伦！小姐，请接受我的赞美。这戒指连您都配得上。」
她红着脸颔首微笑致意，红得很好看。

===Original===
“Ah, you Americans!” De Morbihan sighed. “You fill us with envy: you have the souls of poets and the wealth of princes!”
“But we must come to Paris to find beautiful things for our womenfolk!”
“Take care, though, lest you go too far, Monsieur Bannon.”
“How so﻿—too far?”
“You might attract the attention of the Lone Wolf. They say he’s on the prowl once more.”
The American laughed a trace contemptuously. Lanyard’s fingers tightened on his knife and fork; otherwise he made no sign. A sidelong glance into a mirror at his elbow showed Roddy still absorbed in the *Daily Mail*.

===Chinese===
「啊，你们美国人！」德·莫尔比昂叹道，「真叫我们眼红：你们有诗人的灵魂，还有王侯的家财！」
「可我们还非得到巴黎来，才能为家里的女眷寻着漂亮物件！」
「不过您得当心，别走过头了，班农先生（Bannon）。」
「怎么说——走过头？」
「您可能会招来独狼的注意。听说他又出来游荡了。」
美国人笑了，笑意里带一丝轻蔑。兰亚德的手指在刀叉上收紧了一瞬；除此之外，他不动声色。一侧眼瞥向手边的镜子：罗迪仍埋头于《每日邮报》（*Daily Mail*）。

===Original===
The girl bent forward with a look of eager interest.
“The Lone Wolf? Who is that?”
“You don’t know him in America, mademoiselle?”
“No.﻿ ﻿…”
“The Lone Wolf, my dear Lucia,” the valetudinarian explained in a dryly humourous tone, “is the sobriquet fastened by some imaginative French reporter upon a celebrated criminal who seems to have made himself something of a pest over here, these last few years. Nobody knows anything definite about him, apparently, but he operates in a most individual way and keeps the police busy trying to guess where he’ll strike next.”

===Chinese===
姑娘身子前倾，一脸急切的兴味。
「独狼？那是什么人？」
「小姐在美国没听人说起过他？」
「没有。……」
「我亲爱的露西娅，」那位病骨支离的人用干巴巴的诙谐口吻解说道，「独狼是哪一位想象力丰富的法国记者，给一个大名鼎鼎的罪犯安上的诨号。这几年，此公在咱们这儿俨然成了个不大不小的祸害。明摆着，谁也拿不准他的底细，可他作案独有一套路数，害得警方整日价忙于猜测：他下一锤子要砸在哪里。」

===Original===
The girl breathed an incredulous exclamation.
“But I assure you!” De Morbihan protested. “The rogue has had a wonderfully successful career, thanks to his dispensing with confederates and confining his depredations to jewels and similar valuables, portable and easy to convert into cash. Yet,” he added, nodding sagely, “one isn’t afraid to predict his race is almost run.”
“You don’t tell me!” the older man exclaimed. “Have they picked up the scent﻿—at last?”
“The man is known,” De Morbihan affirmed.
By now the conversation had caught the interest of several loitering waiters, who were listening open-mouthed. Even Roddy seemed a bit startled, and for once forgot to make business with his newspaper; but his wondering stare was exclusively for De Morbihan.

===Chinese===
姑娘轻轻呼出一声不敢相信的惊叹。
「这可是千真万确！」德·莫尔比昂抗议道，「这无赖的营生干得漂亮极了——全亏他不雇同伙，又只对珠宝一类的值钱物下手：轻便易携，变现又快。不过，」他煞有介事地点点头，「不妨放胆预言：他的赛程快跑到头了。」
「竟有这等事！」老先生叫了起来，「这么说，他们嗅到踪迹了——总算？」
「此人的身份，已经查明。」德·莫尔比昂断言。
到这时，这番话已勾起了几名闲晃着的侍者的兴趣，一个个张着嘴听。连罗迪似乎也怔了一怔，破天荒忘了拿报纸做戏；不过他那一脸讶异的瞪视，从头到尾只落在德·莫尔比昂一人身上。

===Original===
Lanyard put down knife and fork, swallowed a final mouthful of Haut Brion, and lighted a cigarette with the hand of a man who knew not the meaning of nerves.
“Garçon!” he called quietly; and ordered coffee and cigars, with a liqueur to follow.﻿ ﻿…
“Known!” the American exclaimed. “They’ve caught him, eh?”
“I didn’t say that,” De Morbihan laughed; “but the mystery is no more﻿—in certain quarters.”
“Who is he, then?”
“That﻿—monsieur will pardon me﻿—I’m not yet free to state. Indeed, I may be indiscreet in saying as much as I do. Yet, among friends﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
兰亚德放下刀叉，咽下最后一口奥比良（Haut Brion）葡萄酒，用一只不知「紧张」二字为何物的手，点起一支香烟。
「Garçon!」（侍者！）他轻声唤道，点了咖啡和雪茄，随后再来一杯利口酒。……
「查明啦！」美国人嚷起来，「这么说，人已经抓着了，嗯？」
「我可没这么说，」德·莫尔比昂笑道，「不过，谜团已不复存在——在某些圈子里。」
「那么他是谁？」
「那一点——还请先生恕罪——我尚不便明言。说实在的，我讲到现在这一步，只怕已经是多嘴了。不过，自家朋友之间嘛……」

===Original===
His shrug implied that, as far as *he* was concerned, waiters were unhuman and the other guests of the establishment nonexistent.
“But,” the American persisted, “perhaps you can tell us how they got on his track?”
“It wasn’t difficult,” said De Morbihan: “indeed, quite simple. This tone of depreciation is becoming, for it was my part to suggest the solution to my friend, the Chief of the Sûreté. He had been annoyed and distressed, had even spoken of handing in his resignation because of his inability to cope with this gentleman, the Lone Wolf. And since he is my friend, I too was distressed on his behalf, and badgered my poor wits until they chanced upon an idea which led us to the light.”
“You won’t tell us?” the girl protested, with a little moue of disappointment, as the Frenchman paused provokingly.
“Perhaps I shouldn’t. And yet﻿—why not? As I say, it was elementary reasoning﻿—a mere matter of logical deduction and elimination. One made up one’s mind the Lone Wolf must be a certain sort of man; the rest was simply sifting France for the man to fit the theory, and then watching him until he gave himself away.”

===Chinese===
他一耸肩，那意思分明是：就*他*而论，侍者算不得人，馆子里的其他客人更是等于不存在。
「可是，」美国人追问不休，「也许您能告诉我们，他们是怎么寻上他踪迹的？」
「这不难，」德·莫尔比昂道，「说穿了，简单得很。我这自谦的口气倒也贴切，因为谜底正是鄙人献给我那位朋友——保安局（la Sûreté）局长的。他被这位独狼先生惹得又恼又苦，甚至说过要递辞呈，因为拿此人毫无办法。他既是我的朋友，我便也替他难受，把我这可怜的脑筋磨了又磨，直到磨出一个念头，把我们领到了亮处。」
「您不肯讲给我们听？」法国人故意卖关子地一顿，姑娘微微撅起小嘴，失望地抗议。
「也许本不该讲。可话说回来——为什么不能讲？如我方才所言，这不过是初级推理——区区一桩逻辑演绎加排除法的工夫。先认定独狼必是某一类人；剩下的，无非是把全法国筛一遍，找出合乎这理论的人，然后盯住他，等他自己露出马脚。」

===Original===
“You don’t imagine we’re going to let you stop there?” The American demanded in an aggrieved tone.
“No? I must continue? Very well: I confess to some little pride. It was a feat. He is cunning, that one!”
De Morbihan paused and shifted sideways in his chair, grinning like a mischievous child.
By this manoeuvre, thanks to the arrangement of mirrors lining the walls, he commanded an indirect view of Lanyard; a fact of which the latter was not unaware, though his expression remained unchanged as he sat﻿—with a corner of his eye reserved for Roddy﻿—speculating whether De Morbihan were telling the truth or only boasting for his own glorification.
“Do go on﻿—please!” the girl begged prettily.

===Chinese===
「您总不至于以为，我们就让您说到这儿打住吧？」美国人用受了亏待的口气追讨下文。
「不行么？那我非讲下去不可？好吧：我承认这里头有我几分小小的得意。这可是一桩绝活。那个人，狡猾得很哪！」
德·莫尔比昂顿了顿，朝椅子边上一侧，咧嘴而笑，活像个促狭的孩子。
这么一挪，托四面墙上镜子布局的福，他恰好斜斜望见了兰亚德；后者对此并非毫无察觉，只是面色纹丝不动地坐着——眼角给罗迪留着一角——心里盘算：德·莫尔比昂讲的究竟是实话，还是只为自我标榜的信口开河。
「快讲下去呀——求您了！」姑娘楚楚动人地央告。

===Original===
“I can deny you nothing, mademoiselle.﻿ ﻿… Well, then! From what little was known of this mysterious creature, one readily inferred he must be a bachelor, with no close friends. That is clear, I trust?”
“Too deep for me, my friend,” the elderly man confessed.
“Impenetrable reticence,” the Count expounded, sententious﻿—and enjoying himself hugely﻿—“isn’t possible in the human relations. Sooner or later one is doomed to share one’s secrets, however reluctantly, even unconsciously, with a wife, a mistress, a child, or with some trusted friend. And a secret between two is﻿—a prolific breeder of platitudes! Granted this line of reasoning, the Lone Wolf is of necessity not only unmarried but practically friendless. Other attributes of his will obviously comprise youth, courage, imagination, a rather high order of intelligence, and a social position﻿—let us say, rather, an ostensible business﻿—enabling him to travel at will hither and yon without exciting comment. So far, good! My friend the Chief of the Sûreté forthwith commissioned his agents to seek such an one, and by this means several fine fish were enmeshed in the net of suspicion, carefully scrutinized, and one by one let go﻿—all except one, the veritable man. Him they sedulously watched, shadowing him across Europe and back again. He was in Berlin at the time of the famous Rheinart robbery, though he compassed that coup without detection; he was in Vienna when the British embassy there was looted, but escaped by a clever ruse and managed to dispose of his plunder before the agents of the Sûreté could lay hands on him; recently he has been in London, and there he made love to, and ran away with, the diamonds of a certain lady of some eminence. You have heard of Madame Omber, eh?”
Now by Roddy’s expression it was plain that, if Madame Omber’s name wasn’t strange in his hearing, at least he found this news about her most surprising. He was frankly staring, with a slackened jaw and with stupefaction in his blank blue eyes.
Lanyard gently pinched the small end of a cigar, dipped it into his coffee, and lighted it with not so much as a suspicion of tremor. His brain, however, was working rapidly in effort to determine whether De Morbihan meant this for warning, or was simply narrating an amusing yarn founded on advance information and amplified by an ingenious imagination. For by now the news of the Omber affair must have thrilled many a Continental telegraph-wire.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
「小姐的事儿，我一概无法拒绝。……那么，是这样的！就人们对这个神秘家伙仅有的那点了解来看，一推便知：他必是单身汉一个，且没有亲近的朋友。这一点，我讲明白了吧？」
「太玄乎，我听不懂，我的朋友。」老先生坦白道。
「滴水不漏的缄默，」伯爵字斟句酌地敷陈——自己则乐在其中——「在人与人的交往里是办不到的。或早或晚，谁都注定要同人分享自己的秘密，不管多么勉强，甚至全然不自觉——同妻子、同情妇、同孩子，或是同某位信得过的朋友。而两人之间的秘密嘛——恰是陈词滥调的多产母鸡！循此推理，独狼便非但无妻，而且几乎无友，此乃必然。他的其余特质，显然应包括：年轻、胆量、想象力、相当高的智力，再加一份社会地位——不如说，一门摆在外面的营生——好教他天南地北随意来去，而不惹人物议。到此为止，很好！我那位保安局长朋友当即遣密探四出寻访这样一号人；循此法，好几尾好鱼落进了嫌疑的罗网，经细细盘查，又一尾尾放归原水——只除了一尾，那真正的正主。对这一位，他们盯得密不透风，横穿欧洲盯过去，又盯回来。柏林莱茵纳特（Rheinart）大劫案之际，他在柏林，只是那一票办得神不知鬼不觉；维也纳英国使馆遭劫时，他在维也纳，却凭一手巧计金蝉脱壳，赶在保安局密探染指之前，把赃物处置了个干净；新近他到了伦敦，在那儿向某位颇有名望的贵妇人大献殷勤，随后携其钻石扬长而去。欧姆贝夫人的大名，二位总听说过吧？」
这会儿，从罗迪的脸色便看得分明：欧姆贝夫人的名字，他就算不生，这桩新闻至少教他大吃一惊。他索性瞪直了眼，下巴松垮，呆滞的蓝眼睛里一片惊愕。
兰亚德轻轻掐了掐雪茄细的一头，往咖啡里蘸了蘸，点上火，手上连一丝颤意的影子也没有。可他的脑筋却在飞转，竭力判定：德·莫尔比昂这番话是存心敲山震虎，还是不过拿先得到的消息打底、再靠灵动的想象力添枝加叶，讲一段逗趣的故事。因为到此刻，欧姆贝事件的新闻，想必早已教欧陆的电报线震颤过不知多少回了。……

===Original===
“Madame Omber﻿—of course!” the American agreed thoughtfully. “Everyone has heard of her wonderful jewels. The real marvel is that the Lone Wolf neglected so shining a mark as long as he did.”
“But truly so, monsieur!”
“And they caught him at it, eh?”
“Not precisely: but he left a clue﻿—and London, to boot﻿—with such haste as would seem to indicate he knew his cunning hand had, for once, slipped.”
“Then they’ll nab him soon?”
“Ah, monsieur, one must say no more!” De Morbihan protested. “Rest assured the Chief of the Sûreté has laid his plans: his web is spun, and so artfully that I think our unsociable outlaw will soon be making friends in the Prison of the Santé.﻿ ﻿… But now we must adjourn. One is sorry. It has been so very pleasant.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「欧姆贝夫人——那还用说！」美国人若有所思地附和，「她那些稀世珠宝，谁没有耳闻。真正的奇事倒是：这么一块亮晃晃的靶子，独狼竟晾了那么久才下手。」
「千真万确，先生！」
「那么这一回，总算失手被逮了？」
「算不上：不过他留下了一条线索——连伦敦也一并撇下了——那份仓促，倒像是他自己心里明白：这只狡猾的手，破天荒打了个滑。」
「那么他们很快就能拿住他了？」
「啊，先生，多一个字也不能说啦！」德·莫尔比昂拦道，「放一百个心，保安局长早已布下棋局：网已织就，而且织得这般精巧，我想咱们这位不喜结交的法外之徒，不久便要去桑泰监狱（the Santé）里广结良朋了。……好了，眼下我们该告退了。真叫人遗憾。今晚实在畅快之至。……」

===Original===
A waiter conjured the bill from some recess of his waistcoat and served it on a clean plate to the American. Another ran bawling for the vestiaire. Roddy glued his gaze afresh to the *Daily Mail*. The party rose.
Lanyard noticed that the American signed instead of settling the bill with cash, indicating that he resided at Troyon’s as well as dined there. And the adventurer found time to reflect that it was odd for such as he to seek that particular establishment in preference to the palatial modern hostelries of the Rive Droit﻿—before De Morbihan, ostensibly for the first time espying Lanyard, plunged across the room with both hands outstretched and a cry of joyous surprise not really justified by their rather slight acquaintanceship.
“Ah! Ah!” he clamoured vivaciously. “It is Monsieur Lanyard, who knows all about paintings! But this is delightful, my friend﻿—one grand pleasure! You must know my friends.﻿ ﻿… But come!”
And seizing Lanyard’s hands, when that one somewhat reluctantly rose in response to this surprisingly over-exuberant greeting, he dragged him willy-nilly from behind his table.
“And you are American, too. Certainly you must know one another. Mademoiselle Bannon﻿—with your permission﻿—my friend, Monsieur Lanyard. And Monsieur Bannon﻿—an old, dear friend, with whom you will share a passion for the beauties of art.”

===Chinese===
一名侍者从马甲的什么褶缝里变戏法似的摸出账单，盛在一只干净盘子里，端给美国人。另一名一路喊着人去唤衣帽间。罗迪把目光重新粘上《每日邮报》（*Daily Mail*）。那一席起身了。
兰亚德注意到，美国人不付现钞，只签了账——可见他不只在特鲁瓦旅馆用饭，也在此下榻。冒险家还来得及寻思：这等人物，放着右岸（Rive Droit）那些宫殿式的摩登大旅馆不投，偏寻这么一家，未免古怪——便在此时，德·莫尔比昂一副这才头一回望见兰亚德的做派，张着双臂横贯餐厅扑将过来，嘴里一声又惊又喜的大叫——以他们那点泛泛之交，实在当不起这一声。
「啊！啊！」他热热闹闹地嚷道，「这不是兰亚德先生嘛——那位于绘画无所不通的行家！这可太叫人开心了，我的朋友——天大的快事！您务必认一认我的朋友们。……来！」
被这出人意料的过火热络一迎，兰亚德老大不情愿地起了身；德·莫尔比昂抓住他的双手，不由分说，把他从餐桌后面拖了出来。
「您也是美国人。二位合该彼此认识。班农小姐（Mademoiselle Bannon）——请您许可——我的朋友，兰亚德先生。这一位是班农先生——我的一位亲爱的老友，您二位正好同怀一腔对艺术之美的痴爱。」

===Original===
The hand of the American, when Lanyard clasped it, was cold, as cold as ice; and as their eyes met that abominable cough laid hold of the man, as it were by the nape of his neck, and shook him viciously. Before it had finished with him, his sensitively coloured face was purple, and he was gasping, breathless﻿—and infuriated.
“Monsieur Bannon,” De Morbihan explained disconnectedly﻿—“it is most distressing﻿—I tell him he should not stop in Paris at this season﻿—”
“It is nothing!” the American interposed brusquely between paroxysms.
“But our winter climate, monsieur﻿—it is not fit for those in the prime of health﻿—”
“It is I who am unfit!” Bannon snapped, pressing a handkerchief to his lips﻿—“unfit to live!” he amended venomously.

===Chinese===
兰亚德握住美国人的手时，那只手冰冷，冷得像冰；两人目光一触，那阵可憎的咳嗽便掐住了此人，仿佛掐住他的后颈，狠狠摇撼。待它发作完毕，他那张本就血色敏感的脸已憋成紫色，喘作一团，上气不接下气——而且怒不可遏。
「班农先生，」德·莫尔比昂东拉西扯地解释着，「这真叫人难过——我一直劝他，这个时节不该留在巴黎——」
「没事！」美国人在两阵咳嗽的间隙里硬邦邦地插上一句。
「可我们这冬天的气候，先生——连正当盛年、体魄最健的人都消受不起——」
「消受不起的是我！」班农厉声道，一方手帕抵着嘴唇——「不配活着！」他恶狠狠地改了口。

===Original===
Lanyard murmured some conventional expression of sympathy. Through it all he was conscious of the regard of the girl. Her soft brown eyes met his candidly, with a look cool in its composure, straightforward in its enquiry, neither bold nor mock-demure. And if they were the first to fall, it was with an effect of curiosity sated, without hint of discomfiture.﻿ ﻿… And somehow the adventurer felt himself measured, classified, filed away.
Between amusement and pique he continued to stare while the elderly American recovered his breath and De Morbihan jabbered on with unfailing vivacity; and he thought that this closer scrutiny discovered in her face contours suggesting maturity of thought beyond her apparent years﻿—which were somewhat less than the sum of Lanyard’s﻿—and with this the suggestion of an elusive, provoking quality of wistful languor, a hint of patient melancholy.﻿ ﻿…
“We are off for a glimpse of Montmartre,” De Morbihan was explaining﻿—“Monsieur Bannon and I. He has not seen Paris in twenty years, he tells me. Well, it will be amusing to show him what changes have taken place in all that time. One regrets mademoiselle is too fatigued to accompany us. But you, my friend﻿—now if you would consent to make our third, it would be most amiable of you.”
“I’m sorry,” Lanyard excused himself; “but as you see, I am only just in from the railroad, a long and tiresome journey. You are very good, but I﻿—”

===Chinese===
兰亚德喃喃道了一句照例的慰问。这半晌，他始终意识到姑娘的注视。她那双温柔的棕眼，坦然迎着他的目光，神色沉静里带着凉意，探询得直截了当，既不孟浪，也不假作含羞。纵然先垂下的是她的眼睛，那样子也只是好奇心已告餍足，毫无半分局促。……不知怎的，冒险家只觉得自己被掂量过了、分过类了、编档存卷了。
他又是好笑、又是着恼，只管端详，任那位年长的美国人缓过气来，任德·莫尔比昂滔滔不绝、兴头不减；他觉得，这般贴近细看之下，她的眉目轮廓间自有一种超越实际年纪的思想的老成——她的年纪，尚比兰亚德的岁数略小几岁——与此相映的，还有一种难以捉摸、撩人又恼人的怅惘慵懒之致，一丝沉得住气的忧郁。……
「我们要去蒙马特走一遭，」德·莫尔比昂正解说着，「我和班农先生。他告诉我，他有二十年没见巴黎了。好啊，领他看看这些年里的变迁，必定有趣。只遗憾小姐太过劳乏，不能同往。至于您，我的朋友——您若肯赏光做我们的第三人，那真是再周到不过了。」
「抱歉，」兰亚德推辞道，「不过您也看见了，我刚下火车，一路又长又累人。多谢您好意，可我——」

===Original===
“Good!” De Morbihan exclaimed with violence. “I? On the contrary, I am a very selfish man; I seek but to afford myself the pleasure of your company. You lead such a busy life, my friend, romping about Europe, here one day, God-knows-where the next, that one must make one’s best of your spare moments. You will join us, surely?”
“Really I cannot tonight. Another time perhaps, if you’ll excuse me.”
“But it is always this way!” De Morbihan explained to his friends with a vast show of mock indignation. “ ‘Another time, perhaps’﻿—his invariable excuse! I tell you, not two men in all Paris have any real acquaintance with this gentleman whom all Paris knows! His reserve is proverbial﻿—‘as distant as Lanyard,’ we say on the boulevards!” And turning again to the adventurer, meeting his cold stare with the De Morbihan grin of quenchless effrontery﻿—“As you will, my friend!” he granted. “But should you change your mind﻿—well, you’ll have no trouble finding us. Ask any place along the regular route. We see far too little of one another, monsieur﻿—and I am most anxious to have a little chat with you.”
“It will be an honour,” Lanyard returned formally.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
「好意！」德·莫尔比昂激烈地叫起来，「我？恰恰相反，我是个顶顶自私的人；我图的不过是沾您的光，讨一晌相处的乐子。我的朋友，您过的日子多忙啊——在整个欧洲撒欢儿，今天在这儿，明天天知道在哪儿——旁人只好抓紧您零星的空当。您一定会来的，是不是？」
「今晚实在不行。改日再约吧，还望您见谅。」
「他永远是这样！」德·莫尔比昂向他的朋友们摆出一副大张旗鼓的佯怒之态，解说道，「『改日再约』——他雷打不动的托词！我告诉二位，全巴黎没有两个人真正结识这位全巴黎无人不识的先生！他的矜持是出了名的——林荫大道上人人都说：『矜远如兰亚德』！」说着他又转向冒险家，以德·莫尔比昂那副扑不灭的厚脸皮式的嬉笑，迎上他冷淡的注视——「悉听尊便，我的朋友！」他慨然应允，「不过，您若是回心转意——放心，找我们不费事。沿着常规路线，随便哪家一问便知。咱们相见得太少了，先生——我可是巴望着同您小小叙谈一回呢。」
「不胜荣幸。」兰亚德一板一眼地答道。……

===Original===
In his heart he was pondering several most excruciating methods of murdering the man. What did he mean? How much did he know? If he knew anything, he must mean ill, for assuredly he could not be ignorant of Roddy’s business, or that every other word he uttered was rivetting suspicion on Lanyard of identity with the Lone Wolf, or that Roddy was listening with all his ears and staring into the bargain!
Decidedly something must be done to silence this animal, should it turn out he really did know anything!﻿ ﻿…
It was only after profound reflection over his liqueur (while Roddy devoured his *Daily Mail* and washed it down with a third bottle of Bass) that Lanyard summoned the maitre-d’hôtel and asked for a room.
It would never do to fix the doubts of the detective by going elsewhere that night. But, fortunately, Lanyard knew that warren which was Troyon’s as no one else knew it; Roddy would find it hard to detain him, should events seem to advise an early departure.

===Chinese===
他心里，正盘算着好几种处死此人最叫人痛快的法子。他这是什么意思？他知道多少？他但凡知道一点，就必是不怀好意：因为他断然不可能不晓得罗迪的来头；不可能不晓得，自己吐出的每一个字，都在把「兰亚德即独狼」的嫌疑一记一记地铆死；也不可能不晓得，罗迪正竖着两耳在听，还瞪着两眼在瞧！
倘若他当真知道些什么，那就断然得想个法子，教这头畜生永远闭嘴！……
直到对着那杯利口酒深刻反省了一番（其间罗迪把他的《每日邮报》（*Daily Mail*）吞吃下肚，又用第三瓶巴斯啤酒送了下去），兰亚德方才唤来餐厅总管，讨了一个房间。
这一夜若另投别处，势必把侦探的疑心钉死，万万使不得。所幸，特鲁瓦旅馆那座兔子窝，天下再无人比兰亚德更了然于胸；万一情势示意宜及早动身，罗迪要想扣住他，可没有那么容易。

## IV A Stratagem / 四、一条妙计

===Original===
When the maitre-d’hôtel had shown him all over the establishment (innocently enough, en route, furnishing him with a complete list of his other guests and their rooms: memoranda readily registered by a retentive memory) Lanyard chose the bedchamber next that occupied by Roddy, in the second storey.
The consideration influencing this selection was﻿—of course﻿—that, so situated, he would be in position not only to keep an eye on the man from Scotland Yard but also to determine whether or no Roddy were disposed to keep an eye on him.
In those days Lanyard’s faith in himself was a beautiful thing. He could not have enjoyed the immunity ascribed to the Lone Wolf as long as he had without gaining a power of sturdy self-confidence in addition to a certain amount of temperate contempt for spies of the law and all their ways.
Against the peril inherent in this last, however, he was self-warned, esteeming it the most fatal chink in the armour of the lawbreaker, this disposition to underestimate the acumen of the police: far too many promising young adventurers like himself were annually laid by the heels in that snare of their own infatuate weaving. The mouse has every right, if he likes, to despise the cat for a heavy-handed and bloodthirsty beast, lacking wit and imagination, a creature of simple force-majeure; but that mouse will not advisedly swagger in cat-haunted territory; a blow of the paw is, when all’s said and done, a blow of the paw﻿—something to numb the wits of the wiliest mouse.

===Chinese===
餐厅总管领着他把整个馆舍上上下下看了一遍（途中 en route 还天真得很，顺带把其余住客连同各人房号的完整名单全供了出来：这些备忘，一副过目不忘的好记性当即照单收录在案），兰亚德便挑了二楼紧挨着罗迪的那间卧室。
左右这一抉择的考虑嘛——自然是——占了这位置，他不但能盯住苏格兰场来客的动静，还能顺便查明罗迪是否有意反过来盯他的梢。
想当年，兰亚德对自身的信心堪称一桩美事。他若非练出一副坚实的自信，外加对法律的密探及其全套伎俩怀有几分有分寸的轻蔑，也不可能顶着独狼的名号逍遥这么久还安然无恙。
不过对这后一种轻蔑所伏的凶险，他倒有先见之明，深知那是犯法者甲胄上最致命的一处破绽——低估警方的精明：年年都有好些前程似锦的年轻冒险家，跟他一路货，栽进自己痴心织就的罗网里束手就擒。老鼠若有兴致，尽可以鄙夷猫，骂它是只手脚重、心肠狠的畜生，缺智少谋、没一点想头，一味靠 force-majeure（强力压人）吃饭；可这只老鼠断不会煞有介事地跑到猫群出没的地界上大摇大摆；说到底，猫爪子终归是猫爪子——再刁滑的耗子挨上一下，也要懵上半晌。

===Original===
Considering Roddy, he believed it to be impossible to gauge the limitations of that essentially British intelligence﻿—something as self-contained as a London flat. One thing only was certain: Roddy didn’t always think in terms of beef and Bass; he was nobody’s facile fool; he could make a shrewd inference as well as strike a shrewd blow.
Reviewing the scene in the restaurant, Lanyard felt measurably warranted in assuming not only that Roddy was interested in De Morbihan, but that the Frenchman was well aware of that interest. And he resented sincerely his inability to feel as confident that the Count, with his gossip about the Lone Wolf, had been merely seeking to divert Roddy’s interest to putatively larger game. It was just possible that De Morbihan’s identification of Lanyard with that mysterious personage, at least by innuendo, had been unintentional. But somehow Lanyard didn’t believe it had.
The two questions troubled him sorely: Did De Morbihan *know*, did he merely suspect, or had he only loosed an aimless shot which chance had sped to the right goal? Had the mind of Roddy proved fallow to that suggestion, or had it, with its simple national tenacity, been impatient of such side issues, or incredulous, and persisted in focusing its processes upon the personality and activities of Monsieur le Comte Remy de Morbihan?
However, one would surely learn something illuminating before very long. The business of a sleuth is to sleuth, and sooner or later Roddy must surely make some move to indicate the quarter wherein his real interest lay.

===Chinese===
至于罗迪，他相信那副彻头彻尾的英式头脑深浅莫测——像伦敦的公寓一般自成一统。只有一件事可以断言：罗迪的念头并不总绕着牛排和巴斯啤酒（Bass）打转；他不是谁都能轻易糊弄的傻瓜；他出手能又准又狠，推断起来同样一针见血。
回想餐厅里那一幕，兰亚德觉得自己颇有几分把握断定：不但罗迪对德·莫尔比昂存着兴趣，而且这位法国人对这份兴趣也了然于胸。叫他真心气闷的是，他偏偏无法同样自信地认定：伯爵拿独狼的闲话来饶舌，只是想引着罗迪把兴趣挪到据说个头更大的猎物身上去。德·莫尔比昂把兰亚德同那位神秘人物划上等号——至少是旁敲侧击的一提——倒也未必不是无心之失。可不知怎么，兰亚德偏不信。
这两个问题搅得他好不难受：德·莫尔比昂究竟是*心中有数*，是仅止起疑，还是不过信手放了一枪、偏叫造化把它送进了正靶？罗迪那副头脑，对那记暗示是寸草不生、毫无着落，还是凭它那股朴实的民族性执拗，不耐烦这类枝节，依旧把全部心思钉死在雷米·德·莫尔比昂伯爵阁下（Monsieur le Comte Remy de Morbihan）的为人与行止上？
不过，要不了多久，总能探得几分启人疑窦的亮光。密探的本分就在侦探，罗迪迟早必得有所动作，好教人看出他真正的兴趣究竟落在哪一头。

===Original===
Just at present, reasoning from noises audible through the bolted door that communicated with the adjoining bedchamber, the business of a sleuth seemed to comprise going to bed. Lanyard, shaving and dressing, could distinctly hear a tuneless voice contentedly humming “Sally in Our Alley,” a rendition punctuated by one heavy thump and then another and then by a heartfelt sigh of relief﻿—as Roddy kicked off his boots﻿—and followed by the tapping of a pipe against grate-bars, the squeal of a window lowered for ventilation, the click of an electric-light, and the creaking of bedsprings.
Finally, and before Lanyard had finished dressing, the man from Scotland Yard began placidly to snore.
Of course, he might well be bluffing; for Lanyard had taken pains to let Roddy know that they were neighbours, by announcing his selection in loud tones close to the communicating door.
But this was a question which the adventurer meant to have answered before he went out.﻿ ﻿…
It was hard upon twelve o’clock when the mirror on the dressing-table assured him that he was at length point-device in the habit and apparel of a gentleman of elegant nocturnal leisure. But if he approved the figure he cut, it was mainly because clothes interested him and he reckoned his own impeccable. Of their tenant he was feeling just then a bit less sure than he had half-an-hour since; his regard was louring and mistrustful. He was, in short, suffering reaction from the high spirits engendered by his cross-Channel exploits, his successful getaway, and the unusual circumstances attendant upon his return to this memory-haunted mausoleum of an unhappy childhood. He even shivered a trifle, as if under premonition of misfortune, and asked himself heavily: Why not?

===Chinese===
眼下，单凭那扇闩上的、通往隔壁卧室的房门里传出的种种声响来判断，密探的本分似乎还包括上床睡觉。兰亚德一面修面更衣，一面听得真真切切：一副五音不全的嗓子正心满意足地哼着《我们巷子里的萨莉》（Sally in Our Alley），这段演唱不时被一记闷响、紧跟着又一记闷响打断，末了还添上一声由衷的如释重负——那是罗迪踢脱了靴子——随后是烟斗磕着炉条的笃笃声、放窗透气时窗框的吱嘎声、电灯的咔哒一响，还有床绷子的吱吱呀呀。
到后来，不等兰亚德穿戴停当，苏格兰场来客已安然打起呼噜来了。
当然，这也尽有可能是装的；兰亚德早费了番手脚让罗迪知道两人做了紧邻——他紧贴着那扇相通的房门，高声宣布了自己挑定的房间。
可这个问题，冒险家打定主意要在出门之前讨出一个答案来。……
将近 12 点，梳妆台的镜子才向他担保：他总算从头到脚、分毫不差地装扮停当，成了位出入夜场的风雅闲士。不过，他若还算中意自己这副扮相，多半是因为衣裳本就是他的赏心乐事，而他自信这一身无可挑剔。至于穿这身衣裳的人，比之半个钟头之前，他眼下倒没有那么拿得准了；他打量自己的目光阴沉沉的，满是狐疑。简而言之，他这是在偿还一阵亢奋之后的疲软——渡海那一连串壮举、漂漂亮亮的脱身、重返这座勾魂摄魄的陵墓（他那不快童年之所寄）的种种非常际遇，方才叫他意气飞扬来着。他甚至微微打了个寒噤，仿佛大难将临的预感压上心头，沉甸甸地问自己：凭什么不会呢？

===Original===
For, logically considered, a break in the run of his luck was due. Thus far he had played, with a success almost too uniform, his dual role, by day the amiable amateur of art, by night the nameless mystery that prowled unseen and preyed unhindered. Could such success be reasonably expected to attend him always? Should he count De Morbihan’s yarn a warning? Black must turn up every so often in a run of red: every gambler knows as much. And what was Michael Lanyard but a common gambler, who persistently staked life and liberty against the blindly impartial casts of Chance?﻿ ﻿…
With one last look round to make certain there was nothing in the calculated disorder of his room to incriminate him were it to be searched in his absence, Lanyard enveloped himself in a long full-skirted coat, clapped on an opera hat, and went out, noisily locking the door. He might as well have left it wide, but it would do no harm to pretend he didn’t know the bedchamber keys at Troyon’s were interchangeable﻿—identically the same keys, in fact, that had been in service in the days of Marcel the wretched.
A single half-power electric bulb now modified the gloom of the corridor; its fellow made a light blot on the darkness of the courtyard. Even the windows of the conciergerie were black.
None the less, Lanyard tapped them smartly.

===Chinese===
因为照道理讲，他这一路鸿运也该轮到折一阵子了。到如今为止，他这双重角色演得运气好得几乎太匀净：白天是温文尔雅的艺术爱好者，夜里是来无影、去无踪、掠食无阻的无名之谜。这样的顺遂，难道能指望它一伴到底？德·莫尔比昂那一番话，该不该算个警兆？红运连庄，黑总要时不时翻出来：这点道理哪个赌客不晓。而迈克尔·兰亚德又算得了什么，不过一个寻常赌客，执拗地拿性命和自由作注，去赌造化蒙着眼掷出来的骰子罢了。……
临出门他又环视一周，确信房内那番刻意布置的凌乱里，没有任何东西能在他外出遭搜时连累他，兰亚德便裹上一件下摆长阔的大衣，扣一顶折叠大礼帽，出房而去，锁门锁得山响。这扇门索性敞着也无所谓，不过装作不晓得特鲁瓦旅馆的客房钥匙把把通用，倒也无伤大雅——事实上，如今用的还是倒霉蛋马塞尔年月里使的原班钥匙，一把不差。
此刻只有一只半明半暗的电灯泡勉强调和着走廊的幽暗；它的同伴在院子的黑地里印出一小片光斑。连门房小屋（conciergerie）的窗子都黑着。
尽管如此，兰亚德还是脆生生地敲了两下窗。

===Original===
“*Cordon!*” he demanded in a strident voice. “*Cordon, s’il vous plait!*”
“*Eh?*” A startled grunt from within the lodge was barely audible. Then the latch clicked loudly at the end of the passageway.
Groping his way in the direction of this last sound, Lanyard found the small side door ajar. He opened it, and hesitated a moment, looking out as though questioning the weather; simultaneously his deft fingers wedged the latch back with a thin slip of steel.
No rain, in fact, had fallen within the hour; but still the sky was dense with a sullen rack, and still the sidewalks were inky wet.

===Chinese===
「*Cordon*（看门的）！」他扯着嗓子喝道，「*Cordon, s'il vous plait*（劳驾，看门的）！」
「*Eh*（嗯）？」门房小屋里传出一声惊惶的咕哝，勉强听得见。跟着，走道尽头的门闩咔哒一声大响。
兰亚德循着这最后一声响摸过去，发觉那扇小小的边门已开了条缝。他拉开门，踌躇片刻，抬眼望外，像是在掂量天气；与此同时，他灵巧的手指已用一片薄钢片把闩舌别了回去。
这一个钟头里其实滴雨未落；天却仍旧堆着一层愠怒的乱云，人行道也仍旧湿得墨黑。

===Original===
The street was lonely and indifferently lighted, but a swift searching reconnaissance discovered nothing that suggested a spy skulking in the shelter of any of the nearer shadows.
Stepping out, he slammed the door and strode briskly round the corner, as if making for the cab-rank that lines up along the Luxembourg Gardens side of the rue de Medicis; his boot-heels made a cheerful racket in that quiet hour; he was quite audibly going away from Troyon’s.
But instead of holding on to the cab-rank, he turned the next corner, and then the next, rounding the block; and presently, reapproaching the entrance to Troyon’s, paused in the recess of a dark doorway and, lifting one foot after another, slipped rubber caps over his heels. Thereafter his progress was practically noiseless.
The smaller door yielded to his touch without a murmur. Inside, he closed it gently, and stood a moment listening with all his senses﻿—not with his ears alone but with every nerve and fibre of his being﻿—with his imagination, to boot. But there was never a sound or movement in all the house that he could detect.

===Chinese===
街上冷冷清清，灯也点得敷衍，但他飞快而仔细地扫视一周，近处几团暗影里并不见有什么蹲着暗探的形迹。
他跨出门去，砰地甩上门，大步流星地绕过街角，像是直奔梅迪西斯街（rue de Medicis）沿卢森堡公园一侧排开的那列马车候点；在这清静时辰，他的靴跟敲出一路欢畅的响动；任谁都听得出，他正远远离开特鲁瓦旅馆而去。
但他没往候车处去，而是拐过下一个街角，再拐一个，把这一圈街区兜了个整；不多时又踅回特鲁瓦旅馆门口，在一处黑洞洞的门洞凹处站定，抬起一只脚、再抬起一只脚，往靴跟上套了橡胶软垫。自此他行进起来，几乎悄无声息。
那扇小门在他手底下一声不吭地开了。进得门去，他轻轻掩上它，伫立片刻，倾尽周身感官去听——不光用耳朵，而是全副神经、一寸寸血肉——外带想象力。可整座房子里，他能察觉的声息与动静，一概没有。

===Original===
And no shadow could have made less noise than he, slipping cat-footed across the courtyard and up the stairs, avoiding with super-developed sensitiveness every lift that might complain beneath his tread. In a trice he was again in the corridor leading to his bedchamber.
It was quite as gloomy and empty as it had been five minutes ago, yet with a difference, a something in its atmosphere that made him nod briefly in confirmation of that suspicion which had brought him back so stealthily.
For one thing, Roddy had stopped snoring. And Lanyard smiled over the thought that the man from Scotland Yard might profitably have copied that trick of poor Bourke’s, of snoring like the Seven Sleepers when most completely awake.﻿ ﻿…
It was naturally no surprise to find his bedchamber door unlocked and slightly ajar. Lanyard made sure of the readiness of his automatic, strode into the room, and shut the door quietly but by no means soundlessly.

===Chinese===
再没有哪个影子能比他出声更少了：他猫着步溜过院子、溜上楼梯，凭着练到通神的敏感，避开每一块踩上去要翻翘抱怨的地板。转眼间，他又站到了通往自己卧室的那条走廊里。
走廊还是五分钟前那副幽暗空落的模样，却又有些不同，气氛里多了点什么，教他微微一颔首，印证了那个害他这样蹑手蹑脚折回来的疑心。
头一件，罗迪不打呼噜了。兰亚德想到，可怜的伯克有一手绝活——人最清醒的时候，偏能打出七睡仙（Seven Sleepers）一般的呼噜——苏格兰场来客若肯学去，倒大有赚头，不由得莞尔。……
发觉自己卧室的门没锁、还虚掩着一线，自然算不得意外。兰亚德验明自动手枪随时可发，便大步跨进房去，随手带上房门——轻是轻，却绝非全然无声。

===Original===
He had left the shades down and the hangings drawn at both windows; and since these had not been disturbed, something nearly approaching complete darkness reigned in the room. But though promptly on entering his fingers closed upon the wall-switch near the door, he refrained from turning up the lights immediately, with a fancy of impish inspiration that it would be amusing to learn what move Roddy would make when the tension became too much even for his trained nerves.
Several seconds passed without the least sound disturbing the stillness.
Lanyard himself grew a little impatient, finding that his sight failed to grow accustomed to the darkness because that last was too absolute, pressing against his staring eyeballs like a black fluid impenetrably opaque, as unbroken as the hush.
Still, he waited: surely Roddy wouldn’t be able much longer to endure such suspense﻿ ﻿…
And, surely enough, the silence was abruptly broken by a strange and moving sound, a hushed cry of alarm that was half a moan and half a sob.

===Chinese===
他出门前把两扇窗帘都放了下来，帘幔也拉得严严实实；这些东西既无人惊动，房里便君临着一种近乎全然的黑暗。不过，一进门他的手指虽已扣住门边墙上的开关，却忍着没有立刻开灯——一时鬼灵动念，觉得倒要看看：等那份紧张连罗迪那副训练有素的神经都撑不住了，他会使出什么招来。
好几秒钟过去了，没有一丝声响惊扰这份静谧。
兰亚德自己倒先有点沉不住气了：他发觉自己的眼睛怎么也习惯不了这黑暗，因为它太纯粹了，像一种不透一丝光的黑色流体，紧贴着他瞪圆的眼珠，同这片岑寂一样密不透风。
他仍旧等着：这样的悬念，罗迪总归撑不了多久了吧……
果然，寂静蓦地被一种古怪而揪心的声响打破——一声压低的惊呼，半是呻吟，半是啜泣。

===Original===
Lanyard himself was startled: for that was never Roddy’s voice!
There was a noise of muffled and confused footsteps, as though someone had started in panic for the door, then stopped in terror.
Words followed, the strangest he could have imagined, words spoken in a gentle and tremulous voice:
“In pity’s name! who are you and what do you want?”
Thunderstruck, Lanyard switched on the lights.
At a distance of some six paces he saw, not Roddy, but a woman, and not a woman merely, but the girl he had met in the restaurant.

===Chinese===
兰亚德自己吓了一跳：那绝不是罗迪的声音！
接着是一阵窒闷而杂乱的脚步声，仿佛有人慌里慌张地抢向房门，又吓得半途站住。
随后是说话声，说得再匪夷所莫过于此了，那嗓音轻柔而发颤：
「看在老天的份上！你是谁？想要什么？」
兰亚德如遭雷击，啪地开了灯。
六步开外，他看见的不是罗迪，而是一个女人——而且岂止是女人，正是餐厅里遇见过的那位姑娘。

## V Anticlimax / 五、虎头蛇尾

===Original===
The surprise was complete; none, indeed, was ever more so; but it’s a question which party thereto was the more affected.
Lanyard stared with the eyes of stupefaction. To his fancy, this thing passed the compass of simple incredulity: it wasn’t merely improbable, it was preposterous; it was anticlimax exaggerated to the proportions of the grotesque.
He had come prepared to surprise and bully rag the most astute police detective of whom he had any knowledge; he found himself surprised and discountenanced by *this*﻿ ﻿… !
Confusion no less intense informed the girl’s expression; her eyes were fixed to his with a look of blank enquiry; her face, whose colouring had won his admiration two hours since, was colourless; her lips were just ajar; the fingers of one hand touched her cheek, indenting it.
The other hand caught up before her the long skirts of a pretty robe-de-chambre, beneath whose edge a hand’s-breadth of white silk shimmered and the toe of a silken mule was visible. Thus she stood, poised for flight, attired only in a dressing-gown over what, one couldn’t help suspecting, was her nightdress: for her hair was down, and she was unquestionably all ready for her bed.﻿ ﻿…
But Bourke’s patient training had been wasted if this man proved one to remain long at loss. Rallying his wits quickly from their momentary rout, he reasserted command over them, and if he didn’t in the least understand, made a brave show of accepting this amazing accident as a commonplace.

===Chinese===
这份意外来得十足彻底；天底下再没有比它更彻底的了；只是两造之中究竟哪一方受的震动更大，倒要打个问号。
兰亚德瞪着眼，满眼惊愕。在他想来，这桩事早越出了单纯「难以置信」的疆界：岂止是离奇，简直是荒谬绝伦；一场虎头蛇尾的急转直下，还被夸张到了怪诞的规模。
他原是有备而来，打算出其不意，把他所识得的最精明的警探连唬带诈地收拾一顿；如今却发现自己反被*这个*……惊得进退失据！
姑娘脸上的慌乱也不遑多让；她双眼直勾勾锁住他，目光里一片茫然的探询；两个钟头前还曾赢得他赞赏的那张脸，此刻血色尽褪；双唇微微开启；一只手的指尖抵着面颊，压出几点浅窝。
另一只手在身前拢起一件漂亮晨衣（robe-de-chambre）的长摆，衣摆下缘露出一掌宽的白绸，幽幽泛光，还看得见一只缎面软拖鞋的鞋尖。她就这样立着，作势欲逃，身上只罩一件晨衣，底下——不由人不怀疑——便是睡裙：因为她一头长发披散，分明已万事停当、只待就寝。……
不过，倘若这个人当真会久久地茫然失措，伯克那番耐心的调教便算白费了。他转眼间便把一时溃散的心神重整旗鼓，再度号令于胸；纵然丝毫不曾明白，也十足做足了架势，只当这桩惊人的意外不过是寻常事一桩。

===Original===
“I beg your pardon, Miss Bannon﻿—” he began with a formal bow.
She interrupted with a gasp of wondering recognition: “Mr. Lanyard!”
He inclined his head a second time: “Sorry to disturb you﻿—”
“But I don’t understand﻿—”
“Unfortunately,” he proceeded smoothly, “I forgot something when I went out, and had to come back for it.”
“But﻿—but﻿—”

===Chinese===
「恕我冒昧，班农小姐——」他一板一眼地躬身开场。
她认出了他，惊得倒抽一口气，打断了他：「兰亚德先生！」
他再次颔首：「抱歉搅扰了你——」
「可我不明白——」
「不巧，」他不慌不忙地接着说，「我出门时落下了件东西，只好回来取。」
「可是——可是——」

===Original===
“Yes?”
Suddenly her eyes, for the first time detached from his, swept the room with a glance of wild dismay.
“This room,” she breathed﻿—“I don’t know it﻿—”
“It is mine.”
“Yours! But﻿—”
“That is how I happened to﻿—interrupt you.”

===Chinese===
「嗯？」
蓦地，她的目光头一回从他身上挪开，惶惶然把房间扫视一周。
「这间房，」她低低吐出，「我不认得——」
「是我的房间。」
「你的！可是——」
「所以我才碰巧——打搅了你。」

===Original===
The girl shrank back a pace﻿—two paces﻿—uttering a low-toned monosyllable of understanding, an “*O!*” abruptly gasped. Simultaneously her face and throat flamed scarlet.
“*Your* room, Mr. Lanyard!”
Her tone so convincingly voiced shame and horror that his heart misgave him. Not that alone, but the girl was very good to look upon.
“I’m sure,” he began soothingly; “it doesn’t matter. You mistook a door﻿—”
“But you don’t understand!” She shuddered.﻿ ﻿… “This dreadful habit! And I was hoping I had outgrown it! How can I ever explain﻿—?”
“Believe me, Miss Bannon, you need explain nothing.”

===Chinese===
姑娘退了一步——又退了两步——低低吐出一个恍然的单音，一声猛然抽出的「*哦！*」。与此同时，她的脸和颈项霎时烧起一片猩红。
「*你的*房间，兰亚德先生！」
她的语调把羞惭与骇异表达得那样逼真，教他心里老大不安。不单如此，这姑娘还生得实在耐看。
「我担保，」他开始柔声劝慰，「不打紧的。你走岔了门——」
「可你不明白呀！」她打了个寒噤。……「这要命的习惯！我还巴望着随着年龄增长早把它摆脱了呢！这教我怎么解释——？」
「相信我，班农小姐，你什么都不必解释。」

===Original===
“But I must﻿ ﻿… I wish to﻿ ﻿… I can’t bear to let you think﻿ ﻿… But surely you can make allowances for sleepwalking!”
To this appeal he could at first return nothing more intelligent than a dazed repetition of the phrase.
So that was how﻿ ﻿… Why hadn’t he thought of it before? Ever since he had turned on the lights, he had been subjectively busy trying to invest her presence there with some plausible excuse. But somnambulism had never once entered his mind. And in his stupidity, at pains though he had been to render his words inoffensive, he had been guilty of constructive incivility.
In his turn, Lanyard coloured warmly.
“I beg your pardon,” he muttered.
The girl paid no attention; she seemed self-absorbed, thinking only of herself and the anomalous position into which her infirmity had tricked her. When she did speak, her words came swiftly:

===Chinese===
「可我必须……我想要……我受不了让你以为……不过，梦游这种事，你总可以多多包涵的吧！」
对这声恳求，他起初只答得上一句傻愣愣的复读，再拿不出更聪明的回应。
原来如此……他怎么早没想到？打从开亮灯那刻起，他就在心里忙个不停，想替她在此现身安一个说得通的理由。可梦游一节压根不曾入过他的脑海。而他竟愚钝至此——尽管已经字斟句酌避免失礼——终究还是构成了「推定失礼」之罪。
这一回，轮到兰亚德脸上升起一片热红。
「请你原谅，」他讷讷道。
姑娘浑没留意；她似乎自顾自出神，一心只想着自己，想着这毛病将她哄入的尴尬境地。待她再开口，话便急急涌出：

===Original===
“You see﻿ ﻿… I was so frightened! I found myself suddenly standing up in darkness, just as if I had jumped out of bed at some alarm; and then I heard somebody enter the room and shut the door stealthily﻿ ﻿… Oh, please understand me!”
“But I do, Miss Bannon﻿—quite.”
“I am so ashamed﻿—”
“Please don’t consider it that way.”
“But now that you know﻿—you don’t think﻿—”
“My dear Miss Bannon!”

===Chinese===
「你瞧……我吓坏了！我发现自己一下子站在黑地里，就像被什么警报惊得跳下了床；接着又听见有人进屋、蹑手蹑脚地掩上门……哦，请你千万体谅我！」
「我体谅的，班农小姐——完全体谅。」
「我太难为情了——」
「请别往那上头想。」
「可如今你知道了——你不会以为——」
「我亲爱的班农小姐！」

===Original===
“But it must be so hard to credit! Even I﻿ ﻿… Why, it’s more than a year since this last happened. Of course, as a child, it was almost a habit; they had to watch me all the time. Once﻿ ﻿… But that doesn’t matter. I *am* so sorry.”
“You really mustn’t worry,” Lanyard insisted. “It’s all quite natural﻿—such things do happen﻿—are happening all the time﻿—”
“But I don’t want you﻿—”
“I am nobody, Miss Bannon. Besides I shan’t mention the matter to a soul. And if ever I am fortunate enough to meet you again, I shall have forgotten it completely﻿—believe me.”
There was convincing sincerity in his tone. The girl looked down, as though abashed.
“You are very good,” she murmured, moving toward the door.

===Chinese===
「可这实在教人难以置信！连我自己……嗐，上一回出这种事，还是一年多以前。当然喽，小时候这几乎是家常便饭；他们只得时时刻刻盯着我。有一回……不过那不相干。我*实在*抱歉得很。」
「你真不必担心，」兰亚德执意说道，「这一切再自然不过——这类事时有发生——时时刻刻都在发生呢——」
「可我不愿你——」
「我是个无足轻重的人，班农小姐。何况这事我对谁也不会提起。倘若我有幸还能与你重逢，那时我必已把它忘了个一干二净——请相信我。」
他的语气里有一种令人信服的诚挚。姑娘垂下眼帘，仿佛有些赧然。
「你人真好，」她低声道，朝门口走去。

===Original===
“I am very fortunate.”
Her glance of surprise was question enough.
“To be able to treasure this much of your confidence,” he explained with a tentative smile.
She was near the door; he opened it for her, but cautioned her with a gesture and a whispered word: “Wait. I’ll make sure nobody’s about.”
He stepped noiselessly into the hall and paused an instant, looking right and left, listening.
The girl advanced to the threshold and there checked, hesitant, eyeing him anxiously.

===Chinese===
「我是非常幸运。」
她惊讶的一瞥，已足够代替问句。
「有幸把你这一点点信任珍藏起来，」他带着一丝试探的微笑解释道。
她已到门边；他替她拉开门，却又打个手势、附耳低语叮嘱：「等等。我去看看外面有没有人。」
他悄然迈进走廊，顿了一顿，左右张望，侧耳谛听。
姑娘挪到门槛处便停住，迟疑不决，惶惶地拿眼睛望着他。

===Original===
He nodded reassurance: “All right﻿—coast’s clear!”
But she delayed one moment more.
“It’s you who are mistaken,” she whispered, colouring again beneath his regard, in which admiration could not well be lacking, “It is I who am fortunate﻿—to have met a﻿—gentleman.”
Her diffident smile, together with the candour of her eyes, embarrassed him to such extent that for the moment he was unable to frame a reply.
“Good night,” she whispered﻿—“and thank you, thank you!”
Her room was at the far end of the corridor. She gained its threshold in one swift dash, noiseless save for the silken whisper of her garments, turned, flashed him a final look that left him with the thought that novelists did not always exaggerate, that eyes could shine like stars.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
他点头示意宽心：「好了——平安无事！」
可她又耽搁了片刻。
「弄错了的是你，」她悄声道，在他的注视下再度红了脸——那注视里断然少不了几分倾慕，「幸运的是我——能遇见一位——君子。」
她那羞怯的微笑，连同眼中的坦白，窘得他一时竟组织不出一句答话来。
「晚安，」她悄声说，「还有，谢谢你，谢谢你！」
她的房间在走廊那一头。她疾步一蹿便到了门槛，除了一身衣衫绸缎的窸窣细语，悄没声儿；随即回身，朝他投来飞快的最后一瞥，教他心头存下一个念头：小说家的话并非总是夸张——人的眼睛，当真能亮如星辰。……

===Original===
Her door closed softly.
Lanyard shook his head as if to dissipate a swarm of annoying thoughts, and went back into his own bedchamber.
He was quite content with the explanation the girl had given, but being the slave of a methodical and pertinacious habit of mind, spent five busy minutes examining his room and all that it contained with a perseverance that would have done credit to a Frenchman searching for a mislaid sou.
If pressed, he would have been put to it to name what he sought or thought to find. What he did find was that nothing had been tampered with and nothing more﻿—not even so much as a dainty, lace-trimmed wisp of sheer linen bearing the lady’s monogram and exhaling a faint but individual perfume.

===Chinese===
她的房门轻轻掩上了。
兰亚德摇了摇头，像是要驱散一大群恼人的念头，转身回自己房里去。
对姑娘给出的解释他相当满意；但他素来受制于一种井然有序、锲而不舍的心性，还是忙碌碌花去五分钟，把房间和房中一切逐一检视，那股执着劲儿，便是安在满屋寻找一枚丢失的苏（sou）的法国人身上，也足增光彩。
若真有人逼问，他也说不上来自己找的什么、指望发现什么。他实际查明的只是：什么都没被动过，此外再无别的——连一件雅致的、镶蕾丝边的薄麻小物也不曾多出来，那种绣着小姐姓名花押、透着一缕清淡而独有香气的小物件。

===Original===
Which, when he came to consider it, seemed hardly playing the game by the book.
As for Roddy, Lanyard wasted several minutes, off and on, listening attentively at the communicating door; but if the detective had stopped snoring, his respiration was loud enough in that quiet hour, a sound of harsh monotony.
True, that proved nothing; but Lanyard, after the fiasco of his first attempt to catch his enemy awake, was no more disposed to be hypercritical; he had his fill of being ingenious and profound. And when presently he again left Troyon’s (this time without troubling the repose of the concierge) it was with the reflection that, if Roddy were really playing possum, he was welcome to whatever he could find of interest in the quarters of Michael Lanyard.

===Chinese===
细想起来，这可算不上照章法行事。
至于罗迪，兰亚德断断续续费了好几分钟，凑在那扇连通门上凝神谛听；那侦探纵然鼾声停了，呼吸声在这夜阑人静的时分也足够响亮，粗厉而单调，一声接一声。
不错，这什么也证明不了；但兰亚德自从头一回想趁仇家醒着逮他一下而砸了锅，便再无意吹毛求疵；耍聪明、装高深，他已经受够了。不多时他再度离开特鲁瓦旅馆（这一回没去搅扰门房的清梦），心里想着：罗迪若果真在装睡，那么迈克尔·兰亚德的房间里凡有什么可看之物，尽管请他随意。

## XXI Apostate / 二十一、叛帮者

===Original===
He was singularly free from any sort of exultation over the manner in which he had at once compassed his own escape and brought down catastrophe upon his self-appointed murderers; his mood was quick with wonder and foreboding and bewilderment. The more closely he examined the affair, the more strange and inexplicable it bulked in his understanding. He had not thought to defy the Pack and get off lightly; but he had looked for no such overt effort at disciplining him so long as he kept out of the way and suspended his criminal activities. An unwilling recruit is a potential traitor in the camp; and retired competition isn’t to be feared. So it seemed that Wertheimer hadn’t believed his protestations, or else Bannon had rejected the report which must have been made him by the girl. In either case, the Pack had not waited for the Lone Wolf to prove his insincerity; it hadn’t bothered to declare war; it had simply struck; with less warning than a rattlesnake gives, it had struck﻿—out of the dark﻿—at his back.
And so﻿—Lanyard swore grimly﻿—even so would he strike, now that it was his turn, now that his hour dawned.
But he would have given much for a clue to the riddle. Why must he be saddled with this necessity of striking in self-defence? Why had this feud been forced upon him, who asked nothing better than to be let alone? He told himself it wasn’t altogether the professional jealousy of De Morbihan, Popinot and Wertheimer; it was the strange, rancorous spite that animated Bannon.
But, again, why?

===Chinese===
对于自己一举脱身、反教大祸降临到那帮自封的刽子手头上的手段，他竟没有半分沾沾自喜；心头翻腾的，尽是惊奇、凶兆与惶惑。这桩事他越是细究，越显得离奇费解，堵在心头挥之不去。他原不曾指望公然反抗「群狼」还能不受惩处；但只要他退避三舍、就此罢手，他们竟还会这样明火执仗地来「整肃」他，这是他没有料到的。不情愿入伙的新兵，终究是营中潜在的叛徒；洗手不干的对头，也就无可畏惧。如此说来，不是维特海默不曾信他的剖白，便是班农驳回了那姑娘必已呈上的报告。无论哪一种，「群狼」都没有坐等独狼自行露出虚情假意；连宣战也嫌费事，它干脆就是一击；连响尾蛇出击前那一点警示都不给，从黑暗里，朝他背后，便是一击。
于是——兰亚德咬牙切齿地立下誓来——轮到他时，他也要这样出手；如今正轮到他，如今他的时辰已然破晓。
可这谜团若能寻得半点线索，他情愿付出不菲的代价。为什么偏偏是他，要背上这不得不出手自卫的重负？他别无所求，只求人家放他清静，这场冤仇却为何偏要强加于他？他对自己说，这不尽是德·莫尔比昂、波皮诺与维特海默的同行嫉妒；更是那股鼓荡在班农胸中、古怪而怨毒的恶意。
可是，这又是为了什么？

===Original===
Could it be that Bannon so resented the aid and encouragement Lanyard had afforded the girl in her abortive attempt to escape? Or was it, perhaps, that Bannon held Lanyard responsible for the arrest and death of Greggs?
Could it be possible that there was really anything substantial at the bottom of Wertheimer’s wild yarn about the pretentiously named “International Underworld Unlimited”? Was this really a demonstration of purpose to crush out competition﻿—“and hang the expense”?
Or was there some less superficially tangible motive to be sought? Did Bannon entertain some secret, personal animus against Michael Lanyard himself as distinguished from the Lone Wolf?
Debating these questions from every angle but to no end, he worked himself into a fine fury of exasperation, vowing he would consummate this one final coup, sequestrate himself in England until the affair had blown over, and in his own good time return to Paris to expose De Morbihan (presuming he survived the wreck in the Bois) exterminate Popinot utterly, drive Wertheimer into permanent retirement at Dartmoor, and force an accounting from Bannon though it were surrendered together with that invalid’s last wheezing breaths.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
莫非是班农恨透了兰亚德——恨他在那姑娘出逃未遂一事里施与的援手与鼓劲？又或者，是班农把格雷格斯被捕身死这笔账，记在了兰亚德头上？
维特海默曾天花乱坠地吹过一个名号堂皇的「国际黑社会无限公司」（International Underworld Unlimited），莫非那番鬼话底下，竟真有些实在的底子？这当真是存心做给人看，要碾碎一切竞争——「不惜血本，在所不计」？
还是说，另有某种不那么浮在面上的动机可寻？莫非班农对迈克尔·兰亚德这个人——撇开「独狼」的名号不谈——暗地里挟着什么私怨？
这些问题他翻来覆去、四面八方地辩了个够，终归一无所获，反倒把自己激成一团熊熊的恼火，赌咒发誓：这最后一票得手之后，他便隐居英国，待风头过去，再从容回到巴黎，揭穿德·莫尔比昂（前提是他在布洛涅林苑那场车祸中留得性命），把波皮诺斩尽杀绝，把维特海默打发到达特穆尔（Dartmoor）监狱里颐养天年，再逼班农交出这笔账——哪怕那账目是随着那位病夫最后几声喘息一并吐出来的。……

===Original===
In this temper he arrived, past one in the morning, under the walls of the hôtel Omber, and prudently selected a new point of attack. In the course of his preliminary examinations of the walls, it hadn’t escaped him that their brick-and-plaster construction was in bad repair; he had marked down several spots where the weather had eaten the outer coat of plaster completely away. At one of these, midway between the avenue and the junction of the side-streets, he hesitated.
As he had foreseen, the mortar that bound the bricks together was all dry and crumbling; it was no great task to work one of them loose, making a foothold from which he might grasp with a gloved hand the glass-toothed curbing, cast his ulster across this for further protection, and swing himself bodily atop the wall.
But there, momentarily, he paused in doubt and trembling. In that exposed and comfortless perch, the lifeless street on one hand, the black mystery of the neglected park on the other, he was seized and shaken by a sudden revulsion of feeling like a sickness of his very soul. Physical fear had nothing to do with this, for he was quite alone and unobserved; had it been otherwise faculties trained through a lifetime to such work as this and now keyed to concert pitch would not have failed to give warning of whatever danger his grosser senses might have overlooked.
Notwithstanding, he was afraid as though Fear’s very self had laid hold of his soul by the heels and would not let it go until its vision of itself was absolute. He was afraid with a great fear such as he had never dreamed to know; who knew well the wincing of the flesh from risk of pain, the shuddering of the spirit in the shadow of death, and horror such as had gripped him that morning in poor Roddy’s bedchamber.

===Chinese===
揣着这腔心绪，凌晨一点过后，他来到了欧姆贝宅的高墙之下，并谨慎地择定一处新的突破口。先前勘察围墙时他早留意到：这砖砌灰抹的墙体年久失修；有好几处外层的灰皮已被风雨剥蚀得干干净净，他都一一记在心里。在其中一处——恰在大道与两侧街巷交会口之间的中段——他迟疑地停了下来。
不出所料，胶合砖块的灰浆尽已干透酥散；撬松其中一块并非难事，垫在脚下借力，戴着手套的一只手便攀住了墙头那玻璃利齿森然的压顶，再把长毛粗呢大衣铺将上去添一层护垫，整个人一荡便上了墙顶。
可就在那上头，他一时竟踌躇迟疑，微微发颤。在那样一个四面无遮、无处着力的栖身高处，一手边是死气沉沉的街衢，另一手边是荒园黑洞洞的谜团，一阵情感的陡然逆转攫住了他、摇撼着他，仿佛灵魂本身害起病来。这与肉体的惧怕毫不相干，因为他分明孑然一身、无人窥见；倘若不然，那副为此等行当磨砺了一生、此刻又绷到临战音高的诸般官能，断不会不预先示警，把他粗钝五官可能漏掉的险情一一补上。
尽管如此，他还是怕，仿佛「恐惧」的本尊攥住了他灵魂的脚跟，非把它对自身的观照修到圆满绝对，便决不放它走。他怀着一种做梦也不曾领略过的大恐惧——皮肉之怕痛楚临身时的抽缩，心神之临死亡阴影时的战栗，还有那天清晨在可怜的罗迪卧房里攫住他的那阵恐怖，这些滋味他全都识得。

===Original===
But none of these had in any way taught him the measure of such fear as now possessed him, so absolute that he quaked like a naked soul in the inexorable presence of the Eternal.
He was afraid of himself, in panic terror of that ego which tenanted the shell of functioning, sensitive stuff called Michael Lanyard: he was afraid of the strange, silent, incomprehensible Self lurking occult in him, that masked mysterious Self which in its inscrutable whim could make him fine or make him base, that Self impalpable and elusive as any shadow yet invincibly strong, his master and his fate, in one the grave of Yesterday, the cup of Today, the womb of Tomorrow.﻿ ﻿…
He looked up at the tired, dull faces of those old dwellings that loomed across the way with blind and lightless windows, sleeping without suspicion that he had stolen in among them﻿—the grim and deadly thing that walked by night, the Lone Wolf, creature of pillage and rapine, scourged slave of that Self which knew no law.﻿ ﻿…
Then slowly that obsession lifted like the passing of a nightmare; and with a start, a little shiver and a sigh, Lanyard roused and went on to do the bidding of his Self for its unfathomable ends.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
但这一切都不曾教他掂出此刻盘踞他心头这种恐惧的分量——它如此绝对，教他浑身战栗，恰似赤裸的魂灵立于永恒那不容分说的临在之前。
他怕的是他自己，惶惶不可终日于那个寄居在名为迈克尔·兰亚德的、官能灵敏的躯壳里的自我；他怕的是那个潜藏身内、幽秘难解的「本我」——那戴面具的神秘自我，凭其莫测的兴之所至，能令他高贵，也能令他卑污；那个自我虚缥如影、难觅难即，却又无坚不摧，是他的主宰，也是他的命运，一身而三任：「昨日」的坟墓，「今日」的杯盏，「明日」的母胎。……
他抬眼仰望街对面那些老宅疲倦而黯淡的面容——瞎了似的无光窗户森然耸峙，睡得毫无疑心，全不知他已潜身其间——那个趁夜行走的阴森致命之物，独狼，劫掠与强取的造物，被那目无法度的本我所鞭笞驱策的奴隶。……
随后，那魔魇般的缠缚缓缓消散，如一场噩梦终于行过；兰亚德猛然惊觉，打个轻颤，叹一口气，起身继续去奉行本我的差遣，成全它那深不可测的图谋。……

===Original===
Dropping silently to the soft, damp turf, he made himself one with the shadows of the park, as mute, intangible and fugitive as they, until presently coming out beneath the stars, on an open lawn running up to the library wing of the hôtel, he approached a shallow stone balcony which jutted forth eight feet above the lawn﻿—an elevation so inconsiderable that, with one bound grasping its stone balustrade, the adventurer was upon it in a brace of seconds.
Nor did the long French windows that opened on the balcony offer him any real hindrance: a penknife quickly removed the dried putty round one small, lozenge-shaped pane, then pried out the pane itself; a hand through this space readily found and turned the latch; a cautious pressure opened the two wings far enough to admit his body; and﻿—he stood inside the library.
He had made no sound; and thanks to thorough familiarity with the ground, he needed no light. The screen of cinnabar afforded all the protection he required; and because he meant to accomplish his purpose and be out of the house with the utmost expedition, he didn’t trouble to explore beyond a swift, casual review of the adjoining salons.
The clock was chiming the three-quarters as he knelt behind the screen and grasped the combination-knob.

===Chinese===
他悄然落向松软湿润的草地，把自己化入园中的暗影，同它们一样缄默、无形、倏忽难捉；不多时便来到星光下的一片开阔草坪，草坪一路铺展到宅邸书房翼楼跟前。他凑近一方浅浅的石砌露台——露台从墙身探出八英尺，凌于草坪之上——离地如此之低，这位冒险家纵身一跃，攀住石栏杆，不过两秒钟工夫便已立定台上。
通上露台的落地长窗也没真给他添多少阻碍：一把铅笔刀很快剜净一小格菱形玻璃四周的干油灰，把玻璃起了出来；一只手从豁口探入，轻易便摸到并拧开窗闩；谨慎地稍一加力，两扇窗敞开到刚容身子通过的宽度——于是，他已立在书房之中。
他没有弄出半点声响；亏得对这地方烂熟于胸，连灯也不必点。那架朱砂屏风给他的遮蔽已绰乎有余；又因他打定主意办成正事便以最快的速度出宅，除了对毗邻几间客厅匆匆一瞥，他也懒得再去探看。
时钟正敲三刻，他在屏风后跪下，握住了密码锁的旋钮。

===Original===
But he did not turn it. That mellow music died out slowly, and left him transfixed, there in the silence and gloom, his eyes staring wide into blackness at nothing, his jaw set and rigid, his forehead knotted and damp with sweat, his hands so clenched that the nails bit deep into his palms; while he looked back over the abyss yawning between the Lone Wolf of tonight and the man who had, within the week, knelt in that spot in company with the woman he loved, bent on making restitution that his soul might be saved through her faith in him.
He was visited by clear vision of himself: the thief caught in his crime by his conscience﻿—or whatever it was, what for want of a better name he must call his conscience: this thing within him that revolted from his purpose, mutinied against the dictates of his Self, and stopped his hand from reaping the harvest of his cunning and daring; this sense of honour and of honesty that in a few brief days had grown more dear to him than all else in life, knitting itself inextricably into the fibre of his being, so that to deny it were against Nature.﻿ ﻿…
He closed his eyes to shut out the accusing vision, and knelt on, unstirring, though torn this way and that in the conflict of man’s dual nature.

===Chinese===
可他没有转动它。那圆润的钟鸣缓缓消歇，把他钉在原地，钉在寂静与幽暗里：双眼圆睁，直望进黑暗中的虚空；牙关咬紧，僵直如铁；额角拧作一团，汗水涔涔；双手攥得指甲深掐进掌心——他回望那道豁开的深渊，深渊的一边是今夜的独狼，另一边是不出一个礼拜之前跪在同一位置的那个人：那人身边伴着他所爱的女人，一心只想归还赃物，好教自己的灵魂因她的信而得救。
一幅清晰的幻象造访了他：他看见自己，一个行窃之时被良心当场人赃并获的贼——姑且称它良心吧，管它究竟是什么，只缘想不出更好的名字，他只得管它叫良心：那是他体内的什么东西，背弃他的图谋，抗命于本我的号令，止住他的手，不许他收割狡黠与胆魄的收成；那是一种荣誉感与诚实感，短短几日之内，已变得比生命中其余一切都要亲贵，丝丝缕缕织进他生命的纤维，再难剥离，以至于否认它便属悖逆天性。……
他闭上双眼，想把那幅兴师问罪的幻象挡在外面，兀自跪着，一动不动，任凭人性两半的交战把他撕过来、扯过去。

===Original===
Minutes passed without his knowledge.
But in time he grew more calm; his hands relaxed, the muscles of his brow smoothed out, he breathed more slowly and deeply; his set lips parted and a profound sigh whispered in the stillness. A great weariness upon him, he rose slowly and heavily from the floor, and stood erect, free at last and forever from that ancient evil which so long had held his soul in bondage.
And in that moment of victory, through the deep hush reigning in the house, he detected an incautious footfall on the parquetry of the reception-hall.

===Chinese===
几分钟过去了，他浑然不觉。
但渐渐地，他平静下来；双手松开，眉间纠结的肌肉舒展，呼吸也放缓放深；紧抿的双唇启开，一声深长的叹息在静谧中低低掠过。一阵巨大的倦意罩上他身，他缓慢而沉重地从地板上立起，站直了身子——终于、而且永远地，摆脱了那长久把他灵魂锁在枷押之中的太古邪恶。
而就在得胜的这一刻，透过满宅笼罩的深深静默，他察觉到接待大厅的镶木地板上，响起了一声失于轻率的脚步。

## XII Awakening / 十二、梦醒时分

===Original===
It was late afternoon when Lanyard wakened from sleep so deep and dreamless that nothing could have induced it less potent than sheer systemic exhaustion, at once nervous, muscular and mental.
A profound and stifling lethargy benumbed his senses. There was stupor in his brain, and all his limbs ached dully. He opened dazed eyes upon blank darkness. In his ears a vast silence pulsed.
And in that strange moment of awakening he was conscious of no individuality: it was, for the time, as if he had passed in slumber from one existence to another, sloughing en passant all his threefold personality as Marcel Troyon, Michael Lanyard, and the Lone Wolf. Had any one of these names been uttered in his hearing just then it would have meant nothing to him﻿—or little more than nothing: he was for the time being merely *himself*, a shell of sensations enclosing dull embers of vitality.
For several minutes he lay without moving, curiously intrigued by this riddle of identity: it was but slowly that his mind, like a blind hand groping round a dark chamber, picked up the filaments of memory.

===Chinese===
兰亚德从沉酣无梦的睡眠中醒来时，已是日暮时分；那一觉又深又沉、了无梦影，若非神经、筋肉与心智一并耗竭净尽的全身性疲乏，任凭什么也催不出这样一场睡眠。
一种深重而令人窒息的倦怠麻木了他的感官。他脑中一团昏沉，四肢无不隐隐作痛。他睁开惺忪的双眼，面对的是一片空茫的黑暗。耳际搏动着浩大的寂静。
在这奇异的初醒时刻，他浑然不觉自己有何身份：一时之间，仿佛他已在酣睡中从一个存在渡入另一个存在，顺路（*en passant*）蜕尽了身为马塞尔·特鲁瓦、迈克尔·兰亚德与独狼的三重人格。这些名字里随便哪一个，此刻若在他耳边响起，对他都将毫无意义——或者说，比毫无意义强不了多少：他眼下只是*他自己*，一副感知的躯壳，裹着几星暗淡的生命余烬。
一连好几分钟他躺着不动，被这道身份之谜勾起了说不清的兴趣：他的心智如同一只盲手在暗室里四处摸索，只是缓缓地、一根一根拾起记忆的游丝。

===Original===
One by one the connections were renewed, the circuits closed.﻿ ﻿…
But, singularly enough in his understanding, his first thought was of the girl upstairs in the studio, unconsciously his prisoner and hostage﻿—rather than of himself, who lay there, heavy with loss of sleep, languidly trying to realize himself.
For he was no more as he had been. Wherein the difference lay he couldn’t say, but that a difference existed he was persuaded﻿—that he had changed, that some strange reaction in the chemistry of his nature had taken place during slumber. It was as if sleep had not only repaired the ravages of fatigue upon the tissues of his brain and body, but had mended the tissues of his soul as well. His thoughts were fluent in fresh channels, his interests no longer the interests of the Michael Lanyard he had known, no longer self-centred, the interests of the absolute ego. He was concerned less for himself, even now when he should be most gravely so, than for another, for the girl Lucia Bannon, who was nothing to him, whom he had yet to know for twenty-four hours, but of whom he could not cease to think if he would.
It was her plight that perturbed him, from which he sought an outlet﻿—never his own.

===Chinese===
一个个衔接重新接上，一道道回路次第闭合。……
然而——依他自己想来也够奇怪——他的头一个念头竟是楼上画室里那姑娘，懵然不知自己做着他的囚徒与人质；而不是他自己，尽管他正躺在那儿，带着一脑子睡眠的沉重，恹恹地试图认清自身。
因为他已不复是从前的他了。差异何在他说不上来，但差异确确实实存在，这一点他深信不疑——他变了，酣睡之中，他天性的化学起了某种奇异的反应。仿佛睡眠不仅修复了疲劳在他脑与肢体组织上留下的创痕，更把他灵魂的组织也缝补了一番。他的思绪在崭新的河道里流利地奔涌；他的关切已不再是他所熟识的那个迈克尔·兰亚德的关切，不再以自我为圆心，不再是那个绝对自我的关切。他为自己操心得少了——哪怕眼下本该是他最该为自己焦心的时候——为另一个人操心得多：为露西娅·班农那姑娘，她于他本属陌路，相识还不满二十四小时，可他纵想不去想她，也办不到。
令他心神不宁的是她的处境，他为之苦苦谋求出路的，也是她的处境——而从不他自己的。

===Original===
Yet his own was desperate enough.﻿ ﻿…
Baffled and uneasy, he at length bethought him of his watch. But its testimony seemed incredible: surely the hour could not be five in the afternoon!﻿—surely he could not have slept so close upon a full round of the clock!
And if it were so, what of the girl? Had she, too, so sorely needed sleep that the brief November day had dawned and waned without her knowledge?
That question was one to rouse him: in an instant he was up and groping his way through the gloom that enshrouded bedchamber and dining-room to the staircase door in the hall. He found this fast enough, its key still safe in his pocket, and unlocking it quietly, shot the beam of his flash-lamp up that dark well to the door at the top; which was tight shut.

===Chinese===
然而他自己的处境也已够凶险。……
又惑又躁之间，他终于想起了怀表。可表上的证词简直叫人难以置信：这时辰怎么可能是下午五点！——他总不至于一觉睡得只差一点点，就兜满了整整一圈钟点！
倘若果真如此，那姑娘又当如何？她是否也困倦到了那般田地，以致短短十一月的白昼从破晓到消磨殆尽，她都浑然不觉？
这一问足以唤他起身：转眼间他已起来，摸黑穿过笼罩着卧室与餐室的昏暗，走到门厅里那道楼梯门前。他很快就摸到了门——钥匙还安然揣在口袋里——他悄没声儿地开了锁，掀亮手电，把光柱射上那口黑洞洞的楼梯井，直照顶层那扇门；门关得严严实实。

===Original===
For several moments he attended to a taciturn silence broken by never a sound to indicate that he wasn’t a lonely tenant of the little dwelling, then irresolutely lifted a foot to the first step﻿—and withdrew it. If she continued to sleep, why disturb her? He had much to do in the way of thinking things out; and that was a process more easily performed in solitude.
Leaving the door ajar, then, he turned to one of the front windows, parted its draperies, and peered out, over the little garden and through the iron ribs of the gate, to the street, where a single gas-lamp, glimmering within a dull golden halo of mist, made visible the scant length of the impasse Stanislas, empty, rain-swept, desolate.
The rain persisted with no hint of failing purpose.﻿ ﻿…
Something in the dreary emptiness of that brief vista deepened the shadow in his mood and knitted a careworn frown into his brows.

===Chinese===
有好一会儿他谛听着那阵沉默——沉默得没有半点声息，可证明这小宅里住的并非他孤身一人——随后他踌躇着抬起一只脚，踏上第一级楼梯——又缩了回来。她若还在睡，何必去搅扰？他有一大堆事情需要想个明白；而这桩功夫，独处时做来最是容易。
于是他把门虚掩着，转身走向一扇临街的窗，拨开帘幔，朝外张望：越过小小的园子，透过铁栅门的骨条，望向街道——那里一盏煤气灯在昏黄雾气的光晕里幽幽发亮，照出斯坦尼斯拉斯死巷（impasse Stanislas）短短一截巷身，空空荡荡，任雨横扫，满目荒凉。
雨下个不停，丝毫没有罢休的意思。……
那短短一瞥里凄凉的空旷，有什么东西加深了他心头的阴翳，把一副忧心忡忡的愁容拧上了他的眉间。

===Original===
Abstractedly he sought the kitchen and, making a light, washed up at the tap, then foraged for breakfast. Persistence turned up a spirit-stove, a half-bottle of methylated, a packet of tea, a tin or two of biscuit, as many more of potted meats: leftovers from the artist’s stock, dismally scant and uninviting in array. With these he made the discovery that he was half-famished, and found no reason to believe that the girl would be in any better case. An expedition to the nearest charcuterie was indicated; but after he had searched for and found an old raincoat of Solon’s, Lanyard decided against leaving the girl alone. Pending her appearance, he filled the spirit-stove, put the kettle on to boil, and lighting a cigarette, sat himself down to watch the pot and excogitate his several problems.
In a fashion uncommonly clearheaded, even for him, he assembled all the facts bearing upon their predicament, his and Lucia Bannon’s, jointly and individually, and dispassionately pondered them.﻿ ﻿…
But insensibly his thoughts reverted to their exotic phase of his awakening, drifting into such introspection as he seldom indulged, and led him far from the immediate riddle, by strange ways to a revelation altogether unpresaged and a resolve still more revolutionary.
A look of wonder flickered in his brooding eyes; and clipped between two fingers, his cigarette grew a long ash, let it fall, and burned down to a stump so short that the coal almost scorched his flesh. He dropped it and crushed out the fire with his heel, all unwittingly.

===Chinese===
他心不在焉地寻到厨房，点了灯，在水龙头下胡乱洗漱一番，便着手搜罗早饭。一番锲而不舍的翻检，翻出了一只酒精炉、半瓶变性酒精、一包茶叶、一两听饼干，还有同样一两听肉酱罐头：都是那位画家存货里的残余，摆开来寒伧得可怜，引不起半点胃口。可借着这些他才发现自己已饿了个半死，也没有理由相信那姑娘的情况能好到哪儿去。看来该往最近的熟食店（*charcuterie*）跑一趟；可是等他翻寻出梭伦（Solon）的一件旧雨衣之后，兰亚德还是拿定主意，不肯让姑娘独自留下。趁她还没露面，他给酒精炉添足了燃料，坐上水壶烧水，点起一支香烟，坐下来守着壶，细想他那几桩难题。
以一种即便对他而言也属罕见的清明，他把与他俩困境相关的事实——他的与露西娅·班农的，合起来算与分开来算——统统归拢到一处，不动声色地细细思量。……
可不知不觉间，他的思绪又飘回了初醒时那奇异的境界，沉入那种他极少涉足的内省，引着他远远离开眼前的谜题，循着条条奇僻的小径，走到一个全然未曾逆料的启示面前，以及一个更要天翻地覆的决心跟前。
他那双沉思的眼睛里掠过一抹惊异的神色；夹在两指间的香烟积起长长一段烟灰，簌地跌落，又一路烧到只剩短短一截，烟头几乎燎到皮肉。他把它一丢，用脚跟碾灭了火——浑然不觉自己在做什么。

===Original===
Slowly but irresistibly his world was turning over beneath his feet.﻿ ﻿…
The sound of a footfall recalled him as from an immeasurable remove; he looked up to see Lucia at pause upon the threshold, and rose slowly, with effort recollecting himself and marshalling his wits against the emergency foreshadowed by her attitude.
Tense with indignation, quick with disdain, she demanded, without any preface whatever: “Why did you lock me in?”
He stammered unhappily: “I beg your pardon﻿—”

===Chinese===
他的天地正在脚下缓缓地、却不可抗拒地翻覆过来。……
一阵脚步声把他从无可计量的遥远之处唤了回来；他抬头看见露西娅停在门口，便缓缓起身，努力收摄心神，调集全部心智，去应付她那神色所预示的变故。
她满腔义愤，一脸鄙夷，开门见山便质问：「你为什么把我锁在屋里？」
他局促地讷讷道：「请你原谅——」

===Original===
“*Why* did you lock me in?”
“I’m sorry﻿—”
“Why did you﻿—”
But she interrupted herself to stamp her foot emphatically; and he caught her up on the echo of that:

===Chinese===
「你*到底*为什么把我锁在屋里？」
「对不起——」
「你为什么——」
可她话到中途自己截断了自己，狠狠一顿脚；他便趁着那一声的余响接口道：

===Original===
“If you must know, because I wasn’t trusting you.”
Her eyes darkened ominously: “Yet you insisted I should trust you!”
“The circumstances aren’t parallel: you’re not a notorious malefactor, wanted by the police of every capital in Europe, hounded by rivals to boot﻿—fighting for life, liberty and”﻿—he laughed shortly﻿—“the pursuit of happiness!”
She caught her breath sharply﻿—whether with dismay or mere surprise at his frankness he couldn’t tell.

===Chinese===
「你非要知道不可的话——因为我不敢信任你。」
她的眼珠不祥地暗了暗：「可你偏一口咬定，要我信任你！」
「情形不可同日而语：你可不是什么恶名昭彰的凶徒，被欧洲各国首都的警察通缉，还外加仇家的穷追不舍——正为着性命、自由，还有」——他短促地一笑——「追求幸福而苦斗！」
她猛地倒抽一口气——是惊惶，还是仅仅为他这份坦白吃惊，他无从判断。

===Original===
“Are you?” she demanded quickly.
“Am I what?”
“What you’ve just said﻿—”
“A crook﻿—and all that? Miss Bannon, you *know* it!”

===Chinese===
「你是吗？」她急急追问。
「我是什么？」
「你刚才说的那号人——」
「贼——还有那一整套？班农小姐，这你*明明知道*！」

===Original===
“The Lone Wolf?”
“You’ve known it all along. De Morbihan told you﻿—or else your father. Or, it may be, you were shrewd enough to guess it from De Morbihan’s bragging in the restaurant. At all events, it’s plain enough, nothing but desire to find proof to identify me with the Lone Wolf took you to my room last night﻿—whether for your personal satisfaction or at the instigation of Bannon﻿—just as nothing less than disgust with what was going on made you run away from such intolerable associations.﻿ ﻿… Though, at that, I don’t believe you even guessed how unspeakably vicious those were!”
He paused and waited, anticipating furious denial or refutation; such would, indeed, have been the logical development of the temper in which she had come down to confront him.
Rather than this, she seemed calmed and sobered by his charge; far from resenting it, disposed to concede its justice; anger deserted her expression, leaving it intent and grave. She came quietly into the room and faced him squarely across the table.

===Chinese===
「独狼？」
「你早从头到尾都知道了。德·莫尔比昂告诉了你——要么就是你父亲。要不然，也许是你够机灵，光凭德·莫尔比昂在餐厅里的大话便猜着了。无论如何，事情明明白白：昨夜引你去我房间的，无非是想寻个证据，把我和独狼认作一人——不论是为了你个人的满足，还是奉了班农先生的指使；正如叫你从那伙教人无法容忍的党羽中逃开的，也无非是对眼前勾当的深恶痛绝。……不过话虽如此，那班人下作到何等不可言说的地步，我看你连猜都猜不着！」
他顿住话头，静待一场怒不可遏的否认或驳斥；照她下来对质时的那副火气，顺理成章的发展本该如此。
孰料非但如此，她倒像被他这一番指责安抚得平静、清醒了；非但不以为忤，反倒倾向于承认他说得在理；怒意从她脸上退去，只留下专注与凝重。她静静走进屋来，隔着桌子与他端端正正地正面相对。

===Original===
“You thought all that of me﻿—that I was capable of spying on you﻿—yet were generous enough to believe I despised myself for doing it?”
“Not at first.﻿ ﻿… At first, when we met back there in the corridor, I was sure you were bent on further spying. Only since waking up here, half an hour ago, did I begin to understand how impossible it would be for you to lend yourself to such villainy as last night’s.”
“But if you thought that of me then, why did you﻿—?”
“It occurred to me that it would be just as well to prevent your reporting back to headquarters.”

===Chinese===
「你原来这么看我——看我竟会干出暗中监视你的事来——却又宽厚到肯相信我干这事时心里也在鄙薄自己？」
「起初不是。……起初，我们在走廊里碰面那会儿，我还拿准了你是铁了心要再刺探一番。直到半小时前在这儿醒来，我才开始明白，要你屈就昨夜那等卑劣的勾当，是何等办不到的事。」
「可你当时既然那么看我，为什么又——？」
「我转念一想，拦住你、不让你回去向总部复命，倒也不失为上策。」

===Original===
“But now you’ve changed your mind about me?”
He nodded: “Quite.”
“But why?” she demanded in a voice of amazement. “Why?”
“I can’t tell you,” he said slowly﻿—“I don’t know why. I can only presume it must be because﻿—I can’t help believing in you.”

===Chinese===
「可如今你对我的看法又变了？」
他点点头：「全变了。」
