「可为什么？」她用满含惊诧的声音追问，「为什么？」
「我说不上来，」他缓缓道——「我不知道为什么。我只能猜想，多半是因为——我没法不相信你。」

===Original===
Her glance wavered: her colour deepened. “I don’t understand﻿ ﻿…” she murmured.
“Nor I,” he confessed in a tone as low.﻿ ﻿…
A sudden grumble from the teakettle provided welcome distraction. Lanyard lifted it off the flames and slowly poured boiling water on a measure of tea in an earthenware pot.
“A cup of this and something to eat’ll do us no harm,” he ventured, smiling uneasily﻿—“especially if we’re to pursue this psychological enquiry into the whereforeness of the human tendency to change one’s mind!”

===Chinese===
她的目光摇曳起来，脸颊的红晕深了。「我不明白……」她低声说。
「我也不明白，」他承认道，声音一样低。……
水壶里突然咕嘟作响，解了一场围。兰亚德把它从炉火上端下来，往一只陶壶里的茶叶上缓缓冲下滚水。
「喝上一杯，再吃点东西，于我们总没有害处，」他试探着搭话，笑得不大自在——「尤其是，咱们若还要把这场心理学探究进行下去，追问一番人心何以善变的话！」

## XIII Confessional / 十三、剖白

===Original===
And then, when the girl made no response, but remained with troubled gaze focused on some remote abstraction, “You will have tea, won’t you?” he urged.
She recalled her thoughts, nodded with the faintest of smiles﻿—“Yes, thank you!”﻿—and dropped into a chair.
He began at once to make talk in effort to dissipate that constraint which stood between them like an unseen alien presence: “You must be very hungry?”
“I am.”
“Sorry I’ve nothing better to offer you. I’d have run out for something more substantial, only﻿—”
“Only﻿—?” she prompted, coolly helping herself to biscuit and potted ham.

===Chinese===
接着，见姑娘仍不作答，一双惴惴的目光只定在渺远的出神处，他又催了一句：「您总要用点茶吧？」
她收回心绪，点点头，浮起一丝若有若无的微笑——「好，谢谢你！」——便在椅中落了座。
他当即找起话来，想驱散横在两人之间的那层拘谨——它像个无形的异类，杵在中间：「您一定很饿了吧？」
「是饿了。」
「抱歉，我拿不出更好的东西款待您。本想出去弄点更顶饿的吃食，只是——」
「只是——？」她接了一句，一面神色淡淡地自行取用起饼干和罐头火腿。


===Original===
“I didn’t think it wise to leave you alone.”
“Was that before or after you’d made up your mind about me﻿—the latest phase, I mean?” she persisted with a trace of malice.
“Before,” he returned calmly﻿—“likewise, afterwards. Either way you care to take it, it wouldn’t have been wise to leave you here. Suppose you had waked up to find me gone, yourself alone in this strange house﻿—”
“I’ve been awake several hours,” she interposed﻿—“found myself locked in, and heard no sound to indicate that you were still here.”
“I’m sorry: I was overtired and slept like a log.﻿ ﻿… But assuming the case: you would have gone out, alone, penniless﻿—”

===Chinese===
「我觉得把您一人撂在这儿，未免不智。」
「这话是在您拿定主意之前还是之后说的——我是说，对我最近这一阶段的看法？」她追问，带着一丝促狭。
「之前，」他安然答道——「之后也是。随您往哪边理解，把您留在这儿总归不智。假使您一觉醒来，发觉我人走了，只剩您独自守着这陌生的宅子——」
「我已经醒了几个钟头了，」她插话道——「发觉自己被锁在屋里，又听不见半点声息，说明您还在。」
「对不住：我实在乏透了，睡得像根木头。……不过真到那步田地：您就得只身出门，身无分文——」


===Original===
“Through a locked door, Mr. Lanyard?”
“I shouldn’t have left it locked,” he explained patiently.﻿ ﻿… “You would have found yourself friendless and without resources in a city to which you are a stranger.”
She nodded: “True. But what of that?”
“In desperation you might have been forced to go back﻿—”

===Chinese===
「隔着上了锁的门么，兰亚德先生？」
「那我就不会让它锁着，」他耐心解释道。……「那样一来，您就会在这座举目无亲的城市里，落得孤立无援、两手空空。」
她点头：「这倒是。可那又怎样？」
「走投无路之下，您说不定只得回去——」


===Original===
“And report the outcome of my investigation!”
“Pressure might have been brought to induce admissions damaging to me,” Lanyard submitted pleasantly. “Whether or no, you’d have been obliged to renew associations you’re well rid of.”
“You feel sure of that?”
“But naturally.”
“How can you be?” she challenged. “You’ve yet to know me twenty-four hours.”

===Chinese===
「好去汇报我侦查的成果呀！」
「他们或许会施压，诱您吐出对我不利的口供，」兰亚德语气可亲地申说道。「且不论这一层，您总免不了重新搭上那帮您巴不得甩脱的关系。」
「您就这么有把握？」
「这是自然。」
「凭什么有？」她发难道。「您认识我还不到二十四个钟头呢。」


===Original===
“But perhaps I know the associations better. In point of fact, I do. Even though you may have stooped to play the spy last night, Miss Bannon﻿—you couldn’t keep it up. You had to fly further contamination from that pack of jackals.”
“Not﻿—you feel sure﻿—merely to keep you under observation?”
“I do feel sure of that. I have your word for it.”
The girl deliberately finished her tea, and sat back, regarding him steadily beneath level brows. Then she said with an odd laugh: “You have your own way of putting one on honour!”

===Chinese===
「可那帮关系，我或许比您清楚。说实话，我确实清楚。哪怕您昨夜当真自贬身价做了一回探子，班农小姐——您也断难久干。您终究得逃开那群豺狼，免得再受沾染。」
「不是——您很有把握——单单为了监视您？」
「这层我确实有把握。您的话可以为凭。」
姑娘不慌不忙地用完茶，靠回椅背，在平直的眉线下静静打量着他。而后她古怪地一笑：「您要人以名誉作保，倒自有一套办法！」

===Original===
“I don’t need to﻿—with you.”
She analyzed this with gathering perplexity. “What do you mean by that?”
“I mean, I don’t need to put you on your honour﻿—because I’m sure of you. Even were I not, still I’d refrain from exacting any pledge, or attempting to.” He paused and shrugged before continuing: “If I thought you were still to be distrusted, Miss Bannon, I’d say: ‘There’s a free door; go when you like, back to the Pack, turn in your report, and let them act as they see fit.’﻿ ﻿… Do you think I care for them? Do you imagine for one instant that I fear any one﻿—or all﻿—of that gang?”
“That rings suspiciously of egoism!”
“Let it,” he retorted. “It’s pride of caste, if you must know. I hold myself a grade better than such cattle; I’ve intelligence, at least.﻿ ﻿… I can take care of myself!”

===Chinese===
「对您——我用不着这一套。」
她细细掂量这话，愈发困惑：「这是什么意思？」
「我是说，我用不着拿名誉作保来拘着您——因为我信得过您。就算信不过，我也不至于强索什么承诺，连试都不会去试。」他顿了顿，耸耸肩，方才接着说：「倘使我当真还信不过您，班农小姐，我就会说：『门是开着的；您想走便走，回群狼那儿去交您的报告，随他们发落好了。』……您以为我把他们放在心上？您可曾有一瞬想到，那一伙里随便哪一个——或是倾巢齐上——我会怕？」
「这话听着可像自我陶醉！」
「像就像罢，」他反唇相讥。「您一定要知道，那叫阶层的傲气。我自认比那班牛马高出一等；至少，我还有几分头脑。……我还照看得住自己！」


===Original===
If he might read her countenance, it expressed more than anything else distress and disappointment.
“Why do you boast like this﻿—to me?”
“Less through self-satisfaction than in contempt for a pack of murderous mongrels﻿—impatience that I have to consider such creatures as Popinot, Wertheimer, De Morbihan and﻿—all their crew.”
“And Bannon,” she corrected calmly﻿—“you meant to say!”

===Chinese===
倘若他没有看错她的脸色，那上面写得最多的，是痛心与失望。
「您何必这样自吹——偏对我说？」
「与其说是自鸣得意，不如说是出于对一帮嗜杀的杂种的鄙夷——一想到我还得把波皮诺、维特海默、德·莫尔比昂之流以及——他们那一伙放进盘算，就满心不耐。」
「还有班农，」她平静地替他补全——「您是想说的！」


===Original===
“Wel‑l﻿—” he stammered, discountenanced.
“It doesn’t matter,” she assured him. “I quite understand, and strange as it may sound, I’ve very little feeling in the matter.” And then she acknowledged his stupefied stare with a weary smile. “I know what I know,” she added, with obscure significance.﻿ ﻿…
“I’d give a good deal to know how much you know,” he muttered in his confusion.
“But what do *you* know?” she caught him up﻿—“against Mr. Bannon﻿—against my father, that is﻿—that makes you so ready to suspect both him and me?”
“Nothing,” he confessed﻿—“I *know* nothing; but I suspect everything and everybody.﻿ ﻿… And the more I think of it, the more closely I examine that brutal business of last night, the more I seem to sense his will behind it all﻿—as one might glimpse a face in darkness through a lighted lattice.﻿ ﻿… Oh, laugh if you like! It sounds high-flown, I know. But that’s the effect I get.﻿ ﻿… What took you to my room, if not his orders? Why does he train with De Morbihan, if he’s not blood-kin to that breed? Why are you running away from him if not because you’ve found out his part in that conspiracy?”

===Chinese===
「这个——嘛——」他讷讷起来，乱了方寸。
「不打紧，」她宽慰他道。「我很明白，说出来也许奇怪，我在这件事上几乎无所谓。」随即她对上他目瞪口呆的凝视，报以倦怠的一笑。「我知道我所知道的，」她补上一句，意味幽深。……
「您到底知道多少，我倒不惜代价想知道，」他心慌意乱地咕哝道。
「可*您*又知道些什么？」她抓住话锋——「拿班农先生——也就是拿我父亲——来说，究竟是什么，叫您这么轻易就疑心起他和我两个？」
「什么也不知道，」他供认道——「我什么也*不*知道；可我什么都疑、谁都疑。……这事我想得越多，昨夜那桩凶残的勾当我端详得越细，就越觉得幕后处处有他的意志——就像人隔着一扇亮着灯的格子窗，能瞥见暗处的一张脸。……哼，您要笑就笑罢！我知道这话听来玄乎。可我得到的印象便是如此。……若非奉他之命，您深夜进我房间做什么？他若不是那一路货色，又何必同德·莫尔比昂厮混？您若不是识破了他与那桩阴谋的干系，又何必从他身边逃走？」


===Original===
His pause and questioning look evoked no answer; the girl sat moveless and intent, meeting his gaze inscrutably. And something in her impassive attitude worked a little exasperation into his temper.
“Why,” he declared hotly﻿—“if I dare trust to intuition﻿—forgive me if I pain you﻿—”
She interrupted with impatience: “I’ve already begged you not to consider my feelings, Mr. Lanyard! If you dared trust to your intuition﻿—what then?”
“Why, then, I could believe that Mr. Bannon, your father﻿ ﻿… I could believe it was his order that killed poor Roddy!”
There could be no doubting her horrified and half-incredulous surprise.

===Chinese===
他停住话头，目光探问，却没换来回答；姑娘纹丝不动地坐着，全神贯注，莫测高深地迎着他的目光。她那份无动于衷里有些什么，在他火性里搅起了几分恼意。
「这么说吧，」他冲口而出——「倘若我竟敢信自己的直觉——若叫您痛心，恕我冒昧——」
她不耐烦地打断：「我已经求过您，别顾虑我的感受了，兰亚德先生！倘若您敢信自己的直觉——那又怎样？」
「那样，我就会相信班农先生，令尊……我就会相信，要了可怜的罗迪性命的，正是他的一道号令！」
她那份惊骇与半信半疑的错愕，做不得假。


===Original===
“Roddy?” she iterated in a whisper almost inaudible, with face fast blanching. “Roddy﻿—!”
“Inspector Roddy of Scotland Yard,” he told her mercilessly, “was murdered in his sleep last night at Troyon’s. The murderer broke into his room by way of mine﻿—the two adjoin. He used my razor, wore my dressing-gown to shield his clothing, did everything he could think of to cast suspicion on me, and when I came in assaulted me, meaning to drug and leave me insensible to be found by the police. Fortunately﻿—I was beforehand with him. I had just left him drugged, insensible in my place, when I met you in the corridor.﻿ ﻿… You didn’t know?”
“How can you ask?” the girl moaned.
Bending forward, an elbow on the table, she worked her hands together until their knuckles shone white through the skin﻿—but not as white as the face from which her eyes sought his with a look of dumb horror, dazed, pitiful, imploring.
“You’re not deceiving me? But no﻿—why should you?” she faltered. “But how terrible, how unspeakably awful!﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「罗迪？」她低低地重复，声息几不可闻，脸色飞快地褪成煞白。「罗迪——！」
「苏格兰场的罗迪探长，」他毫不容情地告诉她，「昨夜在特鲁瓦旅馆于睡梦之中遇了害。凶手借我的房间闯进他的屋子——两间相邻。他用的是我的剃刀，罩的是我的晨衣以免污了衣裳，千方百计把嫌疑往我身上引，我一进门他便动手袭击，想把我药翻了撂在原地，等警察来发现。所幸——我抢在了他前头。我刚把他麻翻、不省人事地撂在我屋里，转身就在走廊里撞见了您。……您原先不知道？」
「您怎么问得出口？」姑娘呻吟道。
她俯身向前，一支手肘支在桌上，两手绞来绞去，直绞得指节隔着皮肉泛白——却还不及她的脸白；那张脸上，一双眼睛望着他，目光里是无声的惊怖，茫然、可怜、哀恳。
「您不是在诓我？不——您又何必诓我？」她颤声道。「可这多可怕，可怕得叫人说都说不出来！……」

===Original===
“I’m sorry,” Lanyard mumbled﻿—“I’d have held my tongue if I hadn’t thought you knew﻿—”
“You thought I knew﻿—and didn’t lift a finger to save the man?” She jumped up with a blazing face. “Oh, how could you?”
“No﻿—not that﻿—I never thought that. But, meeting you then and there, so opportunely﻿—I couldn’t ignore the coincidence; and when you admitted you were running away from your father, considering all the circumstances, I was surely justified in thinking it was realization, in part at least, of what had happened that was driving you away.”
She shook her head slowly, her indignation ebbing as quickly as it had risen. “I understand,” she said; “you had some excuse, but you were mistaken. I ran away﻿—yes﻿—but not because of that. I never dreamed﻿ ﻿…”
She fell silent, sitting with bowed head and twisting her hands together in a manner he found it painful to watch.

===Chinese===
「对不住，」兰亚德含混道——「我原当您知道，否则断不敢开口——」
「您当我知道——却连一根手指也不肯抬，救一救那个人？」她霍然起身，满脸涨红。「哦，您怎么能这样？」
「不——不是那意思——我从未那样想过。只是，恰在那时那地遇上您——时机那么凑巧，这份巧合我无法置之不理；而后来您又承认是从父亲身边出逃。综观前后种种情形，我认定驱使您出走的，至少有一部分，是您省悟了已然发生的事——这样想，总不算全无道理罢。」
她缓缓摇头，那股义愤来得急，退得也急。「我明白，」她说；「您不是全无理由，只是想岔了。我是逃了出来——是的——却不是为了那个。我做梦也不曾梦到……」
她沉默下来，垂着头坐在那里，两手绞在一处，那模样他看着心里发疼。


===Original===
“But please,” he implored, “don’t take it so much to heart, Miss Bannon. If you knew nothing, you couldn’t have prevented it.”
“No,” she said brokenly﻿—“I could have done nothing﻿ ﻿… But I didn’t know. It isn’t that﻿—it’s the horror and pity of it. And that you could think﻿—!”
“But I didn’t!” he protested﻿—“truly I did not. And for what I did think, for the injustice I did do you, believe me, I’m truly sorry.”
“You were quite justified,” she said﻿—“not only by circumstantial evidence but to a degree in fact. You must know﻿ ﻿… now I must tell you﻿ ﻿…”
“Nothing you don’t wish to!” he interrupted. “The fact that I practically kidnapped you under pretence of doing you a service, and suspected you of being in the pay of that Pack, gives me no title to your confidence.”
“Can I blame you for thinking what you did?” She went on slowly, without looking up﻿—gaze steadfast to her interlaced fingers: “Now for my own sake I want you to know what otherwise, perhaps, I shouldn’t have told you﻿—not yet, at all events. I’m no more Bannon’s daughter than you’re his son. Our names sound alike﻿—people frequently make the same mistake. My name is Shannon﻿—Lucy Shannon. Mr. Bannon called me Lucia because he knew I didn’t like it, to tease me; for the same reason he always kept up the pretence that I was his daughter when people misunderstood.”

===Chinese===
「可是求您，」他恳求道，「别这么往心里去，班农小姐。您既然毫不知情，便无从阻止。」
「是啊，」她语声断续——「我原本什么也做不成……可我并不知道。难过的不在这上头——是这件事的恐怖与可悯。而您竟会那么想——！」
「可我没有！」他分辩道——「真的没有。至于我原先那些念头，那些委屈您的地方，请相信我，我是真心抱歉。」
「您那样想原不为过，」她说——「不但情理上推得过去，论事实也有几分。您应当知道……如今我必须告诉您……」
「不愿说的就不必说！」他打断道。「我打着为您效劳的旗号，实则近乎劫走了您，又疑心您受着群狼的雇用，这些都不配领受您的信托。」
「您先前那么想，难道怪得了您？」她缓缓说下去，头也不抬——目光定在十指交扣的手上：「如今为我自己的缘故，我要让您知道一件原也许不会说的事——至少眼下不会说。我不是班农的女儿，正如您不是他的儿子。我们两个的姓氏读音相近——旁人常犯一样的错。我姓香农——露西·香农。班农先生偏叫我露西娅，因为他知道我不喜欢，存心戏弄我；也是为着同样的缘故，每逢旁人误会，他总一口咬定我是他的女儿。」


===Original===
“But﻿—if that is so﻿—then what﻿—?”
“Why﻿—it’s very simple.” Still she didn’t look up. “I’m a trained nurse. Mr. Bannon is consumptive﻿—so far gone, it’s a wonder he didn’t die years ago: for months I’ve been haunted by the thought that it’s only the evil in him keeps him alive. It wasn’t long after I took the assignment to nurse him that I found out something about him.﻿ ﻿… He’d had a haemorrhage at his desk; and while he lay in coma, and I was waiting for the doctor, I happened to notice one of the papers he’d been working over when he fell. And then, just as I began to appreciate the sort of man I was employed by, he came to, and saw﻿—and knew. I found him watching me with those dreadful eyes of his, and though he was unable to speak, knew my life wasn’t safe if ever I breathed a word of what I had read. I would have left him then, but he was too cunning for me, and when in time I found a chance to escape﻿—I was afraid I’d not live long if ever I left him. He went about it deliberately; to keep me frightened, and though he never mentioned the matter directly, let me know plainly, in a hundred ways, what his power was and what would happen if I whispered a word of what I knew. It’s nearly a year now﻿—nearly a year of endless terror and﻿ ﻿…”
Her voice fell; she was trembling with the recrudescent suffering of that yearlong servitude. And for a little Lanyard felt too profoundly moved to trust himself to speak; he stood aghast, staring down at this woman, so intrinsically and gently feminine, so strangely strong and courageous; and vaguely envisaging what anguish must have been hers in enforced association with a creature of Bannon’s ruthless stamp, he was rent with compassion and swore to himself he’d stand by her and see her through and free and happy if he died for it﻿—or ended in the Santé!
“Poor child!” he heard himself murmuring﻿—“poor child!”

===Chinese===
「可是——若是这样——那又——？」
「这个嘛——说来简单。」她仍不抬头。「我是个受过专门训练的护士。班农先生患着痨病——病到那步田地，多年前居然没死，本身已是奇迹：一连几个月，有个念头总萦绕着我——撑住他这条命的，不过是他身上那股邪气。我接下看护他的差事不久，便看出了几分他的底细。……那天他在书桌边咯了血；他昏迷不醒、我干等着医生的当口，无意间瞧见了他倒下前研读的一页文件。而恰在我开始咂摸出自己受雇的是个什么人的时候，他苏醒过来，看见了——也心知肚明。我发觉他正拿那双骇人的眼睛盯着我；他虽还开不了口，我却明白，我读到的东西但凡吐露一字，我这条命便不算安稳。当时我就想离他而去，可他比我奸猾得多；待到后来我寻着脱身的机会——我又怕一旦真离了他，自己也活不长久。这一切他是处心积虑布下的；为着叫我日夜惊惶，他虽从不正面提及此事，却用上百种法子叫我清清楚楚地领教：他的权势有多大，我知道的事倘若走漏半个字，会有什么下场。如今快一年了——将近一年无休无止的恐怖，还有……」
她的声音低了下去；那一年苦役的旧痛复发，她浑身发颤。兰亚德一时间心潮激荡太深，不敢信口开口；他愕然伫立，俯望着这个女子——骨子里这般温婉的女人气，却又这般出奇地坚韧、勇敢；隐隐想到她被迫与班农那种狠毒坯子厮守周旋，该熬过了怎样的苦楚，他怜意如裂，暗暗立誓：哪怕拼上性命——哪怕自己落个蹲桑泰监狱的下场！——也要守在她身边，护她渡尽难关，还她自由与幸福。
「可怜的姑娘！」他听见自己喃喃着——「可怜的姑娘！」


===Original===
“Don’t pity me!” she insisted, still with face averted. “I don’t deserve it. If I had the spirit of a mouse, I’d have defied him; it needed only courage enough to say one word to the police﻿—”
“But who is he, then?” Lanyard demanded. “What is he, I mean?”
“I hardly know how to tell you. And I hardly dare: I feel as if these walls would betray me if I did.﻿ ﻿… But to me he’s the incarnation of all things evil.﻿ ﻿…” She shook herself with a nervous laugh. “But why be silly about it? I don’t really know what or who he is: I only suspect and believe that he is a man whose life is devoted to planning evil and ordering its execution through his lieutenants. When the papers at home speak of ‘The Man Higher Up’ they mean Archer Bannon, though they don’t know it﻿—or else I’m merely a hysterical woman exaggerating the impressions of a morbid imagination.﻿ ﻿… And that’s all I know of him that matters.”
“But why, if you believe all this﻿—how did you at length find courage﻿—?”
“Because I no longer had courage to endure; because I was more afraid to stay than to go﻿—afraid that my own soul would be forfeit. And then, last night, he ordered me to go to your room and search it for evidence that you were the Lone Wolf. It was the first time he’d ever asked anything like that of me. I was afraid, and though I obeyed, I was glad when you interrupted﻿—glad even though I had to lie the way I did.﻿ ﻿… And all that worked on me, after I’d gone back to my room, until I felt I could stand it no longer; and after a long time, when the house seemed all still, I got up, dressed quietly and﻿ ﻿… That is how I came to meet you﻿—quite by accident.”

===Chinese===
「别可怜我！」她仍偏着脸，执意道。「我不配。我但凡有老鼠那点血性，也早同他拼了；所需的不过是一点勇气，对警察说一个字就够——」
「那么他到底是谁？」兰亚德诘问道。「我是说，他是个什么人？」
「我简直不知从何说起。也简直不敢：我总觉得这几面墙都会出卖我，只要我一开口。……可在我眼里，他就是万般邪恶的化身。……」她打个寒噤，神经质地一笑。「可何必犯傻呢？他究竟是什么、是谁，我并不真知道：我只是疑心并且相信，这是个把毕生精力用来谋划罪恶、再驱遣党羽付诸实行的人。美国的报章说起『幕后主脑』（The Man Higher Up）时，指的就是阿切尔·班农，只是报馆自己并不知情——再不然，我不过是个神经过敏的女人，把病态想象炮制出来的印象夸大了罢了。……关紧要的，我知道的就这么多。」
「可您既然信这一切——后来又怎么寻着了勇气——？」
「因为我已经没有勇气再熬下去；因为留下来比逃走更叫我害怕——我怕赔上的是自己的灵魂。再说，昨夜他命我去您房间，搜查您就是独狼的证据。这样的事，他还是头一回差遣我。我害怕，虽则从了命，您进来打断的时候，我心里却暗暗庆幸——哪怕只得像方才那样撒谎，也庆幸。……待我回房之后，这一切在心里翻腾，直折腾得我再受不住；过了许久，整座宅子似乎都静了，我便起身，悄悄穿好衣裳，还有……我就是这样遇上您的——纯属偶然。」


===Original===
“But you seemed so frightened at first when you saw me﻿—”
“I was,” she confessed simply; “I thought you were Mr. Greggs.”
“Greggs?”
“Mr. Bannon’s private secretary﻿—his right-hand man. He’s about your height and has a suit like the one you wear, and in that poor light﻿—at the distance I didn’t notice you were clean-shaven﻿—Greggs wears a moustache﻿—”
“Then it was Greggs murdered Roddy and tried to drug me!﻿ ﻿… By George, I’d like to know whether the police got there before Bannon, or somebody else, discovered the substitution. It was a telegram to the police, you know, I sent from the Bourse last night!”

===Chinese===
「可您初见我时，好像怕得厉害——」
「我是怕，」她坦然承认；「我把您当成格雷格斯先生了。」
「格雷格斯？」
「班农先生的私人秘书——他的左右手。他跟您差不多高，也有一套同您这身相仿的西装，光线那么暗——隔着那段距离，我没瞧出您脸上刮得干净——格雷格斯是留着两撇胡子的——」
「那么杀害罗迪、又想药倒我的，就是这个格雷格斯了！……天哪，我倒想知道，警察究竟赶没赶在班农——或者别的什么人——发现掉包之前到场。您可知道，昨夜从交易所打给警察的那封电报，就是我发的！」

===Original===
In his excitement Lanyard began to pace the floor rapidly; and now that he was no longer staring at her, the girl lifted her head and watched him closely as he moved to and fro, talking aloud﻿—more to himself than to her.
“I wish I knew!﻿ ﻿… And what a lucky thing, you did meet me! For if you’d gone on to the Gare du Nord and waited there.﻿ ﻿… Well, it isn’t likely Bannon didn’t discover your flight before eight o’clock this morning, is it?”
“I’m afraid not.﻿ ﻿…”
“And they’ve drawn the deadline for me round every conceivable exit from Paris: Popinot’s Apaches are picketed everywhere. And if Bannon had found out about you in time, it would have needed only a word﻿ ﻿…”
He paused and shuddered to think what might have ensued had that word been spoken and the girl been found waiting for her train in the Gare du Nord.
“Mercifully, we’ve escaped that. And now, with any sort of luck, Bannon ought to be busy enough, trying to get his precious Mr. Greggs out of the Santé, to give us a chance. And a fighting chance is all I ask.”

===Chinese===
兰亚德激动起来，开始在地板上疾步踱转；他既不再盯着她，姑娘便抬起头，紧紧注视着他来回走动、高声说话——那话与其说是对她讲，不如说是对自己。
「但愿我能知道！……还有，亏得您偏偏撞上了我！您若是一径去了北站，在那儿干等。……哼，今天早晨八点之前班农还没发觉您出逃——这样的好事，怕是指望不上罢？」
「我怕是指望不上。……」
「况且他们已替我布下天罗地网，把巴黎一切想得到的出口都圈了进去：波皮诺的阿帕奇到处设着暗桩。倘若班农及时发觉了您的行踪，那只需一句话……」
他顿住了，一想到那句话倘若出口、姑娘被人发现在北站里候着她的火车，后文会是如何，便不寒而栗。
「谢天谢地，这一层总算躲过了。如今只要运气不算太坏，班农光是为把他那位宝贝格雷格斯先生从桑泰监狱里捞出来，就该忙得团团转，正好给我们一个机会。而我要的，无非一个放手一搏的机会。」


===Original===
“Mr. Lanyard”﻿—the girl bent toward him across the table with a gesture of eager interest﻿—“have you any idea why he﻿—why Mr. Bannon hates you so?”
“But does he? I don’t know!”
“If he doesn’t, why should he plot to cast suspicion of murder on you, and why be so anxious to know whether you were really the Lone Wolf? I saw his eyes light up when De Morbihan mentioned that name, after dinner; and if ever I saw hatred in a man’s face, it was in his as he watched you, when you weren’t looking.”
“As far as I know, I never heard of him before,” Lanyard said carelessly. “I fancy it’s nothing more than the excitement of a manhunt. Now that they’ve found me out, De Morbihan and his crew won’t rest until they’ve got my scalp.”

===Chinese===
「兰亚德先生，」姑娘隔着桌子朝他俯过身来，一副急切关切的神气——「您可知道为什么他——为什么班农先生这样恨您？」
「他恨我么？我倒不知道！」
「他若不恨，何必设局把杀人的嫌疑往您身上栽，又何必急不可耐地想知道您究竟是不是独狼？晚饭后德·莫尔比昂提起那个名号时，我看见他眼睛一亮；我若曾在谁脸上见过仇恨，那便是在他趁您不察时盯着您的那张脸上。」
「据我所知，我从前压根没听说过此人，」兰亚德淡然道。「我想那不过是围捕猎物的兴奋劲儿。如今他们既识破了我的底，德·莫尔比昂和他那一伙不剥下我的头皮，是决计不肯罢休的。」


===Original===
“But why?”
“Professional jealousy. We’re all crooks, all in the same boat, only I won’t row to their stroke. I’ve always played a lone hand successfully; now they insist on coming into the game and sharing my winnings. And I’ve told them where they could go.”
“And because of that, they’re willing to﻿—”
“There’s nothing they wouldn’t do, Miss Shannon, to bring me to my knees or see me put out of the way, where my operations couldn’t hurt their pocketbooks. Well﻿ ﻿… all I ask is a fighting chance, and they shall have their way!”
Her brows contracted. “I don’t understand.﻿ ﻿… You want a fighting chance﻿—to surrender﻿—to give in to their demands?”

===Chinese===
「可是为什么呢？」
「同行相妒呗。我们全是贼，同在一条船上，只是我不肯照他们的鼓点划桨。我一向单干，单干得顺风顺水；如今他们硬要入局，分我的彩头。我也早已回敬过他们：请便，爱去哪儿去哪儿。」
「就为这个，他们就肯——」
「为了叫我屈膝下跪，或者干脆把我除掉、好叫我的营生再伤不着他们的钱袋，他们没有什么干不出来的，香农小姐。好嘛……我要的不过一个放手一搏的机会，他们的道儿且由他们走去！」
她蹙起了眉。「我不明白。……您要一个放手一搏的机会——却是为了投降——为了依了他们的要求？」


===Original===
“In a way﻿—yes. I want a fighting chance to do what I’d never in the world get them to credit﻿—give it all up and leave them a free field.”
And when still she searched his face with puzzled eyes, he insisted: “I mean it; I want to get away﻿—clear out﻿—chuck the game for good and all!”
A little silence greeted this announcement. Lanyard, at pause near the table, resting a hand on it, bent to the girl’s upturned face a grave but candid regard. And the deeps of her eyes that never swerved from his were troubled strangely in his vision. He could by no means account for the light he seemed to see therein, a light that kindled while he watched like a tiny flame, feeble, fearful, vacillant, then as the moments passed steadied and grew stronger but ever leaped and danced; so that he, lost in the wonder of it and forgetful of himself, thought of it as the ardent face of a happy child dancing in the depths of some brown autumnal woodland.﻿ ﻿…
“You,” she breathed incredulously﻿—“you mean, you’re going to stop﻿—?”

===Chinese===
「在某种意义上——是的。我要一个放手一搏的机会，去做一件他们打死也不会信的事——把这一切尽数抛开，把地盘整个让给他们。」
见她仍满眼困惑地端详他的脸，他执意道：「我是认真的；我要脱身——抽身——彻头彻尾甩了这场把戏，永不再沾！」
这番宣言换来片刻的静默。兰亚德在桌边停住，一只手搭着桌面，向姑娘仰起的脸投去庄重而坦率的注视。她眼底深处那不曾从他脸上移开的一片，在他眼里异样地动荡。他全然说不上来，自己在那里面看见的算是怎样一缕光——他望着，那光便像一朵小小的火苗似地燃起，微弱、怯惧、摇曳不定，继而随着光阴点滴过去，渐稳渐壮，却始终跳跃飞舞；于是他看得出神，浑然忘我，只觉那像一张热切的小脸，在深秋赭色的林地深处欢舞。……
「您，」她气息难信地吐出——「您是说，您这就收手——？」


===Original===
“I *have* stopped, Miss Shannon. The Lone Wolf has prowled for the last time. I didn’t know it until I woke up, an hour or so ago, but I’ve turned my last job.”
He remarked her hands were small, in keeping with the slightness of her person, but somehow didn’t seem so﻿—wore a look of strength and capability, befitting hands trained to a nurse’s duties; and saw them each tightfisted but quivering as they rested on the table, as though their mistress struggled to suppress the manifestation of some emotion as powerful as unfathomable to him.
“But why?” she demanded in bewilderment. “But why do you say that? What can have happened to make you﻿—?”
“Not fear of that Pack!” he laughed﻿—“not that, I promise you.”
“Oh, I know!” she said impatiently﻿—“I know that very well. But still I don’t understand.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「我不是要收手，是*已经*收手了，香农小姐。独狼行猎，昨夜是最后一遭。约莫一个钟头前我醒来才明白过来，但那一票，确是我此生收山的最后一票。」
他注意到她的手很小，同她纤小的身量正相称，可不知怎的又不显小——自有一股干练之气，配得上受过护士训练的一双手；他又看见那双手搁在桌上，各自攥得紧紧的，却在颤动，仿佛这双手的主人正竭力按捺着某种情绪的外露——那情绪何等强烈，于他又何等深不可测。
「可是为什么？」她惶惑地追问。「您为什么这么说？到底出了什么事，叫您——？」
「可不是怕了那群狼！」他笑道——「绝不是那个，我向您担保。」
「哦，这我知道！」她不耐烦地说——「那个我很清楚。可我还是不明白。……」

===Original===
“If it won’t bore you, I’ll try to explain.” He drew up his chair and sat down again, facing her across the littered table. “I don’t suppose you’ve ever stopped to consider what an essentially stupid animal a crook must be. Most of them are stupid because they practise clumsily one of the most difficult professions imaginable, and inevitably fail at it, yet persist. They wouldn’t think of undertaking a job of civil engineering with no sort of preparation, but they’ll tackle a dangerous proposition in burglary without a thought, and pay for failure with years of imprisonment, and once out try it again. That’s one kind of criminal﻿—the ninety-nine percent class﻿—incurably stupid! There’s another class, men whose imagination forewarns them of dangers and whose mental training, technical equipment and sheer manual dexterity enable them to attack a formidable proposition like a modern safe﻿—by way of illustration﻿—and force its secret. They’re the successful criminals, like myself﻿—but they’re no less stupid, no less failures, than the other ninety-nine in our every hundred, because they never stop to think. It never occurs to them that the same intelligence, applied to any one of the trades they must be masters of, would not only pay them better, but leave them their self-respect and rid them forever of the dread of arrest that haunts us all like the memory of some shameful act.﻿ ﻿… All of which is much more of a lecture than I meant to inflict upon you, Miss Shannon, and sums up to just this: *I*’ve stopped to think.﻿ ﻿…”
With this he stopped for breath as well, and momentarily was silent, his faint, twisted smile testifying to self-consciousness; but presently, seeing that she didn’t offer to interrupt, but continued to give him her attention so exclusively that it had the effect of fascination, he stumbled on, at first less confidently.
“When I woke up it was as if, without my will, I had been thinking all this out in my sleep. I saw myself for the first time clearly, as I have been ever since I can remember﻿—a crook, thoughtless, vain, rapacious, ruthless, skulking in shadows and thinking myself an amazingly fine fellow because, between coups, I would play the gentleman a bit, venture into the light and swagger in the haunts of the gratin! In my poor, perverted brain I thought there was something fine and thrilling and romantic in the career of a great criminal and myself a wonderful figure﻿—an enemy of society!”
“Why do you say this to me?” she demanded abruptly, out of a phase of profound thoughtfulness.

===Chinese===
「您若不嫌絮烦，我就试着解说解说。」他把椅子拉近，重新坐下，隔着杯盘零乱的桌子与她相对。「我想您从没停下来想过，一个贼这畜生，骨子里原得有多蠢。他们多半蠢在：把一门想得到的行当里最难干的营生做得笨手笨脚，注定一败涂地，却偏要一条道走到黑。若叫他们毫无准备去承接一项土木工程，他们想也不敢想；可入室行窃这样性命交关的活计，他们眼也不眨便敢下手，败了便拿几年苦牢来抵，一出狱照干不误。这是一类罪犯——百分之九十九的那一类——蠢得不可救药！另有一类人：想象力替他们预警凶险，心智的修习、技艺的装备，再加上天生的手上功夫，使他们攻得动一道像现代保险柜这样难缠的题目——姑且打个比方——并撬出其中的秘密。他们是得手的罪犯，譬如我——可比起我们每一百人里的另外九十九个，他们不更少一分蠢、不更少一分败，因为他们从不曾停下来想一想。他们从没想到：同一样的智力，移用到他们势必精通的那几门行当中的随便哪一门上，不但进项更丰，还能给他们留下自尊，并使他们永远摆脱那缠着我们不放的落网之惧——它像一桩丑事的记忆，阴魂不散。……这一番话，已远不止我本想硬塞给您的说教了，香农小姐，归结起来不过一句：*我*停下来想过了。……
说完他也停下来换气，一时无语；那抹浅淡而微斜的笑意，正为他的忸怩作证。但不多时，见她无意打断，仍把全副心思给了他，那专注竟有摄人之效，他便磕磕绊绊说下去，起初的口气已不似方才笃定。
「我醒来的时候，仿佛趁我睡着、不由我做主，这一切早已在心里推演完毕。我头一回把自己看了个真切，看清了自记事以来的一贯面目——一个贼：没心没肺，虚荣，贪婪，狠辣，匿影潜形于暗处，却自以为是个了不得的人物，只因两票之间，我也偶尔扮一扮绅士，斗胆走进亮处，到*上流精华*（*gratin*）出没的场所去扬威！在我那可怜而扭曲的脑子里，竟以为大盗的生涯里有什么高贵、刺激而罗曼蒂克的物事，以为自己是个盖世奇男子——一个社会的公敌！」
「您对我说这些做什么？」她从一阵深沉的思索里蓦地发问。


===Original===
He lifted an apologetic shoulder. “Because, I fancy, I’m no longer self-sufficient. I was all of that, twenty-four hours ago; but now I’m as lonesome as a lost child in a dark forest. I haven’t a friend in the world. I’m like a stray pup, grovelling for sympathy. And you are unfortunate enough to be the only person I can declare myself to. It’s going to be a fight﻿—I know that too well!﻿—and without something outside myself to struggle toward, I’ll be heavily handicapped. But if﻿ ﻿…” He faltered, with a look of wistful earnestness. “If I thought that you, perhaps, were a little interested, that I had your faith to respect and cherish﻿ ﻿… if I dared hope that you’d be glad to know I had won out against odds, it would mean a great deal to me, it might mean my salvation!”
Watching her narrowly, hanging upon her decision with the anxiety of a man proscribed and hoping against hope for pardon, he saw her eyes cloud and shift from his, her lips parted but hesitant; and before she could speak, hastily interposed:
“Please don’t say anything yet. First let me demonstrate my sincerity. So far I’ve done nothing to persuade you but﻿—talk and talk and talk! Give me a chance to prove I mean what I say.”

===Chinese===
他耸起一边肩膀，聊表歉意。「因为，我想，我不再是自给自足的了。二十四小时以前我还是；可如今，我孤单得像黑林子里走失的孩子。这世上我举目无亲。我像条离群的奶狗，伏地乞怜。而不幸的是，您是我唯一能够剖白的人。这将是一场苦斗——这一点我知道得太透了！——若没有一个自身之外的什么教我奋力趋赴，我便要吃沉重的亏。可倘若……」他讷讷起来，一脸怅惘的恳切。「倘若我想，您或许有几分关切，我有一份您的信赖可敬可宝……倘若我敢指望，您闻说我力克艰险会为我欢喜，那于我便重逾千金，说不定便是我的得救！」
他细细觑着她，像一名遭通缉的人悬着心等赦令、万难之中犹存万一之望；只见她眼里起了云翳，目光从他身上移开，唇启了却迟疑；不等她开口，他急忙插进来说：
「请先什么都别说。且容我证实我的诚意。到目前为止，我说服您靠的只是——空谈，空谈，还是空谈！给我一个机会，证明我言出由衷。」


===Original===
“How”﻿—she enunciated only with visible effort and no longer met his appeal with an open countenance﻿—“how can you do that?”
“In the long run, by establishing myself in some honest way of life, however modest; but now, and principally, by making reparation for at least one crime I’ve committed that’s not irreparable.”
He caught her quick glance of enquiry, and met it with a confident nod as he placed between them the morocco-bound jewel-case.
“In London, yesterday,” he said quietly, “I brought off two big coups. One was deliberate, the other the inspiration of a moment. The one I’d planned for months was the theft of the Omber jewels﻿—here.”
He tapped the case and resumed in the same manner: “The other job needs a diagram: Not long ago a Frenchman named Huysman, living in Tours, was mysteriously murdered﻿—a poor inventor, who had starved himself to perfect a stabilizator, an attachment to render aeroplanes practically foolproof. His final trials created a sensation and he was on the eve of selling his invention to the Government when he was killed and his plans stolen. Circumstantial evidence pointed to an international spy named Ekstrom﻿—Adolph Ekstrom, once Chief of the Aviation Corps of the German Army, cashiered for general blackguardism with a suspicion of treason to boot. However, Ekstrom kept out of sight; and presently the plans turned up in the German War Office. That was a big thing for Germany; already supreme with her dirigibles, the acquisition of the Huysman stabilizator promised her ten years’ lead over the world in the field of aeroplanes.﻿ ﻿… Now yesterday Ekstrom came to the surface in London with those selfsame plans to sell to England. Chance threw him my way, and he mistook me for the man he’d expected to meet﻿—Downing Street’s secret agent. Well﻿—no matter how﻿—I got the plans from him and brought them over with me, meaning to turn them over to France, to whom by rights they belong.”

===Chinese===
「怎么」——她吐字艰难，说得显然吃力，也不再以坦白的脸色迎他恳求的目光——「怎么个证实法？」
「从长远说，靠自己在某种诚实的营生里立足，哪怕卑微；而眼下，首要的，是弥补我犯下的罪孽里至少一桩——那一桩还不至无可挽回。」
他接住她迅疾探询的一瞥，以笃定的一颔首作答，一面把那只摩洛哥革面的珠宝匣放到两人中间。
「昨天，在伦敦，」他静静地说，「我干成了两票大的。一票出于蓄谋，另一票出于一时的灵光。筹划了几个月的那一票，是盗窃欧姆贝夫人的珠宝——就在这儿。」
他敲了敲匣子，仍以先前的口吻续道：「另一票得画张图才说得清：不久前，一个住在图尔的法国人，名叫于伊斯曼，被神秘地谋杀了——一个穷发明家，为了完善一种稳定器（stabilizator）——一种能使飞机几乎万无一失的附件——把自己饿了个半死。他的最后几次试飞轰动一时，眼看就要把发明卖给政府，人却遇了害，图纸也遭窃。种种情由都指向一个名叫埃克斯特罗姆的国际间谍——阿道夫·埃克斯特罗姆，曾任德国陆军航空部队长官，因无恶不作、兼有通敌之嫌而被革职。不过埃克斯特罗姆始终匿不出面；不久，图纸出现在德国陆军部里。这对德国是件大事；她的飞艇本已称雄天下，再得于伊斯曼稳定器，在飞机领域便可领先世界十年。……而昨天，埃克斯特罗姆在伦敦露了面，揣着的正是那套图纸，要卖与英国。机缘把他抛到我的道上，他把我错认成他要等的人——唐宁街的密探。于是——不问怎么得来的罢——图纸到了我手里，又随我一道渡了海；我打算把它们交还法国——按理，它们本就归法国所有。」


===Original===
“Without consideration?” the girl enquired shrewdly.
“Not exactly. I had meant to make no profit of the affair﻿—I’m a bit squeamish about tainted money!﻿—but under present conditions, if France insists on rewarding me with safe conduct out of the country, I shan’t refuse it.﻿ ﻿… Do you approve?”
She nodded earnestly: “It would be worse than criminal to return them to Ekstrom.﻿ ﻿…”
“That’s my view of the matter.”
“But these?” The girl rested her hand upon the jewel-case.

===Chinese===
「不取分文么？」姑娘尖锐地问。
「也不尽然。我本不打算从这桩事里牟利——不义之财，我还有几分忌惮！——只是以眼下的处境，法国若定要酬谢我，许我平安离境，我也不推辞。……您赞成么？」
她恳切地点头：「若把它们还给埃克斯特罗姆，那就比犯罪还不如了。……」
「这也是我对此事的看法。」
「那这些呢？」姑娘把手搁在了珠宝匣上。


===Original===
“Those go back to Madame Omber. She has a home here in Paris that I know very well. In fact, the sole reason why I didn’t steal them here was that she left for England unexpectedly, just as I was all set to strike. Now I purpose making use of my knowledge to restore the jewels without risk of falling into the hands of the police. That will be an easy matter.﻿ ﻿… And that brings me to a great favour I would beg of you.”
She gave him a look so unexpectedly kind that it staggered him. But he had himself well in hand.
“You can’t now leave Paris before morning﻿—thanks to my having overslept,” he explained. “There’s no honest way I know to raise money before the pawnshops open. But I’m hoping that won’t be necessary; I’m hoping I can arrange matters without going to that extreme. Meanwhile, you agree that these jewels must be returned?”
“Of course,” she affirmed gently.
“Then﻿ ﻿… will you accompany me when I replace them? There won’t be any danger: I promise you that. Indeed, it would be more hazardous for you to wait for me elsewhere while I attended to the matter alone. And I’d like you to be convinced of my good faith.”

===Chinese===
「这些要物归原主，还给欧姆贝夫人。她在巴黎的宅邸，我了如指掌。说起来，我当初未在巴黎下手，唯一的原因就是她不巧临时去了英国，而那时我已一切就绪、只待动手。如今我正要借这份熟络，把珠宝悄悄归还，还不至于落到警察手里。那是易如反掌的事。……这便引出一桩大事——我想求您帮我一个忙。」
她向他投来的目光温存得出乎意料，倒叫他心神一晃。好在他把自己拿捏得很稳。
「如今天亮之前您走不出巴黎了——怪我睡过了头，」他解释道。「当铺开门之前，我想不出什么正经法子筹钱。但我盼着不至于走到那一步；我盼着不必行此下策，也能把事情安排妥帖。这且不谈，您总认可，这些珠宝必须归还罢？」
「当然，」她轻声应允。
「那么……我送还珠宝的时候，您肯陪我同去么？不会有半点危险：这个我向您担保。说实在的，您若在别处干等、任我独自去办这件事，反倒更悬。我也想让您亲眼见证我的诚意。」

===Original===
“Don’t you think you can trust me for that as well?” she asked, with a flash of humour.
“Trust you!”
“To believe﻿ ﻿… Mr. Lanyard,” she told him gently but earnestly, “I do believe.”
“You make me very happy,” he said﻿ ﻿… “but I’d like you to see for yourself.﻿ ﻿… And I’d be glad not to have to fret about your safety in my absence. As a bureau of espionage, Popinot’s brigade of Apaches is without a peer in Europe. I am positively afraid to leave you alone.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「这件事，您不也信得过我么？」她问，眼里闪过一丝谐趣。
「信得过您！」
「信我信……兰亚德先生，」她温和而恳切地对他说，「我信。」
「您这话叫我好生欢喜，」他说……「可我还是想让您亲眼见证。……再者，我不在的时候无须为您的安危悬心，我也乐得放心。论谍报机关，波皮诺那支阿帕奇别动队全欧无出其右。把您独自留下，我是着实不敢。……」


===Original===
She was silent.
“Will you come with me, Miss Shannon?”
“That is your sole reason for asking this of me?” she insisted, eyeing him steadily.
“That I wish you to believe in me﻿—yes.”
“Why?” she pursued, inexorable.

===Chinese===
她沉默着。
「您随我同去么，香农小姐？」
「您向我提这个请求，就只有这一个缘故？」她目不转睛地盯着他，执意追问。
「我要您信我——是这一个。」
「为什么？」她穷究不舍。


===Original===
“Because﻿ ﻿… I’ve already told you.”
“That you want someone’s good opinion to cherish.﻿ ﻿… But why, of all people, me﻿—whom you hardly know, of whom what little you do know is hardly reassuring?”
He coloured, and boggled his answer.﻿ ﻿… “I can’t tell you,” he confessed in the end.
“Why can’t you tell me?”

===Chinese===
「因为……我已经告诉过您了。」
「说您要一份旁人的青眼来宝爱。……可普天之下这么多人，为什么偏偏是我——一个您几乎不识的人，您识得的那一点点，又多半难以教人安心？」
他红了脸，话在嘴边直打绊。……「我告诉不得您，」他末了招认。
「为什么告诉不得？」


===Original===
He stared at her miserably.﻿ ﻿… “I’ve no right.﻿ ﻿… In spite of all I’ve said, in spite of the faith you so generously promise me, in your eyes I must still figure as a thief, a liar, an impostor﻿—self-confessed. Men aren’t made over by mere protestations, nor even by their own efforts, in an hour, or a day, or a week. But give me a year: if I can live a year in honesty, and earn my bread, and so prove my strength﻿—then, perhaps, I might find the courage, the﻿—the effrontery to tell you why I want your good opinion.﻿ ﻿… Now I’ve said far more than I meant or had any right to. I hope,” he ventured pleadingly﻿—“you’re not offended.”
Only an instant longer could she maintain her direct and unflinching look. Then, his meaning would no more be ignored. Her lashes fell; a tide of crimson flooded her face; and with a quick movement, pushing her chair a little from the table, she turned aside. But she said nothing.
He remained as he had been, bending eagerly toward her.
And in the long minute that elapsed before either spoke again, both became oddly conscious of the silence brooding in that lonely little house, of their isolation from the world, of their common peril and mutual dependence.
“I’m afraid,” Lanyard said, after a time﻿—“I’m afraid I know what you must be thinking. One can’t do your intelligence the injustice to imagine that you haven’t understood me﻿—read all that was in my mind and”﻿—his voice fell﻿—“in my heart. I own I was wrong to speak so transparently, to suggest my regard for you, at such a time, under such conditions. I am truly sorry, and beg you to consider unsaid all that I should not have said.﻿ ﻿… After all, what earthly difference can it make to you if one thief more decides suddenly to reform?”

===Chinese===
他凄惶地望着她。……「我没有那份权利。……尽管我说了这许多，尽管您慨然许我以信赖，在您眼里，我必定还是个贼、一个说谎的、一个招摇撞骗的——而且自己招认不讳。人是不会凭几句表白脱胎换骨的，便是凭自身努力也办不到——一小时不能，一天不能，一个星期也不能。可给我一年：倘若我能清清白白活上一年，自食其力挣得面包，就此证明我的底气——那时，或许，我或许能寻着勇气、那——那份放肆，来告诉您，我为什么宝爱您的青眼。……如今我已说得远过初衷、也远逾本分了。但愿，」他小心探问——「您没有动气。」
她那直白而不避不让的目光，又堪堪撑得了一瞬。随即，他的用意再不容她装聋作哑。睫毛垂落；一片绯红潮水似地漫上脸来；她倏地一动，把椅子从桌边推开些许，侧过身去。却一言不发。
他仍保持着原样，热切地向她俯着身。
在两人重又开口之前那漫长的一分钟里，他们都异样地觉出这座孤零零小宅里栖伏的寂静，觉出自身与世界的隔绝，觉出共同的危难与相互的依存。
「我怕，」隔了一忽儿，兰亚德开口——「我怕我猜得到您此刻在想什么。若说您竟没有听懂我——没有读出我心里、还有」——他的声音低了下去——「我心里的一切，那也未免太看轻您的聪明了。我承认，选在这样的时候、这样的处境，把话说得这般露骨，暗示我对您的情意，是我错了。我真心抱歉，求您只当我没说那些不该说的。……说到底，这世上又多一个贼忽然决意改过自新，于您又能有半点干系？」


===Original===
That brought her abruptly to her feet, to show him a face of glowing loveliness and eyes distractingly dimmed and softened.
“No!” she implored him breathlessly﻿—“please﻿—you mustn’t spoil it! You’ve paid me the finest of compliments, and one I’m glad and grateful for﻿ ﻿… and would I might think I deserved!﻿ ﻿… You say you need a year to prove yourself? Then﻿—I’ve no right to say this﻿—and you must please not ask me what I mean﻿—then I grant you that year. A year I shall wait to hear from you from the day we part, here in Paris.﻿ ﻿… And tonight, I will go with you, too, and gladly, since you wish it!”
And then as he, having risen, stood at loss, thrilled, and incredulous, with a brave and generous gesture she offered him her hand.
“Mr. Lanyard, I promise.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
这话使她霍地立起，向他显示了一张容光焕发的脸，和一双叫人心神迷乱的、蒙眬而柔婉的眼睛。
「不！」她气息急促地恳求他——「求您——您千万不可毁了它！您给了我天下最好的恭维，我为此欢喜、感激……但愿我当得起！……您说您要用一年来证明自己？那么——这话我原没有权利说——也请您千万莫问我用意何在——那么这一年，我许给您。自我们在巴黎分手那天起，我将等您一整年，等您的音信。……还有今晚，我也随您同去，甘心情愿，既然这是您的意愿！」
而后，见他起身立在那里，惊喜交集、将信将疑、不知所措，她以一个勇敢而大方的姿态，向他伸出了手。
「兰亚德先生，我允诺。……」


===Original===
To every woman, even the least lovely, her hour of beauty: it had not entered Lanyard’s mind to think this woman beautiful until that moment. Of her exotic charm, of the allure of her pensive, plaintive prettiness, he had been well aware; even as he had been unable to deny to himself that he was all for her, that he loved her with all the strength that was his; but not till now had he understood that she was the one woman whose loveliness to him would darken the fairness of all others.
And for a little, holding her tremulous hand upon his fingertips as though he feared to bruise it with a ruder contact, he could not take his eyes from her.
Then reverently he bowed his head and touched his lips to that hand﻿ ﻿… and felt it snatched swiftly away, and started back, aghast, the idyll roughly dissipated, the castle of his dreams falling in thunders round his ears.
In the studio-skylight overhead a pane of glass had fallen in with a shattering crash as ominous as the Trump of Doom.

===Chinese===
天下每个女人，哪怕最不标致的，都有她艳光照人的一刻：直到这一刻之前，兰亚德压根不曾起念觉得这个女人是美的。她那异域风致的魅力，她那含愁带怨的秀媚所生的诱惑，他早已了然；正如他无法向自己否认，他已把整颗心都押在她身上，倾尽他全部的气力爱着她；只是直到此刻他才懂得：唯有这个女子的美，在他眼里能压熄天下其余的姿色。
有好一阵，他把那只微颤的手托在指尖上，仿佛怕稍重一点的触碰便会碰伤它，目光再也无法从她身上挪开。
而后他虔敬地垂下头，将唇印上那只手……却觉它被倏地抽走；他惊得向后一跳，骇然失色——田园牧歌被粗暴地驱散，他梦中的城堡轰隆隆坍塌在耳畔。
头顶画室的天窗上，一块玻璃哗啦一声砸落下来，那碎响不祥，直如末日审判的号角。

## XXVII Daybreak / 二十七、破晓

===Original===
The Englishman smiled cheerfully in response to Lanyard’s cry of astonishment.
“In effect,” he observed, stripping off his gauntlets, “you’re right, Mr. Lanyard. ‘Wertheimer’ isn’t my name, but it is so closely identified with my﻿—ah﻿—insinuative personality as to warrant the misapprehension. I shan’t demand an apology so long as you permit me to preserve an incognito which may yet prove somewhat useful.”
“Incognito!” Lanyard stammered, utterly discountenanced. “Useful!”
“You have my meaning exactly; although my work in Paris is now ended, there’s no saying when it may not be convenient to be able to go back without establishing a new identity.”
Before Lanyard replied to this the look of wonder in his eyes had yielded to one of understanding.

===Chinese===
英国人笑吟吟地，应着兰亚德那一声惊呼。
「的确，」他摘下驾驶长手套，说道，「你说得对，兰亚德先生。『维特海默』并非我的本名，只是它与我这——啊——惯会钻营攀附的脾性早已难分难解，闹出这误会事出有因。只要你容我保留这层匿名行藏——它说不定还有用处——我便不要求你赔礼。」
「匿名行藏！」兰亚德结结巴巴，全然失了方寸。「有用！」
「你正说中了我的意思；虽说我在巴黎的差事如今已然了结，可谁说得准什么时候，不必另造一个新身份便能回去一趟，不致派不上用场。」
兰亚德还未及作答，眼中的惊异之色已然化作了然。

===Original===
“Scotland Yard, eh?” he queried curtly.
Wertheimer bowed. “Special agent,” he added.
“I might have guessed, if I’d had the wit of a goose!” Lanyard affirmed bitterly. “But I must admit﻿ ﻿…”
“Yes,” the Englishman assented pleasantly; “I did pull your leg﻿—didn’t I? But not more than our other friends. Of course, it’s taken some time: I had to establish myself firmly as a shining light of the swell mob over here before De Morbihan would take me to his hospitable bosom.”
“I presume I’m to consider myself under arrest?”

===Chinese===
「苏格兰场，嗯？」他简短地问道。
维特海默躬身一礼。「特派密探。」他补充道。
「我要是有鹅那么点机灵，早该猜到了！」兰亚德恨恨地说。「不过我必须承认……」
「是啊，」英国人和颜悦色地应道；「我的确耍了你——不是么？不过比起咱们其余几位朋友来，也不算更甚。当然，这费了些时日：我得先在本地时髦窃帮里牢牢立住脚，做一颗耀眼的明星，德·莫尔比昂才肯把我收进他那好客的怀抱。」
「我想，我该把自己看作用被捕之身了？」

===Original===
With a laugh, the Englishman shook his head vigorously.
“No, thank you!” he declared. “I’ve had too convincing proof of your distaste for interference in your affairs. You fight too sincerely, Mr. Lanyard﻿—and I’m a tired sleuth this very morning as ever was! I would need a week’s rest to fit me for the job of taking you into custody﻿—a week and some able-bodied assistance!﻿ ﻿… But,” he amended with graver countenance, “I will say this: if you’re in England a week hence, I’ll be tempted to undertake the job on general principles. I don’t in the least question the sincerity of your intention to behave yourself hereafter; but as a servant of the King, it’s my duty to advise you that England would prefer you to start life anew﻿—as they say﻿—in another country. Several steamers sail for the States before the end of the week: further details I leave entirely to your discretion. But go you must,” he concluded firmly.
“I understand﻿ ﻿…” said Lanyard; and would have said more, but couldn’t. There was something suspiciously like a mist before his eyes.
Avoiding the faces of his sweetheart and the Englishman, he turned aside, put forth a hand blindly to a wing of the biplane to steady himself, and stood with head bowed and limbs trembling.
Moving quietly to his side, the girl took his other hand and held it tight.﻿ ﻿…
Presently Lanyard shook himself impatiently and lifted his head again.

===Chinese===
英国人朗声一笑，使劲摇头。
「不，多谢！」他宣称。「你有多厌恶旁人插手你的事务，我已领教得再透彻不过。你动起手来太实打实了，兰亚德先生——而我今儿一大早，恰是有史以来最疲乏的一条侦探！要把你捉拿归案，我总得先歇上一个礼拜——一个礼拜，外加几名身强力壮的帮手！……不过，」他神色转为郑重，修正道，「有一句我得说在前头：过一个礼拜你若还在英格兰，单凭原则，我恐怕就要动这个差事的念头了。你此后安分做人的诚意，我毫不多疑；可身为主之仆，我有责任奉劝你：英格兰宁愿你——照俗话说的——到别的国度去重新做人。本周末前有几班轮船启程赴美：其余细节，悉听尊便。但你非走不可，」他斩钉截铁地下了断语。
「我明白……」兰亚德说；本想再说几句，却说不出来。他眼前浮起一层什么，怎么看怎么像雾。
他避开爱人与英国人的脸，侧过身去，恍然伸出一只手，扶住双翼机的一只机翼撑住身子，垂首而立，四肢发颤。
姑娘悄然移步到他身旁，握住他另一只手，紧紧攥住。……
少顷，兰亚德不耐地一振精神，重新抬起头来。

===Original===
“Sorry,” he said, apologetic﻿—“but your generosity﻿—when I looked for nothing better than arrest﻿—was a bit too much for my nerves!”
“Nonsense!” the Englishman commented with brusque good-humour. “We’re all upset. A drop of brandy will do us no end of good.”
Unbuttoning his leather surtout, he produced a flask from an inner pocket, filled its metal cup, and offered it to the girl.
“You first, if you please, Miss Shannon. No﻿—I insist. You positively need it.”
She allowed herself to be persuaded, drank, coughed, gasped, and returned the cup, which Wertheimer promptly refilled and passed to Lanyard.
The raw spirits stung like fire, but proved an instant aid to the badly jangled nerves of the adventurer. In another moment he was much more himself.

===Chinese===
「对不住，」他歉然道——「可你们这份慷慨——在我原以为再没有比被捕更好的下场时——实在有点叫我的神经承受不住！」
「废话！」英国人粗声粗气却不失好脾气地评论。「咱们全都失了常态。来一口白兰地，于咱们大有好处。」
他解开皮质外套（surtout）的纽扣，从内袋里摸出一只扁酒瓶，斟满那只金属杯盖，递与姑娘。
「请你先来，香农小姐。别推辞——我坚持。你实在需要它。」
她依言被劝动，饮了一口，呛咳起来，倒抽一口气，交回杯子；维特海默随即重新斟满，递给兰亚德。
烈酒如火般灼刺，却立时调理了冒险家那错乱不堪的神经。转眼之间，他已恢复了大半本相。

===Original===
Drinking in turn, Wertheimer put away the flask. “That’s better!” he commented. “Now I’ll be able to cut along with this blessed machine without fretting over the fate of Ekstrom. But till now I haven’t been able to forget﻿—”
He paused and drew a hand across his eyes.
“It was, then, Ekstrom﻿—you think?” Lanyard demanded.
“Unquestionably! De Morbihan had learned﻿—I know﻿—of your bargain with Ducroy; and I know, too, that he and Ekstrom spent each morning in the hangars at St. Germain, after your sensational evasion. It never entered my head, of course, that they had any such insane scheme brewing as that﻿—else I would never have so giddily arranged with Ducroy﻿—through the Sûreté, you understand﻿—to take Vauquelin’s place.﻿ ﻿… Besides, who else could it have been? Not De Morbihan, for he’s crippled for life, thanks to that affair in the Bois; not Popinot, who was on his way to the Santé, last I saw of him; and never Bannon﻿—he was dead before I left Paris for Port Aviation.”
“Dead!”

===Chinese===
维特海默轮着也饮了一口，收起酒瓶。「这就好多了！」他评道。「如今我可以驾着这架宝贝机器赶路，不必再为埃克斯特罗姆的下场悬心了。只是直到此刻之前，我一直忘不掉——」
他顿住话头，抬手在眼睛上抹了一把。
「那么说，那是埃克斯特罗姆——你以为？」兰亚德追问。
「毋庸置疑！德·莫尔比昂已经得知——这个我知道——你同迪克罗的那桩交易；我还知道，自打你那场轰动一时的金蝉脱壳之后，他同埃克斯特罗姆每个上午都泡在圣日耳曼的飞机棚里。当然，我做梦也不曾想到，他们竟在酝酿那样丧心病狂的勾当——否则我决不至于那样晕头晕脑地通过保安局（la Sûreté）——你明白——与迪克罗安排停当，去顶替沃克兰上尉。……再说，还能有谁？不会是德·莫尔比昂，多亏布洛涅林苑里那场好戏，他这辈子算是残了；不会是波皮诺，我上回见他时，他正在押赴桑泰监狱的路上；更决计不会是班农——我离开巴黎动身赴航空港（Port Aviation）之前，他就已经死了。」
「死了！」

===Original===
“Oh, quite!” the Englishman affirmed nonchalantly, “When we arrested him at three this morning﻿—charged with complicity in the murder of Roddy﻿—he flew into a passion that brought on a fatal haemorrhage. He died within ten minutes.”
There was a little silence.﻿ ﻿…
“I may tell you, Mr. Lanyard,” the Englishman resumed, looking up from the motor, to which he was paying attentions with monkey-wrench and oilcan, “that you were quite off your bat when you ridiculed the idea of the ‘International Underworld Unlimited.’ Of course, if you *hadn’t* laughed, I shouldn’t feel quite as much respect for you as I do; in fact, the chances are you’d be in handcuffs or in a cell of the Santé, this very minute.﻿ ﻿… But, absurd as it sounded﻿—and was﻿—the ‘Underworld’ project was a pet hobby of Bannon’s﻿—who’d been the brains of a gang of criminals in New York for many years. He was a bit touched on the subject: a monomaniac, if you ask me. And his enthusiasm won De Morbihan and Popinot over﻿ ﻿… and me! He took a wonderful fancy to me, Bannon did; I really was appointed first-lieutenant in Greggs’ stead.﻿ ﻿… So you first won my sympathy by laughing at my offer,” said Wertheimer, restoring the oilcan to its place in the tool-kit; “wherein you were very wise.﻿ ﻿… In fact, my personal feeling for you is one of growing esteem, if you’ll permit me to say so. You’ve most of the makings of a man. Will you shake hands﻿—with a copper’s nark?”
He gave Lanyard’s hand a firm and friendly grasp, and turned to the girl.

===Chinese===
「哦，的确！」英国人淡淡地证实，「今天凌晨三点我们逮捕他时——罪名为谋杀罗迪案从犯——他勃然大怒，引动一场致命的大出血。不出十分钟便一命呜呼。」
一阵短短的沉默。……
「不妨告诉你，兰亚德先生，」英国人接着说了下去，一面从引擎上抬起眼来——方才他正拿活动扳手和油壶对它殷勤伺候——「你当初嘲笑『国际黑社会无限公司』那主意，实在是错得离谱。当然，你若是*没有*笑，我对你的敬意便断不会像如今这般深；说不得，此刻你早已戴上手铐，蹲在桑泰监狱的单间里了。……不过，那计划听来荒唐——实际也荒唐——『黑社会』这项工程却是班农的心头珍玩——多年来，他一直是纽约一伙罪犯的智囊。他在这题目上有点魔怔：要我说，就是个偏执狂。而他的热忱把德·莫尔比昂和波皮诺都拽下了水……还有我！班农对我青睐得很，真的；我竟真被任命接替格雷格斯，坐上了『副统领』的位子。……所以说，你先是靠嗤笑我的提议赢得了我的好感，」维特海默说着，将油壶放回工具箱原位；「这一点你聪明得很。……老实说，恕我直言，我对你的私人情谊，敬意正与日俱增。大丈夫应具的资质，你已十有其九。可愿握一握手——同一个警察局的眼线（copper's nark）？」
他紧紧而友好地一握兰亚德的手，随即转向姑娘。

===Original===
“Goodbye, Miss Shannon. I’m truly grateful for the assistance you gave us. Without you, we’d have been sadly handicapped. I understand you have sent in your resignation? It’s too bad: the Service will feel the loss of you. But I think you were right to leave us, the circumstances considered.﻿ ﻿… And now it’s goodbye and good luck! I hope you may be happy.﻿ ﻿… I’m sure you can’t go far without coming across a highroad or a village; but﻿—for reasons not unconnected with my profession﻿—I prefer to remain in ignorance of the way you go.”
Releasing her hand, he stepped back, saluted the lovers with a smile and gay gesture, and clambered briskly to the pilot’s seat of the biplane.
When firmly established, he turned the switch of the starting mechanism.
The heavy, distinctive hum of the great motor filled that isolated hollow in the Downs like the purring of a dynamo.
With a final wave of his hand, Wertheimer grasped the starting-lever.

===Chinese===
「再会，香农小姐。你给予我们的助力，我由衷感激。没有你，我们势将大大受制，吃亏不小。我听说你已递上辞呈？太可惜了：本机关将要痛感失去你。不过，就情势而论，我认为你离开我们是对的。……好了，就此别过，祝你好运！愿你幸福。……我担保你们走不出多远，准会撞上一条大路或一座村庄；只是——为着一些与我这一行不无干系的缘故——你们往哪个方向走，我情愿一无所知。」
他放开她的手，退后一步，含笑以潇洒的手势向这对爱侣致意，随即轻捷地攀上双翼机的驾驶座。
坐定之后，他打开了启动装置的开关。
大引擎那低沉独特的嗡鸣，宛如发电机的震颤，顿时灌满丘原（the Downs）上这片与世隔绝的洼地。
维特海默最后挥了挥手，握住了启动杆。

===Original===
Its brool deepening, the *Parrott* stirred, shot forward abruptly. In two seconds it was fifty yards distant, its silhouette already blurred, its wheels lifting from the rim of the hollow.
Then lightly it leaped, soared, parted the mists, vanished.﻿ ﻿…
For some time Lanyard and Lucy Shannon remained motionless, clinging together, hand-in-hand, listening to the drone that presently dwindled to a mere thread of sound and died out altogether in the obscurity above them.
Then, turning, they faced each other, smiling a trace uncertainly, a smile that said: “So all that is finished!﻿ ﻿… Or, perhaps, we dreamed it!”﻿ ﻿…
Suddenly, with a low cry, the girl gave herself to Lanyard’s arms; and as this happened the mists parted and bright sunlight flooded the hollow in the Downs.

===Chinese===
低啸愈见深沉，*帕罗特*双翼机一颤，骤然向前窜出。不过两秒钟，它已在五十码开外，侧影已然模糊，机轮正渐离洼地的边缘。
继而它轻轻一跃，扶摇而起，破开雾霭，杳然消失。……
好半晌，兰亚德同露西·香农一动不动地伫立，手牵着手相互依偎，听着那嗡鸣渐渐低微下去，终至只剩一缕游丝般的声息，彻底消殒在他们头顶的苍茫高处。
而后两人转过身来，面面相对，唇边各噙一丝微觉恍惑的笑意，那笑仿佛在说：「这一切总算完了！……再不然，就是我们做了一场梦！」……
蓦然间，姑娘低呼一声，投进兰亚德的怀抱；也正在此时，雾霭豁然中开，明亮的阳光漫溢了丘原上的这片洼地。

## IX Disaster / 九、大祸临头

===Original===
Having fulfilled his purpose of making himself acquainted with the personnel of the opposition, Lanyard slammed the door in its face, thrust his hands in his pockets, and sauntered downstairs, chuckling, his nose in the air, on the best of terms with himself.
True, the fat was in the fire and well ablaze: he had to look to himself now, and go warily in the shadow of their enmity. But it was something to have faced down those four, and he wasn’t seriously impressed by any one of them.
Popinot, perhaps, was the most dangerous in Lanyard’s esteem; a vindictive animal, that Popinot; and the creatures he controlled, a murderous lot, drug-ridden, drink bedevilled, vicious little rats of Belleville, who’d knife a man for the price of an absinthe. But Popinot wouldn’t move without leave from De Morbihan, and unless Lanyard’s calculations were seriously miscast, De Morbihan would restrain both himself and his associates until thoroughly convinced Lanyard was impregnable against every form of persuasion. Murder was something a bit out of De Morbihan’s line﻿—something, at least, which he might be counted on to hold in reserve. And by the time he was ready to employ it, Lanyard would be well beyond his reach. Wertheimer, too, would deprecate violence until all else failed; his half-caste type was as cowardly as it was blackguard; and cowards kill only impulsively, before they’ve had time to weigh consequences. There remained “Smith,” enigma; a man apparently gifted with both intelligence and character.﻿ ﻿… But if so, what the deuce was *he* doing in such company?

===Chinese===
既已遂了心愿、把对面那班人马的底细认了个清楚，兰亚德便当着他们的面砰地摔上门，双手插进衣袋，鼻孔朝天，一路轻笑、悠然踱下楼去，同自己交情好得不能再好。
不错，肥肉已然下了锅、还烧得正旺：如今他得处处照管自己，在他们敌意的阴影里放轻脚步行事了。可话说回来，能凭一股气势镇住那四位，总算是一桩成绩，况且他们里头没有哪一个叫他真心忌惮。
依兰亚德的估量，最危险的或许得数波皮诺；那是头睚眦必报的畜生，这位波皮诺；他驱使的那班东西，则是一窝杀人不眨眼的货色——毒品浸透、烧酒迷心，贝尔维尔（Belleville）出品的歹毒小耗子，为一杯苦艾酒的价钱就敢捅人一刀。可波皮诺没有德·莫尔比昂点头绝不动弹；而且，除非兰亚德的算盘打得大错特错，德·莫尔比昂必会按住自己、也按住他那班同伙，直到彻底死心、确信兰亚德软硬不吃、水泼不进为止。凶杀这门营生，多少出了德·莫尔比昂的本行——至少，是可以指望他留作后手的东西。而等他打算启用这一手时，兰亚德早已远走高飞、够他不着了。维特海默也一样，不到山穷水尽不会赞成动粗；他那副黑白混血的做派，无赖几分，便也怯懦几分；而怯懦的人杀人，只在一时冲动、还来不及掂量后果的当口。剩下的便是「史密斯」（Smith），一团谜；此人看去兼备头脑与胆识。……可若当真如此，*他*究竟在那路人中间胡混什么？

===Original===
Still, there he was: and the association damned him beyond consideration. His sorts were all of a piece, beneath the consideration of men of spirit.﻿ ﻿…
At this point, the self-complacence bred of his contempt for Messrs. de Morbihan et Cie. bred in its turn a thought that brought the adventurer up standing.
The devil! Who was he, Michael Lanyard, that held himself above such vermin, yet lived in such a way as practically to invite their advances? What right was his to resent their opening the door to confraternity, as long as he trod paths so closely parallel to theirs that only a sophist might discriminate them? What comforting distinction was to be drawn between on the one hand a blackmailer like Wertheimer, a *chevalier-d’industrie* like De Morbihan, or a patron of Apaches like Popinot, and on the other himself whose bread was eaten in the sweat of thievery?
He drew a long face; whistled softly; shook his head; and smiled a wry smile.
“Glad I didn’t think of that two minutes ago, or I’d never have had the cheek﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
话虽如此，人确凿是在那里的：单凭这份交往，他便已被打入另册、不值一顾了。他这路人都是一个模子铸出来的，有血性的人理都不该理。……
想到这里，那份由轻蔑德·莫尔比昂暨公司（Messrs. de Morbihan et Cie.）诸位先生而生出的洋洋自得，又反过来生出一个念头，把这位冒险家惊得当场立定。
见鬼！他迈克尔·兰亚德算老几，自命高出这班虫豸一等，过活的方式却分明是在邀他们来攀交情？只要他走的道同他们的道平行得那么紧、紧到唯有诡辩家才分辨得开，他又有什么权利怪人家敞开「同门」之门？一边是维特海默那样的敲诈者、德·莫尔比昂那样的*chevalier-d’industrie*（「实业骑士」，骗子的雅称）、波皮诺那样的阿帕奇供养人，一边是面包蘸着盗窃的汗水吃的他自己——这两造之间，还能画出什么叫人宽心的界线？
他拉长了一张脸；轻轻打了声呼哨；摇了摇头；又扯出一丝苦笑。
「幸亏两分钟前没想起这一茬，不然我哪还拉得下这张脸……」

===Original===
Without warning, incongruously and, in his understanding, inexplicably, he found himself beset by recurrent memory of the girl, Lucia Bannon.
For an instant he saw her again, quite vividly, as last he had seen her: turning at the door of her bedchamber to look back at him, a vision of perturbing charm in her rose-silk dressing-gown, with rich hair loosened, cheeks softly glowing, eyes brilliant with an emotion illegible to her one beholder.﻿ ﻿…
What had been the message of those eyes, flashed down the dimly lighted length of that corridor at Troyon’s, ere she vanished?
Adieu? Or au revoir?﻿ ﻿…
She had termed him, naively enough, and a gentleman.
But if she knew﻿—suspected﻿—even dreamed﻿—that he was what he was?﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
毫无征兆、不伦不类、照他自己的理解也全无来由地，他发觉自己被那姑娘——露西娅·班农——的回忆缠上了身。
刹那间他又见到了她，活生生如在目前，正是最后一面的光景：她在卧房门口回身望他，一袭玫瑰色绸晨衣，衬出一派扰人心神的妩媚风致；一头丰发松松散落，双颊晕着柔光，一双眸子亮灼灼的，盛着一种她那仅有的观众读不出来的情绪。……
在特鲁瓦旅馆那条灯光昏暗的长廊尽头、她隐没之前，那双眼睛一路闪送过来的，究竟是怎样一个讯息？
是 Adieu（永别）？还是 au revoir（后会有期）？……
她方才还天真得可以地夸他，称他是一位绅士来着。
可她若知道——起疑——哪怕仅仅梦见——他竟是这么一号人物呢？……

===Original===
He shook his head again, but now impatiently, with a scowl and a grumble:
“What’s the matter with me anyway? Mooning over a girl I never saw before tonight! As if it matters a whoop in Hepsidam what she thinks!﻿ ﻿… Or is it possible I’m beginning to develop a rudimentary conscience, at this late day? Me!﻿ ﻿…”
If there were anything in this hypothesis, the growing-pains of that late-blooming conscience were soon enough numbed by the hypnotic spell of clattering chips, an ivory ball singing in an ebony race, and croaking croupiers.
For Lanyard’s chair at the table of chemin-de-fer had been filled by another and, too impatient to wait a vacancy, he wandered on to the salon dedicated to roulette, tested his luck by staking a note of five hundred francs on the black, won, and incontinently subsided into a chair and an oblivion that endured for the space of three-quarters of an hour.

===Chinese===
他又摇了摇头，这一回却不耐烦了，眉头一皱，嘟哝一声：
「我到底是怎么了？为一个今晚才头回见着的姑娘痴痴出神！她怎么想，在这海普西丹（Hepsidam）鬼地方又有个什么紧要！……莫非到了这步田地，我竟开始萌发一星半点原始形态的良心了？我！……」
这假设若真有几分道理，那么那颗迟开花朵般的良心，其生长的阵痛也很快便叫催眠术一般的东西麻醉了过去——筹码相击的脆响、乌木轮道里象牙小球的清吟，还有荷官（croupier）们嘶哑的唱喏。
原来兰亚德在铁路牌（chemin-de-fer）赌桌边的位子已叫人占了去，他又没耐性候缺，便信步踱进专供轮盘赌（roulette）的厅堂，拿一张五百法郎的票子押黑，试了试手气，赢了，当下便一屁股沉进椅子和忘却里，一沉便是三刻钟。

===Original===
At the end of that period he found himself minus his heavy winnings at chemin-de-fer and ten thousand francs of his reserve fund to boot.
By way of lining for his pockets there remained precisely the sum which he had brought into Paris that same evening, less subsequent general disbursements.
The experience was nothing novel in his history. He rose less resentful than regretful that his ill-luck obliged him to quit just when play was most interesting, and resignedly sought the cloakroom for his coat and hat.
And there he found De Morbihan﻿—again!﻿—standing all garmented for the street, mouthing a huge cigar and wearing a look of impatient discontent.
“At last!” he cried in an aggrieved tone as Lanyard appeared in the offing. “You do take your time, my friend!”

===Chinese===
三刻钟期满，他发觉自己折了铁路牌赢来的大笔进项不算，还倒贴了备用金一万法郎。
至于口袋的衬里，剩下的不多不少，正是他当晚带进巴黎的那笔数目——再减去此后的各项杂支。
这番阅历在他的历史上原不算新鲜。他起身时怨怼少、懊悔多——只怪手气不济，偏在赌兴最浓的当口逼他退场——便认了命，寻衣帽间去取大衣帽子。
而在那儿他撞见了德·莫尔比昂——又是他！——只见此人一身出客的披挂，嘴里斜叼着支大雪茄，一脸的不耐烦与不痛快。
「可算来了！」兰亚德的人影刚一露头，他便委屈地嚷道，「您可真会消磨功夫，我的朋友！」

===Original===
Lanyard smothered with a smile whatever emotion was his of the moment.
“I didn’t imagine you really meant to wait for me,” he parried with double meaning, both to humour De Morbihan and hoodwink the attendant.
“What do you think?” retorted the Count with asperity﻿—“that I’m willing to stand by and let you moon round Paris at this hour of the morning, hunting for a taxicab that isn’t to be found and running God-knows-what risk of being stuck up by some misbegotten Apache? But I should say not! I mean to take you home in my car, though it cost me a half-hour of beauty sleep not lightly to be forfeited at my age!”
The significance that underlay the semi-humourous petulance of the little man was not wasted.
“You’re most amiable, Monsieur le Comte!” Lanyard observed thoughtfully, while the attendant produced his hat and coat. “So now, if you’re ready, I won’t delay you longer.”

===Chinese===
兰亚德拿一个微笑，把此刻心头无论什么样的情绪都掩了过去。
「我还不信您当真要等我呢，」他一语双关地挡了回去，既是要迎合德·莫尔比昂，也好蒙过那名侍者。
「您以为呢？」伯爵刻薄地回敬——「以为我就乐意袖手旁观，任您大清早这个时辰在巴黎满城闲逛，去找一辆压根儿寻不见的出租汽车，还天晓得冒多大的险、叫哪个野路上生出来的阿帕奇给劫了道？我才不呢！我要用我的车送您回府，哪怕这得赔掉半个钟头的养颜觉——在我这年纪，可不敢等闲糟践！」
这小个子半嗔半谐的怨言底下藏着的深意，兰亚德一点没漏，领受了个通透。
「伯爵先生（Monsieur le Comte），您真是客气极了！」兰亚德若有所思地说着，侍者正把他的帽子大衣取了来。「那么，您若是备好了，我就不再耽误您了。」

===Original===
In another moment they were outside the clubhouse, its doors shut behind them, while before them, at the curb, waited that same handsome black limousine which had brought the adventurer from L’Abbaye.
Two swift glances, right and left, showed him an empty street, bare of hint of danger.
“One moment, monsieur!” he said, detaining the Count with a touch on his sleeve. “It’s only right that I should advise you﻿ ﻿… I’m armed.”
“Then you’re less foolhardy than one feared. If such things interest you, I don’t mind admitting I carry a life-preserver of my own. But what of that? Is one eager to go shooting at this time of night, for the sheer fun of explaining to sergents de ville that one has been attacked by Apaches?﻿ ﻿… Providing always one lives to explain!”
“It’s as bad as that, eh?”
“Enough to make me loath to linger at your side in a lighted doorway!”

===Chinese===
转眼间两人已出了夜总会的大门，门扇在身后阖上；眼前路缘石旁，正候着那辆气派的黑色大轿车——先前把这位冒险家从「修道院」（L'Abbaye）载来的，便是它。
左右飞快各扫一眼，但见街面空空，不见一丝危险的端倪。
「且慢，先生！」他说着，往伯爵袖上一搭，留住了他。「有件事理当奉告……我是带枪的。」
「这么说，您还不像人家怕的那样有勇无谋。您若对这类物件感兴趣，我不妨承认，我自己也随身揣着件保命的玩意儿。可那又怎样？谁乐意在这个时辰的夜里放枪，纯为着寻个乐子——回头好向街头巡警（sergents de ville）解释，说是遭了阿帕奇拦路？……前提还得是人有命解释！」
「竟凶险到这步田地了，嗯？」
「凶险得叫我不乐意陪着您，在亮着灯的门洞里多站一秒！」

===Original===
Lanyard laughed in his own discomfiture. “Monsieur le Comte,” said he, “there’s a dash in you of what your American pal, Mysterious Smith, would call sporting blood, that commands my unstinted admiration. I thank you for your offered courtesy, and beg leave to accept.”
De Morbihan replied with a grunt of none too civil intonation, instructed the chauffeur “To Troyon’s,” and followed Lanyard into the car.
“Courtesy!” he repeated, settling himself with a shake. “That makes nothing. If I regarded my own inclinations, I’d let you go to the devil as quick as Popinot’s assassins could send you there!”
“This is delightful!” Lanyard protested. “First you must see me home to save my life, and then you tell me your inclinations consign me to a premature grave. Is there an explanation, possibly?”
“On your person,” said the Count, sententious.
“Eh?”

===Chinese===
兰亚德为自己的狼狈失声笑了。「伯爵先生，」他说，「您身上有那么一点——用您那位美国朋友『神秘的史密斯』（Mysterious Smith）的话说——好斗的血性，令我心悦诚服、佩服无已。多谢您慨然赐惠，请容我拜领。」
德·莫尔比昂拿一声哼哼作答，腔调算不得客气，吩咐司机（chauffeur）「去特鲁瓦旅馆」，随兰亚德上了车。
「赐惠！」他把自己安顿坐稳，一抖身子，又重复了一遍。「这算不得什么。我若依着自己的性子，任凭波皮诺的刺客多快把您打发去见魔鬼，我就多快撒手不管！」
「妙不可言！」兰亚德抗声道，「起初您非要送我回府，为的是保我一命；转眼又告诉我，照您的性子，恨不能我早进坟墓。这里面可有什么讲究，嗯？」
「在您身上。」伯爵说得斩钉截铁。
「嗯？」

===Original===
“You carry your reason with you, my friend﻿—in the shape of the Omber loot.”
“Assuming you are right﻿—”
“You never went to the rue du Bac, monsieur, without those jewels: and I have had you under observation ever since.”
“What conceivable interest,” Lanyard pursued evenly, “do you fancy you’ve got in the said loot?”
“Enough, at least, to render me unwilling to kiss it adieu by leaving you to the mercies of Popinot. You don’t imagine I’d ever hear of it again, when his Apaches had finished with you?”
“Ah!﻿ ﻿… So, after all, your so-called organization isn’t founded on that reciprocal trust so essential to the prosperity of such﻿—enterprises!”

===Chinese===
「缘由随身带在您身上呢，我的朋友——以欧姆贝那票赃物的形貌。」
「就算您说中了——」
「先生，您没有一回上巴克街（rue du Bac）是不带那些珠宝的：打那以后，我便一直把您置于监视之下。」
「那么依您想来，」兰亚德不动声色地追问，「您在这批赃物里，能有什么想得出来的好处？」
「至少多得叫我不情愿就此同它吻别——不情愿把您扔给波皮诺发落。您总不至于以为，等他的阿帕奇把您消遣完了，我还听得到那票东西的下落吧？」
「啊！……这么说，你们这个所谓的组织，到底也不是建立在那份互信上的——而这类——事业——要兴旺发达，互信原是顶要紧的！」

===Original===
“Amuse yourself as you will with your inferences, my friend,” the Count returned, unruffled; “but don’t forget my advice: pull wide of Popinot!”
“A vindictive soul, eh?”
“One may say that.”
“You can’t hold him?”
“That one? No fear! You were anything but wise to bait him as you did.”
“Perhaps. It’s purely a matter of taste in associates.”

===Chinese===
「我的朋友，您的推论爱怎么消遣随您，」伯爵不慌不忙地回敬，「只是别忘了我给您的忠告：离波皮诺远着点儿！」
「一个记仇的性子，嗯？」
「可以这么说。」
「您拿他没办法？」
「那位？没门儿！您方才那样撩他，实在是再不智不过了。」
「也许。这纯粹是个择伴的口味问题。」

===Original===
“If I were the fool you think me,” mused the Count “I’d resent that innuendo. As it happens, I’m not. At least, I can wait before calling you to account.”
“And meantime profit by your patience?”
“But naturally. Haven’t I said as much?”
“Still, I’m perplexed. I can’t imagine how you reckon to declare yourself in on the Omber loot.”
“All in good time: if you were wise, you’d hand the stuff over to me here and now, and accept what I chose to give you in return. But inasmuch as you’re the least wise of men, you must have your lesson.”
“Meaning﻿—?”

===Chinese===
「我若当真是您想的那种傻瓜，」伯爵沉吟道，「这含讥带讽可就要开罪于我了。偏偏我不是。至少，在跟您算总账之前，我等得起。」
「这期间就顺便拿您的耐心赚一笔？」
「自然。我不是把话都说到这份上了么？」
「可我还是纳闷。我想不出，您打算怎么个章法，把自己申报成欧姆贝赃物里的一份子。」
「火候到了自然见分晓：您若是个明白人，此刻就把货交到我手上，我乐意给什么，您便拿什么。无奈您是天底下头一号不明白的人，那只好领受一顿教训。」
「此话怎讲——？」

===Original===
“The night brings counsel: you’ll have time to think things over. By tomorrow you’ll be coming to offer me those jewels in exchange for what influence I have in certain quarters.”
“With your famous friend, the Chief of the Sûreté, eh?”
“Possibly. I am known also at La Tour Pointue.”
“I confess I don’t follow you, unless you mean to turn informer.”
“Never that.”
“It’s a riddle, then?”

===Chinese===
「夜里自生智谋：您有的是工夫细想。到明天，您自会来找我，拿那些珠宝，换我在某些地方的一点势力。」
「托您那位大名鼎鼎的朋友——保安局长——的福，嗯？」
「也未可知。尖塔（La Tour Pointue）那座衙门里，我也是个熟人。」
「恕我直言跟不上您了——除非您是打算改行当告密的人。」
「决计不是。」
「那么，这是一则谜语了？」

===Original===
“For the moment only.﻿ ﻿… But I will say this: it will be futile, your attempting to escape Paris; Popinot has already picketted every outlet. Your one hope resides in me; and I shall be at home to you until midnight tomorrow﻿—today, rather.”
Impressed in spite of himself, Lanyard stared. But the Count maintained an imperturbable manner, looking straight ahead. Such calm assurance would hardly be sheer bluff.
“I must think this over,” Lanyard mused aloud.
“Pray don’t let me hinder you,” the Count begged with mild sarcasm. “I have my own futile thoughts.﻿ ﻿…”
Lanyard laughed quietly and subsided into a reverie which, undisturbed by De Morbihan, endured throughout the brief remainder of their drive; for, thanks to the smallness of the hour, the streets were practically deserted and offered no obstacle to speed; while the chauffeur was doubtless eager for his bed.

===Chinese===
「眼下而已。……但有句话我撂在这儿：您想逃出巴黎，徒劳无功；波皮诺已经把每一条出路都设了卡。您唯一的指望，全系在我身上；明天——不，该说今天——午夜之前，我在家恭候您。」
兰亚德不由得动容，直着眼打量他。伯爵却端凝如恒，目视前方。这般气定神闲的笃定，多半不至于是虚声恫吓。
「这事我得好好琢磨琢磨。」兰亚德出声自语。
「千万别让我碍了您的事，」伯爵温言相激，「我自己也有我那点不中用的念头。」
兰亚德轻轻一笑，沉入遐想；德·莫尔比昂也不来打搅，这遐想便一直绵延到车程将尽的短短一程——亏得时辰已晚，街上几乎绝了人迹，车行如飞、一无阻碍；何况司机十有八九也正惦着他那张床。

===Original===
As they drew near Troyon’s, however, Lanyard sat up and jealously reconnoitered both sides of the way.
“Surely you don’t expect to be kept out?” the Count asked dryly. “But that just shows how little you appreciate our good Popinot. He’ll never object to your locking yourself up where he knows he can find you﻿—but only to your leaving without permission!”
“Something in that, perhaps. Still, I make it a rule to give myself the benefit of every doubt.”
There was, indeed, no sign of ambush that he could detect in any quarter, nor any indication that Popinot’s Apaches were posted thereabouts. Nevertheless, Lanyard produced his automatic and freed the safety-catch before opening the door.
“A thousand thanks, my dear Count!”
“For what? Doing myself a service? But you make me feel ashamed!”

===Chinese===
然而车到特鲁瓦旅馆左近，兰亚德便坐直身子，格外经心地侦察起道路两旁。
「您总不至于防着进不了门吧？」伯爵干巴巴地问，「可这恰恰见出，您太不领教我们那位好波皮诺了。您把自己锁进一处他笃定找得到您的地方，他绝无异议——他不乐意的，只是您不打招呼就走！」
「这话兴许有几分道理。不过我立有规矩：凡有疑处，一概姑且作有利于己想。」
果然，四面八方任他如何察看，既不见埋伏的迹象，也无半点风声表明波皮诺的阿帕奇在这一带布了桩。饶是如此，兰亚德还是掏出自动手枪，开了保险，方才去推车门。
「多谢多谢，亲爱的伯爵！」
「谢什么？给自己办了件差事么？您倒叫我过意不去了！」

===Original===
“I know,” agreed Lanyard, depreciatory; “but that’s the way I am﻿—a little devil﻿—you really can’t trust me! Adieu, Monsieur le Comte.”
“Au revoir, monsieur!”
Lanyard saw the car round the corner before turning to the entrance of Troyon’s, keeping his weather-eye alert the while. But when the car was gone, the street seemed quite deserted and as soundless as though it had been the thoroughfare of some remote village rather than an artery of the pulsing old heart of Paris.
Yet he wasn’t satisfied. He was as little susceptible to psychic admonition as any sane and normal human organism, but he was just then strongly oppressed by intuitive perception that there was something radically amiss in his neighbourhood. Whether or not the result of the Count’s open intimations and veiled hints working upon a nature sensitized by excitement and fatigue, he felt as though he had stepped from the cab into an atmosphere impregnated to saturation with nameless menace. And he even shivered a bit, perhaps because of the chill in that air of early morning, perhaps because a shadow of premonition had fallen athwart his soul.﻿ ﻿…
Whatever its cause, he could find no reason for this; and shaking himself impatiently, pressed a button that rang a bell by the ear of the concierge, heard the latch click, thrust the door wide, and reentered Troyon’s.

===Chinese===
「我知道，」兰亚德自谦地应和，「可我生就这号人——一个小恶魔——您实在是信不过我的！Adieu，伯爵先生。」
「Au revoir，先生！」
兰亚德望着汽车拐过街角，方才转向特鲁瓦旅馆的大门，其间始终留着一双警戒的眼。可汽车一去，这条街便显得空空落落、悄然无声，仿佛是哪个穷乡僻壤的村道，而不是巴黎这颗苍老而搏动的心脏上的一条通衢。
然而他心下难安。他同任何一个神志清明的正常人体一样，本最不易受什么灵异的警示，可就在此刻，一种直觉却沉沉地压着他：左近有什么东西从根本上坏了事。这感觉无论是不是伯爵那些明言暗语、落在被兴奋与疲惫磨得敏感的性子上酿出来的，他只觉得自己仿佛刚下车便一脚踏进了一团空气里——那空气让无以名状的凶险浸渍到了饱和。他甚至微微打了个寒噤，或许是因为清晨空气里的那一分寒意，或许是因为一道预感的阴影斜斜掠过了他的灵魂。……
不拘缘起为何，他寻不出个所以然来；于是他不耐烦地一抖精神，揿下按钮——铃声在门房（concierge）耳边响起，听得门闩咔哒一响，他推门而入，重新踏进了特鲁瓦旅馆。

===Original===
Here reigned a silence even more marked than that of the street, a silence as heavy and profound as the grave’s, so that sheer instinct prompted Lanyard to tread lightly as he made his way down the passage and across the courtyard toward the stairway; and in that hush the creak of a greaseless hinge, when the concierge opened the door of his quarters to identify this belated guest, seemed little less than a profanity.
Lanyard paused and delved into his pockets, nodding genially to the blowsy, sleepy old face beneath the guardian’s nightcap.
“Sorry to disturb monsieur,” he said politely, further impoverishing himself in the sum of five francs in witness to the sincerity of his regret.
“I thank monsieur; but what need to consider me? It’s my duty. And what is one interruption more or less? All night they come and go.﻿ ﻿…”
“Good night, monsieur,” Lanyard cut short the old man’s garrulity; and went on up the stairs, now a little wearily, of a sudden newly conscious of his vast and enervating fatigue.

===Chinese===
这里君临着一种比街上更甚的寂静，一种坟墓般又沉又深的死寂，叫兰亚德全凭本能便放轻了脚步，一路沿过道、穿院子，走向楼梯；而在那一片岑寂里，门房打开自己住处的门、来辨认这位迟归客人时，那缺油合页的吱呀一响，直近乎一种亵渎。
兰亚德停步，往口袋里掏摸，朝守夜睡帽底下那张浮肿惺忪的老脸亲切颔首。
「抱歉搅扰了先生，」他客客气气地说，又破费了五法郎，以见其歉忱之笃。
「多谢先生；可何必把我算在心上？这是我的本分。多一回搅扰少一回搅扰，又算得了什么？一整夜他们进进出出。」
「晚安，先生。」兰亚德截断了老人的絮叨，径自上楼，脚步已略见疲沓——他这才恍然惊觉，那一身浩大的倦怠正磨筋蚀骨地涌上来。

===Original===
He thought longingly of bed, yawned involuntarily and, reaching his door, fumbled the key in a most unprofessional way; there were weights upon his eyelids, a heaviness in his brain.﻿ ﻿…
But the key met with no resistance from the wards; and in a trice, appreciating this fact, Lanyard was wide-awake again.
No question but that he had locked the door securely, on leaving after his adventure with the charming somnambulist.﻿ ﻿…
Had she, then, taken a whim to his room?
Or was this but proof of what he had anticipated in the beginning﻿—a bit of sleuthing on the part of Roddy?
He entertained little doubt as to the correctness of this latter surmise, as he threw the door open and stepped into the room, his first action being to grasp the electric switch and twist it smartly.

===Chinese===
他一心向往着床铺，不由自主打了个呵欠，行到房门口，竟全然外行似的摸索起钥匙来；眼皮上坠着秤砣，脑子里一团滞重。……
可钥匙探进锁槽，竟未遇丝毫阻滞；一转念省悟了这一层，兰亚德登时睡意全消。
毫无疑问，同那位迷人的梦游人一番奇遇之后离开时，他把房门锁得牢牢实实。……
那么，是她一时兴起，又光顾过他的房间？
抑或，这不过印证了他起初便有的预料——罗迪又来了一手侦探功夫？
他对后一种猜测的正确性几乎不存疑问，一面便推门进房，头一个动作就是摸住电灯开关，利落地一拧。

===Original===
But no light answered.
“Hello!” he exclaimed softly, remembering that the lights could readily have been turned off at the bulbs. “What’s the good of that?”
In the same breath he started violently, and swung about.
The door had closed behind him, swiftly but gently, eclipsing the faint light from the hall, leaving what amounted to stark darkness.
His first impression was that the intruder﻿—Roddy or whoever﻿—had darted past him and out, pulling the door to in that act.
Before he could consciously revise this misconception he was fighting for his life.

===Chinese===
灯却没有应声亮起。
「喂！」他轻轻唤了一声，想起灯多半是叫人从灯座那里顺手关了的。「这一手是什么意思？」
话音未落，他猛然一惊，霍地回身。
房门已在他身后阖拢——疾速，却轻悄——把门厅里那一星微光遮蔽净尽，留下的是不折不扣的一片漆黑。
他的第一个印象是：那闯入者——罗迪也罢，是谁也罢——已从他身边掠过、夺门而出，顺势带上了门。
他还来不及有意识地修正这一误解，已在为性命相搏了。

===Original===
So unexpected, so swift and sudden fell the assault, that he was caught completely off guard: between the shutting of the door and an onslaught whose violence sent him reeling to the wall, the elapsed time could have been measured by the fluttering of an eyelash.
And then two powerful arms were round him, pinioning his hands to his sides, his feet were tripped up, and he was thrown with a force that fairly jarred his teeth, half-stunning him.
For a breath he lay dazed, struggling feebly; not long, but long enough to enable his antagonist to shift his hold and climb on top of his body, where he squatted, bearing down heavily with a knee on either of Lanyard’s forearms, hands encircling his neck, murderous thumbs digging into his windpipe.
He revived momentarily, pulled himself together, and heaved mightily in futile effort to unseat the other.
The sole outcome of this was a tightening pressure on his throat.
The pain grew agonizing; Lanyard’s breath was almost completely shut off; he gasped vainly, with a rattling noise in his gullet; his eyeballs started; a myriad coruscant lights danced and interlaced blindingly before them; in his ears there rang a roaring like the voice of heavy surf breaking upon a rockbound coast.

===Chinese===
袭击来得这样意外、这样迅疾突兀，他被打了个措手不及：自门扇阖拢，到那一阵猛得把他撞得踉跄退墙的攻势，其间所耗的时光，尽可以拿眼睫毛的一颤去量。
跟着，两条强有力的臂膀箍住了他，把他的双手死死别在身体两侧；脚下被一绊，他被人掼倒在地，那力道直震得他牙齿打颤，人也掼得半昏。
有一息工夫，他躺在那里发懵，软弱地挣扎；时间不长，却尽够他的对手变换钳制、翻身骑上他的身体——那人蹲踞其上，两膝各以千钧之势压住兰亚德一边小臂，双手环扼他的脖颈，两根杀气腾腾的拇指直抠进他的气管。
他一时回魂，攒齐心神，猛力一挺，想掀翻身上那人——徒劳无功。
此举唯一的后果，是喉间勒得更紧了。
痛楚渐至酷烈；兰亚德的气息几乎被断绝净尽；他徒然喘息，喉头格格作响；眼球暴突；眼前千万点火花乱迸乱织，晃得他目不见物；耳中嗡然震响，宛如重浪扑击礁岸的怒号。

===Original===
And of a sudden he ceased to struggle and lay slack, passive in the other’s hands.
Only an instant longer was the clutch on his throat maintained. Both hands left it quickly, one shifting to his head to turn and press it roughly cheek to floor. Simultaneously he was aware of the other hand fumbling about his neck, and then of a touch of metal and the sting of a needle driven into the flesh beneath his ear.
That galvanized him; he came to life again in a twinkling, animate with threefold strength and cunning. The man on his chest was thrown off as by a young earthquake; and Lanyard’s right arm was no sooner free than it shot out with blind but deadly accuracy to the point of his assailant’s jaw. A click of teeth was followed by a sickish grunt as the man lurched over.﻿ ﻿…
Lanyard found himself scrambling to his feet, a bit giddy perhaps, but still sufficiently master of his wits to get his pistol out before making another move.

===Chinese===
而冷不防地，他忽然停止了挣扎，浑身瘫软，任凭摆布在那人手中。
扼喉的钳制只又维持了一瞬。两只手迅速撤离，一只移上他的头，把他的脸粗暴地拧向地板、死死压住；同时他觉出另一只手正沿他颈间摸索，接着——金属的一凉、针头的一刺，一枚针扎进了他耳根下的皮肉。
这一记如电击般激活了他；转瞬之间他重又还魂，凭空添了三倍的膂力与狡黠。胸口上那人像挨了一场小地震，被掀翻下去；兰亚德右臂方一脱困，便盲然而又致命地精准，直捣袭击者下颌的颌尖。牙关一磕，一声闷哼，那人歪倒过去。……
兰亚德发觉自己正手脚并用地爬将起来，兴许有几分天旋地转，但神智尚足以做主：先拔出手枪，再作道理。

## XVIII Enigma / 十八、谜团

===Original===
He found no reason to believe she had left him other than voluntarily, or that their adventures since the escape from the impasse Stanislas had been attended upon by spies of the Pack. He could have sworn they hadn’t been followed either to or from the rue des Acacias; their way had been too long and purposely too roundabout, his vigilance too lively, for any sort of surveillance to have been practised without his remarking some indication thereof, at one time or another.
On the other hand (he told himself) there was every reason to believe she hadn’t left him to go back to Bannon; concerning whom she had expressed herself too forcibly to excuse a surmise that she had preferred his protection to the Lone Wolf’s.
Reasoning thus, he admitted, one couldn’t blame her. He could readily see how, illuded at first by a certain romantic glamour, she had not, until left to herself in the garden, come to clear perception of the fact that she was casting her lot with a common criminal’s. Then, horror overmastering her of a sudden she had fled﻿—wildly, blindly, he didn’t doubt. But whither? He looked in vain for her at their agreed rendezvous, the Sacré Cœur. She had neither money nor friends in Paris.
True: she had mentioned some personal jewellery she planned to hypothecate. Her first move, then, would be to seek the mont-de-piété﻿—not to force himself again upon her, but to follow at a distance and ward off interference on Bannon’s part.

===Chinese===
他找不出任何理由相信，她离开他不是出于自愿；也不信自打逃出斯坦尼斯拉斯死巷（impasse Stanislas）以来，他们这一路的历险曾有「群狼」的密探随行窥伺。他敢发誓，无论去阿卡西亚街（rue des Acacias）还是从那里离开，他们都不曾被人盯上；路走得太长，又故意绕得太迂回，他的警惕又太鲜活，无论何时，要想有什么盯梢得逞而不叫他瞧出一点端倪，是断乎不能的。
反过来说（他对自己讲），倒有一万个理由相信，她离开他绝不是要回到班农身边去；提起那个人时她的言辞太决绝了，谁也没法猜想她竟会舍弃独狼的保护，另投那人的羽翼。
他承认，照这样想来，谁也怪不着她。他不难明白，起初她是被某种传奇式的光华迷了眼，直到孤零零剩在园子里，才恍然看清自己正把命运同一个惯犯拴在一起。于是恐怖霎时压倒了她，她逃了——逃得狂乱，逃得盲目，这他毫不怀疑。可是逃向了哪里？他在两人约定的碰头处圣心堂（Sacré-Cœur）白等了一场。她在巴黎既没有钱，也没有朋友。
不过也是：她提过自己有几件随身首饰，打算典押了换钱。那么她的头一步，必是去公立当铺（mont-de-piété）——他不必再强行凑到她跟前，只须远远跟着，挡开班农那边的纠缠就是。

===Original===
The Government pawnshop had its invitation for Lanyard himself: he was there before the doors were open for the day; and fortified by loans negotiated on his watch, cigarette-case, and a ring or two, retired to a café commanding a view of the entrance on the rue des Blancs-Manteaux, and settled himself against a daylong vigil.
It wasn’t easy; drowsiness buzzed in his brain and weighted his eyelids; now and again, involuntarily, he nodded over his glass of black coffee. And when evening came and the mont-de-piété closed for the night, he rose and stumbled off, wondering if possibly he had napped a little without his knowledge and so missed her visit.
Engaging obscure lodgings close by the rue des Acacias, he slept till nearly noon of the following day, then rose to put into execution a design which had sprung full-winged from his brain at the instant of wakening.
He had not only his car but a chauffeur’s license of long standing in the name of Pierre Lamier﻿—was free, in short, to range at will the streets of Paris. And when he had levied on the stock of a secondhand clothing shop and a chemist’s, he felt tolerably satisfied it would need sharp eyes﻿—whether the Pack’s or the Préfecture’s﻿—to identify “Pierre Lamier” with either Michael Lanyard or the Lone Wolf.

===Chinese===
这家官营当铺对兰亚德本人也不无吸引力：天还没开门他就到了；拿怀表、烟盒和一两枚戒指押了几笔款子，壮了壮行囊，便退进一家正对着布朗芒托街（rue des Blancs-Manteaux）入口的咖啡馆，安顿下来，打一场整日的守望。
这差事不容易：瞌睡在脑子里嗡嗡作响，压得眼皮直坠；一杯接一杯的黑咖啡，还是不由自主地对着杯子打盹。等到暮色四合、公立当铺关门歇夜，他起身踉跄而去，心里犯着嘀咕：会不会自己不知不觉眯了一小会儿，恰恰错过了她来？
他在阿卡西亚街近旁寻了个不起眼的住处，一觉睡到次日将近晌午，起身便着手施行一个主意——那主意在他睁眼的一刹那，便已羽翼俱全地从脑子里蹦了出来。
他不但有那辆汽车，还有一张以皮埃尔·拉米埃（Pierre Lamier）之名登着的、年头已久的司机执照——总之，尽可以在巴黎街头自由驰骋。等他又从一家旧衣铺和一家药房的货架上筹措了一番，便觉得相当满意：要想把「皮埃尔·拉米埃」认作迈克尔·兰亚德或者独狼，无论「群狼」还是警察总局，都得有双顶尖的利眼才行。

===Original===
His face, ears and neck he stained a weather-beaten brown, a discreet application of rouge along his cheekbones enhancing the effect of daily exposure to the winter winds and rains of Paris; and he gave his hands an even darker shade, with the added verisimilitude of fingernails inked into permanent mourning. Also, he refrained from shaving: a stubble of two days’ neglect bristled upon his chin and jowls. A rusty brown ulster with cap to match, shoddy trousers boasting conspicuous stripes of leaden colour, and patched boots completed the disguise.
Monsieur and madame of the conciergerie he deceived with a yarn of selling his all to purchase the motorcar and embark in business for himself; and with their blessing, sallied forth to scout Paris diligently for sight or sign of the woman to whom his every heartbeat was dedicated.
By the close of the third day he was ready to concede that she had managed to escape without his aid.
And he began to suspect that Bannon had fled the town as well; for the most diligent enquiries failed to educe the least clue to the movements of the American following the fire at Troyon’s.

===Chinese===
他把脸、耳朵和脖颈染成饱经风霜的棕色，颧骨上又敷了恰到好处的一抹胭脂，添出日日受着巴黎冬天风吹雨打的气象；两只手染得更深些，连指甲也描成经年不褪的黑边，愈发逼真。胡子他也索性不刮：下巴和腮帮上炸起两天没收拾的胡茬。一件锈棕色长毛粗呢大衣配一顶同色便帽，一条亮着铅色醒目条纹的充呢裤子，一双打了补丁的靴子——化装便齐了。
