门房小屋（conciergerie）里的门房先生和门房太太，他则用一套瞎话哄了过去，说自己变卖了全部家当买下这辆车，要自谋生计；得了两口子的祝福，他便出门去，为寻访那个系着他每一次心跳的女人的影踪，勤勤恳恳地踏遍巴黎。
到第三天日暮，他只得认了：她果然没靠他帮衬，自己脱身走了。
他也开始疑心班农同样逃出了巴黎；因为任凭他如何卖力打探，特鲁瓦旅馆那场大火之后，这个美国人的行踪竟查不出半点线索。

===Original===
As for Troyon’s, it was now nothing more than a gaping excavation choked with ashes and charred timbers; and though still rumours of police interest in the origin of the fire persisted, nothing in the papers linked the name of Michael Lanyard with their activities. His disappearance and Lucy Shannon’s seemed to be accepted as due to death in the holocaust; the fact that their bodies hadn’t been recovered was no longer a matter for comment.
In short, Paris had already lost interest in the affair.
Even so, it seemed, had the Pack lost interest in the Lone Wolf; or else his disguise was impenetrable. Twice he saw De Morbihan “flânning” elegantly on the Boulevards, and once he passed close by Popinot; but neither noticed him.
Toward midnight of the third day, Lanyard, driving slowly westward on the boulevard de la Madeleine, noticed a limousine of familiar aspect round a corner half a block ahead and, drawing up in front of Viel’s, discharge four passengers.

===Chinese===
至于特鲁瓦旅馆，如今只剩一个豁开的大坑，塞满灰烬和焦木；虽然警界对火因兴致未减的传言不绝于耳，报上却再没有一个字把迈克尔·兰亚德的名字同这些动静联系起来。他的失踪和露西·香农的失踪，看来已被认定是葬身于那场大火；尸首未曾寻获这一节，也不再有人议论了。
一句话，巴黎早把这件事丢开了。
「群狼」对独狼，似乎也同样失了兴致；再不，就是他那副化装无懈可击。他两回瞧见德·莫尔比昂在林荫大道上风雅地「flânning」（闲逛），还有一回同波皮诺擦肩而过；可谁也没认出他来。
第三天将近午夜，兰亚德驾车沿马德莱娜大道缓缓西行，瞧见一辆眼熟的豪华轿车在前头半条街区外拐过街角，停在维尔餐馆（Viel’s）门前，放下四名乘客。

===Original===
The first was Wertheimer; and at sight of his rather striking figure, decked out in evening apparel from Conduit street and Bond, Lanyard slackened speed.
Turning as he alighted, the Englishman offered his hand to a young woman. She jumped down to the sidewalk in radiant attire and a laughing temper.
Involuntarily Lanyard stopped his car; and one immediately to the rear, swerving out to escape collision, shot past, its driver cursing him freely; while a sergent de ville scowled darkly and uttered an imperative word.
He pulled himself together, somehow, and drove on.

===Chinese===
头一个下来的是维特海默；一眼望见他那一身从伦敦康迪特街和邦德街（Conduit Street and Bond Street）置办来的晚装、身影煞是惹眼，兰亚德便放慢了车速。
那英国人下车时回过身来，向一位年轻姑娘伸出手。她跳到人行道上，衣装明艳，笑意盈盈。
兰亚德不由自主地刹住了车；紧贴在后的一辆慌忙打偏躲让，呼地蹿了过去，司机把他好一顿臭骂；一旁的巡警（sergent de ville）也黑着脸，厉声喝出一个字。
他不知怎么才定住神，继续驱车前行。

===Original===
The girl was entering the restaurant by way of the revolving door, Wertheimer in attendance; while De Morbihan, having alighted, was lending a solicitous arm to Bannon.
Quite automatically the adventurer drove on, rounded the Madeleine, and turned up the boulevard Malesherbes. Paris and all its brisk midnight traffic swung by without claiming a tithe of his interest: he was mainly conscious of lights that reeled dizzily round him like a multitude of malicious, mocking eyes.﻿ ﻿…
At the junction with the boulevard Haussmann a second sergent de ville roused him with a warning about careless driving. He went more sanely thereafter, but bore a heart of utter misery; his eyes still wore a dazed expression, and now and again he shook his head impatiently as though to rid it of a swarm of tormenting thoughts.
So, it seemed, he had all along been her dupe; all the while that he had been ostentatiously shielding her from harm and diffidently discovering every evidence of devotion, she had been laughing in her sleeve and planning to return to the service she pretended to despise, with her report of a fool self-duped.

===Chinese===
姑娘正从旋转门进餐馆，维特海默殷勤随侍；德·莫尔比昂下了车，正把一条体贴的胳膊搀给班农。
冒险家全凭机械地驱车向前，绕过马德莱娜，拐上马勒塞尔布大道（boulevard Malesherbes）。巴黎和它午夜川流不息的车马从身旁掠过，分毫也引不动他的兴致：他满眼只觉四面灯火团团乱转，像无数只含着恶意、带着讥诮的眼睛。……
到了与奥斯曼大道（boulevard Haussmann）交会的路口，又一个巡警把他从恍惚里喝醒，警告他开车不上心。此后他清醒了些，心里却揣着十足的凄苦；那双眼仍旧一片茫然，还不时焦躁地甩甩头，仿佛要把一大群噬人的念头从脑袋里甩出去。
原来如此——看来他从头到尾都是她手里的傻子；他一面招摇地替她挡灾护难，一面又怯生生地捧上一颗心的种种凭据，而她却一直暗自好笑，盘算着回到她口口声声鄙薄的差事里去，捎上一份报告，报告有个蠢货自己骗了自己。

===Original===
A great anger welled in his bosom.
Turning round, he made back to the boulevard de la Madeleine, and on one pretext and another contrived to haunt the neighbourhood of Viel’s until the party reappeared, something after one o’clock.
It was plain that they had supped merrily; the girl seemed in the gayest humour, Wertheimer a bit exhilarated, De Morbihan much amused; even Bannon﻿—bearing heavily on the Frenchman’s arm﻿—was chuckling contentedly. The party piled back into De Morbihan’s limousine and was driven up the avenue des Champs Élysées, pausing at the Élysée Palace Hotel to drop Bannon and the girl﻿—his daughter?﻿—whoever she was!
Whither it went thereafter, Lanyard didn’t trouble to ascertain. He drove morosely home and went to bed, though not to sleep for many hours: bitterness of disillusion ate like an acid in his heart.

===Chinese===
一股怒火从胸中涌起。
他调转车头，重回马德莱娜大道，借着这样那样的由头，在维尔餐馆左近徘徊不去，直到一点钟敲过，那一行人重新露面。
一望便知这顿夜宵吃得尽兴：姑娘兴致最高，维特海默微有醺然，德·莫尔比昂乐不可支；连班农——整个人沉沉压在那法国人的胳膊上——也心满意足地呵呵笑着。一行人重新挤进德·莫尔比昂的轿车，沿香榭丽舍大道（avenue des Champs Élysées）驶去，在爱丽舍宫大旅馆（Élysée Palace Hotel）门口停下，让班农和姑娘下了车——他的女儿？管她到底是谁！
此后那车去了哪里，兰亚德懒得去查。他郁郁地驾车回寓，上床躺下，却有好几个钟头睡不着：幻灭的苦涩像酸液一样啮着他的心。

===Original===
But for all his anguish, he continued in an uncertain temper. He had turned his back on the craft of which he was acknowledged master﻿—for a woman’s sake; for nothing else (he argued) had he dedicated himself to poverty and honest effort; and what little privation he had already endured was hopelessly distasteful to him. The art of the Lone Wolf, his consummate cunning and subtlety, was still at his command; with only himself to think of, he was profoundly contemptuous of the antagonism of the Pack; while none knew better than he with what ease the riches of careless Paris might be diverted to his own pockets. A single step aside from the path he had chosen﻿—and tomorrow night he might dine at the Ritz instead of in some sordid cochers’ cabaret!
And since no one cared﻿—since *she* had betrayed his faith﻿—what mattered?
Why not﻿ ﻿… ?
Yet he could not come to a decision; the next day saw him obstinately, even a little stupidly, pursuing the course he had planned before his disheartening disillusionment.

===Chinese===
可是苦恼归苦恼，他的心气依旧摇摆不定。他已经背弃了那门公认可称宗师的手艺——为了一个女人；若不是为这个（他暗自辩道），他又何至于发愿去过清贫安分的日子；而已经尝到的那一点点清苦，他又委实深恶痛绝。独狼的本事，那炉火纯青的机巧诡谲，仍在他指掌之间；只消顾自己一个人，他压根儿不把「群狼」的敌意放在眼里；而漫不经心的巴黎有多少财货可以轻而易举地流进他的腰包，这世上更没有人比他清楚。从眼下选定的路上只消旁逸一步——明晚他便可坐进丽兹（the Ritz）用膳，而不必窝在什么寒碜的车夫酒馆里了！
既然没有一个人把他放在心上——既然连*她*都辜负了他的痴信——还有什么要紧？
何不……？
然而他终究下不了决心；第二天，他还是固执地——甚至有点犯傻地——照着那场灰心幻灭之前定下的路子行事。

===Original===
Because his money was fast ebbing and motives of prudence alone﻿—if none more worthy﻿—forbade an attempt to replenish his pocketbook by revisiting the little *rez-de-chaussée* in the rue Roget and realizing on its treasures, he had determined to have a taximeter fitted to his car and ply for hire until time or chance should settle the question of his future.
Already, indeed, he had complied with the police regulations, and received permission to convert his voiture de remise into a taxicab; and leaving it before noon at the designated depot, he was told it would be ready for him at four with the “clock” installed. Returning at that hour, he learned that it couldn’t be ready before six; and too bored and restless to while away two idle hours in a café, he wandered listlessly through the streets and boulevards﻿—indifferent, in the black melancholy oppressing him, whether or not he were recognized﻿—and eventually found himself turning from the rue St. Honoré through the place Vendôme to the rue de la Paix.
This was not wise, a perilous business, a course he had no right to pursue. And Lanyard knew it. None the less, he persisted.
It was past five o’clock﻿—deep twilight beneath a cloudless sky﻿—the life of that street of streets fluent at its swiftest. All that Paris knew of wealth and beauty, fashion and high estate, moved between the curbs. One needed the temper of a Stoic to maintain indifference to the allure of its pageant.
Trudging steadily, he of the rusty brown ulster all but touched shoulders with men who were all that he had been but a few days since﻿—hale, hearty, well-fed, well-dressed symbols of prosperity﻿—and with exquisite women, exquisitely gowned, extravagantly be-furred and bejewelled, of glowing faces and eyes dark with mystery and promise: spirited creatures whose laughter was soft music, whose gesture was pride and arrogance.
One and all looked past, over, and through him, unaffectedly unaware that he existed.

===Chinese===
他的钱正在飞快地见底，而单凭审慎这一层——哪怕提不出更体面的理由——也不容他再闯罗热街那间底层（*rez-de-chaussée*）小宅，把里面的珍藏变卖了来充实钱袋；于是他打定主意给汽车装上计程器，出去揽雇载客，听凭时间或机缘来裁断他的前途。
其实他已经办妥了警章手续，领到许可，把那辆出租汽车（voiture de remise）改成计程车；中午前把车留在指定的修车场，人家告诉他四点钟装好「钟表」就能交车。到点去取，又听说六点前决计备不齐；他心烦意乱，懒得在咖啡馆里消磨两个钟头的闲工夫，便无精打采地逛过一条条街道和大道——在压得他喘不过气的黑沉沉的愁绪里，认出不认出都无所谓了——不知不觉，竟从圣奥诺雷街（rue St. Honoré）拐过旺多姆广场（place Vendôme），转上了和平街（rue de la Paix）。
这可不聪明，是桩冒险的营生，一条他无权再走的路。兰亚德心里明白。可他还是走了下去。
这时已过五点——无云的天底下暮色深沉——这条街中之街，人潮正淌得最急。巴黎所有的富贵与美色、时髦与显贵，尽在两沿道牙之间移动。没有斯多葛学派的道行，谁也保不住对这满目华彩的淡漠。
他穿着那件锈棕色大衣一路蹭行，肩膀几乎蹭着那些男男女女——男的，正是他几天前的写照：健壮、快活、脑满肠肥、衣冠楚楚，一尊尊兴旺的化身；女的，个个娇艳，穿得精致，裘皮珠宝铺张到奢华，脸庞灼灼生辉，眼睛因神秘与期许而深黑：这些神采飞扬的造物，笑声是柔曼的音乐，一举手一投足都是骄傲与倨傲。
这些人一律从他身旁望过去、越过他、看穿他，浑然不觉世上还有他这么一号人。

===Original===
The roadway, its paving worn as smooth as glass, and tonight by grace of frost no less hard, rang with a clatter of hoofs high and clear above the resonance of motors. A myriad lights filled the wide channel with diffused radiance. Two endless ranks of shopwindows, facing one another﻿—across the tide, flaunted treasures that kings might pardonably have coveted﻿—and would.
Before one corner window, Lanyard paused instinctively.
The shop was that of a famous jeweller. Separated from him by only the thickness of plate-glass was the wealth of princes. Looking beyond that display, his attention focused on the interior of an immense safe, to which a dapper French salesman was restoring velvet-lined trays of valuables. Lanyard studied the intricate, ponderous mechanism of the safe-door with a thoughtful gaze not altogether innocent of sardonic bias. It wore all the grim appearance of a strongbox that, once locked, would prove impregnable to everything save acquaintance with the combination and the consent of the time-lock. But give the Lone Wolf twenty minutes alone with it, twenty minutes free from interruption﻿—he, the one man living who could seduce a time-lock and leave it apparently inviolate!﻿ ﻿…
To one side of that window stood a mirror, set at an angle, and suddenly Lanyard caught its presentment of himself﻿—a gaunt and hungry apparition, with a wolfish air he had never worn when rejoicing in his sobriquet, staring with eyes of predaceous lustre.

===Chinese===
车行道上的路面磨得镜面也似，今晚又承霜冻之惠，硬得分毫不减，马蹄的嘚嘚声高亢清脆，盖过马达的嗡鸣。千万盏灯把这条宽阔的河道映得一片流光。两列望不到头的橱窗隔街相望——隔着人潮——争相炫耀着连帝王也不妨垂涎的珍宝——而且真会垂涎。
在街角一扇橱窗前，兰亚德本能地收住了脚。
这是一家著名珠宝商的铺子。隔着一块厚玻璃板，王侯的财富便近在眼前。越过那排陈列，他的目光落在一只巨大保险柜的内膛上——一个衣着利落的法国店员，正把一托盘一托盘衬着天鹅绒的珍宝送回去。兰亚德端详着柜门那繁复而沉重的机关，眼神若有所思，未免带几分揶揄的偏心。它一副铁面无情的坚壁模样，一旦锁上，除非通晓暗码、再得定时锁首肯，任什么都攻它不动。可是，只消让独狼同它单独厮守二十分钟——不受打扰的二十分钟——他，这世上唯一能把定时锁哄得服服帖帖、事后还完好如初的人！……
橱窗一侧斜支着一面镜子，兰亚德猛然瞥见里面映出的自己——一个瘦骨嶙峋、面带饥色的幽灵，一脸狼气——当年顶着那个诨名得意时，他可从未有过这般神情——正瞪着一双掠食者发亮的眼睛。

===Original===
Alarmed and fearing lest some passerby be struck by this betrayal, he turned and moved on hastily.
But his mind was poisoned by this brutal revelation of the wide, deep gulf that yawned between the Lone Wolf of yesterday and Pierre Lamier of today; between Michael Lanyard the debonnaire, the amateur of fine arts and fine clothing, the beau sabreur of gentlemen-cracksmen and that lean, worn, shabby and dispirited animal who had glared back at him from the jeweller’s mirror.
He quickened his pace, with something of that same instinct of self-preservation that bids the dipsomaniac avert his eyes and hurry past the corner gin-mill, and turned blindly off into the rue Danou, toward the avenue de l’Opéra.

===Chinese===
他吃了一惊，生怕哪个路人撞见这副泄露底细的模样，忙转身匆匆走开。
可是这番无情的揭示毒了他的心：昨日之独狼与今日之皮埃尔·拉米埃之间，横着一道又宽又深的鸿沟；一边是温文尔雅的迈克尔·兰亚德，美术与华服的鉴赏家，绅士窃贼里风流倜傥的 beau sabreur（佩剑美男子）；另一边，却是珠宝商镜子里瞪着他的那头干瘦、憔悴、褴褛、委顿的畜生。
他加快脚步，带着几分酒鬼经过街角酒馆时移开目光、疾走勿视的那同一股自保本能，昏头昏脑地拐进达努街（rue Danou），朝歌剧院大道（avenue de l’Opéra）而去。

===Original===
But this only made it worse for him, for he could not avoid recognition of the softly glowing windows of the Café de Paris that knew him so well, or forget the memory of its shining rich linen, its silver and crystal, its perfumed atmosphere and luxury of warmth and music and shaded lights, its cuisine that even Paris cannot duplicate.
And the truth came home to him, that he was hungry not with that brute appetite he had money enough in his pocket to satisfy, but with the lust of fleshpots, for rare viands and old vintage wines, to know once more the snug embrace of a dress-coat and to breathe again the atmosphere of ease and station.
In sudden panic he darted across the avenue and hurried north, determined to tantalize himself no longer with sights and sounds so provocative and so disturbing.

===Chinese===
可这一来他更不好受了：巴黎咖啡馆（Café de Paris）那一扇扇柔光盈盈的窗子认得他，他没法不相认；也没法忘掉那里雪亮讲究的桌布、银器与水晶、氤氲的香气、暖意与音乐与纱灯交陈的奢华，还有连巴黎城也复制不出的那一手烹调。
他这才明白过来：他饿的并非兜里的钱足够打发的那种粗鄙饥肠，而是对肥甘声色的贪馋——馋珍馐，馋陈年佳酿，想再领受夜礼服贴身的熨帖，再呼吸那种安逸与身份的空气。
他猛地一阵慌乱，蹿过大道，急急往北而去，打定主意，不再让这等撩人乱人的声色逗弄自己。

===Original===
Halfway across the boulevard des Capucines, to the east of the Opéra, he leapt for his life from a man-killing taxi, found himself temporarily marooned upon one of those isles of safety which Paris has christened “thank-Gods,” and stood waiting for an opening in the congestion of traffic to permit passage to the farther sidewalk.
And presently the policeman in the middle of the boulevard signalled with his little white wand; the stream of eastbound vehicles checked and began to close up to the right of the crossing, upon which they encroached jealously; and a taxi on the outside, next the island, overshot the mark, pulled up sharply, and began to back into place. Before Lanyard could stir, its window was opposite him, and he was looking in, transfixed.
There was sufficient light to enable him to see clearly the face of the passenger﻿—its pale oval and the darkness of eyes whose gaze clung to his with an effect of confused fascination.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
在歌剧院以东的卡皮西纳大道上，他横穿到一半，为躲一辆要人命的出租车纵身一跃，一时被困在巴黎人唤作「谢天谢地」（thank-Gods）的那类安全岛上，只得站着干等，等堵塞的车流让出一道口子，好放他到对面的人行道去。
不多时，大道中央的警察挥起小白棍；东行的车流勒住，开始向横道线右侧挤拢，一辆辆眼红地蚕食着横道；外侧紧挨安全岛的一辆出租车冲过了头，急急刹住，往回倒着找位置。兰亚德还不及动弹，那车窗已正对着他，他朝里一望，登时僵住了。
光线足以教他看清乘客的脸——那苍白的鹅蛋脸，那双深黑的眼睛，目光胶在他脸上，带着一种惶乱痴迷的意味。……

===Original===
She sat quite motionless until one white-gloved hand moved uncertainly toward her bosom.
That brought him to; unconsciously lifting his cap, he stepped back a pace and started to move on.
At this, she bent quickly forward and unlatched the door. It swung wide to him.
Hardly knowing what he was doing, he accepted the dumb invitation, stepped in, took the empty seat, and closed the door.

===Chinese===
她端坐不动，直到一只戴白手套的手迟疑地移向胸前。
这才把他惊醒；他无意识地举了举帽，退后一步，抬脚要走。
见状，她倏地俯身向前，拨开了车门。门朝他敞开。
他也几乎不知自己做了什么，竟领了这无声的邀请，跨进去，在空座上坐定，带上了车门。

===Original===
Almost at once the car moved on with a jerk, the girl sinking back into her corner with a suggestion of breathlessness, as though her effort to seem composed had been almost too much for her strength.
Her face, turned toward Lanyard, seemed wan in the half light, but immobile, expressionless; only her eyes were darkly quick with anticipation.
On his part, Lanyard felt himself hopelessly confounded, in the grasp of emotions that would scarce suffer him to speak. A great wonder obsessed him that she should have opened that door to him no less than that he should have entered through it. Dimly he understood that each had acted without premeditation; and asked himself, was she already regretting that momentary weakness.
“Why did you do that?” he heard himself demand abruptly, his voice harsh, strained, and unnatural.

===Chinese===
车几乎立刻一颠一簸地开动起来，姑娘缩回她的角落里，隐隐透着气喘，仿佛方才强自镇定的那一番用力，已几乎耗尽了她的气力。
她转向兰亚德的那张脸，在半明半暗里显得苍白，却纹丝不动，毫无表情；只有那双眼睛黑沉沉地闪动，盛满期待。
兰亚德这边，只觉得自己乱作一团，全没了主意，种种情绪攥住了他，教他几乎开不了口。他满心骇异：她竟会对他打开那扇门，恰如他竟会从那扇门里走进去。他隐隐明白，两人都是不假思索地动的作；又暗自嘀咕：她此刻可已在懊悔那一时的软弱了么？
「你为什么那样做？」他听见自己劈头问道，嗓音粗厉、发紧、不像自己的。

===Original===
She stiffened slightly, with a nervous movement of her shoulders.
“Because I saw you﻿ ﻿… I was surprised; I had hoped﻿—believed﻿—you had left Paris.”
“Without you? Hardly!”
“But you must,” she insisted﻿—“you *must* go, as quickly as possible. It isn’t safe﻿—”

===Chinese===
她微微一僵，肩膀神经质地动了动。
「因为我瞧见了你……我吃了一惊；我原指望——原相信——你已经离开巴黎了。」
「丢下你一走？那怎么会！」
「可你必须走，」她执意道——「你*必须*走，越快越好。这里不安全——」

===Original===
“I’m all right,” he insisted﻿—“able-bodied﻿—in full possession of my senses!”
“But any moment you may be recognized﻿—”
“In this rig? It isn’t likely.﻿ ﻿… Not that I care.”
She surveyed his costume curiously, perplexed.

===Chinese===
「我好得很，」他也执意道——「四肢齐全——头脑十足清楚！」
「可随时会有人认出你——」
「就凭这行头？不大可能。……认出来我也不在乎。」
她好奇又困惑地打量他的装束。

===Original===
“Why are you dressed that way? Is it a disguise?”
“A pretty good one. But in point of fact, it’s the national livery of my present station in life.”
“What do you mean by that?”
“Simply that, out of my old job, I’ve turned to the first resort of the incompetent: I’m driving a taxi.”

===Chinese===
「你怎么穿成这样？是化装么？」
「相当不错的一套。可说实在的，这是我眼下这份营生的全国统一号衣。」
「这话怎么说？」
「简单说，丢了旧行当，我改投了无能者的头一条出路：开出租车。」

===Original===
“Isn’t it awfully﻿—risky?”
“You’d think so; but it isn’t. Few people ever bother to look at a chauffeur. When they hail a taxi they’re in a hurry, as a rule﻿—preoccupied with business or pleasure. And then our uniforms are a disguise in themselves: to the public eye we look like so many Chinamen!”
“But you’re mistaken: I knew you instantly, didn’t I? And those others﻿—they’re as keen-witted as I﻿—certainly. Oh, you should not have stopped on in Paris!”
“I couldn’t go without knowing what had become of you.”
“I was afraid of that,” she confessed.
“Then why﻿—?”

===Chinese===
「那岂不是——太冒险了？」
「你这么想，其实不然。肯费神看司机一眼的人少得很。人家招手叫车的时候，多半赶时间——满脑子不是生意就是玩乐。再说我们这身制服本身就是化装：在大众眼里，我们个个长得跟中国人似的！」
「可你错了：我一眼就认出了你，不是么？还有那些人——他们跟我一样眼尖——准没错。唉，你实在不该赖在巴黎不走！」
「不知道你的下落，我走不了。」
「我就怕是这个，」她招认道。
「那为什么——？」

===Original===
“Oh, I know what you’re going to say! Why did I run away from you?” And then, since he said nothing, she continued unhappily: “I can’t tell you﻿ ﻿… I mean, I don’t know how to tell you!”
She kept her face averted, sat gazing blankly out of the window; but when he sat on, mute and unresponsive﻿—in point of fact not knowing what to say﻿—she turned to look at him, and the glare of a passing lamp showed her countenance profoundly distressed, mouth tense, brows knotted, eyes clouded with perplexity and appeal.
And of a sudden, seeing her so tormented and so piteous, his indignation ebbed, and with it all his doubts of her were dissipated; dimly he divined that something behind this dark fabric of mystery and inconsistency, no matter how inexplicable to him, excused all her apparent faithlessness and instability of character and purpose. He could not look upon this girl and hear her voice and believe that she was not at heart as sound and sweet, tender and loyal, as any that ever breathed.
A wave of tenderness and compassion brimmed his heart; he realized that he didn’t matter, that his amour propre was of no account﻿—that nothing mattered so long as she were spared one little pang of self-reproach.
He said, gently: “I wouldn’t have you distress yourself on my account, Miss Shannon﻿ ﻿… I quite understand there must be things I *can’t* understand﻿—that you must have had your reasons for acting as you did.”
“Yes,” she said unevenly, but again with eyes averted﻿—“I had; but they’re not easy, they’re impossible to explain﻿—to you.”

===Chinese===
「噢，我知道你要说什么！我为什么从你身边跑掉？」他既不作声，她便怏怏地续道：「我没法告诉你……我是说，我不知道怎么说出口！」
她始终偏着脸，怔怔地望着窗外；可他只顾闷坐着，一言不发，无动于衷——实在是不知道说什么好——她便回过头来看他，一盏迎面驶过的路灯照见她的脸：满面深重的苦楚，嘴唇绷紧，眉心打结，眼睛蒙着困惑与哀恳的云翳。
蓦地，见她这般煎熬、这般可怜，他的怒气退了下去，连带着对她的一切疑团也烟消云散；他隐隐悟到，在这团晦暗的神秘与悖谬背后，自有一段隐情，无论他多么难以索解，总归开脱了她表面上的负心，开脱了她性情与心志上的一切反复。他不能看着这姑娘、听着她的声音，还信不过她的心地竟不像世上任何一个人那样纯正可爱、温柔忠贞。
一阵怜爱涨满了他的心；他明白了，他自己算不得什么，他的自尊也算不得什么——只要能免她受一分自责的刺痛，什么都没有关系。
他温和地说：「香农小姐，别为我难受了……我很明白，必定有些事我*无法*明白——你那样做，自有你的道理。」
「是的，」她说得断断续续，又把目光移开——「我有道理；可这些道理不好讲，讲给你听是万万讲不清的。」

===Original===
“Yet﻿—when all’s said and done﻿—I’ve no right to exact any explanation.”
“Ah, but how can you say that, remembering what we’ve been through together?”
“You owe me nothing,” he insisted; “whereas I owe you everything, even unquestioning faith. Even though I fail, I have this to thank you for﻿—this one not-ignoble impulse my life has known.”
“You mustn’t say that, you mustn’t think it. I don’t deserve it. You wouldn’t say it﻿—if you knew﻿—”

===Chinese===
「不过——说到底——我本无权逼你解释什么。」
「唉，想到咱们一同经历了那些，你怎么能这么说？」
「你不欠我什么，」他执意道；「倒是我欠你一切，连那不问情由的信任也欠你。纵然我失了足，我仍有这一件要谢你——我这一辈子里，总算有过这么一回不算卑污的冲动。」
「别这么说，千万别这么想。我当不起。你要是知道了——你就不会说了——」

===Original===
“Perhaps I can guess enough to satisfy myself.”
She gave him a swift, sidelong look of challenge, instinctively on the defensive.
“Why,” she almost gasped﻿—“what do you think﻿—?”
“Does it matter what I think?”

===Chinese===
「或许我猜也猜得够多，尽够我自己受用了。」
她飞快地横他一眼，带着挑衅，本能地摆出防御的架势。
「怎么，」她几乎倒抽一口气——「你想到哪儿去了——？」
「我怎么想，要紧么？」

===Original===
“It does, to me: I wish to know!”
“Well,” he conceded reluctantly, “I think that, when you had a chance to consider things calmly, waiting back there in the garden, you made up your mind it would be better to﻿—to use your best judgment and﻿—extricate yourself from an embarrassing position﻿—”
“You think that!” she interrupted bitterly. “You think that, after you had confided in me; after you’d confessed﻿—when I made you, led you on to it﻿—that you cared for me; after you’d told me how much my faith meant to you﻿—you think that, after all that, I deliberately abandoned you because I suddenly realized you had been the Lone Wolf﻿—!”

===Chinese===
「对我，要紧：我要知道！」
「好吧，」他不情愿地让了步，「我想，你独自留在后面园子里的时候，得了机会静下来把事情掂量一番，便打定主意，还是——还是见机行事——把自己从尴尬的境地里摘出去——」
「你竟这么想！」她苦涩地打断他。「你向我交过心；你招认过——是我逼的，是我引你说的——说你心里有我；你告诉过我，我的信任对你多么要紧——你竟想，经过了这一切，我会处心积虑地甩掉你，就因为我猛然发觉你原来是独狼——！」

===Original===
“I’m sorry if I hurt you. But what can I think?”
“But you are wrong!” she protested vehemently﻿—“quite, quite wrong! I ran away from myself﻿—not from you﻿—and with another motive, too, that I can’t explain.”
“You ran away from yourself﻿—not from me?” he repeated, puzzled.
“Don’t you understand? Why make it so hard for me? Why make me say outright what pains me so?”

===Chinese===
「若伤了你的心，对不住。可教我怎么想呢？」
「可你错了！」她激烈地抗辩——「彻头彻尾，彻头彻尾地错了！我是从我自己身边逃开——不是从你——另外还有一层缘由，我没法解释。」
「你从你自己身边逃开——不是从我？」他困惑地重复了一遍。
「你还不明白么？何苦逼我到这个地步？何苦逼我把这样痛的事明明白白说出口？」

===Original===
“Oh, I beg of you﻿—”
“But if you won’t understand otherwise﻿—I must tell you, I suppose.” She checked, breathless, flushed, trembling. “You recall our talk after dinner, that night﻿—how I asked what if you found out you’d been mistaken in me, that I had deceived you; and how I told you it would be impossible for me ever to marry you?”
“I remember.”
“It was because of that,” she said﻿—“I ran away; because I hadn’t been talking idly; because you *were* mistaken in me, because I *was* deceiving you, because I could never marry you, and because﻿—suddenly﻿—I came to know that, if I didn’t go then and there, I might never find the strength to leave you, and only suffering and unhappiness could come of it all. I had to go, as much for your sake as for my own.”

===Chinese===
「噢，我求你——」
「可你若不肯换个法子明白——看来我只好告诉你了。」她顿住，气喘吁吁，脸涨红着，浑身发颤。「你还记得那天晚饭后咱们说的话——我问过你，倘若你发现看错了人、发现我一直在骗你，那便如何；我还告诉过你，我永远也不可能嫁给你——记得么？」
「我记得。」
「正是因为那个，」她说——「我才跑掉的；因为我那些话并非随口说说；因为你*确实*看错了人，因为我*确实*在骗你，因为我永远嫁不得你，还因为——突然之间——我明白了：若不当机立断一走，我也许再也聚不起离开你的力气，而这一切到头来只会有痛苦和不幸。我不得不走，一半为你，一半为我自己。」

===Original===
“You mean me to understand, you found you were beginning to﻿—to care a little for me?”
She made an effort to speak, but in the end answered only with a dumb inclination of her head.
“And ran away because love wasn’t possible between us?”
Again she nodded silently.

===Chinese===
「你要我明白的是，你发觉自己开始——开始有点儿把我放在心上了？」
她张口想说，终究只默默点了点头。
「又因为咱们之间不可能有爱，才逃开的？」
她又默默点头。

===Original===
“Because I had been a criminal, I presume!”
“You’ve no right to say that﻿—”
“What else can I think? You tell me you were afraid I might persuade you to become my wife﻿—something which, for some inexplicable reason, you claim is impossible. What other explanation can I infer? What other explanation is needed? It’s ample, it covers everything, and I’ve no warrant to complain﻿—God knows!”
She tried to protest, but he cut her short.

===Chinese===
「我猜，是因为我犯过案吧！」
「你无权这么说——」
「可教我往别处想？你告诉我，你怕的是我或许会劝你做我的妻子——而这件事，出于某种说不清道不明的缘故，你说断断不能。除此之外，我还能推想出什么旁的解释？又还需要什么旁的解释？这一条尽够了，把一切都包圆了，我也无权抱怨——天知道！」
她想抗辩，他打断了她。

===Original===
“There’s one thing I don’t understand at all! If that is so, if your repugnance for criminal associations made you run away from me﻿—why did you go back to Bannon?”
She started and gave him a furtive, frightened glance.
“You knew that?”
“I saw you﻿—last night﻿—followed you from Viel’s to your hotel.”

===Chinese===
「有一件事我全然不懂！倘若当真如此，倘若你对与罪犯同流这层嫌恶教你从我身边逃开——那你为什么回到班农那里去？」
她一震，偷觑了他一眼，眼里满是惊惶。
「你知道了？」
「我看见了——昨天夜里——从维尔餐馆一路跟到你们的旅馆。」

===Original===
“And you thought,” she flashed in a vibrant voice﻿—“you thought I was in his company of my own choice!”
“You didn’t seem altogether downcast,” he countered. “Do you wish me to understand you were with him against your will?”
“No,” she said slowly.﻿ ﻿… “No: I returned to him voluntarily, knowing perfectly what I was about.”
“Through fear of him﻿—?”

===Chinese===
「于是你就想，」她嗓音发颤，火一样地发作——「你就想，我是心甘情愿跟他在一起的！」
「你瞧着可不像怎么愁苦，」他反唇相讥。「你要我明白，你同他在一起是身不由己么？」
「不，」她缓缓地说。……「不：我是自愿回到他身边的，而且完全清楚自己在做什么。」
「因为怕他——？」

===Original===
“No. I can’t claim that.”
“Rather than me﻿—?”
“You’ll never understand,” she told him a little wearily﻿—“never. It was a matter of duty. I had to go back﻿—I had to!”
Her voice trailed off into a broken little sob. But as, moved beyond his strength to resist, Lanyard put forth a hand to take the white-gloved one resting on the cushion beside her, she withdrew it with a swift gesture of denial.

===Chinese===
「不。这我承认不了。」
「宁要他、不要我——？」
「你永远不会明白的，」她的语气透着倦意——「永远不会。那是一桩本分。我不得不回去——我不得不！」
她的声音低下去，散成一小声呜咽。兰亚德再也按捺不住，伸出手去，想握住她搁在旁边座垫上那只戴白手套的手，她却猛地缩了回去，断然不肯。

===Original===
“No!” she cried. “Please! You mustn’t do that﻿ ﻿… You only make it harder﻿ ﻿…”
“But you love me!”
“I can’t. It’s impossible. I would﻿—but I may not!”
“Why?”

===Chinese===
「不！」她喊道。「求你！你不能这样……你只会把事情弄得更难……」
「可你是爱我的！」
「我不能。那不可能。我愿意——可我不能够！」
「为什么？」

===Original===
“I can’t tell you.”
“If you love me, you must tell me.”
She was silent, the white hands working nervously with her handkerchief.
“Lucy!” he insisted﻿—“you must say what stands between you and my love. It’s true, I’ve no right to ask, as I had no right to speak to you of love. But when we’ve said as much as we have said﻿—we can’t stop there. You will tell me, dear?”

===Chinese===
「我无法告诉你。」
「你要是爱我，就得告诉我。」
她不作声，一双白皙的手绞着手帕，焦躁不安。
「露西！」他执拗地说——「你必须说出来，是什么横在你我的爱之间。不错，我无权问，正如当初我无权向你诉说爱情一样。可是话都说到这个地步了——咱们不能就此打住。你肯告诉我么，亲爱的？」

===Original===
She shook her head: “It﻿—it’s impossible.”
“But you can’t ask me to be content with that answer!”
“Oh!” she cried﻿—“*how* can I make you understand?﻿ ﻿… When you said what you did, that night﻿—it seemed as if a new day were dawning in my life. You made me believe it was because of me. You put me above you﻿—where I’d no right to be; but the fact that you thought me worthy to be there, made me proud and happy: and for a little, in my blindness, I believed I could be worthy of your love and your respect. I thought that, if I could be as strong as you during that year you asked in which to prove your strength, I might listen to you, tell you everything, and be forgiven.﻿ ﻿… But I was wrong, how wrong I soon learned.﻿ ﻿… So I had to leave you at whatever cost!”
She ceased to speak, and for several minutes there was silence. But for her quick, convulsive breathing, the girl sat like a woman of stone, staring dry-eyed out of the window. And Lanyard sat as moveless, the heart in his bosom as heavy and cold as a stone.

===Chinese===
她摇了摇头：「这——这不可能。」
「可你总不能指望我听了这话就心满意足！」
「噢！」她喊道——「我要*怎样*才能叫你明白？……那天夜里，你把那些话说出口——我仿佛觉得生命里透出了新的一天的曙光。你教我相信那是因为我。你把我捧到你之上——那本是我无福消受的地方；可你想我配站在那里，这一层就叫我又骄傲又欢喜：有一小会儿，在盲目里，我竟相信自己配得上你的爱与敬重。我想，倘若在你要求用来证明你坚忍的那一年里，我也能像你一样坚强，我或许可以听你倾诉，把一切告诉你，求得宽恕。……可我错了，错得多深，我不久便知道了。……所以我不得不离开你，不惜任何代价！」
她不再说了，好几分钟一片沉寂。除了急促抽动的呼吸，姑娘端坐如石女，干着一双眼望定窗外。兰亚德也一动不动地坐着，胸中那颗心沉重冰冷，也如一块石头。

===Original===
At length, lifting his head, “You leave me no alternative,” he said in a voice dull and hollow even in his own hearing: “I can only think one thing﻿ ﻿…”
“Think what you must,” she said lifelessly: “it doesn’t matter, so long as you renounce me, put me out of your heart and﻿—leave me.”
Without other response, he leaned forward and tapped the glass; and as the cab swung in toward the curb, he laid hold of the door-latch.
“Lucy,” he pleaded, “don’t let me go believing﻿—”

===Chinese===
末了他抬起头。「你让我别无选择，」他说，那嗓音连自己听来都晦哑空洞：「我只能往一处想了……」
「你怎么想都由你，」她木然说：「都不要紧，只要你断了对我的念想，把我从心里撵出去，并且——离开我。」
他没有再答话，俯身敲了敲玻璃；车子朝道牙一侧拐去时，他握住了车门拉手。
「露西，」他恳求道，「别让我这么想着走——」

===Original===
She seemed suddenly infused with implacable hostility. “I tell you,” she said cruelly﻿—“I don’t care what you think, so long as you go!”
The face she now showed him was ashen; its mouth was hard; her eyes shone feverishly.
And then, as still he hesitated, the cab pulled up and the driver, leaning back, unlatched the door and threw it open.
With a curt, resigned nod, Lanyard rose and got out.

===Chinese===
她仿佛霎时间燃起了难消的敌意。「我告诉你，」她狠心说——「你怎么想，我不在乎，只要你走！」
她此刻示人的那张脸灰白如死灰；嘴角冷硬；双眼烧着热病似的光。
接着，他还迟疑着，车已停稳，司机回过身来，拨开门闩，把车门甩开。
兰亚德木然一点头，认了命，起身下了车。

===Original===
Immediately the girl bent forward and grasped the speaking-tube; the door slammed; the cab drew away and left him standing with the pose, with the gesture of one who has just heard his sentence of death pronounced.
When he roused to know his surroundings, he found himself standing on a corner of the avenue du Bois.
It was bitter cold in the wind sweeping down from the west, and it had grown very dark. Only in the sky above the Bois a long reef of crimson light hung motionless, against which leafless trees lifted gnarled, weird silhouettes.
While he watched, the pushing crimson ebbed swiftly and gave way to mauve, to violet, to black.

===Chinese===
姑娘立刻俯身抓住传话筒；车门砰地关上；车开走了，撇下他站在那里——那身姿，那手势，活像一个刚听完死刑判决的人。
等他回过神来认清四下，才发觉自己站在布瓦大道（avenue du Bois）的一个街角上。
西来的风扫过街面，寒冷刺骨，天已经黑透了。唯有布洛涅林苑（the Bois）上空，一道长长的暗红光带悬着不动，几株秃树衬着它，托起虬曲诡谲的剪影。
他望着望着，那股执拗的红光倏然退去，让位给紫色，让位给紫黑，让位给漆黑。

## XI Flight / 十一、亡命奔逃

===Original===
Now when Lanyard had locked the door, he told himself that the gruesome peace of those two bedchambers was ensured, barring mischance, for as long as the drug continued to hold dominion over the American; and he felt justified in reckoning that period apt to be tolerably protracted; while not before noon at earliest would any hôtelier who knew his business permit the rest of an Anglo-Saxon guest to be disturbed﻿—lacking, that is, definite instructions to the contrary.
For a full minute after withdrawing the key the adventurer stood at alert attention; but the heavy silence of that sinister old rookery sang in his ears untroubled by any untoward sound.﻿ ﻿…
That wistful shadow of his memories, that cowering Marcel of the so-dead yesterday in acute terror of the hand of Madame Troyon, had never stolen down that corridor more quietly: yet Lanyard had taken not five paces from his door when that other opened, at the far end, and Lucia Bannon stepped out.
He checked then, and shut his teeth upon an involuntary oath: truly it seemed as though this run of the devil’s own luck would never end!
Astonishment measurably modified his exasperation. What had roused the girl out of bed and dressed her for the street at that unholy hour? And why her terror at sight of him?

===Chinese===
兰亚德锁好房门，心中自忖：只要药力仍主宰着那个美国人，不出岔子，那两间卧房里阴森森的安宁便有了保障；而这段光景，他估摸着大可以拖得相当长久——再者，但凡懂行的旅馆老板，无论如何也得挨到正午，才肯让一位盎格鲁-撒克逊客人的安眠受扰——除非有人明明白白吩咐下来，另当别论。
收起钥匙之后，这位冒险家肃立戒备了足足一分钟；然而那座凶险老巢里沉重的一片寂静，在他耳中嗡嗡低唱，不曾掺进半点异样的声息。……
他记忆里那个惹人怅惘的旧影，那个随着死透了的昨日一并埋葬的马塞尔——当年慑于特鲁瓦老板娘的手，怕得魂不附体——溜下那条走廊，也从不曾这样轻悄：然而兰亚德出了自己房门不过五步，走道尽头另一扇门应声而开，露西娅·班农走了出来。
他猛地收步，咬紧牙关，把一句冲口而出的咒骂生生咽了回去：当真，这一连串邪门透顶的运道，瞧着竟像永远没个尽头似的！
愕然多少冲淡了他的恼意。是什么把这姑娘从床上唤起，在这邪门的时辰穿戴停当、准备上街？她又为何一见他便露出惧色？

===Original===
For that the surprise was no more welcome to her than to him was as patent as the fact that she was prepared to leave the hotel forthwith, enveloped in a businesslike Burberry rainproof from her throat to the hem of a tweed walking-skirt, and wearing boots both stout and brown. And at sight of him she paused and instinctively stepped back, groping blindly for the knob of her bedchamber door; while her eyes, holding to his with an effect of frightened fascination, seemed momentarily to grow more large and dark in her face of abnormal pallor.
But these were illegible evidences, and Lanyard was intent solely on securing her silence before she could betray him and ruin incontinently that grim alibi which he had prepared at such elaborate pains. He moved toward her swiftly, with long and silent strides, a lifted hand enjoining rather than begging her attention, aware as he drew nearer that a curious change was colouring the complexion of her temper: she passed quickly from dread to something oddly like relief, from repulsion to something strangely like welcome; and dropping the hand that had sought the doorknob, in her turn moved quietly to meet him.
He was grateful for this consideration, this tacit indulgence of the wish he had as yet to voice; drew a little hope and comfort from it in an emergency which had surprised him without resource other than to throw himself upon her generosity. And as soon as he could make himself heard in the clear yet concentrated whisper that was a trick of his trade, a whisper inaudible to ears a yard distant from those to which it was pitched, he addressed her in a manner at once peremptory and apologetic.
“If you please, Miss Bannon﻿—not a word, not a whisper!”
She paused and nodded compliance, questioning eyes steadfast to his.

===Chinese===
这意外之于她，同他一样不受欢迎——此点显而易见，一如她分明打算即刻离店：一件干净利落的巴宝莉（Burberry）雨衣从颈项直裹到粗花呢步行裙的裙裾，脚上一双结实的棕色靴子。一看见他，她便顿住，本能地退后半步，盲目地摸索身后卧房的门把手；而她的目光凝住他的目光，带着一种又惊又魅的意味，在那张白得反常的脸上，似乎一刻比一刻显得更大、更黑。
不过这些凭据都读不出名目，兰亚德一心只求在她出卖自己、把他煞费苦心布下的那套阴冷的不在场把戏当场砸掉之前，先封住她的口。他大步流星、悄无声息地向她走去，抬起一只手，与其说是恳请，不如说是晓谕她注意；行得近了，他觉察她心绪的颜色正起着奇妙的变化：她迅速由惊惧转为一种古怪的近乎宽慰，由嫌拒转为一种奇异的近乎欢迎；那只摸向门把手的手垂了下去，她反倒安安静静迎上前来。
他感激这份体谅，这份对他尚未出口的心愿的默许；这场急变杀了他一个措手不及，他别无凭借，唯有仰仗她的宽厚，此刻便从这中间汲取了一点希望与安慰。待到能够施展出他那一行的绝技——一种清亮而凝敛的低语，离受话的耳朵一码开外便无从听见——他开口了，语气斩截，却又赔着小心。
「劳驾，班农小姐——一个字也别说，一丝气也别出！」
她驻足点头应承，一双含问的眼睛牢牢望定他。

===Original===
Doubtfully, wondering that she betrayed so little surprise, he pursued as one committed to a forlorn hope:
“It’s vitally essential that I leave this hotel without it becoming known. If I may count on you to say nothing﻿—”
She gave him reassurance with a small gesture. “But how?” she breathed in the least of whispers. “The concierge﻿—!”
“Leave that to me﻿—I know another way. I only need a chance﻿—”
“Then won’t you take me with you?”
“Eh?” he stammered, dashed.

===Chinese===
他心存疑虑，又诧异她怎地丝毫不露惊讶，便像把最后一线指望押了上去似的，续道：
「我离开这家旅馆，万万不能教人知晓。倘若我能指望您只字不提——」
她做个小小的手势叫他放心。「可是怎么走呢？」她用最细弱的声音吐出一句。「门房——！」
「那个交给我——我另认得一条路。我只消一个机会——」
「那您肯不肯带我一道走？」
「呃？」他讷讷出口，一时受窘。

===Original===
Her hands moved toward him in a flutter of entreaty: “I too must leave unseen﻿—I *must*! Take me with you﻿—out of this place﻿—and I promise you no one shall ever know﻿—”
He lacked time to weigh the disadvantages inherent in her proposition; though she offered him a heavy handicap, he had no choice but to accept it without protest.
“Come, then,” he told her﻿—“and not a sound﻿—”
She signified assent with another nod; and on this he turned to an adjacent door, opened it gently, whipped out his flash-lamp, and passed through. Without sign of hesitancy, she followed; and like two shadows they dogged the dancing spotlight of the flash-lamp, through a linen-closet and service-room, down a shallow well threaded by a spiral of iron steps and, by way of the long corridor linking the kitchen-offices, to a stout door secured only by huge, old-style bolts of iron.
Thus, in less than two minutes from the instant of their encounter, they stood outside Troyon’s back door, facing a cramped, malodorous alleyway﻿—a dark and noisome souvenir of that wild medieval Paris whose effacement is an enduring monument to the fame of the good Baron Haussmann.

===Chinese===
她的双手带着一阵恳求的震颤朝他伸来：「我也必须不被人瞧见地离开——我*非如此不可*！带我走吧——离开这个地方——我向您担保，谁也永远不会知道——」
他无暇掂量这提议里固有的种种不利；虽说她递过来的是一份不轻的拖累，他却别无选择，只能毫无异议地接下来。
「那就来吧，」他告诉她——「千万别出一点声响——」
她再一颔首表示应允；于是他转向近旁一扇门，轻轻推开，唰地亮出手电，一闪身进去了。她不见半分迟疑，跟了进去；两道黑影似的，他们咬着手电那一团跳动的光斑，穿过布巾间和杂用间，沿一口浅井般的竖道盘下一道铁梯，再经由连接厨房诸房的长廊，来到一扇厚实的门前——把守它的不过是几根老式的大铁闩。
就这样，自两人相遇还不到两分钟，他们已置身特鲁瓦旅馆后门外，面对一条窄促恶臭的小巷——那是狂野的中世纪巴黎留下的一件阴暗发臭的纪念物，而将它从地面上抹去，正是好心的奥斯曼男爵（Baron Haussmann）不朽声名的一座永久丰碑。

===Original===
Now again it was raining, a thick drizzle that settled slowly, lacking little of a fog’s opacity; and the faint glimmer from the street lamps of that poorly lighted quarter, reflected by the low-swung clouds, lent Lanyard and the girl little aid as they picked their way cautiously, and always in complete silence, over the rude and slimy cobbles of the foul back way. For the adventurer had pocketed his lamp, lest its beams bring down upon them some prowling creature of Popinot’s; though he felt passably sure that the alley had been left unguarded in the confidence that he would never dream of its existence, did he survive to seek escape from Troyon’s.
For all its might and its omniscience, Lanyard doubted if the Pack had as yet identified Michael Lanyard with that ill-starred Marcel who once had been as intimate with this forgotten way as any skulking tom of the quarter.
But with the Lone Wolf confidence was never akin to foolhardiness; and if on leaving Troyon’s he took the girl’s hand without asking permission and quite as a matter-of-course, and drew it through his arm﻿—it was his left arm that he so dedicated to gallantry; his right hand remained unhampered, and never far from the grip of his automatic.
Nor was he altogether confident of his companion. The weight of her hand upon his arm, the fugitive contacts of her shoulder, seemed to him, just then, the most vivid and interesting things in life; the consciousness of her personality at his side was like a shaft of golden light penetrating the darkness of his dilemma. But as minutes passed and their flight was unchallenged, his mood grew dark with doubts and quick with distrust. Reviewing it all, he thought to detect something too damnably adventitious in the way she had nailed him, back there in the corridor of Troyon’s. It was a bit too coincidental﻿—“a bit thick!”﻿—like that specious yarn of somnambulism she had told to excuse her presence in his room. Come to examine it, that excuse had been far too clumsy to hoodwink any but a man bewitched by beauty in distress.

===Chinese===
这时又下起雨来，浓密的毛毛雨缓缓沉降，与一场浓雾的遮蔽相去无几；那个灯火稀落的街区里，街灯的微光经低垂的云层一反，对兰亚德和姑娘帮不上什么忙，两人小心翼翼、始终全然缄默地在这条污秽后道粗砺滑腻的圆石上择路而行。冒险家已把手电收进口袋，唯恐那光柱会招来波皮诺手下什么夜游的爪牙；不过他相当有把握：这条巷子无人看守——对方笃信，他纵然活着离开特鲁瓦旅馆寻路出逃，也决计梦不到世上还有这么一条通路。
群狼尽管势大而无所不知，兰亚德却怀疑他们至今未必已把迈克尔·兰亚德同那个命途多舛的马塞尔对上号——当年那孩子熟悉这条被遗忘的通道，不亚于本区任何一只潜行的雄猫。
然而在独狼这里，自信从来便不等于莽撞；出了特鲁瓦旅馆，他径自执起姑娘的手，也不请示，全然当作理所当然，把它挽进自己臂弯——他奉献给骑士殷勤的是左臂；右手则始终腾着，从不曾远离那把自动手枪的握把。
对这位同伴，他也不敢全然放心。她的手压在他臂上的分量、她肩膀偶然的碰触，在他此刻看来，竟是人生中最鲜活、最饶有意味的东西；意识到她的存在就在自己身侧，便如一道金色的光柱，刺穿他困局的黑暗。可是几分钟过去，一路亡命无人拦阻，他的心境反倒因疑窦而阴沉、因猜防而敏锐。前前后后一番检点，他觉得她在特鲁瓦旅馆走廊里堵住他的那一手，未免巧得邪门、巧得可恨。「未免过火了！」——正如她为现身他房间所编的那套梦游鬼话。细究起来，那套托辞拙劣至极，除了被落难美人迷住心窍的男人，谁也哄骗不过。

===Original===
Who was she, anyway? And what her interest in him? What had she been after in his room?﻿—this American girl making a first visit to Paris in company with her venerable ruin of a parent? Who, for that matter, was Bannon? If her story of sleepwalking were untrue, then Bannon must have been at the bottom of her essay in espionage﻿—Bannon, the intimate of De Morbihan, and an American even as the murderer of poor Roddy was an American!
Was this singularly casual encounter, then, but a cloak for further surveillance? Had he in his haste and desperation simply played into her hands, when he burdened himself with the care of her?
But it seemed absurd; to think that she﻿ ﻿… a girl like her, whose every word and gesture was eloquent of gentle birth and training﻿ ﻿… !
Yet﻿—what *had* she wanted in his room? Somnambulists are sincere indeed in the indulgence of their failing when they time their expeditions so opportunely﻿—and arm themselves with keys to fit strange doors. Come to think of it, he had been rather willfully blind to that flaw in her excuse.﻿ ﻿… Again, why should she be up and dressed and so madly bent on leaving Troyon’s at half-past four in the morning? Why couldn’t she wait for daylight at least? What errand, reasonable duty or design could have roused her out into the night and the storm at that weird hour? He wondered!

===Chinese===
她究竟是什么人？对他有何图谋？深夜到他房间里找什么？——这位由风烛残年的老父陪着初游巴黎的美国姑娘？再说，班农又是何等人物？倘若她的梦游故事是编的，那么她那番刺探行径背后，必是班农主使——班农，德·莫尔比昂的密友，而美国人，正像杀害可怜罗迪的凶手也是个美国人！
那么，这场凑巧得离奇的邂逅，难道只是继续监视的一层幌子？他在仓皇与绝望之中把照应她的担子揽上肩头，岂非正中她的下怀？
可这念头又荒唐得很；想想她……像她这样的姑娘，一言一语、一举一动，无不诉说着良好的出身与教养……！
然而——她到底*要*在他房间里找什么？梦游症患者倒也真是诚恳，纵容自己这毛病的时候，竟会把夜游的时辰掐得那么巧——还会备好钥匙去开陌生房门。细想之下，他对那套托辞里的破绽，实在是存心闭眼装瞎。……再说，她为什么深更半夜起身穿戴齐全，早晨四点半便发了疯似的非要离开特鲁瓦旅馆？哪怕等到天亮再走，难道不成吗？什么差事、什么说得过去的义务或图谋，能把她在这种邪门的时辰催出门去、投入黑夜与风雨？他纳闷得很！

===Original===
And momentarily he grew more jealously heedful of her, critical of every nuance in her bearing. The least trace of added pressure on his arm, the most subtle suggestion that she wasn’t entirely indifferent to him or regarded him in any way other than as the chance-found comrade of an hour of trouble, would have served to fix his suspicions. For such, he told himself, would be the first thought of one bent on beguiling﻿—to lead him on by some intimation, the more tenuous and elusive the more provocative, that she found his person not altogether objectionable.
But he failed to detect anything of this nature in her manner.
So, what was one to think? That she was mental enough to appreciate how ruinous to her design would be any such advances?﻿ ﻿…
In such perplexity he brought her to the end of the alley and there pulled up for a look round before venturing out into the narrow, dark, and deserted side street that then presented itself.

===Chinese===
于是他一刻比一刻更戒备地留意她，挑剔她举止间的每一丝细微变化。她的手若在他臂上多添一分压力，若有一丝半缕最微妙的暗示，表明她对他并非全然无动于衷，或不只是把他当作患难时刻萍水相逢的同伴，都足以坐实他的疑心。因为他对自己说，存心迷人耳目的女人，头一个念头必然如此——用某种暗示吊着他，越虚无缥缈、越难捉摸便越撩人，示意她觉得他这个人还算不惹人厌。
然而在她的一举一动里，他捕捉不到半点此类迹象。
那么，该作何解释？难道她头脑够清醒，明白这类挑逗于她的大计只会是毁灭性的？……
他就这样满腹狐疑地把姑娘带到巷子尽头，停下来四下张望一番，才敢涉足眼前那条狭窄、黑暗而空无一人的侧街。

===Original===
At this the girl gently disengaged her hand and drew away a pace or two; and when Lanyard had satisfied himself that there were no Apaches in the offing, he turned to see her standing there, just within the mouth of the alley, in a pose of blank indecision.
Conscious of his regard, she turned to his inspection a face touched with a fugitive, uncertain smile.
“Where are we?” she asked.
He named the street; and she shook her head. “That doesn’t mean much to me,” she confessed; “I’m so strange to Paris, I know only a few of the principal streets. Where is the boulevard St. Germain?”
Lanyard indicated the direction: “Two blocks that way.”
“Thank you.” She advanced a step or two, but paused again. “Do you know, possibly, just where I could find a taxicab?”

===Chinese===
这时姑娘轻轻抽回手，退开一两步；待兰亚德确信四下并无阿帕奇流氓出没，回过头来，却见她站在巷口里侧，一副全然拿不定主意的模样。
察觉他在打量，她朝他转过脸来，脸上掠过一丝转瞬即逝、捉摸不定的微笑。
「我们这是在哪儿？」她问。
他报出街名；她摇了摇头。「这名字对我说不上什么，」她坦言，「我对巴黎太生疏了，只认得几条大街。圣日耳曼大道在哪儿？」
兰亚德指了指方向：「往那边过两条街。」
「谢谢。」她往前挪了一两步，却又顿住。「您可知道，我在哪儿能叫到一辆出租汽车？」

===Original===
“I’m afraid you won’t find any hereabouts at this hour,” he replied. “A fiacre, perhaps﻿—with luck: I doubt if there’s one disengaged nearer than Montmartre, where business is apt to be more brisk.”
“Oh!” she cried in dismay. “I hadn’t thought of that.﻿ ﻿… I thought Paris never went to sleep!”
“Only about three hours earlier than most of the world’s capitals.﻿ ﻿… But perhaps I can advise you﻿—”
“If you would be so kind! Only, I don’t like to be a nuisance﻿—”
He smiled deceptively: “Don’t worry about that. Where do you wish to go?”

===Chinese===
「恐怕这个时辰，这一带是叫不到的，」他答道。「出租马车（fiacre）倒或许——得碰运气：我疑心最近的空车也得去蒙马特找，那边生意往往更热闹些。」
「哎呀！」她懊丧地叫出声。「这我可没想到。……我还当巴黎从来不睡觉呢！」
「不过比世上多数国都早睡约莫三个钟头罢了。……要不，或许我能给您出个主意——」
「您若肯帮忙就太好了！只是，我不想讨人嫌——」
他虚实莫辨地一笑：「这个不必担心。您想去哪儿？」

===Original===
“To the Gare du Nord.”
That made him open his eyes. “The Gare du Nord!” he echoed. “But﻿—I beg your pardon﻿—”
“I wish to take the first train for London,” the girl informed him calmly.
“You’ll have a while to wait,” Lanyard suggested. “The first train leaves about half-past eight, and it’s now not more than five.”
“That can’t be helped. I can wait in the station.”
He shrugged: that was her own lookout﻿—if she were sincere in asserting that she meant to leave Paris; something which he took the liberty of doubting.

===Chinese===
「去北站。」
这话叫他睁大了眼。「北站！」他应声重复。「可是——恕我冒昧——」
「我想赶去伦敦的头一班火车，」姑娘不动声色地告诉他。
「那您可有得等了，」兰亚德提点她。「头班车八点半左右才发，如今还不到五点。」
「那也没法子。我可以在车站里等。」
他耸耸肩：那是她自家的事——倘若她声称要离开巴黎属实的话；而这一点，他斗胆表示怀疑。

===Original===
“You can reach it by the Métro,” he suggested﻿—“the Underground, you know; there’s a station handy﻿—St. Germain des Prés. If you like, I’ll show you the way.”
Her relief seemed so genuine, he could have almost believed in it. And yet﻿—!
“I shall be very grateful,” she murmured.
He took that for whatever worth it might assay, and quietly fell into place beside her; and in a mutual silence﻿—perhaps largely due to her intuitive sense of his bias﻿—they gained the boulevard St. Germain. But here, even as they emerged from the side street, that happened which again upset Lanyard’s plans: a belated fiacre hove up out of the mist and ranged alongside, its driver loudly soliciting patronage.
Beneath his breath Lanyard cursed the man liberally, nothing could have been more inopportune; he needed that uncouth conveyance for his own purposes, and if only it had waited until he had piloted the girl to the station of the Métropolitain, he might have had it. Now he must either yield the cab to the girl or﻿—share it with her.﻿ ﻿… But why not? He could readily drop out at his destination, and bid the driver continue to the Gare du Nord; and the Métro was neither quick nor direct enough for his design﻿—which included getting under cover well before daybreak.

===Chinese===
「坐地铁（Métro）就能到，」他提议道——「就是地下铁道，您知道的；近处便有一站——圣日耳曼德佩（St. Germain des Prés）。您若愿意，我送您过去。」
她的释然显得那样真切，他几乎要信了。然而——！
「那我真是感激不尽，」她低声道。
这话成色几何，他姑且照单全收，悄然在她身侧落了半步；两人一路相对无言——多半是因为她凭直觉觉察了他的成见——就这样到了圣日耳曼大道。可就在他们步出侧街的一刻，偏又生出一桩搅乱兰亚德盘算的事：一辆夜归的出租马车从雾里冒了出来，靠到近旁，车夫大声兜揽生意。
兰亚德压着嗓门，把那车夫结结实实咒骂了一通——来得实在再不合时宜也没有了；他自己正用得上这件寒碜的代步家伙，倘若它晚来一步，等他把姑娘送到地铁站，车便归他支配了。如今他要么把车让给姑娘，要么——与她同乘。……可这又有何不可？到了目的地他尽可下车，吩咐车夫继续赶往北站；何况地铁对他此行的图谋来说，既不够快，也不够直——他的计划里还包括赶在天亮之前妥妥藏身。

===Original===
Somewhat sulkily, then, if without betraying his temper, he signalled the cocher, opened the door, and handed the girl in.
“If you don’t mind dropping me en route﻿ ﻿…”
“I shall be very glad,” she said﻿ ﻿… “anything to repay, even in part, the courtesy you’ve shown me!”
“Oh, please don’t fret about that.﻿ ﻿…”
He gave the driver precise directions, climbed in, and settled himself beside the girl. The whip cracked, the horse sighed, the driver swore; the aged fiacre groaned, stirred with reluctance, crawled wearily off through the thickening drizzle.
Within its body a common restraint held silence like a wall between the two.

===Chinese===
于是他暗暗着恼——面上却分毫不露——招手唤过车夫（cocher），拉开车门，扶姑娘上了车。
「要是您不介意我半路下车的话……」
「我求之不得，」她说……「但求能报答一二，也好偿还您待我的这份殷勤！」
「哎呀，这桩就不必挂怀了。……」
他向车夫交代了确切的去处，登车在姑娘身旁坐定。鞭花一炸，马儿长叹一声，车夫骂骂咧咧；那辆老掉牙的马车呻吟一声，老大不情愿地挪动起来，在渐浓的细雨里疲惫地爬行而去。
车厢里，一种彼此心照的拘谨维系着沉默，像一堵墙横亘在两人之间。

===Original===
The girl sat with face averted, reading through the window what corner signs they passed: rue Bonaparte, rue Jacob, rue des Saints Pères, Quai Malquais, Pont du Carrousel; recognizing at least one landmark in the gloomy arches of the Louvre; vaguely wondering at the inept French taste in nomenclature which had christened that vast, louring, echoing quadrangle the place du Carrousel, unliveliest of public places in her strange Parisian experience.
And in his turn, Lanyard reviewed those well-remembered ways in vast weariness of spirit﻿—disgusted with himself in consciousness that the girl had somehow divined his distrust.﻿ ﻿…
“The Lone Wolf, eh?” he mused bitterly. “Rather, the Cornered Rat﻿—if people only knew! Better still, the Errant﻿—no!﻿—the Arrant Ass!”
They were skirting the Palais Royal when suddenly she turned to him in an impulsive attempt at self-justification.
“What *must* you be thinking of me, Mr. Lanyard?”

===Chinese===
姑娘侧脸坐着，透过车窗读着一路掠过的街角路牌：波拿巴街、雅各布街、圣父街（rue des Saints Pères）、马尔凯滨河道、卡鲁塞尔桥（Pont du Carrousel）；卢浮宫阴沉沉的拱廊，总算让她认出了一处地标；她又隐隐纳闷，法国人取名的品味竟这般笨拙，会给那片宏大阴森、回声荡荡的方场起名叫卡鲁塞尔广场（place du Carrousel）——在她这趟陌生的巴黎见闻里，再没有比它更死气沉沉的公共场所了。
兰亚德这边，也在心力交瘁中重温着那些烂熟于心的街路——想到姑娘不知怎么已看穿了他的不信任，他不由得对自己生厌。……
「独狼，嗯？」他自嘲得发苦。「倒不如说是被逼进死角的老鼠——可惜世人无从知晓！再好听些，是游侠骑士——不！——是头不折不扣的蠢驴！」
马车正沿着王宫花园外缘行进，她忽然转向他，一阵冲动，想要为自己辩白几句。
「您这会儿*一定*把我往不堪处想了，兰亚德先生？」

===Original===
He was startled: “I? Oh, don’t consider me, please. It doesn’t matter what I think﻿—does it?”
“But you’ve been so kind; I feel I owe you at least some explanation﻿—”
“Oh, as for that,” he countered cheerfully, “I’ve got a pretty definite notion you’re running away from your father.”
“Yes. I couldn’t stand it any longer﻿—”
She caught herself up in full voice, as though tempted but afraid to say more. He waited briefly before offering encouragement.
“I hope I haven’t seemed impertinent.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
他吃了一惊：「我？哦，请别把我算在内。我怎么想无关紧要——不是吗？」
「可您一直这样好心；我总觉得，至少该向您解释几句——」
「哦，说到这个，」他轻快地接过话头，「我心里已有个八九不离十的谱：您是在躲着令尊出走。」
「是的。我实在再也忍受不下去了——」
话到中途她蓦地收住，声音本是满满的，此刻像是既想说下去又不敢多说。他略等了等，才出言宽解。
「但愿我没有显得唐突。……」

===Original===
“No, no!”
Than this impatient negative his pause of invitation evoked no other recognition. She had subsided into her reserve, but﻿—he fancied﻿—not altogether willingly.
Was it, then, possible that he had misjudged her?
“You’ve friends in London, no doubt?” he ventured.
“No﻿—none.”
“But﻿—”

===Chinese===
「没有，没有！」
他那留话静候的停顿，只换来这么一声不耐烦的否认。她又缩回了矜持之中，不过——他恍惚觉得——并非全然心甘情愿。
那么，莫非是他错看了她？
「您在伦敦总有朋友吧？」他试探道。
「没有——一个也没有。」
「可是——」

===Original===
“I shall manage very well. I shan’t be there more than a day or two﻿—till the next steamer sails.”
“I see.” There had sounded in her tone a finality which signified desire to drop the subject. None the less, he pursued mischievously: “Permit me to wish you bon voyage, Miss Bannon﻿ ﻿… and to express my regret that circumstances have conspired to change your plans.”
She was still eyeing him askance, dubiously, as if weighing the question of his acquaintance with her plans, when the fiacre lumbered from the rue Vivienne into the place de la Bourse, rounded that frowning pile, and drew up on its north side before the blue lights of the all-night telegraph bureau.
“With permission,” Lanyard said, unlatching the door, “I’ll stop off here. But I’ll direct the cocher very carefully to the Gare du Nord. Please don’t even tip him﻿—that’s my affair. No﻿—not another word of thanks; to have been permitted to be of service﻿—it is a unique pleasure, Miss Bannon. And so, good night!”
With an effect that seemed little less than timid, the girl offered her hand.

===Chinese===
「我会应付得来的。我在那儿至多待一两天——等下一班邮轮启航就走。」
「我明白了。」她话音里透出一股决绝，示意不愿再谈这个话题。饶是如此，他还是促狭地追了一句：「请容我祝您 bon voyage（一路平安），班农小姐……并深表遗憾：情势作弄，竟教您的计划横生变故。」
她还在侧目打量他，将信将疑，像在掂量他对她的计划究竟知情多少；恰在此时，马车轰隆隆从维维安纳街（rue Vivienne）拐进交易所广场（place de la Bourse），绕过那座阴沉沉的大楼，在它北侧通宵电报局的蓝灯前停了下来。
「恕我失陪，」兰亚德说着，拨开门闩，「我在这儿下车。不过我会把话给车夫交代得妥妥帖帖，送您去北站。请连小费也别赏——那是我的事。不——道谢的话一句也莫再说了；能获准为您效劳——实乃平生一大幸事，班农小姐。那么，晚安！」
姑娘伸出手来，那姿态近乎怯生生的。

===Original===
“Thank you, Mr. Lanyard,” she said in an unsteady voice. “I am sorry﻿—”
But she didn’t say what it was she regretted; and Lanyard, standing with bared head in the driving mist, touched her fingers coolly, repeated his farewells, and gave the driver both money and instructions, and watched the cab lurch away before he approached the telegraph bureau.﻿ ﻿…
But the enigma of the girl so deeply intrigued his imagination that it was only with difficulty that he concocted a noncommittal telegram to Roddy’s friend in the Préfecture﻿—that imposing personage who had watched with the man from Scotland Yard at the platform gates in the Gare du Nord.
It was couched in English, when eventually composed and submitted to the telegraph clerk with a fervent if inaudible prayer that he might be ignorant of the tongue.

===Chinese===
「谢谢您，兰亚德先生，」她声音发颤地说。「我很抱歉——」
可她终归没说抱歉的是什么；兰亚德立在扑面的雾雨中，帽子已脱在手里，淡淡一触她的指尖，又道了两遍珍重，把钱和吩咐一并交给车夫，望着马车蹒跚远去，才转身走向电报局。……
可那姑娘的谜团萦绕在他心头，挥之不去，害得他斟酌了半晌，才拟出一封语焉不详的电报，发给罗迪在巴黎警察总局的那位朋友——就是那位曾同苏格兰场来客一道守在北站站台闸口的气派人物。
电文用英文写就，最终交到报务员手上时，他还在心里默祷——热烈却无声——但愿此人不识英文。

===Original===
> Come at once to my room at Troyon’s. Enter via adjoining room prepared for immediate action on important development. Urgent. Roddy.

Whether or not this were Greek to the man behind the wicket, it was accepted with complete indifference﻿—or, rather, with an interest that apparently evaporated on receipt of the fees. Lanyard couldn’t see that the clerk favoured him with as much as a curious glance before he turned away to lose himself, to bury his identity finally and forever under the incognito of the Lone Wolf.
He couldn’t have rested without taking that one step to compass the arrest of the American assassin; now with luck and prompt action on the part of the Préfecture, he felt sure Roddy would be avenged by Monsieur de Paris.﻿ ﻿… But it was very well that there should exist no clue whereby the author of that mysterious telegram might be traced.﻿ ﻿…
It was, then, not an ill-pleased Lanyard who slipped off into the night and the rain; but his exasperation was elaborate when the first object that met his gaze was that wretched fiacre, back in place before the door, Lucia Bannon leaning from its lowered window, the cocher on his box brandishing an importunate whip at the adventurer.

===Chinese===
> 速来特鲁瓦旅馆我房。经由毗邻房间进入，一切已备，就重大进展立即行动。急。罗迪。

窗口后面那人是否视之为天书，不得而知；电报照单全收，全然无动于衷——或者说，那点兴味，在收足电资的一刻显然便蒸发净尽了。兰亚德看不出那职员曾多赏他一记好奇的眼风；随即他转身离去，就此遁形，把自我的身份最终而永远地埋葬在「独狼」这个假名之下。
不走出这一步、不促成那美国凶手的落网，他本是安不下心来的；如今只消运气尚可、警察总局动作够快，他确信罗迪的仇自有「巴黎先生」（Monsieur de Paris，刽子手）来报。……不过，那封神秘电报的执笔人不留半点可资追索的线索，也实在是万幸。……
就这样，兰亚德并不扫兴地闪身没入夜色和雨幕；可他头一眼撞见的东西，登时叫他火冒三丈——那辆倒霉的马车又停回了门前，露西娅·班农从摇下的车窗里探出身来，车夫坐在驭座上，朝这位冒险家不依不饶地挥着马鞭。

===Original===
He barely escaped choking on suppressed profanity; and for two sous would have swung on his heel and ignored the girl deliberately. But he didn’t dare: close at hand stood a *sergent de ville*, inquisitive eyes bright beneath the dripping visor of his kepi, keenly welcoming this diversion of a cheerless hour.
With at least outward semblance of resignation, Lanyard approached the window.
“I have been guilty of some stupidity, perhaps?” he enquired with lip-civility that had no echo in his heart. “But I am sorry﻿—”
“The stupidity is mine,” the girl interrupted in accents tense with agitation. “Mr. Lanyard, I﻿—I﻿—”
Her voice faltered and broke off in a short, dry sob, and she drew back with an effect of instinctive distaste for public emotion. Lanyard smothered an impulse to demand roughly “Well, what now?” and came closer to the window.

===Chinese===
他把满肚子的粗话强咽回去，险些噎住；换作旁的时候，凭两个苏（sou）的小小意气，他也早掉头而去，存心把这位姑娘晾在一边了。可他不敢：近旁便立着一名 *sergent de ville*（巡警），大檐帽的帽檐滴着水，帽下一双眼睛亮得好事，正巴不得这凄清时辰里来一出消遣。
兰亚德摆出至少表面上的顺从，向车窗走去。
「莫非是我犯了什么糊涂？」他嘴上客气地问，心里却全无这份客气。「总之很抱歉——」
「糊涂的是我，」姑娘打断他，语调因激动而发紧。「兰亚德先生，我——我——」
她的声音颤抖着，一声短促干涩的呜咽戛然而止；她朝后缩了缩，一副天性厌恶当众流露情绪的样子。兰亚德按捺住一句想粗声冲出口的「又怎么了？」，走近车窗。

===Original===
“Something more I can do, Miss Bannon?”
“I don’t know.﻿ ﻿… I’ve just found it out﻿—I came away so hurriedly I never thought to make sure; but I’ve no money﻿—not a franc!”
After a little pause he commented helpfully: “That does complicate matters, doesn’t it?”
“What am I to do? I can’t go back﻿—I won’t! Anything rather. You may judge how desperate I am, when I prefer to throw myself on your generosity﻿—and already I’ve strained your patience﻿—”
“Not much,” he interrupted in a soothing voice. “But﻿—half a moment﻿—we must talk this over.”
Directing the cocher to drive to the place Pigalle, he reentered the cab, suspicion more than ever rife in his mind. But as far as he could see﻿—with that confounded sergo staring!﻿—there was nothing else for it. He couldn’t stand there in the rain forever, gossiping with a girl half-hysterical﻿—or pretending to be.

===Chinese===
「还有什么我能效劳的，班农小姐？」
「我不知道。……我也是刚刚才发现——走得太急，压根没想到要检查一下；可我身无分文——连一个法郎也没有！」
略顿了顿，他好心地评论道：「这可把事情弄复杂了，是不是？」
「我可怎么办？我回不去了——我不回去！怎么都行，就是不能回去。您可想见我走投无路到了什么地步——宁可来仰仗您的慷慨——而我已经把您的耐性磨得够呛——」
「哪里的话，」他宽声宽慰地截住她。「不过——且慢——咱们得好好商量商量。」
他吩咐车夫赶往皮加勒广场（place Pigalle），重新钻进车厢，心里的疑云反倒更浓了。可是依他看——何况还有那个混账 sergo（巡警）死盯着！——别无他法。他总不能一直站在雨里，同一位半歇斯底里——或者装作半歇斯底里——的姑娘絮叨。

===Original===
“You see,” she explained when the fiacre was again under way, “I thought I had a hundred-franc note in my pocketbook; and so I have﻿—but the pocketbook’s back there, in my room at Troyon’s.”
“A hundred francs wouldn’t see you far toward New York,” he observed thoughtfully.
“Oh, I hope you don’t think﻿—!”
She drew back into her corner with a little shudder of humiliation.
As if he hadn’t noticed, Lanyard turned to the window, leaned out, and redirected the driver sharply: “Impasse Stanislas!”

===Chinese===
「您瞧，」马车重新上路后她解释道，「我原以为皮夹子里有一张一百法郎的钞票；也确实有——可皮夹子落在了那边，特鲁瓦旅馆我的房间里。」
「一百法郎，送您去纽约可想走不了多远，」他若有所思地说。
「哎呀，但愿您别以为——！」
她缩回自己的角落里，因受辱而微微一颤。
兰亚德只当没有察觉，转身探出车窗，厉声改了车夫的去处：「斯坦尼斯拉斯死巷（Impasse Stanislas）！」

===Original===
Immediately the vehicle swerved, rounded a corner, and made back toward the Seine with a celerity which suggested that the stables were on the Rive Gauche.
“Where?” the girl demanded as Lanyard sat back. “Where are you taking me?”
“I’m sorry,” Lanyard said with every appearance of sudden contrition; “I acted impulsively﻿—on the assumption of your complete confidence. Which, of course, was unpardonable. But, believe me; you have only to say no and it shall be as you wish.”
“But,” she persisted impatiently﻿—“you haven’t answered me: what is this impasse Stanislas?”
“The address of an artist I know﻿—Solon, the painter. We’re going to take possession of his studio in his absence. Don’t worry; he won’t mind. He is under heavy obligation to me﻿—I’ve sold several canvasses for him; and when he’s away, as now, in the States, he leaves me the keys. It’s a sober-minded, steady-paced neighbourhood, where we can rest without misgivings and take our time to think things out.”

===Chinese===
马车立刻一拐，绕过街角，以快得可疑的速度折向塞纳河——瞧这麻利劲儿，车行的棚舍想必就设在左岸（Rive Gauche）。
「哪儿？」兰亚德坐回原位时，姑娘质问道。「您要带我去哪儿？」
「对不起，」兰亚德一副骤然悔悟的模样，「我行事唐突了——是笃定您对我全然信服，才这么办的。这自然不可原谅。不过请相信我：您只消说个不字，一切悉听尊便。」
「可是，」她不耐烦地追问——「您还没回答我：这斯坦尼斯拉斯死巷究竟是什么地方？」
「是我认识的一位画家的住址——梭伦（Solon），画画的。我们要趁他不在，暂且把他的画室占用一用。别担心；他不会见怪的。他欠着我不小的人情——我替他卖过好几幅画；他远行时——譬如眼下去了美国——钥匙总留给我。那是个规矩本分、四平八稳的地段，咱们大可安枕无忧地歇息，从从容容把事情想个明白。」

===Original===
“But﻿—” the girl began in an odd tone.
“But permit me,” he interposed hastily, “to urge the facts of the case upon your consideration.”
“Well?” she said in the same tone, as he paused.
“To begin with﻿—I don’t doubt you’ve good reason for running away from your father.”
“A very real, a very grave reason,” she affirmed quietly.
“And you’d rather not go back﻿—”

===Chinese===
「可是——」姑娘用一种古怪的声调开了口。
「不过请容我，」他连忙插话，「把案情的各项事实摆出来，供您斟酌。」
「说吧？」他停下来的当儿，她仍用那种声调说。
「首先——我不怀疑，您离家出走自有充分的缘由。」
「一个千真万确、非同小可的缘由，」她平静地断言。
「而您是不愿回去的——」

===Original===
“That is out of the question!”﻿—with a restrained passion that almost won his credulity.
“But you’ve no friends in Paris﻿—?”
“Not one!”
“And no money. So it seems, if you’re to elude your father, you must find some place to hide pro tem. As for myself, I’ve not slept in forty-eight hours and must rest before I’ll be able to think clearly and plan ahead.﻿ ﻿… And we won’t accomplish much riding round forever in this ark. So I offer the only solution I’m capable of advancing, under the circumstances.”
“You are quite right,” the girl agreed after a moment. “Please don’t think me unappreciative. Indeed, it makes me very unhappy to think I know no way to make amends for your trouble.”

===Chinese===
「那是万万不能的！」——那强自压抑的激动，几乎赢得了他的相信。
「可您在巴黎没有朋友——？」
「一个也没有！」
「而且身无分文。如此说来，您既要躲开令尊，总得先寻个地方暂避一时。至于我，已经四十八个钟头不曾合眼，不歇一歇，脑子没法清明，更谈不上筹划后路。……再说，坐在这艘旧方舟里没完没了地兜圈子，也成不了什么气候。所以，情急之下，我拿得出的办法就这一个了。」
「您说得很是，」姑娘沉吟片刻后表示赞同。「请别当我不知好歹。说真的，一想到我竟无以为报、难偿您这番辛苦，我心里就难过得很。」

===Original===
“There may be a way,” Lanyard informed her quietly; “but we’ll not discuss that until we’ve rested up a bit.”
“I shall be only too glad﻿—” she began, but fell silent and, in a silence that seemed almost apprehensive, eyed him speculatively throughout the remainder of the journey.
It wasn’t a long one; in the course of the next ten minutes they drew up at the end of a shallow pocket of a street, a scant half-block in depth; where alighting, Lanyard helped the girl out, paid and dismissed the cocher, and turned to an iron gate in a high stone wall crowned with spikes.
The grille-work of that gate afforded glimpses of a small, dark garden and a little house of two storeys. Blank walls of old tenements shouldered both house and garden on either side.
Unlocking the gate, Lanyard refastened it very carefully, repeated the business at the front door of the house, and when they were securely locked and bolted within a dark reception-hall, turned on the electric light.

===Chinese===
「办法或许是有一个，」兰亚德不动声色地告诉她；「不过得等咱们歇够了再谈。」
「那我真是求之不得——」她开了个头，却又沉默下来；余下的一路，她一言不发，近乎忐忑地打量着他，若有所思。
路不算远；此后十分钟内，马车便停在一条浅口袋似的短街尽头——统共深不过半条街；下车后，兰亚德扶姑娘落地，付了钱打发走车夫，转身面对一道铁门，门后高高的石墙顶上插满尖桩。
铁门的格栅间隐约可见一座幽暗的小花园和一幢两层小楼。两侧老旧公寓光秃秃的山墙，把房子和花园紧紧夹在中间。
兰亚德开了铁门，进去后又仔仔细细把它闩好，到了房子正门前照章再办理一遍；待两人在一间黑暗的门厅里稳稳落了锁、上了闩，他才拧亮电灯。

===Original===
But he granted the girl little more than time for a fugitive survey of this anteroom to an establishment of unique artistic character.
“These are living-rooms, downstairs here,” he explained hurriedly. “Solon’s unmarried, and lives quite alone﻿—his studio-devil and *femme-de-ménage* come in by the day only﻿—and so he avoids that pest a concierge. With your permission, I’ll assign you to the studio﻿—up here.”
And leading the way up a narrow flight of steps, he made a light in the huge room that was the upper storey.
“I believe you’ll be comfortable,” he said﻿—“that divan yonder is as easy a couch as one could wish﻿—and there’s this door you can lock at the head of the staircase; while I, of course, will be on guard below.﻿ ﻿… And now, Miss Bannon﻿ ﻿… unless there’s something more I can do﻿—?”
The girl answered with a wan smile and a little broken sigh. Almost involuntarily, in the heaviness of her fatigue, she had surrendered to the hospitable arms of a huge lounge-chair.

===Chinese===
不过他只给了姑娘一点工夫，把这处别具艺术品格的宅第的前厅匆匆浏览一眼。
「楼下这几间是起居室，」他匆匆解释。「梭伦没成家，独门独户地住着——他的画室小厮（studio-devil）和 *femme-de-ménage*（女管家）都只白天来——所以躲开了门房那路神憎鬼厌的东西。承蒙俯允，我把您安置在画室——就在楼上。」
说着他在前引路，登上一道窄窄的楼梯，在楼上那间大屋子里点亮了灯。
「我想您会住得舒服的，」他说——「那边那张长榻，要多受用有多受用——楼梯口这扇门，您可以从里头锁上；至于我，自然在楼下守夜。……那么，班农小姐……若再没有什么要我效劳的——？」
姑娘报以一丝黯淡的微笑和一声断续的轻叹。疲乏沉甸甸压着，她几乎是身不由己地陷进一把大安乐椅好客的怀抱里。

===Original===
Her weary glance ranged the luxuriously appointed studio and returned to Lanyard’s face; and while he waited he fancied something moving in those wistful eyes, so deeply shadowed with distress, perplexity, and fatigue.
“I’m very tired indeed,” she confessed﻿—“more than I guessed. But I’m sure I shall be comfortable.﻿ ﻿… And I count myself very fortunate, Mr. Lanyard. You’ve been more kind than I deserved. Without you, I don’t like to think what might have become of me.﻿ ﻿…”
“Please don’t!” he pleaded and, suddenly discountenanced by consciousness of his duplicity, turned to the stairs. “Good night, Miss Bannon,” he mumbled; and was halfway down before he heard his valediction faintly echoed.
As he gained the lower floor, the door was closed at the top of the stairs and its bolt shot home with a soft thud. But turning to lock the lower door, he stayed his hand in transient indecision.

===Chinese===
她倦慵的目光扫过这间陈设奢华的画室，又回到兰亚德脸上；他等候的当儿，恍惚觉得那双怅惘的眼睛里有什么在涌动——忧苦、惶惑与倦怠，在眼下投出深深的阴影。
「我真是累极了，」她承认——「比我自己料想的还厉害。不过我确信会歇得很好。……而且我自觉万分幸运，兰亚德先生。您待我的好，远超出我应得的。若不是您，我真不敢想自己会落得什么下场。……」
「请别这么说！」他恳求道，蓦然想起自己的两面做派，脸上讪讪的，转身走向楼梯。「晚安，班农小姐，」他含混地说；走到半途，才听见自己那句道别被轻轻地回应了一声。
他刚到楼下，楼梯顶上的门便关上了，门闩一声轻响推到位。可轮到他锁楼下的门时，他的手却顿住了，一时委决不下。

===Original===
“Damn it!” he growled uneasily﻿—“there can’t be any harm in that girl! Impossible for eyes like hers to lie!﻿ ﻿… And yet﻿ ﻿… And yet!﻿ ﻿… Oh, what’s the matter with me? Am I losing my grip? Why stick at ordinary precaution against treachery on the part of a woman who’s nothing to me and of whom I know nothing that isn’t conspicuously questionable?﻿ ﻿… All because of a pretty face and an appealing manner!”
And so he secured that door, if very quietly; and having pocketed the key and made the round of doors and windows, examining their locks, he stumbled heavily into the bedroom of his friend the artist.
Darkness overwhelmed him then: he was stricken down by sleep as an ox falls under the pole.

===Chinese===
「见鬼！」他忐忑地咕哝——「那姑娘不可能心怀歹意！长着那样一双眼睛的人，怎么会撒谎！……可是……可是！……唉，我是怎么了？手松了吗？对一个于我无关痛痒的女人——除了满身显著的疑点，我对她一无所知——竟连防范背信的寻常小心都不肯用了？……就为了一张漂亮脸蛋、一副楚楚可怜的姿态！」
于是他还是锁上了那扇门，只是动作极轻；收好钥匙，又把各处门窗巡了一遍，逐一查验过锁具，才跌跌撞撞地一头栽进画家朋友的卧室。
黑暗随即将他吞没：睡眠把他击倒，像公牛挨了屠槌，轰然扑地。

## VII L’Abbaye / 七、修道院街

===Original===
His secret uncovered, that essential incognito of his punctured, his vanity touched to the quick﻿—all that laboriously constructed edifice of art and chicane which yesterday had seemed so substantial, so impregnable a wall between the Lone Wolf and the World, today rent, torn asunder, and cast down in ruins about his feet﻿—Lanyard wasted time neither in profitless lamentation or any other sort of repining.

He had much to do before morning: to determine, as definitely as might in discretion be possible, who had fathomed his secret and how; to calculate what chance he still had of pursuing his career without exposure and disaster; and to arrange, if investigation verified his expectations, which were of the gloomiest, to withdraw in good order, with all honours of war, from that dangerous field.

Delaying only long enough to revise plans disarranged by the discoveries of this last bad quarter of an hour, he put out the lights and went out by the courtyard door; for it was just possible that those whose sardonic whim it had been to name themselves “the Pack” might have stationed agents in the street to follow their dissocial brother in crime. And now more than ever Lanyard was firmly bent on going his own way unwatched.

His own way first led him stealthily past the door of the conciergerie and through the court to the public hall in the main body of the building. Happily, there were no lights to betray him had anyone been awake to notice. For thanks to Parisian notions of economy even the best apartment houses dispense with elevator-boys and with lights that burn up real money every hour of the night. By pressing a button beside the door on entering, however, Lanyard could have obtained light in the hallways for five minutes, or long enough to enable any tenant to find his front-door and the keyhole therein; at the end of which period the lamps would automatically have extinguished themselves. Or by entering a narrow-chested box of about the dimensions of a generous coffin, and pressing a button bearing the number of the floor at which he wished to alight, he could have been comfortably wafted aloft without sign of more human agency. But he prudently availed himself of neither of these conveniences. Afoot and in complete darkness he made the ascent of five flights of winding stairs to the door of an apartment on the sixth floor. Here a flash from a pocket lamp located the keyhole; the key turned without sound; the door swung on silent hinges.

Once inside, the adventurer moved more freely, with less precaution against noise. He was on known ground, and alone; the apartment, though furnished, was untenanted, and would so remain as long as Lanyard continued to pay the rent from London under an assumed name.

===Chinese===
秘密已然败露，那层性命交关的匿名伪装被戳穿了，虚荣心更被伤到了痛处——他惨淡经营的那座机巧与诡诈的大厦，昨天还俨然是独狼与尘世之间一堵坚实无比、固若金汤的高墙，今日却已被撕裂、扯碎，化作断砖残瓦倾颓在他脚边——然而兰亚德既不浪费时间作无益的哀叹，也不作任何自怨自艾。

天亮之前他有许多事要做：要在审慎容许的范围内尽可能确定，是谁、又是如何识破了他的秘密；要盘算他这门营生不遭揭穿、不致身败名裂的胜算还剩几成；倘若调查证实了他那些最为阴郁的预想，还得安排一番，从容整队、带着战争的全副荣誉，撤出那片险地。

他只停留了片刻，把被这倒霉的一刻钟里的新发现搅乱的计划重新理过，便熄了灯，从院子那扇门出去——那帮给自己取了个挖苦名号叫「群狼」的家伙，说不定已在街上布下眼线，要盯梢他们这位不合群的犯罪同行。而此刻，兰亚德比任何时候都更铁了心：要走自己的路，而且不许任何人盯着。

他的头一段路，是蹑手蹑脚溜过门房小屋（*conciergerie*）的门，穿过院子，进到楼房主体的公共门厅。所幸厅内没有灯光出卖他，哪怕有人醒着留意也无妨——托巴黎人节俭观念的福，连最好的公寓楼也省下了电梯侍应，更省得点那种通宵每小时都在烧真金白银的灯。不过，进门时按一下门边的电钮，兰亚德本可让楼道亮上五分钟的灯，足够任何房客找到自家大门和门上的锁眼；五分钟一到，灯便自动熄灭。或者，他也可以钻进一只窄胸膛的匣子——尺寸约莫一口宽绰的棺材——按一下标着他要去的楼层号码的按钮，舒舒服服地被送上高处，不见丝毫人力痕迹。但他审慎有加，两样便利一概弃之不用。他徒步摸黑，爬完五段回旋楼梯，来到六楼一套公寓门前。袖珍电筒一闪，照定锁眼；钥匙无声转动；门在不出声的合页上荡开了。

一进门里，这位冒险家行动便自在多了，不再那么顾忌响动。这是知根知底的地界，又只有他一人；那套公寓虽有家具，却无人居住，而且只要兰亚德继续用化名从伦敦按时付租，它便会一直空下去。

===Original===
It was the convenience of this refuge and avenue of retreat, indeed which had dictated his choice of the *rez-de-chaussée*; for the sixth-floor flat possessed one invaluable advantage﻿—a window on a level with the roof of the adjoining building.

Two minutes’ examination sufficed to prove that here at least the Pack had not trespassed.﻿ ﻿…

Five minutes later Lanyard picked the common lock of a door opening from the roof of an apartment house on the farthest corner of the block, found his way downstairs, tapped the door of the conciergerie, chanted that venerable Open Sesame of Paris, “*Cordon, s’il vous plait!*” and was made free of the street by a worthy guardian too sleepy to challenge the identity of this late-departing guest.

He walked three blocks, picked up a taxicab, and in ten minutes more was set down at the Gare des Invalides.

Passing through the station without pause, he took to the streets afoot, following the boulevard St. Germain to the rue du Bac; a brief walk up this timeworn thoroughfare brought him to the ample, open and unguarded porte-cochère of a court walled with beetling ancient tenements.

===Chinese===
说实在的，正是图这处避难所兼退路的便利，他当初才选中了底层（*rez-de-chaussée*）的住处；因为这套六楼房间有一样无价的便利——一扇窗子正与邻楼的屋顶齐平。

两分钟的检视便足以证明：至少在这里，「群狼」还不曾染指。……

五分钟后，兰亚德撬开街区最远一角那栋公寓楼自屋顶开门的一把普通弹子锁，摸下楼去，敲响门房小屋的门，念出巴黎那句古老的「芝麻开门」——「*Cordon, s'il vous plait!*（请拉门绳）」——尽职的看守睡意正浓，懒得盘问这位深夜离去的客人姓甚名谁，便放他上了大街。

他步行三个街区，拦下一辆出租汽车，再过十分钟光景，便在荣军院站（Gare des Invalides）下了车。

他穿过车站片刻不停，徒步上了街，沿圣日耳曼大道走到巴克街（rue du Bac）；顺着这条年深月久的大街走不多远，便来到一扇宽敞、洞开且无人看守的马车大门（*porte-cochère*）前——门内一座院子，四壁是倾压欲坠的古老公寓楼。

===Original===
When he had made sure that the courtyard was deserted, Lanyard addressed himself to a door on the right; which to his knock swung promptly ajar with a clicking latch. At the same time the adventurer whipped from beneath his cloak a small black velvet visor and adjusted it to mask the upper half of his face. Then entering a narrow and odorous corridor, whose obscurity was emphasized by a lonely guttering candle, he turned the knob of the first door and walked into a small, ill-furnished room.

A spare-bodied young man, who had been reading at a desk by the light of an oil-lamp with a heavy green shade, rose and bowed courteously.

“Good morning, monsieur,” he said with the cordiality of one who greets an acquaintance of old standing. “Be seated,” he added, indicating an armchair beside the desk. “It seems long since one has had the honour of a call from monsieur.”

“That is so,” Lanyard admitted, sitting down.

The young man followed suit. The lamplight, striking across his face beneath the greenish penumbra of the shade, discovered a countenance of Hebraic cast.

===Chinese===
确认院中空无一人后，兰亚德便走向右手一扇门；他一叩，门闩咔哒一响，门随即半开。与此同时，冒险家从斗篷底下唰地抽出一只黑丝绒小面罩，戴上，遮住上半张脸。然后他走进一条狭窄而气味刺鼻的走廊——一支孤零零的残烛将那里的幽暗愈发衬得深浓——他拧开第一扇门的把手，走进一间陈设寒酸的小屋。

一个瘦削的年轻人，本来正就着一盏沉甸甸绿罩油灯的光在书桌前读书，此刻起身，客气地鞠了一躬。

「早上好，先生。」他开口，热络得像迎候一位交情深厚的故人。「请坐。」他又添一句，指了指书桌旁的一把扶手椅。「似乎好久不曾蒙先生赏光光临了。」

「正是如此。」兰亚德承认，坐下了。

年轻人也跟着落座。灯光从灯罩绿莹莹的暗影下斜掠过他的脸，照出一张犹太人轮廓的面孔。

===Original===
“Monsieur has something to show me, eh?”

“But naturally.”

Lanyard’s reply just escaped a suspicion of curtness: as who should say, what did you expect? He was puzzled by something strange and new in the attitude of this young man, a trace of reserve and constraint.﻿ ﻿…

They had been meeting from time to time for several years, conducting their secret and lawless business according to a formula invented by Bourke and religiously observed by Lanyard. A note or telegram of innocent superficial intent, addressed to a certain member of a leading firm of jewellers in Amsterdam, was the invariable signal for conferences such as this; which were invariably held in the same place, at an hour indeterminate between midnight and dawn, between on the one hand this intelligent, cultivated and well-mannered young Jew, and on the other hand the thief in his mask.

In such wise did the Lone Wolf dispose of his loot, at all events of the bulk thereof; other channels were, of course, open to him, but none so safe; and with no other receiver of stolen goods could he hope to make such fair and profitable deals.

===Chinese===
「先生带了东西要给我瞧，是吧？」

「那自然。」

兰亚德的回答险些带上生硬——那口吻分明在说：不然你还指望什么？可这年轻人的做派里有一种陌生的新东西让他纳闷，透着几分拘谨与局促。……

几年来他们不时碰头，按照伯克所创、兰亚德恪守如仪的一套规程，做着这桩秘密而不法的买卖。给阿姆斯特丹一家头号珠宝行的某位成员寄一封或拍一通表面上无关痛痒的便笺或电报，便是此类会谈一成不变的信号；会谈又总在同一地点举行，钟点则在午夜与黎明之间不定，一方是这位聪明、有教养、举止得体的年轻犹太人，另一方是戴面罩的窃贼。

独狼便这样脱手他的赃物——至少是其中大头；别的门路当然也向他敞开，但没有一条这样稳妥；换了任何别的销赃人，他也休想做成本这样公道、这样有利可图的买卖。

===Original===
Now inevitably in the course of this long association, though each remained in ignorance of his confederate’s identity, these two had come to feel that they knew each other fairly well. Not infrequently, when their business had been transacted, Lanyard would linger an hour with the agent, chatting over cigarettes: both, perhaps, a little thrilled by the piquancy of the situation; for the young Jew was the only man who had ever wittingly met the Lone Wolf face to face.﻿ ﻿…

Why then this sudden awkwardness and embarrassment on the part of the agent?

Lanyard’s eyes narrowed with suspicion.

In silence he produced a jewel-case of morocco leather and handed it over to the Jew, then settled back in his chair, his attitude one of lounging, but his mind as quick with distrust as the fingers that, under cover of his cloak, rested close to a pocket containing his automatic.

Accepting the box with a little bow, the Jew pressed the catch and discovered its contents. But the richness of the treasure thus disclosed did not seem to surprise him; and, indeed, he had more than once been introduced with no more formality to plunder of far greater value. Fitting a jeweller’s glass to his eye, he took up one after another of the pieces and examined them under the lamplight. Presently he replaced the last, shut down the cover of the box, turned a thoughtful countenance to Lanyard, and made as if to speak, but hesitated.

===Chinese===
于是不可避免地，在这段漫长的交往中，尽管彼此始终不知同伙的底细，这两人却渐渐觉得已相当熟稔。买卖办完之后，兰亚德还不时同这位代理人多待个把钟头，就着香烟闲谈：两人或许都有几分心头发热，为了这境况的妙趣；要知道，这位年轻犹太人是世上唯一一个有意与独狼当面见过的人。……

那么，代理人这边为何突然有了这般尴尬与窘迫？

兰亚德眯起眼睛，满腹狐疑。

他默不作声地取出一只摩洛哥皮首饰匣，递给犹太人，然后往椅背上一靠，姿态闲散，心思却疑虑翻腾——那份迅捷，不亚于斗篷遮掩下贴着自动手枪口袋的那几根手指。

犹太人微微欠身接过匣子，按开搭扣，亮出里面的东西。可这样露出的豪富珍宝似乎并不使他惊讶；说实在的，比这贵重得多的赃物，他早不止一次以同样简单的礼数见识过了。他往眼上嵌了一枚珠宝商的目镜，把首饰一件件拈起，凑在灯下细看。片刻之后，他放回最后一件，合上匣盖，若有所思地转向兰亚德，欲言又止。

===Original===
“Well?” the adventurer demanded impatiently.

“This, I take it,” said the Jew absently, tapping the box, “is the jewellery of Madame Omber.”

“*I* took it,” Lanyard retorted good-naturedly﻿—“not to put too fine a point upon it!”

“I am sorry,” the other said slowly.

“Yes?”

===Chinese===
「怎么样？」冒险家不耐烦地追问。

「我揣摩，」犹太人心不在焉地敲着匣子，「这是欧姆贝夫人的首饰。」

「*我*拿的，」兰亚德好脾气地回敬——「说得再直白不过了！」

「我很遗憾。」对方缓缓说道。

「哦？」

===Original===
“It is most unfortunate﻿ ﻿…”

“May one enquire what is most unfortunate?”

The Jew shrugged and with the tips of his fingers gently pushed the box toward his customer. “This makes me very unhappy,” he admitted: “but I have no choice in the matter, monsieur. As the agent of my principals I am instructed to refuse you an offer for these valuables.”

“Why?”

Again the shrug, accompanied by a deprecatory grimace: “That is difficult to say. No explanation was made me. My instructions were simply to keep this appointment as usual, but to advise you it will be impossible for my principals to continue their relations with you as long as your affairs remain in their present status.”

===Chinese===
「这事极其不巧……」

「可否请教，什么极其不巧？」

犹太人耸耸肩，用指尖把匣子轻轻推回主顾面前。「这叫我非常难过，」他承认，「可这件事上我没有半点选择，先生。作为我东家们的代理人，我奉命不能就这些珍宝向您出价。」

「为什么？」

又是一耸肩，外加一个歉然的怪相：「这很难说。没有人向我作过解释。我奉到的指示只是：照常赴这次约，但须告知您——只要您的处境一仍其现状，我东家们便无法继续与您的关系。」

===Original===
“Their present status?” Lanyard repeated. “What does that mean, if you please?”

“I cannot say, monsieur. I can only repeat that which was said to me.”

After a moment Lanyard rose, took the box, and replaced it in his pocket. “Very well,” he said quietly. “Your principals, of course, understand that this action on their part definitely ends our relations, rather than merely interrupts them at their whim?”

“I am desolated, monsieur, but﻿ ﻿… one must assume that they have considered everything. You understand, it is a matter in which I am wholly without discretion, I trust?”

“O quite!” Lanyard assented carelessly. He held out his hand. “Goodbye, my friend.”

===Chinese===
「『一仍其现状』？」兰亚德复述一遍。「倒要请教，这话是什么意思？」

「我说不上来，先生。人家怎么对我说，我只能怎么转述。」

片刻之后，兰亚德起身，拿起匣子，放回口袋。「很好，」他平静地说。「想必您的东家们明白：他们此举是干脆了结了我们的关系，而不只是凭一时高兴打个中断？」

「我深感遗憾，先生，可是……只能认为，他们已把一切都考虑周全了。您明白，在这件事上我全然无权斟酌行事——您能体谅，是吧？」

「哦，那当然！」兰亚德漫不经心地应道。他伸出手。「再会，我的朋友。」

===Original===
The Jew shook hands warmly.

“Good night, monsieur﻿—and the best of luck!”

There was significance in his last words that Lanyard did not trouble to analyze. Beyond doubt, the man knew more than he dared admit. And the adventurer told himself he could shrewdly surmise most of that which the other had felt constrained to leave unspoken.

Pressure from some quarter had been brought to bear upon that eminently respectable firm of jewel dealers in Amsterdam to induce them to discontinue their clandestine relations with the Lone Wolf, profitable though these must have been.

Lanyard believed he could name the quarter whence this pressure was being exerted, but before going further or coming to any momentous decision, he was determined to know to a certainty who were arrayed against him and how much importance he need attach to their antagonism. If he failed in this, it would be the fault of the other side, not his for want of readiness to accept its invitation.

===Chinese===
犹太人热情地握了他的手。

「晚安，先生——祝您鸿运！」

这临别一语里的深意，兰亚德没费心去细想。毫无疑问，此人知道的比他敢承认的多得多。而冒险家自忖，对方觉得不便出口的那些话，他大半都能精明地猜出个所以然。

必是哪个方面施加了压力，才使得阿姆斯特丹那家极体面的珠宝行断绝与独狼的私下往来——尽管这往来必定利润丰厚。

兰亚德自信叫得出这压力来自何方；但在再往前走一步、作出任何重大决断之前，他打定主意要查个确切无疑：究竟是哪些人列阵与他为敌，这份敌意又值得他掂上几分成色。万一查不明白，那也是对方的过错，怨不得他没做好应邀赴会的准备。

===Original===
In brief, he didn’t for an instant contemplate abandoning either his rigid rule of solitude or his chosen career without a fight; but he preferred not to fight in the dark.

Anger burned in him no less hotly than chagrin. It could hardly be otherwise with one who, so long suffered to go his way without let or hindrance, now suddenly, in the course of a few brief hours, found himself brought up with a round turn﻿—hemmed in and menaced on every side by secret opposition and hostility.

He no longer feared to be watched; and the very fact that, as far as he could see, he wasn’t watched, only added fuel to his resentment, demonstrating as it did so patently the cynical assurance of the Pack that they had him cornered, without alternative other than to supple himself to their will.

To the driver of the first taxicab he met, Lanyard said “L’Abbaye,” then shutting himself within the conveyance, surrendered to the most morose reflections.

Nothing of this mood was, however, apparent in his manner on alighting. He bore a countenance of amiable insouciance through the portals of this festal institution whose proudest boast and﻿—incidentally﻿—sole claim to uniquity is that it never opens its doors before midnight nor closes them before dawn.

===Chinese===
