简而言之，要他不战而弃那条铁一般的独行之规，或弃他选定的这门营生，他连想都不想；只是他不愿意摸黑打这一仗。

他的怒火烧得绝不比懊恼之火温和。换了谁也只能如此：这么久以来无人阻拦、无人作梗，任他独往独来，如今却在短短几个钟头里猛地被勒了个急停——四面八方都是暗中的作对与敌意，围困他，威胁他。

他已不再怕人盯梢；而据他所见根本无人盯梢这一事实，反倒给他的怨火添了柴——它再清楚不过地摆明了「群狼」那副冷笑的笃定：他们自认已把他逼进死角，除了把自己拗得服服帖帖、顺从他们的意志，他别无出路。

拦到头一辆出租汽车，兰亚德对司机只说了声「L'Abbaye（修道院）」，随即把自己关进车厢，沉入最阴郁的思索。

然而下车的时候，这番心境在他举止上不见分毫。他端着一副和蔼可亲、满不在乎的脸色，穿过这家欢宴机构的门廊——它最引以为傲、顺带说也是它唯一自负于世的独特之处，便是从不在午夜前开门，也从不在黎明前打烊。

===Original===
He had moved about with such celerity since entering his flat on the rue Roget that it was even now only two o’clock; an hour at which revelry might be expected to have reached its apogee in this, the soi-disant “smartest” place in Paris.

A less sophisticated adventurer might have been flattered by the cordiality of his reception at the hands of that arbiter elegantiarum the maitre-d’hôtel.

“Ah-h, Monsieur Lanya*rrr*’! But it is long since we have been so favoured. However, I have kept your table for you.”

“Have you, though?”

“Could it be otherwise, after receipt of your honoured order?”

===Chinese===
他自进罗热街公寓之后行动如此迅捷，此刻竟才两点钟；在这家自封的巴黎「最时髦」场所，这个钟点的狂欢正该登峰造极。

换个老练稍逊的冒险家，见那位餐厅总管、这位风雅的仲裁人（*arbiter elegantiarum*）如此殷勤地接待自己，只怕要受宠若惊了。

「啊——哈，兰亚*尔尔*先生！可有些日子不曾这样蒙您赏光啦。不过，您的台子我可是一直给您留着呢。」

「是吗，真给我留着？」

「得了您尊贵的吩咐，还能有别的做法不成？」

===Original===
“No,” said Lanyard coolly, “I presume not, if you value your peace of mind.”

“Monsieur is alone?” This with an accent of disappointment.

“Temporarily, it would seem so.”

“But this way, if you please.﻿ ﻿…”

In the wake of the functionary, Lanyard traversed that frowsy anteroom where doubtful wasters are herded on suspicion in company with the corps of automatic Bacchanalians and figurantes, to the main restaurant, the inner sanctum toward which the naive soul of the travel-bitten Anglo-Saxon aspires so ardently.

===Chinese===
「那是，」兰亚德淡淡地说，「我想你不敢不留——倘若你还爱惜你的太平日子。」

「先生就一位？」这话音里带着失望。

「眼下如此，暂时算是一个人。」

「那么这边请，您哪。……」

兰亚德跟在这位执事身后，穿过那间邋遢的前厅——形迹可疑的浪荡子们被圈在那里，与那班自动机式的酒神信徒、群舞女伶为伍——来到主餐厅，那处内中圣殿：风尘仆仆的盎格鲁-撒克逊人那颗天真的心灵，正向着它热切地朝拜呢。

===Original===
It was not a large room; irregularly octagonal in shape, lined with wall-seats behind a close-set rank of tables; better lighted than most Parisian restaurants, that is to say, less glaringly; abominably ventilated; the open space in the middle of the floor reserved for a handful of haggard young professional dancers, their stunted bodies more or less costumed in brilliant colours, footing it with all the vivacity to be expected of five-francs per night per head; the tables occupied by parties Anglo-Saxon and French in the proportion of five to one, attended by a company of bored and apathetic waiters; a string orchestra ragging incessantly; a vicious buck-nigger on a dais shining with self-complacence while he vamped and shouted “*Waitin’ foh th’ Robuht E. Lee*”﻿ ﻿…

Lanyard permitted himself to be penned in a corner behind a table, ordered champagne not because he wanted it but because it was etiquette, suppressed a yawn, lighted a cigarette, and reviewed the assemblage with a languid but shrewd glance.

He saw only the company of every night; for even in the off-season there are always enough English-speaking people in Paris to make it possible for L’Abbaye Thêléme to keep open with profit: the inevitable assortment of respectable married couples with friends, the men chafing and wondering if possibly all this might seem less unattractive were they footloose and fancy-free, the women contriving to appear at ease with varying degrees of success, but one and all flushed with dubiety; the sprinkling of demimondaines not in the least concerned about *their* social status; the handful of people who, having brought their fun with them, were having the good time they would have had anywhere; the scattering of plain drunks in evening dress.﻿ ﻿… Nowhere a face that Lanyard recognized definitely: no Mr. Bannon, no Comte Remy de Morbihan.﻿ ﻿…

He regarded this circumstance, however, with more vexation than surprise: De Morbihan would surely show up in time; meanwhile, it was annoying to be obliged to wait, to endure this martyrdom of ennui.

He sipped his wine sparingly, without relish, considering the single subsidiary fact which did impress him with some wonder﻿—that he was being left severely to himself; something which doesn’t often fall to the lot of the unattached male at L’Abbaye. Evidently an order had been issued with respect to him. Ordinarily he would have been grateful: tonight he was merely irritated: such neglect rendered him conspicuous.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
这厅堂不算大；形状是不规则的八边形，沿墙设着一圈卡座，卡座前密密排着餐桌；比多数巴黎馆子亮堂些——也就是说，不那么晃眼刺目；通风则糟不可言；厅心空地留给一小拨形容憔悴的年轻职业舞者，他们那发育不良的身躯或多或少裹着艳色行头，蹦跶得起劲非常，正合每人每晚五法郎的身价；餐桌边坐着的客人是盎格鲁-撒克逊人与法国人五比一的比例，伺候他们的是一班百无聊赖、爱答不理的侍者；一支弦乐队不歇气地奏着拉格泰姆；台上一名凶悍的黑鬼满面自得地放着光，一边即兴踏着伴奏，一边扯开嗓子吼「*Waitin' foh th' Robuht E. Lee*（《等待罗伯特·李号》）」……

兰亚德听任自己被圈进一张桌子后面的角落，点了香槟——不是想喝，而是礼数攸关——压下一个哈欠，点上一支烟，用慵懒而精到的一瞥，把满座宾客检阅了一遍。

他看到的只是夜夜都有的那批客人：即便在淡季，巴黎的说英语人也总多到够让泰莱姆修道院开着门还有利可图：少不了的那几组体面夫妇偕朋友而来，男人们心浮气躁，暗自嘀咕着倘若自己光棍一条、无牵无挂，这一切会不会就显得不那么无趣；女人们则设法装出安然自若，深浅不一，却个个脸上泛着犹疑的红晕；零星几位半上流社会的女子，对*她们的*社会地位毫不上心；那一小撮自带乐趣的客人，在这儿消受他们在哪儿都能消受的好时光；还有稀稀落落几摊穿夜礼服、明明白白的醉汉。……放眼望去，没有一张兰亚德确切认得的面孔：没有班农先生，没有雷米·德·莫尔比昂伯爵。……

不过对这一情形，他恼火多于惊讶：德·莫尔比昂迟早总会露面；只是在此期间不得不干等，忍受这场无聊倦怠（*ennui*）的酷刑，实在恼人。

他浅浅呷着酒，无滋无味，心里琢磨着唯一一件确实叫他略感诧异的次要小事——他竟被彻头彻尾地晾在一边；在 L'Abbaye（修道院），落单的爷们儿可难得摊上这待遇。显然，关于他，上头下来过一道吩咐。搁在平常，他该领情：今夜他只觉得恼火：这般冷落反倒把他衬得惹眼。……

===Original===
The fixed round of delirious divertissement unfolded as per schedule. The lights were lowered to provide a melodramatic atmosphere for that startling novelty, the Apache Dance. The coon shouted stridently. The dancers danced bravely on their poor, tired feet. An odious dwarf creature in a miniature outfit of evening clothes toddled from table to table, offensively soliciting stray francs﻿—but shied from the gleam in Lanyard’s eyes. Lackeys made the rounds, presenting each guest with a handful of coloured, featherweight celluloid balls, with which to bombard strangers across the room. The inevitable shamefaced Englishman departed in tow of an overdressed Frenchwoman with pride of conquest in her smirk. The equally inevitable alcoholic was dug out from under his table and thrown into a cab. An American girl insisted on climbing upon a table to dance, but swayed and had to be helped down, giggling foolishly. A Spanish dancing girl was afforded a clear floor for her specialty, which consisted in singing several verses understood by nobody, the choruses emphasized by frantic assaults on the hair of several variously surprised, indignant, and flattered male guests﻿—among them Lanyard, who submitted with resignation.﻿ ﻿…

And then, just when he was on the point of consigning the Pack to the devil for inflicting upon him such cruel and inhuman punishment, the Spanish girl picked her way through the mob of dancers who invaded the floor promptly on her withdrawal, and paused beside his table.

“You’re not angry, *mon coco*?” she pleaded with a provocative smile.

Lanyard returned a smiling negative.

“Then I may sit down with you and drink a glass of your wine?”

===Chinese===
那套固定的狂欢节目按部就班地铺展开来。灯光压暗，为那惊人的新鲜货色——阿帕奇舞（Apache Dance）——敷上一层情节剧的气氛。黑鬼乐手刺耳地高唱。舞女们凭着一双可怜的倦足英勇地跳着。一个身穿微缩版晚礼服的讨厌侏儒，一桌一桌蹒跚而行，死皮赖脸地讨着零星法郎——却怯于兰亚德眼中那道寒光。仆役们穿梭各桌，给每位客人奉上一把轻若羽毛的彩色赛璐珞小球，好让他们隔厅轰炸素不相识的生人。那位少不了的、面带愧色的英国人，被一个衣饰过艳的法国女人牵着走了，她得意的窃笑里满是征服者的骄傲。那位同样少不了的醉汉，则被人从桌子底下刨出来，扔进一辆出租马车。一个美国姑娘非要站上桌子去跳舞，却晃了两晃，只得让人扶下来，兀自傻笑。一位西班牙舞女得了清出的场子表演拿手好戏——唱上好几段谁也听不懂的歌词，副歌部分则以疯狂袭击几位男宾的头发来加强声势，那些人有的愕然、有的愠怒、有的受宠若惊——兰亚德也在其列，逆来顺受地由她施为。……

就在他即将把「群狼」骂去见魔鬼、怨他们害他受这般残忍不人道的酷刑之际，那西班牙舞女——她一退场，舞池随即被人堆占领——从攒动的人群中穿出来，在他桌旁站住了。

「您没生气吧，*mon coco*（我的小哥）？」她带着撩人的笑央告道。

兰亚德报以微笑的摇头。

「那我可以陪您坐下，喝一杯您的酒吗？」

===Original===
“Can’t you see I’ve been saving the bottle for you?”

The woman plumped herself promptly into the chair opposite the adventurer. He filled her glass.

“But you are not happy tonight?” she demanded, staring over the brim as she sipped.

“I am thoughtful,” he said.

“And what does that mean?”

===Chinese===
「看不出我这一瓶一直给你留着吗？」

那女人一屁股坐进冒险家对面的椅子。他给她斟上了酒。

「可您今晚不开心？」她一边呷酒、一边从杯沿上头盯着他，问道。

「我在思索。」他说。

「这话怎么讲？」

===Original===
“I am saddened to contemplate the infirmities of my countrymen, these Americans who can’t rest in Paris until they find some place as deadly as any Broadway boasts, these English who adore beautiful Paris solely because here they may continue to get drunk publicly after half-past twelve!”

“Ah, then it’s *la barbe*, is it not?” said the girl, gingerly stroking her faded, painted cheek.

“It is true: I am bored.”

“Then why not go where you’re wanted?” She drained her glass at a gulp and jumped up, swirling her skirts. “Your cab is waiting, monsieur﻿—and perhaps you will find it more amusing with that Pack!”

Flinging herself into the arms of another girl, she swung away, grinning impishly at Lanyard over her partner’s shoulder.

===Chinese===
「我一想到同胞们的痼疾便觉伤心：这些美国人，到了巴黎也不得安生，非要寻着一处同百老汇任何一家一样要命的地方才肯罢休；这些英国人，热爱美丽的巴黎，只因在这儿过了十二点半还能当众喝得烂醉！」

「啊，这么说就是*la barbe*（腻得要死）了，是不是？」姑娘说着，轻轻摩挲自己那张褪了色的、敷粉涂脂的脸颊。

「是真的：我腻了。」

「那何不去有人惦记您的地方？」她一口喝干杯中酒，跳起来，裙裾旋成一朵花。「您的马车等着呢，先生——说不定跟那伙『群狼』在一处，还更有趣些！」

她一头扎进另一个姑娘的怀里，旋身而去，从舞伴肩头上冲兰亚德狡黠地咧嘴一笑。

## XXIII Madame Omber / 二十三、欧姆贝夫人

===Original===
Before the echo of that crash ceased to reverberate from room to room, Lanyard slipped to one side of the doorway, from which point he could command the perspective of the salons together with a partial view of the front doors. And he was no more than there, in the shadow of the portières, when light from an electrolier flooded the reception-hall.
It showed him a single figure, that of a handsome woman, considerably beyond middle age but still a well-poised, vigorous, and commanding presence, in full evening dress of such magnificence as to suggest recent attendance at some State function.
Standing beneath the light, she was restoring a key to a brocaded handbag. This done, she turned her head and spoke indistinguishably over her shoulder. Promptly there came into view a second woman of about the same age, but even more strong and able of appearance﻿—a serving-woman, in plain, dark garments, undoubtedly madame’s maid.
Handing over the brocaded bag, madame unlatched the throat of her ermine cloak and surrendered it to the servant’s care.
Her next words were audible, and reassuring in as far as they indicated ignorance of anything amiss.
“Thank you, Sidonie. You may go to bed now.”

===Chinese===
那一声轰响的回音尚未在一间间屋子里止歇，兰亚德已侧身闪到门洞一旁；从那个位置，几间客厅的纵深尽收眼底，还望得见正门的一角。他人刚站定，隐进门帘（portières）的暗影里，一盏枝形电烛吊灯的灯光便已灌满前厅。
灯下唯有一道人影：一位仪容华贵的妇人，年纪远逾中年，却依旧仪态沉稳、精神健旺、威仪迫人；一身盛装晚服，富丽得叫人想见她方才必是赴过什么国务大典而来。
她立在灯下，正把一枚钥匙放回锦缎手袋。放罢，她回过头，朝肩后含混地说了句什么。随即第二个人影应声入目，是个年纪相仿的女人，只是筋骨愈发健利——一名女仆，一身素净深色的衣裳，不用问，是夫人的贴身侍女。
夫人把锦缎手袋交了出去，又解开貂皮斗篷的领扣，把它也交托给那仆妇照料。
接下来那几句话便听得真切了，而且颇叫人宽心——听得出她对变故一无所知。
「多谢，西多妮（Sidonie）。你可以去睡了。」

===Original===
“Madame will not need me to undress her?”
“I’m not ready yet. When I am﻿—I’m old enough to take care of myself. Besides, I prefer you to go to bed, Sidonie. It doesn’t improve your temper to lose your beauty sleep.”
“Many thanks, madame. Good night.”
“Good night.”
The maid moved off toward the main staircase, while her mistress turned deliberately through the salons toward the library.

===Chinese===
「夫人不用我伺候宽衣么？」
「我还没打算睡。等要睡时——我这年纪，自己照料自己尽够了。再说，我宁可你去睡，西多妮。误了你的美容觉，你的脾气只会变坏。」
「多谢夫人。晚安。」
「晚安。」
女仆朝大楼梯那边去了，女主人则从容不迫地穿过一间间客厅，朝书房走来。

===Original===
At this, swinging back to the girl in a stride, and grasping her wrist to compel attention, Lanyard spoke in a rapid whisper, mouth close to her ear, but his solicitude so unselfish and so intense that for the moment he was altogether unconscious of either her allure or his passion.
“This way,” he said, imperatively drawing her toward the window by which he had entered: “there’s a balcony outside﻿—a short drop to the ground.” And unlatching the window, he urged her through it. “Try to leave by the back gateway﻿—the one I showed you before﻿—avoiding Ekstrom﻿—”
“But surely you are coming too?” she insisted, hanging back.
“Impossible: there’s no time for us both to escape undetected. I shall keep madame interested only long enough for you to get away. But take this”﻿—and he pressed his automatic into her hand. “No﻿—take it; I’ve another,” he lied, “and you may need it. Don’t fear for me, but go﻿—O my heart!﻿—go!”

===Chinese===
一听这动静，兰亚德一步抢回姑娘身边，攥住她的手腕逼她凝神，嘴凑在她耳畔急促低语；这一番关切无私之至、炽烈之至，一时之间，他全然忘却了她的妩媚，也忘却了自己的情热。
「这边走。」他说着，不由分说把她往自己进来的那扇窗前带：「外面是个露台——往下没多高，一跳便是地面。」说着拔开窗闩，催她翻出窗去。「想法子打后门走——就是我先前指给你看的那一扇——留神避开埃克斯特罗姆——」
「可你不是也要一起走么？」她赖着不肯挪步，执意问道。
「不成：咱们俩要都悄没声儿地脱身，工夫来不及。我只消缠住夫人一小会儿，够你走脱就是。可这个你拿着」——他把自动手枪塞进她手里。「不——拿着；我还有一支。」他撒谎道，「兴许你用得着。别替我担心，走——啊，我的心肝！——快走！」

===Original===
The footfalls of Madame Omber were sounding dangerously near, and without giving the girl more opportunity to protest, Lanyard closed the windows, shot the latch and stole like a cat round the farther side of the desk, pausing within a few feet of the screen and safe.
The desk-lamp was still burning, where the girl had left it behind the cinnabar screen; and Lanyard knew that the diffusion of its rays was enough to render his figure distinctly and immediately visible to one entering the doorway.
Now everything hung upon the temper of the householder, whether she would take that apparition quietly, deceived by Lanyard’s mumming into believing she had only a poor thievish fool to deal with, or with a storm of bourgeois hysteria. In the latter event, Lanyard’s hand was ready planted, palm down, on the top of the desk: should the woman attempt to give the alarm, a single bound would carry the adventurer across it in full flight for the front doors.

===Chinese===
欧姆贝夫人的脚步声已响到近旁，凶险之至；兰亚德不再给姑娘留争辩的余地，关上窗，推严窗闩，随即猫儿一般悄悄绕到书桌另一侧，在离屏风和保险柜只有几英尺处停住，安然无恙。
书桌上的台灯还亮着，正是姑娘方才撂在朱砂屏风后头的那一盏；兰亚德心里明白，那灯光漫散开来，任谁一进门洞，立刻便能把他这条身形看个真切。
如今万事都悬在这位当家主妇的脾性上了：她是会被兰亚德的做作瞒过、只当撞见个偷鸡摸狗的可怜蠢贼，从容处之呢，还是要发作一场市井妇人的歇斯底里。倘是后者，兰亚德早有一只手备好了，手掌朝下按在桌面：那女人只要试图声张报警，他一个纵跃便可飞身过桌，夺路直奔正门。

===Original===
In the doorway the mistress of the house appeared and halted, her quick bright eyes shifting from the light on the floor to the dark figure of the thief. Then, in a stride, she found a switch and turned on the chandelier, a blaze of light.
As this happened, Lanyard cowered, lifting an elbow as though to guard his face﻿—as though expecting to find himself under the muzzle of a revolver.
The gesture had the calculated effect of focusing the attention of the woman exclusively to him, after one swift glance round had shown her a room tenanted only by herself and a cringing thief. And immediately it was made manifest that, whether or not deceived, she meant to take the situation quietly, if in a strong hand.
Her eyes narrowed and the muscles of her square, almost masculine jaw hardened ominously as she looked the intruder up and down. Then a flicker of contempt modified the grimness of her countenance. She took three steps forward, pausing on the other side of the desk, her back to the doorway.

===Chinese===
门洞里现出宅邸女主人的身形，她收步站定，一双明锐的眼睛从地上的灯光移到窃贼的黑影。随即她跨前一步，摸着开关，拧亮了枝形吊灯——一片辉煌夺目的光。
灯光大亮的当口，兰亚德瑟缩了一下，抬起一条胳膊肘护住脸——仿佛料定下一刻便有左轮枪口对准了自己。
这一着正收到算准了的效果：她疾速环视一周，见满室除了自己和这个瑟瑟缩缩的窃贼再无旁人，注意力便尽数聚到他身上。而紧接着便已分明：不管瞒没瞒过她，她都打定主意要稳稳拿住这个局面——只是用的是铁腕。
她眯起双眼，把这闯入者上上下下打量，那方正得近乎男相的下颌绷得铁紧，透出不祥。随即一丝轻蔑掠过，缓和了她脸上的凛厉。她向前三步，在书桌另一侧站定，背朝着门洞。

===Original===
Lanyard trembled visibly.﻿ ﻿…
“Well!”﻿—the word boomed like the opening gun of an engagement﻿—“Well, my man!”﻿—the shrewd eyes swerved to the closed door of the safe and quickly back again﻿—“you don’t seem to have accomplished much!”
“For God’s sake, madame!” Lanyard blurted in a husky, shaken voice, nothing like his own﻿—“don’t have me arrested! Give me a chance! I haven’t taken anything. Don’t call the flics!”
He checked, moving an uncertain hand towards his throat as if his tongue had gone dry.

===Chinese===
兰亚德浑身直打颤，明明白白看得出来。……
「嚯！」——这一个字如开战的号炮般轰然炸响——「嚯，我的伙计！」——那双精明的眼睛倏地转向保险柜紧闭的柜门，又倏地转回——「瞧这样子，你像是没干成什么大事嘛！」
「看在上帝份上，夫人！」兰亚德脱口嚷道，嗓音嘶哑发颤，全不似他本来的声口——「别叫人抓我！给我一条生路吧！我什么也没拿。千万别叫条子（flics）！」
他说不下去了，一只虚浮的手摸向喉咙，仿佛舌头已经干透。

===Original===
“Come, come!” the woman answered, with a look almost of pity. “I haven’t called anyone﻿—as yet.”
The fingers of one strong white hand were drumming gently on the top of the desk; then, with a movement so quick and sure that Lanyard himself could hardly have bettered it, they slipped down to a handle of a drawer, jerked it open, closed round the butt of a revolver, and presented it at the adventurer’s head.
Automatically he raised both hands.
“Don’t shoot!” he cried. “I’m not armed﻿—”

===Chinese===
「好了，好了！」那妇人答道，神情里几乎带着怜悯。「我谁也没叫——眼下还没有。」
她一只有力的白皙之手在桌面上轻轻叩着；蓦然间，那手指溜下去扣住一只抽屉的把手，猛地一拉而开，攥住一支左轮枪的枪柄，枪口直指冒险家的头颅——那动作之快之稳，便连兰亚德自己也未必胜得过。
他不由自主地举起双手。
「别开枪！」他喊道。「我没带家伙——」

===Original===
“Is that the truth?”
“You’ve only to search me, madame!”
“Thanks!” Madame’s accents now discovered a trace of dry humour. “I’ll leave that to you. Turn out your pockets on the desk there﻿—and, remember, I’ll stand no nonsense!”
The weapon covered Lanyard steadily, leaving him no choice but to obey. As it happened, he was glad of the excuse to listen for sounds to tell how the girl was faring in her flight, and made a pretence of trembling fingers cover the slowness with which he complied.

===Chinese===
「这话当真？」
「夫人只管搜我的身！」
「多谢！」夫人的口吻里此刻透出一丝干巴巴的幽默。「这就交给你自己办。把口袋统统翻出来，摊在那桌上——还有，记住，耍花腔我是概不奉陪的！」
枪口稳稳罩定兰亚德，教他无从选择，唯有从命。偏巧他还乐得有这个借口，好凝神去听动静，辨一辨姑娘逃得怎样，便装出一副十指发抖的模样，遮掩他磨磨蹭蹭的服从。

===Original===
But he heard nothing.
When he had visibly turned every pocket inside out, and their contents lay upon the desk, the woman looked the exhibits over incuriously.
“Put them back,” she said curtly. “And then fetch that chair over there﻿—the one in the corner. I’ve a notion I’d like to talk to you. That’s the usual thing, isn’t it?”
“How?” Lanyard demanded with a vacant stare.

===Chinese===
可他什么也没听见。
待他把一个个口袋当众翻了个底朝天，兜里的物件尽数摊在桌上，那妇人兴致寥寥地检视了一遍这些「呈堂证物」。
「都收回去。」她言简意赅。「然后把那边那把椅子搬过来——角落里那一把。我忽然起了个念头，想同你谈谈。照例都是这样的，不是么？」
「怎么讲？」兰亚德一脸茫然，问道。

===Original===
“In all the criminal novels I’ve ever read, the law-abiding householder always sits down and has a sociable chat with the housebreaker﻿—before calling in the police. I’m afraid that’s part of the price you’ve got to pay for my hospitality.”
She paused, eyeing Lanyard inquisitively while he restored his belongings to his pockets. “Now, get that chair!” she ordered; and waited, standing, until she had been obeyed. “That’s it﻿—there! Sit down.”
Leaning against the desk, her revolver held negligently, the speaker favoured Lanyard with a more leisurely inspection; the harshness of her stare was softened, and the anger which at first had darkened her countenance was gone by the time she chose to pursue her catechism.
“What’s your name? No﻿—don’t answer! I saw your eyes waver, and I’m not interested in a makeshift alias. But it’s the stock question, you know.﻿ ﻿… Do you care for a cigar?”

===Chinese===
「在我读过的所有犯罪小说里，守法的好主人总要先坐下来，同那撬门的贼促膝叙谈一番——然后才去唤警察。只怕这一项，也是你须得为我这份殷勤付出的代价。」
她顿了一顿，一面拿探究的目光打量兰亚德把随身物件一一放回口袋。「现在，把椅子搬来！」她下令；自己站着一味等，直等到命令照办。「这就对了——搁那儿！坐。」
说话的人斜倚着书桌，左轮枪随随便便提在手里，赏了兰亚德一回更从容的检阅；她注视中的严酷已然软化，起初郁在她脸上的那层怒意，到她择定时机继续这场盘问时，已消散净尽。
「你叫什么名字？不——别答话！我看见你眼珠在打晃，随口拼凑的假名我不感兴趣。不过这是问案的老套，你是知道的。……来支雪茄如何？」

===Original===
She opened a mahogany humidor on the desk.
“No, thanks.”
“Right﻿—according to Hoyle: the criminal always refuses to smoke in these scenes. But let’s forget the book and write our own lines. I’ll ask you an original question: Why were you acting just now?”
“Acting?” Lanyard repeated, intrigued by the acuteness of this masterful woman’s mentality.
“Precisely﻿—pretending you were a common thief. For a moment you actually made me think you afraid of me. But you’re neither the one nor the other. How do I know? Because you’re unarmed, your voice has changed in the last two minutes to that of a cultivated man, you’ve stopped cringing and started thinking, and the way you walked across the floor and handled that chair showed how powerfully you’re made. If I didn’t have this revolver, you could overpower me in an instant﻿—and I’m no weakling, as women go. So﻿—why the acting?”

===Chinese===
她打开桌上一只红木雪茄保湿盒（humidor）。
「不用，谢谢。」
「不错——照霍伊尔的牌谱（according to Hoyle）：这类场面里，罪犯总是不肯抽烟的。不过咱们且撇开书本，自己编台词吧。我问你一个独出心裁的问题：方才你为什么做戏？」
「做戏？」兰亚德重复道，这位气魄慑人的妇人心思敏锐，倒教他暗暗称奇。
「一点儿不错——装作一个平平常常的小贼。有一阵子你竟真叫我当你怕了我。可你两样都不是。我怎么知道？因为你身上没有武器；方才这两分钟里，你的嗓音已换成受过教养之人的声口；你不再瑟缩，转而开始思索；还有你走过地板、搬弄椅子的那份身手，足见你这副筋骨何等强劲。倘若不是我有这支左轮枪，你转眼间就能制伏我——而论女人，我也绝非弱者。所以——为什么要做戏？」

===Original===
Studying his captor with narrow interest, Lanyard smiled faintly and shrugged, but made no answer. He could do no more than this﻿—no more than spare for time: the longer he indulged madame in her whim, the better Lucy’s chances of scot-free escape. By this time, he reckoned, she would have found her way through the service gate to the street. But he was on edge with unending apprehension of mischance.
“Come, come!” Madame Omber insisted. “You’re hardly civil, my man. Answer my question!”
“You don’t expect me to﻿—do you?”
“Why not? You owe me at least satisfaction of my curiosity, in return for breaking into my house.”

===Chinese===
兰亚德眯起眼睛，兴味深深端详着这位擒住他的人，淡然一笑，耸了耸肩，却不作答。他能做的也止于此了——止于拖延工夫：他越是纵着夫人的兴致与她周旋，露西全身而退的把握便越大。他盘算着，这时候她该已寻着杂役小门出到街上。可他仍旧心弦紧绷，为着没完没了的意外之惧，一刻不得安生。
「好了，好了！」欧姆贝夫人不依不饶。「你可失礼得很哪，我的伙计。回答我的问题！」
「您总不见得指望我回答吧——呃？」
「为什么不见得？你闯了我的宅子，好歹欠我一回好奇心的满足，权当抵偿。」

===Original===
“But if, as you suggest, I am﻿—or was﻿—acting with a purpose, why expect me to give the show away?”
“That’s logic. I knew you could think. More’s the pity!”
“Pity I can think?”
“Pity you can get your own consent to waste yourself like this. I’m an old woman, and I know men better than most; I can see ability in you. So I say, it’s a pity you won’t use yourself to better advantage. Don’t misunderstand me: this isn’t the conventional act; I don’t hold with encouraging a fool in his folly. You’re a fool, for all your intelligence, and the only cure I can see for you is drastic punishment.”

===Chinese===
「可倘若依您高见，我正——或者说曾——别有图谋地做戏，您又何必指望我自拆戏台呢？」
「这倒合逻辑。我早知道你会动脑子。可惜呀！」
「可惜我会动脑子？」
「可惜你竟能说服自己，这样作践你自己。我是个老太婆了，看男人比多数人看得都透；你身怀才具，我瞧得出。所以我说，你不肯把自己派到更好的用场上，可惜了。别误会我：这不是俗套的戏文；怂恿糊涂人一糊涂到底，我不赞成。你纵有满腹聪明，终究是个糊涂人，而依我看，能医你这病的唯有一剂烈药——从重的惩办。」

===Original===
“Meaning the Santé, madame?”
“Quite so. I tell you frankly, when I’m finished lecturing you, off you go to prison.”
“If that’s the case I don’t see I stand to gain much by retailing the history of my life. This seems to be your cue to ring for servants to call the police.”
A trace of anger shone in the woman’s eyes. “You’re right,” she said shortly; “I dare say Sidonie isn’t asleep yet. I’ll get her to telephone while I keep an eye on you.”

===Chinese===
「夫人是说，桑泰监狱？」
「正是。坦白告诉你，等我训诫完毕，你就该进班房了。」
「既然如此，我实在看不出细数生平于我有何进项。看来眼下正是您摇铃唤仆、召警拿人的节骨眼儿。」
妇人眼里闪过一丝怒火。「你说得是，」她干脆地说；「想来西多妮还没睡着。我让她去打电话，我在这儿看着你。」

===Original===
Bending over the desk, without removing her gaze from the adventurer, his captor groped for, found, and pressed a call-button.
From some remote quarter of the house sounded the grumble of an electric bell.
“Pity you’re so brazen,” she observed. “Just a little less side, and you’d be a rather engaging person!”
Lanyard made no reply. In fact he wasn’t listening.

===Chinese===
擒住他的人俯向书桌，目光半分不离这位冒险家，摸索着寻着了唤人铃的按钮，按了下去。
宅子深处某个角落，咕哝起一阵电铃。
「可惜你脸皮这样厚。」她评点道。「少摆一点架子，你本会是个相当惹人喜欢的人！」
兰亚德不答。其实他根本没在听。

===Original===
Under the strain of that suspense, the iron control which had always been his was breaking down﻿—since now it was for another he was concerned. And he wasted no strength trying to enforce it. The stress of his anxiety was both undisguised and undisguisable. Nor did Madame Omber overlook it.
“What’s the trouble, eh? Is it that already you hear the cell door clang in your ears?”
As she spoke, Lanyard left his chair with a movement in the execution of which all his wits cooperated, with a spring as lithe and sure and swift as an animal’s, that carried him like a shot across the two yards or so between them.
The slightest error in his reckoning would have finished him: for the other had been watching for just such a move, and the revolver was nearly level with Lanyard’s head when he grasped it by the barrel, turned that to the ceiling, imprisoned the woman’s wrist with his other hand, and in two movements had captured the weapon without injuring its owner.

===Chinese===
在那番悬虑的重压之下，他与生俱来那副铁打的自制正在垮塌——因为这一回，他牵心的是另一个人。他也犯不上再费力气去强撑。他那焦灼之态，既遮掩不住，也不曾遮掩。这层，欧姆贝夫人也没放过。
「愁什么，嗯？莫非你耳朵里已经听见牢门哐啷一响？」
话犹未落，兰亚德已离座而起，这一动浑身上下每一分机敏通力合作，身形一纵，柔韧、笃定、迅疾如一头野兽，似离弦之箭掠过两人之间约莫两码的距离。
他的计算但凡差之毫厘，便已万劫不复：原来对方一直在候着这一手，那左轮枪眼看便要端平、对上兰亚德的头，说时迟那时快，他一把攥住枪管，将它拧向天花板，另一只手扣死妇人的手腕，前后不过两个动作，已将那凶器缴到手中，而未伤其主分毫。

===Original===
“Don’t be alarmed,” he said quietly. “I’m not going to do anything more violent than to put this weapon out of commission.”
Breaking it smartly, he shot a shower of cartridges to the door, and tossed the now-useless weapon into a wastebasket beneath the desk.
“Hope I didn’t hurt you,” he added abstractedly﻿—“but your pistol was in my way!”
He took a stride toward the door, pulled up, and hung in hesitation, frowning absently at the woman; who, without moving, laughed quietly and watched him with a twinkle of malicious diversion.

===Chinese===
「别惊慌。」他安然道。「我不打算再有什么暴烈的举动，至多不过叫这件家伙报废而已。」
他干脆利落地把枪拗断，一蓬子弹泼洒向门口，又把这件废物丢进桌下的字纸篓里。
「但愿没伤着您。」他心不在焉地补上一句——「只是您这支手枪挡了我的道！」
他向门口抢出一步，又猛地煞住，悬在那里踌躇不决，心不在焉地蹙眉望着那妇人；她却纹丝不动，浅浅笑着，眼里闪过一丝促狭的兴味，瞧他如何。

===Original===
He repaid this with a stare of thoughtful appraisal; from the first he had recognized in her a character of uncommon tolerance and amiability.
“Pardon, madame, but﻿—” he began abruptly﻿—and checked in constrained appreciation of his impudence.
“If that’s permission to interrupt your reverie,” Madame Omber remarked, “I don’t mind telling you, you’re the most extraordinary burglar I ever heard of!”
Footfalls became audible on the staircase﻿—the hasty scuffling of slippered feet.

===Chinese===
他回敬她一记沉吟掂量的凝视；打头一起，他就在她身上认出了一副罕见的宽容与和善的品性。
「冒犯了，夫人，只是——」他突兀地开口——又讪讪收住，强自觉察出自己的唐突。
「若这就是准我打断你的冥想，」欧姆贝夫人说道，「那么我不妨告诉你：你是我平生所闻最离奇的一位窃贼！」
楼梯上起了脚步声——是趿着拖鞋疾奔的窸窣杂沓。

===Original===
“Is that you, Sidonie?” madame called.
The voice of the maid replied: “Yes, madame﻿—coming!”
“Well﻿—don’t, just yet﻿—not till I call you.”
“Very good, madame.”

===Chinese===
「是西多妮吗？」夫人扬声唤道。
女仆的声音应道：「是，夫人——这就来！」
「这个——先别，再等等——等我叫你再来。」
「好的，夫人。」

===Original===
The woman returned complete attention to Lanyard.
“Now, monsieur-of-two-minds, what is it you wish to say to me?”
“Why did you do that?” the adventurer asked, with a jerk of his head toward the hall.
“Tell Sidonie to wait instead of calling for help? Because﻿—well, because you interest me strangely. I’ve got a theory you’re in a desperate quandary and are about to throw yourself on my mercy.”

===Chinese===
妇人将全副心神重新交付兰亚德。
「好了，『三心二意先生』（monsieur-of-two-minds），你要同我说什么呀？」
「您为何那样做？」冒险家朝大厅那边一摆头，问道。
「叫西多妮候着，别去搬救兵？因为——唔，因为你奇异地引起了我的兴味。我有一套猜度：你正陷在万难的窘境里，眼看就要投到我的怜悯之下来了。」

===Original===
“You are right,” Lanyard admitted tersely.
“Ah! Now you do begin to grow interesting! Would you mind explaining why you think I’ll be merciful?”
“Because, madame, I’ve done you a great service, and feel I can count upon your gratitude.”
The Frenchwoman’s eyebrows lifted at this. “Doubtless, monsieur knows what he’s talking about﻿—”

===Chinese===
「您猜对了。」兰亚德寥寥数字，坦然承认。
「啊！这样你才当真开始有趣起来！可否赐教，你凭什么以为我会大发慈悲？」
「因为，夫人，我曾于您有一场大功，自觉可以仰恃您的感念。」
这法国妇人闻言扬起了眉毛。「想来，先生知道自己在说什么——」

===Original===
“Listen, madame: I am in love with a young woman, an American, a stranger and friendless in Paris. If anything happens to me tonight, if I am arrested or assassinated﻿—”
“Is that likely?”
“Quite likely, madame: I have enemies among the Apaches, and in my own profession as well; and I have reason to believe that several of them are in this neighbourhood tonight. I may possibly not escape their attentions. In that event, this young lady of whom I speak will need a protector.”
“And why must I interest myself in her fate, pray?”

===Chinese===
「请听我说，夫人：我恋爱了，恋着一位年轻的姑娘，一个美国人，在巴黎人地生疏、举目无亲。倘若今夜我出了什么事，倘若我被捕，或遭暗算——」
「有这般可能么？」
「大有可能，夫人：我在阿帕奇（Apaches）当中有仇家，在本行里也有；况且我有理由相信，其中几位今夜便在这一带。他们的『照拂』，我未必躲得开。真到那时，我所说的这位年轻姑娘，就需要一位保护人了。」
「那么请问，她的命运与我何干，我何苦上心？」

===Original===
“Because, madame, of this service I have done you﻿ ﻿… Recently, in London, you were robbed﻿—”
The woman started and coloured with excitement: “You know something of my jewels?”
“Everything, madame: it was I who stole them.”
“You? You are, then, that Lone Wolf?”

===Chinese===
「正为这桩功劳，夫人，它是为您效的……新近，在伦敦，您失过窃——」
妇人一惊，激动得涨红了脸：「你知道我那些珠宝的下落？」
「了如指掌，夫人：下手偷它们的，正是在下。」
「你？那么说，你就是那位『独狼』？」

===Original===
“I was, madame.”
“Why the past tense?” the woman demanded, eyeing him with a portentous frown.
“Because I am done with thieving.”
She threw back her head and laughed, but without mirth: “A likely story, monsieur! Have you reformed since I caught you here﻿—?”

===Chinese===
「我曾经是，夫人。」
「为什么用过去时态？」妇人诘问，蹙眉端详他，神色郑重。
「因为我已同偷窃这行当恩断义绝。」
她仰头大笑，笑声里却无半分欢意：「好一个动听的故事，先生！莫非您的改过自新，是打被我在这宅子里拿住那一刻算起的——？」

===Original===
“Does it matter when? I take it that proof, visible, tangible proof of my sincerity, more than a meaningless date, would be needed to convince you.”
“No doubt of that, Monsieur the Lone Wolf!”
“Could you ask better proof than the restoration of your stolen property?”
“Are you trying to bribe me to let you off with an offer to return my jewels?”

===Chinese===
「几时开始，有何相干？依我想，要教您信服，靠的不是个无谓的日子，而是凭据——我这份诚心，须得有看得见、摸得着的明证。」
「那还用说，独狼先生！」
「失物原璧归赵，还有比这更好的明证么？」
「你是想拿归还珠宝作人情，贿赂我放你一马么？」

===Original===
“I’m afraid emergency reformation wouldn’t persuade you﻿—”
“You may well be afraid, monsieur!”
“But if I can prove I’ve already restored your jewels﻿—?”
“But you have not.”

===Chinese===
「我怕这临危抱佛脚的洗心革面，打动不了您——」
「你很可以怕，先生！」
「可倘若我能证明，您的珠宝早已归还了呢——？」
「可你并没有还。」

===Original===
“If madame will do me the favour to open her safe, she will find them there﻿—conspicuously placed.”
“What nonsense﻿—!”
“Am I wrong in assuming that madame didn’t return from England until quite recently?”
“But today, in fact﻿—”

===Chinese===
「夫人若肯赏脸开一开保险柜，自会看见它们就在里头——摆在再显眼不过的地方。」
「一派胡言——！」
「莫非我猜错了——夫人不是新近才从英格兰回来的么？」
「实在说，就是今天——」

===Original===
“And you haven’t troubled to investigate your safe since returning?”
“It had not occurred to me﻿—”
“Then why not test my statement before denying it?”
With an incredulous shrug Madame Omber terminated a puzzled scrutiny of Lanyard’s countenance, and turned to the safe.

===Chinese===
「回来之后，还不曾劳神查看过您的保险柜？」
「这我倒没想到——」
「那么，何不先验证了我的话，再来驳斥不迟？」
欧姆贝夫人将信将疑地一耸肩，收起对兰亚德面孔那番困惑的审视，转身走向保险柜。

===Original===
“But to have done what you declare you have,” she argued, “you must have known the combination﻿—since it appears you haven’t broken this open.”
The combination ran glibly off Lanyard’s tongue. And at this, with every evidence of excitement, at length beginning to hope if not to believe, the woman set herself to open the safe. Within a minute she had succeeded, the morocco-bound jewel-case was in her hand, and a hasty examination had assured her its treasure was intact.
“But why﻿—?” she stammered, pale with emotion﻿—“why, monsieur, *why*?”
“Because I decided to leave off stealing for a livelihood.”

===Chinese===
「可是要办成你自称办成的事，」她反驳道，「你必得知道锁的密码——因为看来你并没有把这柜门撬开。」
那串密码从兰亚德舌尖上流水般滚了出来。至此，那妇人已激动难抑，终于开始生出一线希望——纵然还说不上相信——便动手去开保险柜。不出一分钟，柜门已开，摩洛哥皮面的首饰匣到了她手中，匆匆一验，满匣珍藏完好无缺。
「可是为什么——？」她讷讷而言，激动得面色发白——「为什么，先生，*究竟为什么*？」
「因为我决计洗手不干，不再以偷窃营生。」

===Original===
“When did you bring these jewels here?”
“Within the week﻿—four or five nights since﻿—”
“And then﻿—repented, eh?”
“I own it.”

===Chinese===
「你几时把这些珠宝送到这儿来的？」
「就在本礼拜——四五个夜晚之前——」
「其后——便悔悟了，嗯？」
「我认账。」

===Original===
“But came here again tonight, to steal a second time what you had stolen once?”
“That’s true, too.”
“And I interrupted you﻿—”
“Pardon, madame: not you, but my better self. I came to steal﻿—I could not.”

===Chinese===
「可今夜又来，要把偷过一回的东西再偷第二遭？」
「这也是实情。」
「而搅了你的——是我——」
「恕我直言，夫人：搅了我的不是您，是我那尚存的良知。我为偷而来——却下不了手。」

===Original===
“Monsieur﻿—you do not convince. I fail to fathom your motives, but﻿—”
A sudden shock of heavy trampling feet in the reception-hall, accompanied by a clash of excited voices, silenced her and brought Lanyard instantly to the face-about.
Above that loud wrangle﻿—of which neither had received the least warning, so completely had their argument absorbed them﻿—Sidonie’s accents were audible: “Madame﻿—madame!”﻿—a cry of protest.
“What is it?” madame demanded of Lanyard.
He threw her the word “Police!” as he turned and flung himself into the recess of the window.
But when he wrenched it open the voice of a picket on the lawn saluted him in sharp warning; and when, involuntarily, he stepped out upon the balcony, a flash of flame split the gloom below, a loud report rang in the quiet of the park, and a bullet slapped viciously the stone facing of the window.

===Chinese===
「先生——你没能说服我。你的动机我参详不透，可是——」
前厅里骤然响起沉重的杂沓脚步，又夹着一阵鼎沸的人声，将她的话头截断，教兰亚德霎时回身对门。
那片喧嚷——两人方才争辩得太专注，对此竟未得到半点预警——之上，还听得出西多妮的声气：「夫人——夫人！」——一声抗议的喊。
「什么事？」夫人向兰亚德喝问。
他撂给她两个字：「警察！」随即转身，一头扎进窗凹里。
可他奋力拧开窗子，草坪上暗哨的嗓音劈头给他一声尖厉的警告；待他不由自主跨上露台，一道火光划破楼下的幽暗，一声巨响震彻园中的沉寂，一颗子弹恶狠狠地啄在窗子的石砌窗脸上。

## XXIV Rendezvous / 二十四、约期相会

===Original===
With as little ceremony as though the bullet had lodged in himself, Lanyard tumbled back into the room, tripped, and fell sprawling; while to a tune of clattering boots two sergents de ville lumbered valiantly into the library and pulled up to discover Madame Omber standing calmly, safe and sound, beside her desk, and Lanyard picking himself up from the floor by the open window.
Behind them Sidonie trotted, wringing her hands.
“Madame!” she bleated﻿—“they wouldn’t listen to me, madame﻿—I couldn’t stop them!”
“All right, Sidonie. Go back to the hall. I’ll call you when needed.﻿ ﻿… Messieurs, good morning!”
One of the sergents advanced with an uncertain salute and a superfluous question: “Madame Omber﻿—?” The other waited on the threshold, barring the way.

===Chinese===
兰亚德向后跌回房中，那副毫无仪态的狼狈相，倒像子弹钻进的是他自己的身子；他一个趔趄，摔了个手脚朝天。与此同时，随着皮靴踏得咔咔作响的节拍，两名巡警（sergents de ville）雄赳赳地拥进书房，收步站定，却发现欧姆贝夫人安然无恙、神色自若地立在书桌旁，而兰亚德正从敞开的窗边的地板上爬起身来。
两人身后，西多妮（Sidonie）小跑着跟进来，只顾绞自己那双手。
「夫人！」她哭腔喊道——「他们不听我的，夫人——我拦不住他们呀！」
「好了，西多妮。回门厅去吧。要用你时我叫你。……诸位先生，早安！」
两名巡警之一上前，行了个迟疑不定的礼，问了句多余的话：「欧姆贝夫人——？」另一名则守在门槛上，堵住去路。

===Original===
Lanyard measured the two speculatively: the spokesman seemed a bit old and fat, ripe for his pension, little apt to prove seriously effective in a rough-and-tumble; but the other was young, sturdy, and broad-chested, with the poise of an athlete, and carried in addition to his sword a pistol naked in his hand, while his clear blue eyes, meeting the adventurer’s, lighted up with a glint of invitation.
For the present, however, Lanyard wasn’t taking any. He met that challenge with a look of utter stupidity, folded his arms, lounged against the desk, and watched Madame Omber acknowledge, none too cordially, the other sergent’s query.
“I am Madame Omber﻿—yes. What can I do for you?”
The sergent gaped. “Pardon!” he stammered, then laughed as one who tardily appreciates a joke. “It is well we are arrived in time, madame,” he added﻿—“though it would seem you have not had great trouble with this miscreant. Where is the woman?”
He moved a pace toward Lanyard: handcuffs jingled in his grasp.

===Chinese===
兰亚德掂量着这两位，心里暗自盘算：开口那个似乎上了年纪、发了福，眼看熬到领养老金的份上，真要混战一场怕顶不了大用；另一个却年轻、壮实、胸膛宽厚，有运动员的沉稳桩步，佩剑之外手里还明晃晃攥着一把手枪；那双清澈的蓝眼睛迎上冒险家的目光时，更闪出一缕跃跃欲试的挑衅之光。
不过眼下，兰亚德无意应这个战。他以一脸全然的呆滞迎住那份挑衅，抱起双臂，懒洋洋倚上书桌，只看欧姆贝夫人接待另一位巡警的问话——那态度可算不得热络。
「我就是欧姆贝夫人——不错。诸位有何见教？」
那巡警目瞪口呆。「恕罪（pardon）！」他结结巴巴地说，随即笑了，像个把笑话领会晚了的人。「幸而我们来得及时，夫人，」他补充道——「不过看来，您对付这个恶徒没费多大的事。那个女人在哪儿？」
他朝兰亚德挪近一步：手铐在他掌中叮当作响。

===Original===
“But a moment!” madame interposed. “Woman? What woman?”
Pausing, the older sergent explained in a tone of surprise:
“But his accomplice, naturally! Such were our instructions﻿—to proceed at once to madame’s hôtel, come in quietly by the servants’ entrance﻿—which would be open﻿—and arrest a burglar with his female accomplice.”
Again the stout sergent moved toward Lanyard; again Madame Omber stopped him.
“But one moment more, if you please!”

===Chinese===
「且慢！」夫人插话。「女人？什么女人？」
年长的巡警顿住脚，用诧异的口气解释道：
「自然是他的同伙啦！我们奉的指令正是如此——即刻赶往夫人的府邸，由仆役进出的小门悄悄入宅——那门会敞着——将一名窃贼连同他的女同伙一并捉拿归案。」
胖巡警又一次朝兰亚德挪动；欧姆贝夫人又一次拦住了他。
「不过请再稍候片刻！」

===Original===
Her eyes, dense with suspicion, questioned Lanyard; who, with a significant nod toward the jewel-case still in her hands, gave her a glance of dumb entreaty.
After brief hesitation, “It is a mistake,” madame declared; “there is no woman in this house, to my certain knowledge, who has no right to be here﻿ ﻿… But you say you received a message? I sent none!”
The fat sergent shrugged. “That is not for me to dispute, madame. I have only my orders to go by.”
He glared sullenly at Lanyard; who returned a placid smile that (despite such hope as he might derive from madame’s irresolute manner) masked a vast amount of trepidation. He felt tolerably sure Madame Omber had not sent for police on prior knowledge of his presence in the library. All this, then, would seem to indicate a new form of attack on the part of the Pack. He had probably been followed and seen to enter; or else the girl had been caught attempting to steal away and the information wrung from her by *force majeure*.﻿ ﻿… Moreover, he could hear two more pair of feet tramping through the salons.
Pending the arrival of these last, Madame Omber said nothing more.

===Chinese===
她的目光盛满疑窦，向兰亚德发出质问；兰亚德朝她手中仍捧着的珠宝匣意味深长地点了点头，又投去无言恳求的一瞥。
踌躇片刻，「这是个误会，」夫人断然宣告；「据我确切所知，这宅子里没有一个在此的女人是不该在此的。……可你们说接到了报信？我什么也没送过！」
胖巡警耸了耸肩。「这可不归我争辩，夫人。我不过照章办事。」
他阴沉沉地瞪着兰亚德；兰亚德回他一个平静的微笑——那微笑背后（尽管夫人摇摆不定的态度尚可给他一线指望）掩藏的却是好大一阵心惊肉跳。他相当有把握：欧姆贝夫人并未预知他在书房而召来警察。那么，这一切便像是「群狼」新一轮的攻势了。他多半是叫人盯了梢，进宅时被瞧见了；再不然，便是那姑娘溜走时被逮个正着，吃他们「强力压人」（*force majeure*）的手段，被榨出了口供。……此外，他还听得见又有两双脚，正隆隆踏过一间间客厅。
在这后两位到场之前，欧姆贝夫人再没有说什么。

===Original===
And, unceremoniously enough, the newcomers shouldered into the library﻿—one pompous uniformed body, of otherwise undistinguished appearance, promptly identified by the sergents de ville as monsieur le commissaire of that quarter; the other, a puffy mediocrity, known to Lanyard at least (if apparently to no one else) as Popinot.
At this confirmation of his darkest fears, the adventurer abandoned hope of aid from Madame Omber and began quietly to reckon his chances of escape through his own efforts.
But he was quite unarmed, and the odds were heavy: four against one, all four no doubt under arms, and two at least﻿—the sergents﻿—men of sound military training.
“Madame Omber?” enquired the commissaire, saluting that lady with immense dignity. “One trusts that this intrusion may be pardoned, the circumstances remembered. In an affair of this nature, involving this repository of so historic treasures﻿—”
“That is quite well understood, monsieur le commissaire,” madame replied distantly. “And this monsieur is, no doubt, your aide?”

===Chinese===
而这两位后来者也不客气，径直用肩膀开路挤进书房——一个是制服气派、官威十足的家伙，此外别无异长，两名巡警立刻认出，此公便是本区的分局长先生（monsieur le commissaire）；另一个虚胖平庸，至少兰亚德认得（旁人似乎都认不得），正是波皮诺。
最深的惧怕得了证实，冒险家就此断绝了指望欧姆贝夫人援手的念头，开始悄悄盘算凭一己之力脱身的胜算。
可他手无寸铁，众寡悬殊：四对一，那四个想来个个带家伙，其中至少两个——那两名巡警——受过正经的军事操练。
「欧姆贝夫人？」分局长问道，向那位夫人行了个礼，气度极为庄重。「还望这一番唐突能蒙宽宥，还望体念事出非常。此等性质的案子，牵涉到这样一处珍藏如许历史瑰宝的所在——」
「这一点尽可放心，分局长先生，」夫人淡然答道。「那么这一位，想必是您的副手了？」

===Original===
“Pardon!” the official hastened to identify his companion: “Monsieur Popinot, agent de la Sûreté, who lays these informations!”
With a profound obeisance to Madame Omber, Popinot strode dramatically over to confront Lanyard and explore his features with his small, keen, shifty eyes of a pig; a scrutiny which the adventurer suffered with superficial calm.
“It is he!” Popinot announced with a gesture. “Messieurs, I call upon you to arrest this man, Michael Lanyard, alias ‘The Lone Wolf.’ ”
He stepped back a pace, expanding his chest in vain effort to eclipse his abdomen, and glanced triumphantly at his respectful audience.
“Accused,” he added with intense relish, “of the murder of Inspector Roddy of Scotland Yard at Troyon’s, as well as of setting fire to that establishment﻿—”

===Chinese===
「岂敢（pardon）！」那官员连忙介绍自己的同伴：「这位是波皮诺先生，保安局（la Sûreté）探员（agent de la Sûreté），本案的举发人！」
波皮诺朝欧姆贝夫人深深一躬，随即迈开戏剧化的大步，走到兰亚德面前，用他那一双猪似的细小、锐利、溜转不定的眼睛细细探究他的相貌；这场端详，冒险家面上从容地领受了。
「正是他！」波皮诺一挥手，宣布道。「诸位先生，我要求你们逮捕此人——迈克尔·兰亚德，化名『独狼』。」
他退后一步，扩起胸膛，枉费心机地想把大肚腩比下去，又得意洋洋地扫了一眼对他毕恭毕敬的听众。
「罪状为，」他滋味无穷地补上一句，「在特鲁瓦旅馆谋害苏格兰场的罗迪探长，并纵火焚烧该店——」

===Original===
“For this, Popinot,” Lanyard interrupted in an undertone, “I shall some day cut off your ears!” He turned to Madame Omber: “Accept, if you please, madame, my sincere regrets﻿ ﻿… but this charge happens to be one of which I am altogether innocent.”
Instantly, from lounging against the desk, Lanyard straightened up: and the heavy humidor of brass and mahogany, on which his right hand had been resting, seemed fairly to leap from its place as, with a sweep of his arm, he sent it spinning point-blank at the younger sergent.
Before that one, wholly unprepared, could more than gasp, the humidor caught him a blow like a kick just below the breastbone. He reeled, the breath left him in one great gust, he sat down abruptly﻿—blue eyes wide with a look of aggrieved surprise﻿—clapped both hands to his middle, blinked, turned pale, and keeled over on his side.
But Lanyard hadn’t waited to note results. He was busy. The fat sergent had leaped snarling upon his arm, and was struggling to hold it still long enough to snap a handcuff round the wrist; while the commissaire had started forward with a bellow of rage and two hands extended and itching for the adventurer’s throat.
The first received a half-arm jab on the point of his chin that jarred his entire system, and without in the least understanding how it happened, found himself whirled around and laid prostrate in the commissaire’s path. The latter tripped, fell, and planted two hard knees, with the bulk of his weight atop them, on the apex of the sergent’s paunch.

===Chinese===
「就冲这一条，波皮诺，」兰亚德压低嗓子插话，「我总有一天要割下你的耳朵！」他转向欧姆贝夫人：「还请夫人收纳我由衷的歉意……不过这一桩罪状，恰好是我全然无辜的。」
说时迟那时快，懒倚在书桌上的兰亚德霍然挺直身子：他右手一直撑着的那只黄铜配红木的沉重雪茄保湿盒（humidor），仿佛当真从原处飞跃而起——臂膀一抡，那盒子打着旋儿，照直飞向年轻巡警的面门。
那位全无防备，惊呼都未及出口，保湿盒已似一记脚踢，正正砸在他胸骨下方。他踉跄摇晃，一口气猛地全泄了，一屁股坐倒在地——蓝眼睛瞪得溜圆，满是委屈惊愕的神色——双手捂住肚腹，眨了眨眼，脸色转白，便侧身栽倒。
兰亚德却没等看清战果。他正忙着呢。胖巡警咆哮着扑上他的胳膊，正较着劲要把那条胳膊按牢，好往手腕上扣手铐；分局长则怒吼一声抢上前来，双手齐齐探出，恨不能立时掐住冒险家的喉咙。
头一个挨了一记半臂短拳，正中下巴尖，震得他周身骨架齐齐乱颤；他全然没弄明白这是怎么回事，便已被抡了个转身，四仰八丫躺倒在分局长的进路上。后者绊了一跤，扑跌下来，两块硬膝盖——连同全身的分量压在其上——不偏不倚，跪进巡警肚皮的顶心。

===Original===
At the same time Lanyard, leaping toward the doorway, noticed Popinot tugging at something in his hip-pocket.
Followed a vivid flash, then complete darkness: with a well-aimed kick﻿—an elementary movement of la savate﻿—Lanyard had dislocated the switch of the electric lights, knocking its porcelain box from the wall, breaking the connection, and creating a short-circuit which extinguished every light in that part of the house.
With his way thus apparently cleared, the police in confusion, darkness aiding him, Lanyard plunged on; but in mid-stride, as he crossed the threshold, his ankle was caught by the still prostrate younger sergent and jerked from under him.
His momentum threw him with a crash﻿—and may have spared him a worse mishap; for in the same breath he heard the report of a pistol and knew that Popinot had fired at his fugitive shadow.
As he brought one heel down with crushing force on the sergent’s wrist, freeing his foot, he was dimly conscious of the voice of the commissaire shouting frantic prayers to cease firing. Then the pain-maddened sergent crawled to his knees, lunged blindly forward, knocked the adventurer back in the act of rising, and fell on top of him.

===Chinese===
与此同时，朝门口跃去的兰亚德瞥见，波皮诺正从后裤袋里往外拽什么东西。
紧跟着一道亮光闪过，随后是彻底的黑暗：兰亚德一记精准的踢击——*la savate*（法式踢打术）的一手入门功夫——踢中电灯开关，把瓷闸盒从墙上整个踢落，线路断开，激起短路，宅子这一翼的灯火霎时尽数熄灭。
去路看是扫清了，警察乱作一团，黑暗又帮着他，兰亚德一头抢出；谁知一步跨到门槛正中，脚踝却被仍瘫在地上的年轻巡警一把捞住，猛地从他身下抽了出去。
前冲的势头带得他轰然扑倒——这或许倒免了他一遭更大的横祸；因为就在同一口气的工夫，他听见一声枪响，知道波皮诺已朝他逃窜的影子开了火。
他一只脚跟挟着粉碎之力跺在巡警腕上，挣脱了脚，恍惚间还听得分局长的嗓音，正声嘶力竭地哀告众人停火。随后，那痛得发了狂的巡警挣扎着爬起膝盖，不管不顾往前一扑，把正待起身的冒险家撞了回去，压在他身上。

===Original===
Hampered by two hundred pounds of fighting Frenchman, Lanyard felt his cause was lost, yet battled on﻿—and would while breath was in him.
With a heave, a twist and a squirm, he slipped from under, and swinging a fist at random barked his knuckles against the mouth of the sergent. Momentarily that one relaxed his hold, and Lanyard struggled to his knees, only to go down as the indomitable Frenchman grappled yet a second time.
Now, however, as they fell, Lanyard was on top: and shifting both hands to his antagonist’s left forearm, he wrenched it up and around. There was a cry of pain, and he jumped clear of one no longer to be reckoned with.
Nevertheless, as he had feared, the delay had proved ruinous. He had only found his feet when an unidentified person hurled himself bodily through the gloom and wrapped his arms round Lanyard’s thighs. And as both went down, two others piled up on top.﻿ ﻿…
For the next minute or two, Lanyard fought blindly, madly, viciously, striking and kicking at random. For all that﻿—even with one sergent hors de combat﻿—they were three to one; and though with the ferocity of sheer desperation he shook them all off, at one time, and gained a few yards more, it was only again to be overcome and borne down, crushed beneath the weight of three.

===Chinese===
压着两百磅好勇斗狠的法国肉身，兰亚德自知大势已去，却仍苦战不休——只要一息尚存，便战到底。
他猛一挺劲，拧身一扭，从底下钻了出去，一拳胡乱抡去，指节磕在巡警嘴上。那一位的钳制霎时一松，兰亚德撑起身跪起，却又被那不折不服的法国人再度缠住，摁了下去。
不过这一回落倒，兰亚德反在上风：他双手齐出，扣住对头的左小臂，猛地向上、向后一拧。一声痛呼，他纵身跳开——那一位，已不足为虑了。
然而不出他所惧，这一耽搁误了大事。他刚刚立定脚跟，便有一个来历不明的人影从黑暗里整个人扑来，双臂死死箍住兰亚德的双腿。两人一齐栽倒，又有两个压了上来。……
接下来一两分钟，兰亚德打得全凭本能，疯狂而狠辣，拳脚没头没脑地乱抡。饶是如此——即便已有一名巡警丧失战斗力（hors de combat）——对方仍是三对一；虽然他凭一股绝望逼出来的凶悍，一度把他们尽数抖落，又多抢出几码地面，到头来还是被再度压倒制服，碾在三条大汉的分量底下。

===Original===
His wind was going, his strength was leaving him. He mustered up every ounce of energy, all his wit and courage, for one last effort: fought like a cat, tooth and nail; toiled once more to his knees, with two clinging to him like wolves to the flanks of a stag; shook one off, regained his feet, swayed; and in one final gust of ferocity dashed both fists repeatedly into the face of him who still clung to him.
That one was Popinot; he knew instinctively that this was so; and a grim joy filled him as he felt the man’s clutches relax and fall away, and guessed how brutal was the damage he had done that fat, evil face.
At length free, he made off, running, stumbling, reeling: gained the hall; flung open the door; and heedless of the picket who had fired on him from below the window, dashed down the steps and away.﻿ ﻿…
Three shots sped him through that intricate tangle of night-bound park. But all went wide; the pursuit﻿—what little there was﻿—blundered off at haphazard and lost itself, as well.
He came to the wall, crept along in shelter of its shadow until he found a tree with a low-swung branch that jutted out over the street, climbed this, edged out over the wall, and dropped to the sidewalk.

===Chinese===
他的气息渐竭，气力正一分分离他而去。他搜罗起每一分精力、全部的心智与胆气，作最后一搏：像猫一样张牙舞爪地死斗；重新苦挣着撑起膝盖，两条大汉仍扒在他身上，如群狼扑咬雄鹿的两胁；他甩脱一个，立起身来，晃了两晃；随即在一阵最后的凶狂里，双拳连环，狠狠捣进仍死缠着他的那人的脸。
那人正是波皮诺；他本能地知道就是他；待觉出那人抓挠的手劲松开、滑落，猜到自己把那张肥硕的恶脸毁伤得何等不留情面时，一阵阴沉沉的快意涌上了他的心头。
终于脱得身的他拔腿便走，跑着、绊着、踉跄着：抢到门厅；撞开大门；全然不顾窗外下方曾朝他开枪的那名哨警，一溜烟冲下台阶，逃之夭夭。……
三发子弹送他穿过夜色笼罩、盘曲错结的园林。但全都落了空；追兵——本就寥寥——也胡乱扑了几个方向，自家散了，也没了踪影。
他摸到围墙，贴着墙影的遮蔽匍匐前行，寻到一株枝桠低垂、斜伸出街面上空的树，爬了上去，缘枝挪过墙头，落到了人行道上。

===Original===
A shout from the quarter of the carriage gates greeted his appearance. He turned and ran again. Flying footsteps for a time pursued him; and once, with a sinking heart, he heard the rumble of a motor. But he recovered quickly, regained his wind, and ran well, with long, steady, ground-consuming strides; and he doubled, turned and twisted in a manner to wake the envy of the most subtle fox.
In time he felt warranted in slowing down to a rapid walk.
Weariness was now a heavy burden upon him, and his spirit numb with desperate need of rest; but his pace did not flag, nor his purpose falter from its goal.
It was a long walk if a direct one to which he set himself as soon as confident the pursuit had failed once more. He plodded on, without faltering, to the one place where he might feel sure of finding his beloved, if she lived and were free. He knew that she had not forgotten, and in his heart he knew that she would never again of her own will fail him.﻿ ﻿…
Nor had she: when﻿—weary and spent from that heartbreaking climb up the merciless acclivity of the Butte Montmartre﻿—he staggered rather than walked past the sleepy verger and found his way through the crowding shadows to the softly luminous heart of the basilica of the Sacré-Cœur, he found her there, kneeling, her head bowed upon hands resting on the back of the chair before her: a slight and timid figure, lost and lonely in the long ranks of empty chairs that filled the nave.

===Chinese===
马车大门那一头一声呼喝，迎了他的露面。他转身再跑。飞奔的脚步声追了他一阵；有一回，他还心口一沉，听见了汽车的轰鸣。但他很快缓过劲来，匀回了气息，跑得极好，步幅绵长、沉稳、步步吃地；一路折返、转弯、绕行，那份刁钻，足以教最狡黠的狐狸也要艳羡。
到后来，他自觉尽可以把速度放缓成疾行了。
疲惫此刻已如重负压身，他的心神也因极度的休息之需而麻木了；但他的步子不曾懈怠，他的目标也不曾动摇。
待确信追兵又一次扑空，他便认定一条虽远却直的路线，苦行赶去。他一步不歇地跋涉，奔向唯一一处所在——只要她还活着、还自由，他便笃定能在那里寻见他的心上人。他知道她没有忘；他在心底里知道，从今往后，她再不会出于本心弃他于不顾了。……
她也果然没有忘：当他——熬过那阵令人心碎的攀爬、翻上蒙马特高地（Butte Montmartre）不留情面的陡坡，已累得形销骨立——半是踉跄半是挪步地行过睡眼惺忪的教堂司事（verger），穿过攒聚的暗影，摸进圣心堂（Sacré-Cœur）大殿那柔光盈盈的深处时，他看见她就在那里，跪着，头俯在双手上，双手搭在她面前那张椅子的椅背上：一个纤细怯弱的身影，在中殿一排排的空椅之间，孤单而无所依。

===Original===
Slowly, almost fearfully, he went to her, and silently he slipped into the chair by her side.
She knew, without looking up, that it was he.﻿ ﻿…
After a little her hand stole out, closed round his fingers, and drew him forward with a gentle, insistent pressure. He knelt then with her, hand in hand﻿—filled with the wonder of it, that he to whom religion had been nothing should have been brought to this by a woman’s hand.
He knelt for a long time, for many minutes, profoundly intrigued, his sombre gaze questioning the golden shadows and ancient mystery of the distant choir and shining altar: and there was no question in his heart but that, whatever should ensue of this, the unquiet spirit of the Lone Wolf was forevermore at rest.

===Chinese===
他缓缓地、几乎带着敬畏地走向她，又悄没声息地在她身旁的椅子上坐了下去。
她没有抬头，却知道是他。……
过了一小会儿，她的手悄悄探出，握拢他的手指，以一种温柔而不容分说的力道，把他向前引去。于是他随她一同跪下，手牵着手——满心惊叹于这一幕：宗教于他本是虚无，如今竟被一只女人的手，引到了这里。
他跪了许久，好几分钟，深深为之萦怀，忧郁的目光探询着远处唱诗班席与莹莹圣坛的金影与亘古奥秘：而他心中已别无疑问——无论此事日后如何演变，独狼那颗躁扰不宁的魂灵，从此永远安歇了。

## XVI Restitution / 十六、物归原主

===Original===
For all that success had rewarded his effrontery, Lanyard’s mind was far from easy during the subsequent hour that he spent before attempting to rejoin Lucy Shannon, dodging, ducking and doubling across Paris and back again, with design to confuse and confound any jackals of the Pack that might have picked up his trail as adventitiously as Ekstrom had.
His delight, indeed, in discomfiting his dupe was chilled by apprehension that it were madness, simply because the spy had proved unexpectedly docile, to consider the affaire Ekstrom closed. In the very fact of that docility inhered something strange and ominous, a premonition of evil which was hardly mitigated by finding the girl safe and sound under the wing of madame la concierge, in the little court of private stables, where he rented space for his car, off the rue des Acacias.
Monsieur le concierge, it appeared, was from home; and madame, thick-witted, warmhearted, simple body that she was, discovered a phase of beaming incuriosity most grateful to the adventurer, enabling him as it did to dispense with embarrassing explanations, and to whisk the girl away as soon as he liked.
This last was just as soon as personal examination had reassured him with respect to his automobile﻿—superficially an ordinary motor-cab of the better grade, but with an exceptionally powerful engine hidden beneath its hood. A car of such character, passing readily as the town-car of any family in modest circumstances, or else as what Paris calls a *voiture de remise* (a hackney car without taximeter) was a tremendous convenience, enabling its owner to scurry at will about cab-ridden Paris free of comment. But it could not be left standing in public places at odd hours, or for long, without attracting the interest of the police, and so was useless in the present emergency. Lanyard, however, entertained a shrewd suspicion that his plans might all miscarry and the command of a fast-travelling car soon prove essential to his salvation; and he cheerfully devoted a good half-hour to putting the motor in prime trim for the road.
With this accomplished﻿—and the facts established through discreet interrogation of madame la concierge that no enquiries had been made for “Pierre Lamier,” and that she had noticed no strange or otherwise questionable characters loitering in the neighbourhood of late﻿—he was ready for his first real step toward rehabilitation.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
虽说厚脸皮的招数得了手，兰亚德心头却远谈不上踏实。在设法与露西·香农重新会合之前的那一个钟头里，他东躲西闪、迂回折返，在巴黎穿来穿去又折将回来，一心要搅乱、甩脱「群狼」豢养的豺犬——万一它们也像埃克斯特罗姆那样，纯属偶然地嗅上了他的踪迹呢。
他固然为把耍弄的对象挫辱了一番而得意，这份得意却被一层忧惧浇凉：那名间谍竟出乎意料地俯首帖耳，倘若仅凭这一点便认定埃克斯特罗姆这桩公案（affaire Ekstrom）业已了结，那简直近于疯癫。恰恰在那份驯顺里，便内藏着某种古怪而不祥的东西，一种大祸将至的预感；及至发现姑娘在门房太太（madame la concierge）的羽翼之下安然无恙——就在阿卡西亚街（rue des Acacias）后身一方存放私家马车的偏院里，他租来搁他那辆汽车的地方——这预感也不过稍稍纾解。
看样子，门房先生（Monsieur le concierge）出门不在家；而门房太太这个人呢，脑筋迟钝、心肠火热、浑朴得可以，摆出一副笑吟吟的不闻不问，倒教这位冒险家感激不尽——如此一来，种种难以启齿的解释一概全免，他乐意多早把姑娘带走，便能多早带走。
而这「多早」，也须等他亲手验看过那辆汽车心里才有底——那车表面上是一辆上等的寻常出租汽车，引擎盖底下却暗藏一台马力非凡的发动机。这等品性的车子，轻而易举便能冒充小康之家的自备轿车，或是充作巴黎人所谓的 *voiture de remise*（不装计程器的出租马车），实在方便至极，任凭主人在马车横行的巴黎城里随意奔走，无人置喙。可它若在不三不四的钟点停驻公共场所，或停放长久，便免不了惹起警察的兴味，故而在眼下这场急变里全无用武之地。不过兰亚德肚里雪亮，暗自疑心他的计划未必不会尽数落空，届时一辆快车的驾驭之权，只怕便是保命的根本；于是他兴冲冲地花上足足半个钟头，把发动机调校到上路的第一等状态。
诸事办妥——又借着与门房太太不动声色的盘问，证实了既无人来打听过「皮埃尔·拉米埃」，近顷左近也不曾见什么生面孔或别的可疑角色逗留游荡——他便可以迈出改邪归正的头一步真棋了。……

===Original===
It was past one in the morning when, with the girl on his arm, he issued forth into the dark and drowsy rue des Acacias and, moving swiftly, crossed the avenue de la Grande Armée. Thereafter, avoiding main-travelled highways, they struck southward through tangled side streets to aristocratic Passy, skirted the boulevards of the fortifications, and approached the private park of La Muette.
The hôtel particulier of that wealthy and amiable eccentric, Madame Hélène Omber, was a souvenir of those days when Passy had been suburban. A survival of the Revolution, a vast, dour pile that had known few changes since the days of its construction, it occupied a large, unkempt park, irregularly triangular in shape, bounded by two streets and an avenue, and rendered private by high walls crowned with broken glass. Carriage gates opened on the avenue, guarded by a porter’s lodge; while of three posterns that pierced the walls on the side streets, one only was in general use by the servants of the establishment; the other two were presumed to be permanently sealed.
Lanyard, however, knew better.
When they had turned off from the avenue, he slackened pace and moved at caution, examining the prospect narrowly.
On the one hand rose the wall of the park, topped by naked, soughing limbs of neglected trees; on the other, across the way, a block of tall old dwellings, withdrawn behind jealous garden walls, showed stupid, sleepy faces and lightless eyes.

===Chinese===
凌晨一点已过，他挽着姑娘步出阿卡西亚街黑沉沉、睡意昏昏的街面，脚下加紧，横穿大军团大道（avenue de la Grande Armée）。此后避开大道通衢，折入盘根错节的小街一路向南，直抵气派雍容的帕西（Passy），又沿着防御工事的林荫大道外缘而行，渐渐逼近拉米埃特（La Muette）的私家园林。
那位豪富而和蔼的怪人——欧姆贝夫人埃莱娜（Madame Hélène Omber）——的私邸（hôtel particulier），是帕西尚属郊野的那些年月的遗物。它熬过大革命而幸存，一大座阴沉敦实的建筑，自落成以来几乎未曾改换面目，占据着一片荒芜失修的大园林。园子呈不规则的三角形，两面临街、一面傍道，高墙环抱，墙头插满碎玻璃，遂成与世隔绝之势。马车大门开在大道上，由一所门房小屋看守；而穿通侧街墙垣的三道便门，只有一道常供宅中仆役行走，其余两道据说早已永久封死。
然而兰亚德却深知底细。
一拐下大道，他便放缓脚步，蹑踪般谨慎前行，把四下景致细细打量。
一边是园林的高墙拔地而起，墙头探出无人料理的秃枝，在风里萧萧作响；另一边隔街相对，一排高峻的老宅深藏于悭吝的院墙之内，露出蠢笨困倦的面孔和不点灯的眼睛。

===Original===
Within the perspective of the street but three shapes stirred; Lanyard and the girl in the shadow of the wall, and a disconsolate, misprized cat that promptly decamped like a terror-stricken ghost.
Overhead the sky was breaking and showing ebon patches and infrequent stars through a wind-harried wrack of cloud. The night had grown sensibly colder, and noisy with the rushing sweep of a new-sprung wind.
Several yards from the postern-gate, Lanyard paused definitely, and spoke for the first time in many minutes; for the nature of their errand had oppressed the spirits of both and enjoined an unnatural silence, ever since their departure from the rue des Acacias.
“This is where we stop,” he said, with a jerk of his head toward the wall; “but it’s not too late﻿—”

===Chinese===
街景的纵深之内，会动的影子统共三个：墙荫里的兰亚德与姑娘，还有一只失意受冷落的猫，转眼便如吓破了胆的幽灵般逃遁而去。
头顶的夜空正在破绽百出，从被风驱赶的乱云破絮间露出墨黑的夜色与寥寥可数的星子。夜气分明冷了下来，新起的劲风呼啸扫荡，闹出一片声息。
离便门还有几步远，兰亚德断然收步，多少分钟以来头一回开口；原来此行的性质压着两人的心绪，自打离开阿卡西亚街，便叫他们守着一份反常的缄默。
「咱们就在这儿停。」他说，朝墙那边一扬头，「不过现在回头还来得及——」

===Original===
“For what?” the girl asked quickly.
“I promised you no danger; but now I’ve thought it over, I can’t promise that: there’s always danger. And I’m afraid for you. It’s not yet too late for you to turn back and wait for me in a safer place.”
“You asked me to accompany you for a special purpose,” she argued; “you begged me to come with you, in fact.﻿ ﻿… Now that I have agreed and come this far, I don’t mean to turn back without good reason.”
His gesture indicated uneasy acquiescence. “I should never have asked this of you. I think I must have been a little mad. If anything should come of this to injure you﻿ ﻿… !”

===Chinese===
「来得及做什么？」姑娘急急地问。
「我答应过你不会有危险；可如今细细盘算过，这话许不出了：危险总是有的。我替你担心。你现在转身回去，寻个稳妥些的地方等我，还不算晚。」
「你是为了一桩特殊的目的才请我同行的，」她争辩道，「说实在的，是你恳求我跟你来的。……如今我既已应承，又走到了这一步，没个充分的理由，我不打算回头。」
他做了个手势，表示忐忑不安的默认。「我本不该拿这个来求你。我想我准是有些疯了。倘若此事闹出什么乱子，叫你受了伤害……！」

===Original===
“If you mean to do what you promised﻿—”
“Do you doubt my sincerity?”
“It was your own suggestion that you leave me no excuse for doubt﻿ ﻿…”
Without further remonstrance, if with a mind beset with misgivings, he led on to the gate﻿—a blank door of wood, painted a dark green, deeply recessed in the wall.

===Chinese===
「只要你打算兑现你的承诺——」
「你疑心我的诚意？」
「是你自己说的，不给我留怀疑的借口……」
他不再多劝，心头纵然疑虑纷扰，仍领着她走向那道门——一扇光秃秃的木门，漆作深绿色，深深凹进墙体之内。

===Original===
In proof of his assertion that he had long since made every preparation to attack the premises, Lanyard had a key ready and in the lock almost before they reached it. And the door swung back easily and noiselessly as though on well-greased hinges. As silently it shut them in.
They stood upon a weed-grown gravel path, hedged about with thick masses of shrubbery; but the park was as black as a pocket; and the heavy effluvia of wet mould, decaying weeds and rotting leaves that choked the air, seemed only to render the murk still more opaque.
But Lanyard evidently knew his way blindfold: though motives of prudence made him refrain from using his flash-lamp, he betrayed not the least incertitude in his actions. Never once at loss for the right turning, he piloted the girl swiftly through a bewildering black labyrinth of paths, lawns and thickets.﻿ ﻿…
In due course he pulled up, and she discovered that they had come out upon a clear space of lawn, close beside the featureless, looming bulk of a dark and silent building.

===Chinese===
他早夸下海口，说进袭此宅的种种准备由来已久；这会儿钥匙在手，人还未到，锁孔里已先插进了钥匙，恰是明证。那门应手荡开，轻盈无声，仿佛铰链上足了油；又同样无声无息地，把两人关在了门内。
两人立在一条杂草滋蔓的砾石小径上，四围密匝匝围满灌丛；园中却黑得如同口袋底；湿土、腐草、烂叶的浊重气息壅塞在空气里，倒像把这团昏昧浸染得愈发不透。
但兰亚德分明闭着眼睛也认得路：尽管谨慎起见他按着不用手电，举手投足间却无半分游移。一次也不曾转错过弯，领着姑娘迅捷穿过小径、草坪与树丛织成的令人晕头转向的黑色迷宫。……
到得地头，他收住脚，她这才发觉两人已走上一片空旷的草坪，紧傍着一座黑黢黢、静悄悄的大宅，那建筑不见眉目，黑影迫人。

===Original===
An admonitory grasp tightened upon her fingers, and she caught his singularly penetrating yet guarded whisper:
“This is the back of the house﻿—the service-entrance. From this door a broad path runs straight to the main service gateway; you can’t mistake it; and the gate itself has a spring lock, easy enough to open from the inside. Remember this in event of trouble. We might become separated in the darkness and confusion.﻿ ﻿…”
Gently returning the pressure, “I understand,” she said in a whisper.
Immediately he drew her on to the house, pausing but momentarily before a wide doorway; one half of which promptly swung open, and as soon as they had passed through, closed with no perceptible jar or click. And then Lanyard’s flash-lamp was lancing the gloom on every hand, swiftly raking the bounds of a large, panelled servants’ hall, until it picked out the foot of a flight of steps at the farther end. To this they moved stealthily over a tiled flooring.

===Chinese===
他攥紧她的手指以示告诫，她听得他那格外穿透人心却又按捺着的低语：
「这是宅子的背面——仆役进出的门。从这道门起，一条宽路笔直通到仆役总门；你不会认错的；那门上装的是弹簧锁，从里面开起来容易得很。遇上乱子务必记着这条。黑暗慌乱之中，咱们俩兴许会走散。……」
她轻轻回握。「我记住了。」她低声说。
随即他领她走向宅子，在一道宽阔的门洞前只略停一停；门洞的一扇应声荡开，两人一过便即阖拢，不闻半点磕碰或咔嗒之声。接着兰亚德的手电便如长枪般刺向四下的黑暗，迅速扫过一间镶板护壁的大仆役厅的四界，末了照定远端一段楼梯的梯脚。两人便蹑着脚、踏着瓷砖地面，向那边挪去。

===Original===
The ascent of the staircase was accomplished, however, only with infinite care, Lanyard testing each rise before trusting it with his weight or the girl’s. Twice he bade her skip one step lest the complaints of the ancient woodwork betray them. In spite of all this, no less than three hideous squeals were evoked before they gained the top; each indicating a pause and wait of several breathless seconds.
But it would seem that such servants as had been left in the house, in the absence of its chatelaine, either slept soundly or were accustomed to the midnight concert of those age-old timbers; and without mischance, at length, they entered the main reception-hall, revealed by the dancing spotlight as a room of noble proportions furnished with sombre magnificence.
Here the girl was left alone for a few minutes, while Lanyard darted above-stairs for a review of the state bedchambers and servants’ quarters.
With a sensation of being crushed and suffocated by the encompassing dark mystery, she nerved herself against a protracted vigil. The obscurity on every hand seemed alive with stealthy footfalls, whisperings, murmurings, the passage of shrouded shapes of silence and of menace. Her eyes ached, her throat and temples throbbed, her skin crept, her scalp tingled. She seemed to hear a thousand different noises of alarm. The only sounds she did not hear were those﻿—if any﻿—that accompanied Lanyard’s departure and return. Had he not been thoughtful enough, when a few feet distant, to give warning with the light, she might well have greeted with a cry of fright the consciousness of a presence near her: so silently he moved about. As it was, she was startled, apprehensive of some misadventure, to find him back so soon; for he hadn’t been gone three minutes.

===Chinese===
然而上楼这趟路，也上得万般仔细，每一级梯级都须兰亚德先试探过，才敢把自己或姑娘的分量托付上去。有两回他叫她跨过一级，免得老木料的呻吟出卖了他们。饶是如此，登顶之前还是先后激出三声骇人的锐啸；每一声过后，都得屏息敛气地干等上好几秒。
但看来女主人不在时留守宅中的那些仆役，不是睡得死沉，便是听惯了这副老骨架子半夜开的音乐会；两人终得安然无恙，踏进了正客厅——手电光斑游走之下，显出一间比例恢宏的屋子，陈设富丽而色调幽暗。
姑娘被独自留在厅中几分钟，兰亚德则飞身上楼，把正房卧寝与仆役下处巡查了一遍。
四围黑暗的谜团压着她、挤着她，她只觉窒闷欲绝，便强自振作，预备熬一场漫长的守夜。每一面的昏暗里似乎都游动着鬼祟的脚步、喁喁的私语、嗡嗡的低响，裹殓布似的身影挟着无声与凶险往来穿行。她眼睛发痛，喉咙和太阳穴突突直跳，皮肤发麻，头皮发紧。她仿佛听见一千种报惊的声响。唯独兰亚德来去时那些声息——若当真有过——她一概不曾听见。亏得他心思周密，相距几步便先用手电打个照会，否则蓦然觉出身旁有人，她多半要惊叫出声：他行动便是这般悄无声息。饶是如此，见他这么快便折返，她仍吃了一惊，只怕出了什么意外——他去了还不到三分钟。

===Original===
“It’s quite all right,” he announced in hushed accents﻿—no longer whispering. “There are just five people in the house aside from ourselves﻿—all servants, asleep in the rear wing. We’ve got a clear field﻿—if no excuse for taking foolish chances! However, we’ll be finished and off again in less than ten minutes. This way.”
That way led to a huge and gloomy library at one extreme of a chain of great salons, a veritable treasure-gallery of exquisite furnishings and authentic old masters. As they moved slowly through these chambers Lanyard kept his flash-lamp busy; involuntarily, now and again, he checked the girl before some splendid canvas or extraordinary antique.
“I’ve always meant to happen in some day with a moving-van and loot this place properly!” he confessed with a little affected sigh. “Considered from the viewpoint of an expert practitioner in my﻿—ah﻿—late profession, it’s a sin and a shame to let all this go neglected, when it’s so poorly guarded. The old lady﻿—Madame Omber, you know﻿—has all the money there is, approximately, and when she dies all these beautiful things go to the Louvre; for she’s without kith or kin.”

===Chinese===
「万事无碍。」他压着嗓音宣布——已不必再耳语。「除了咱们俩，宅里统共五口人——全是仆役，睡在后楼。眼前是一片空场——可也没理由去冒糊涂险！不管怎样，十分钟以内咱们办完便走。这边来。」
那一边通向一间硕大而幽暗的书房，坐镇在一串大客厅的尽头；这一串厅堂实打实是座珍宝馆，满陈精美家具与真迹名画。两人缓缓穿行其间，兰亚德的手电一路忙碌；他不时身不由己，在某幅华彩夺目的油画或某件出奇的古董跟前顿住姑娘的脚步。
「我一向盘算着，哪天带着一辆搬家卡车撞进门来，把这份家当像模像样地洗劫一票！」他招认道，还装模作样地小小叹了口气。「按我这——啊——先前行当里行家里手的眼光来看，守卫得这般松懈，却听任这满堂珍宝无人问津，简直是罪过，是造孽。这位老太太——你知道的，欧姆贝夫人——普天下的钱财差不多都归她了；等她一咽气，这些美妙物件全数进卢浮宫；她无亲无故。」

===Original===
“But how did she manage to accumulate them all?” the girl wondered.
“It’s the work of generations of passionate collectors,” he explained. “The late Monsieur Omber was the last of his dynasty; he and his forebears brought together the paintings and the furniture; madame added the Orientals gathered together by her first husband, and her own collection of antique jewellery and precious stones﻿—*her* particular fad.﻿ ﻿…”
As he spoke the light of the flash-lamp was blotted out. An instant later the girl heard a little clashing noise, of curtain rings sliding along a pole; and this was thrice repeated. Then, following another brief pause, a switch clicked; and streaming from the hood of a portable desk-lamp, a pool of light flooded the heart of a vast place of shadows, an apartment whose doors and windows alike were cloaked with heavy draperies that hung from floor to ceiling in long and shining folds. Immense black bookcases lined the walls, their shelves crowded with volumes in rich bindings; from their tops pallid marble masks peered down inquisitively, leering and scowling at the intruders. A huge mantelpiece of carved marble, supporting a great, dark mirror, occupied the best of one wall, beneath it a wide, deep fireplace yawned, partly shielded by a screen of wrought brass and crystal. In the middle of the room stood a library table of mahogany; huge leather chairs and couches encumbered the remainder of its space. And the corner to the right of the fireplace was shut off by a high Japanese screen of cinnabar and gold.

===Chinese===
「可她是怎么攒得起这许多的呢？」姑娘纳罕。
「这是好几代痴心收藏家的心血。」他解说道。「已故的欧姆贝先生是他那一族的殿军；油画和家具，是他偕列祖列宗聚拢起来的；夫人又添上前夫——就是那位欧姆贝先生——汇集的一批东方珍玩，外加她自己收藏的古董首饰与宝石——那是*她*独有的癖好。……」
说话间，手电的光亮倏然被掩灭。转眼姑娘听见一阵轻微的磕碰声，是帘环顺杆滑动的声响；接连响了三回。随后又略停一停，开关咔嗒一声；一盏便携台灯的灯罩下顿时流光泻地，一泓亮色漫过这片浩大幽暗之地的中心。这是一间门窗一概掩着重帏的屋子，帷幔自天花板直垂到地，褶裥修长，泛着幽光。漆黑的巨型书柜沿墙列立，架上挤满装帧华贵的卷帙；柜顶一列苍白的大理石面具探头探脑地俯瞰，冲着这两位不速之客挤眉弄眼、怒目相向。一座雕花大理石的巨型壁炉，托着一面幽深的大镜子，占尽一面好墙；炉前敞着又宽又深的火膛，半掩着一架锤揲黄铜与水晶的屏风。屋子正中立一张红木大书桌；硕大的皮面圈椅与长榻，充塞着其余的空当。壁炉右侧的墙角，则教一架高大的日本朱砂描金屏风隔断。

===Original===
To this Lanyard moved confidently, carrying the lamp. Placing it on the floor, he grasped one wing of the screen with both hands, and at cost of considerable effort swung it aside, uncovering the face of a huge, old-style safe built into the wall.
For several seconds﻿—but not for many﻿—Lanyard studied this problem intently, standing quite motionless, his head lowered and thrust forward, hands resting on his hips. Then turning, he nodded an invitation to draw nearer.
“My last job,” he said with a smile oddly lighted by the lamp at his feet﻿—“and my easiest, I fancy. Sorry, too, for I’d rather have liked to show off a bit. But this old-fashioned tin bank gives no excuse for spectacular methods!”
“But,” the girl objected, “You’ve brought no tools!”

===Chinese===
兰亚德端着台灯，胸有成竹地径向那屏风走去。他把灯搁在地上，双手扣住屏风的一扇，费了好一番气力方才旋开去，露出嵌在墙体里的一座老式大保险柜的正面。
有好几秒钟——不过也就几秒——兰亚德全神贯注地端详这道难题，人纹丝不动，头颅低垂前探，双手叉腰。随后他转过身，点头示意她走近些。
「我的收山之作，」他说，脚下灯盏把他的笑容映照得颇为古怪——「大概也是我顶轻松的一票。遗憾也是真遗憾，我本倒想趁机卖弄一手。可这老掉牙的铁皮柜子，可容不得人摆什么花里胡哨的排场！」
「可是，」姑娘提出异议，「你没带工具！」

===Original===
“Oh, but I have!” And fumbling in a pocket, Lanyard produced a pencil. “Behold!” he laughed, brandishing it.
She knitted thoughtful brows: “I don’t understand.”
“All I need﻿—except this.”
Crossing to the desk, he found a sheet of notepaper and, folding it, returned.

===Chinese===
「哦，带了！」兰亚德往口袋里一摸，掏出一支铅笔。「瞧！」他笑着，挥了它一挥。
她蹙眉思索：「我不明白。」
「我要的就这个——只还缺一样。」
他走到书桌跟前，寻出一张信笺，折了折，又踅了回来。

===Original===
“Now,” he said, “give me five minutes.﻿ ﻿…”
Kneeling, he gave the combination-knob a smart preliminary twirl, then rested a shoulder against the sheet of painted iron, his cheek to its smooth, cold cheek, his ear close beside the dial; and with the practised fingers of a master locksmith began to manipulate the knob.
Gently, tirelessly, to and fro he twisted, turned, raced, and checked the combination, caressing it, humouring it, wheedling it, inexorably questioning it in the dumb language his fingers spoke so deftly. And in his ear the click and whir and thump of shifting wards and tumblers murmured articulate response in the terms of their cryptic code.
Now and again, releasing the knob and sitting back on his heels, he would bend intent scrutiny to the dial; note the position of the combination, and with the pencil jot memoranda on the paper. This happened perhaps a dozen times, at intervals of irregular duration.

===Chinese===
「现在，」他说，「给我五分钟。……」
他跪下去，先把对号旋钮利落地空转了一圈，随即将一侧肩膀抵住那幅漆铁门板，脸颊贴上它光滑冰冷的「脸颊」，耳朵凑在字盘近旁；以开锁巨匠练熟的手指，开始捻弄那旋钮。
他温存地、不知疲倦地拧来转去、疾旋骤停地摆布着那组密码，抚慰它，迁就它，哄诱它，用手指说得那般灵巧的一门哑语，向它发出不容闪避的盘问。锁内锁舌与转轮挪移的咔嗒、嗡鸣与闷响，便在他耳中以那套隐秘电码的辞令，做出字字分明的应答。
他不时松开旋钮，坐回脚跟，凝神细审字盘；记下密码的位置，用铅笔在纸上速记要目。如此这般约摸十来个来回，间隔时长时短。

===Original===
He worked diligently, in a phase of concentration that apparently excluded from his consciousness the near proximity of the girl, who stood﻿—or rather stooped, half-kneeling﻿—less than a pace from his shoulder, watching the process with interest hardly less keen than his own.
Yet when one faint, odd sound broke the slumberous silence of the salons, instantly he swung around and stood erect in a single movement, gaze to the curtains.
But it had only been a premonitory rumble in the throat of a tall old clock about to strike in the room beyond. And as its sonorous chimes heralded two deep-toned strokes, Lanyard laughed quietly, intimately, to the girl’s startled eyes, and sank back before the safe.
And now his task was nearly finished. Within another minute he sat back with face aglow, uttered a hushed exclamation of satisfaction, studied his memoranda for a space, then swiftly and with assured movements threw the knob and dial into the several positions of the combination, grasped the lever-handle, turned it smartly, and swung the door wide open.

===Chinese===
他干得勤谨，那副专注简直把近在肘边的姑娘从意识里排摈了出去；她伫立着——不如说弓着身、半跪半立——离他肩头不足一步，看这过程看得入神，兴致之浓不输他本人多少。
然而一声微弱而古怪的声响打破厅堂酣沉的寂静，他霎时回身，一个动作便站得笔直，目光直射帷幔。
原来不过是邻室里一座高脚老钟临敲之前喉头的一声闷鸣。待它洪亮的乐音报出两记低沉的钟声，兰亚德迎着姑娘惊惶的眼睛，轻轻地、贴心地笑了，重又在保险柜前俯下身去。
此刻他的活计已近告成。不出一分钟，他坐直身子，满面生光，压着嗓子满意地嘘了一声，把速记端详片刻，随即手上迅捷而有把握，将旋钮与字盘逐一拧到密码的各个刻位，握住扳手，利落一转，将柜门豁然敞开。

===Original===
“Simple, eh?” he chuckled, with a glance aside to the girl’s eager face, bewitchingly flushed and shadowed by the lamp’s up-thrown glow﻿—“when one knows the trick, of course! And now﻿ ﻿… if one were not an honest man!”
A wave of his hand indicated the pigeonholes with which the body of the safe was fitted: wide spaces and deep, stored tight with an extraordinary array of leather jewel-cases, packets of stout paper bound with tape and sealed, and boxes of wood and pasteboard of every shape and size.
“They were only her finest pieces, her personal jewels, that Madame Omber took with her to England,” he explained; “she’s mad about them﻿ ﻿… never separated from them.﻿ ﻿… Perhaps the finest collection in the world, for size and purity of water.﻿ ﻿… She had the heart to leave these﻿—all this!”
Lifting a hand he chose at random, dislodged two leather cases, placed them on the floor, and with a blade of his penknife forced their fastenings.

===Chinese===
「容易得很，是不是？」他轻声笑道，斜睨一眼姑娘那张急切的脸——灯上抛的光把它映得红晕灼灼、半明半昧——「当然，得摸着这门道才行！还有呢……倘若在下不是个正人君子！」
他一挥手，指指保险柜内一格一格的鸽笼架：又宽又深，密密实实填满一列可观的收藏——皮面首饰匣、缎带捆扎火漆封口的厚纸包，还有形形色色、大小不一的木匣纸盒。
「欧姆贝夫人随身带去英国的，不过是最精美的几件、她贴身的首饰，」他解说道，「她为这些简直入了魔……从不离身……论颗粒之巨、水色之纯，只怕是天下头一份的收藏……她竟狠得下心，撇下这些——整整这一柜！」
他抬起一只手，随手拣了两只皮匣取下，搁在地上，用铅笔刀的刀刃撬开了搭扣。

===Original===
From the first the light smote radiance in blinding, coruscant welter. Here was nothing but diamond jewellery, mostly in antique settings.
He took up a piece and offered it to the girl. She drew back her hand involuntarily.
“No!” she protested in a whisper of fright.
“But just look!” he urged. “There’s no danger﻿ ﻿… and you’ll never see the like of this again!”

===Chinese===
头一只匣子一开，灯光便劈进一片炫目翻腾的流光溢彩。里面清一色是钻石首饰，多半镶着古式托座。
他拈起一件，递向姑娘。她不由自主地缩回了手。
「不！」她惊惶地低声抗议。
「可你只管看呀！」他怂恿道。「没什么危险……这样的好东西，你今生今世再也见不着第二回！」

===Original===
Stubbornly she withheld her hand. “No, no!” she pleaded. “I﻿—I’d rather not touch it. Put it back. Let’s hurry. I﻿—I’m frightened.”
He shrugged and replaced the jewel; then yielded again to impulse of curiosity and lifted the lid of the second case.
It contained nothing but pieces set with coloured stones of the first order﻿—emeralds, amethysts, sapphires, rubies, topaz, garnets, lapis-lazuli, jacinthes, jades, fashioned by master-craftsmen into rings, bracelets, chains, brooches, lockets, necklaces, of exquisite design: the whole thrown heedlessly together, without order or care.
For a moment the adventurer stared down soberly at this priceless hoard, his eyes narrowing, his breathing perceptibly quickened. Then with a slow gesture, he reclosed the case, took from his pocket that other which he had brought from London, opened it, and held it aside beneath the light, for the girl’s inspection.

===Chinese===
她执拗地不肯伸手。「不，不！」她央告道。「我——我还是不碰的好。放回去吧。咱们快点。我——我害怕。」
他耸耸肩，把那件首饰放了回去；随即又拗不过好奇的冲动，揭开了第二只匣子的盖子。
匣中件件镶的都是头等彩宝——祖母绿、紫水晶、蓝宝石、红宝石、黄玉、石榴石、青金石、红锆石、玉件，经巨匠之手制成戒指、手镯、链子、胸针、坠盒、项链，设计精妙绝伦：而这一切被漫不经心地堆作一处，既无章法，也无人爱惜。
这位冒险家一时间神色凝重地俯视着这批无价之藏，双眼微眯，呼吸分明急促起来。而后他缓缓一个手势，重新扣上匣盖，从口袋里取出他自伦敦带来的那另一只匣子，打开来，移到灯下一旁，请姑娘过目。

===Original===
He looked not once either at its contents or at her, fearing lest his countenance betray the truth, that he had not yet succeeded completely in exorcising that mutinous and rebellious spirit, the Lone Wolf, from the tenement over which it had so long held sway; and content with the sound of her quick, startled sigh of amaze that what she now beheld could so marvellously outshine what had been disclosed by the other boxes, he withdrew it, shut it, found it a place in the safe, and without pause closed the door, shot the bolts, and twirled the dial until the tumblers fairly sang.
One final twist of the lever-handle convincing him that the combination was effectively dislocated, he rose, picked up the lamp, replaced it on the desk with scrupulous care to leave no sign that it had been moved, and looked round to the girl.
She was where he had left her, a small, tense, vibrant figure among the shadows, her eyes dark pools of wonder in a face of blazing pallor.

===Chinese===
他自始至终既不看那匣中之物一眼，也不看她一眼，唯恐面色泄露真相——那桀骜不驯、离经叛道的亡魂「独狼」，还不曾被他从这具被它长期霸占的躯壳里驱除净尽；听得她一声急促而讶异的轻叹——叹的是眼前之物竟这般奇绝，远胜先前诸匣所披露的一切——他便心满意足地收回匣子，阖上，在柜中给它觅了个安身之所，随即不停手地关上柜门，推上门闩，把字盘直拧得锁簧铮铮作响。
扳手上最后再一拧，确信密码已被彻底搅乱，他便起身，端起台灯，小心翼翼将它放回书桌上，不留半点挪动过的痕迹，然后回头望向姑娘。
她还在他留下她的原地，暗影里一个纤小、绷紧、微微颤动的身影，苍白的脸灼灼生光，一双眼睛是两泓惊异的深潭。

===Original===
With a high head and his shoulders well back he made a gesture signifying more eloquently than any words: “All that is ended!”
“And now﻿ ﻿… ?” she asked breathlessly.
“Now for our getaway,” he replied with assumed lightness. “Before dawn we must be out of Paris.﻿ ﻿… Two minutes, while I straighten this place up and leave it as I found it.”

===Chinese===
他昂着头，双肩后展，做了个手势，那意思比任何言语都说得透彻：「这一切都完了！」
「那么现在……？」她屏息问道。
「现在，开溜。」他故作轻快地答道。「天亮以前，咱们必须出巴黎。……给我两分钟，让我把这地方归置妥当，恢复我进门时的原样。」

===Original===
He moved back to the safe, restored the wing of the screen to the spot from which he had moved it, and after an instant’s close examination of the rug, began to explore his pockets.
“What are you looking for?” the girl enquired.
“My memoranda of the combination﻿—”
“I have it.” She indicated its place in a pocket of her coat. “You left it on the floor, and I was afraid you might forget﻿—”

===Chinese===
他回到保险柜跟前，把屏风那一扇旋回原位，又凑近地毯细察了片刻，便开始掏摸自己的口袋。
「你在找什么？」姑娘问。
「我记密码的纸——」
「在我这儿。」她指了指外衣口袋里它所在的方位。「你把它落在地上了，我怕你会忘记——」

===Original===
“No fear!” he laughed. “No”﻿—as she offered him the folded paper﻿—“keep it and destroy it, once we’re out of this. Now those portières﻿ ﻿…”
Extinguishing the desk-light, he turned attention to the draperies at doors and windows.﻿ ﻿…
Within five minutes, they were once more in the silent streets of Passy.

===Chinese===
「怕什么！」他笑道。「不用」——她把折好的纸递给他——「你留着，等咱们脱了身便毁掉它。现在来弄那些门帘（portières）……」
他熄掉台灯，转而去收拾门窗上的帷幔。……
不出五分钟，两人已重新走在帕西阒寂的街道上。

===Original===
They had to walk as far as the Trocadéro before Lanyard found a fiacre, which he later dismissed at the corner in the Faubourg St. Germain.
Another brief walk brought them to a gate in the garden wall of a residence at the junction of two quiet streets.
“This, I think, ends our Parisian wanderings,” Lanyard announced. “If you’ll be good enough to keep an eye out for busybodies﻿—and yourself as inconspicuous as possible in this doorway﻿ ﻿…”
And he walked back to the curb, measuring the wall with his eye.

===Chinese===
两人只得一路步行到特罗卡德罗，兰亚德才寻着一辆出租马车（fiacre）；后来车到圣日耳曼郊区（Faubourg St. Germain）一处街角，他便把它打发了。
再走短短一程，他们来到一座宅邸院墙上的门前，那宅子正坐落在两条僻静街道的交汇处。
「我想，咱们这趟巴黎的漂泊就到此为止了。」兰亚德宣布。「劳驾你替我盯住些好管闲事的人——你自己呢，尽量缩在这个门洞里别惹眼……」
说罢他踱回路缘，拿眼睛丈量那堵墙。

===Original===
“What are you going to do?”
He responded by doing it so swiftly that she gasped with surprise: pausing momentarily within a yard of the wall, he gathered himself together, shot lithely into the air, caught the top curbing with both hands, and﻿ ﻿…
She heard the soft thud of his feet on the earth of the enclosure; the latch grated behind her; the door opened.
“For the last time,” Lanyard laughed quietly, “permit me to invite you to break the law by committing an act of trespass!”

===Chinese===
「你要做什么？」
他以行动作答，快得让她倒抽一口凉气：在离墙不足一码处略一驻足，他把周身一束，轻捷地腾身而起，双手扣住墙头压顶石，随即……
她听见他的双脚落入院中泥土的轻响；身后门闩一声涩响；门开了。
「这是最后一回，」兰亚德悄声笑道，「请允许我邀您触犯一回法律，擅闯一次私地！」

===Original===
Securing the door, he led her to a garden bench secluded amid conventional shrubbery.
“If you’ll wait here,” he suggested﻿—“well, it will be best. I’ll be back as soon as possible, though I may be detained some time. Still, inasmuch as I’m about to break into this hôtel, my motives, which are most commendable, may be misinterpreted, and I’d rather you’d stop here, with the street at hand. If you hear a noise like trouble, you’ve only to unlatch the gate.﻿ ﻿… But let’s hope my purely benevolent intentions toward the French Republic won’t be misconstrued!”
“I’ll wait,” she assured him bravely; “but won’t you tell me﻿—?”
With a gesture, he indicated the mansion back of the garden.

===Chinese===
他闩牢门，领她到一张花园长椅上，那椅子掩在四平八稳的灌木丛中，颇为隐蔽。
「你要是肯在这儿等着，」他建议道，「呃，这样最稳妥。我尽快回来，不过兴许要耽搁些时候。话说回来，我既是要闯进这座宅邸（hôtel），我的动机虽然可敬可佩，也难免遭人曲解；我宁可你就守在这儿，临街在望。你要是听见什么声响像是出了乱子，只管拔开门闩就是。……不过但愿我对法兰西共和国这番纯粹与人为善的好意，不至于遭人误解！」
「我等着。」她壮着胆子向他担保，「可你愿不愿意告诉我——？」
他一抬手，指了指花园后头的那座公馆。

===Original===
“I’m going to break in there to pay an early morning call and impart some interesting information to a person of considerable consequence﻿—nobody less, in fact, than Monsieur Ducroy.”
“And who is that?”
“The present Minister of War.﻿ ﻿… We haven’t as yet the pleasure of each other’s acquaintance; still, I think he won’t be sorry to see me.﻿ ﻿… In brief, I mean to make him a present of the Huysman plans and bargain for our safe-conduct from France.”

===Chinese===
「我要闯进那儿，作一次凌晨的拜访，向一位举足轻重的大人物透露些有趣的消息——说实在的，不是别人，正是迪克罗先生（Monsieur Ducroy）。」
「那是何人？」
「现任的陆军部长。……咱们俩至今彼此无缘识荆；不过我想，他见了我不会不高兴。……简而言之，我打算把于伊斯曼图纸当份礼物奉送，再讲定条件，换咱们安全离境法兰西。」

===Original===
Impulsively she offered her hand and, when he, surprised, somewhat diffidently took it, “Be careful!” she whispered brokenly, her pale sweet face upturned to his. “Oh, do be careful! I am afraid for you.﻿ ﻿…”
And for a little the temptation to take her in his arms was stronger than any he had ever known.﻿ ﻿…
But remembering his stipulated year of probation, he released her hand with an incoherent mumble, turned, and disappeared in the direction of the house.

===Chinese===
她一时冲动，伸出了手；他又是意外、又有些赧然地握住了。「千万当心！」她仰起那张苍白甜美的脸，对他低语，声气断断续续。「哦，千万当心！我为你害怕。……」
而有那么一小会儿，把她拥入怀中的诱惑，比他此生领教过的任何诱惑都要来得强烈。……
但他记起自己许下的为期一年的考验，便含含糊糊咕哝了一句什么，松开她的手，转身朝宅子的方向去了。

## II Return / 二、归来

===Original===
