His return to Troyon’s, whereas an enterprise which Lanyard had been contemplating for several years﻿—in fact, ever since the death of Bourke﻿—came to pass at length almost purely as an affair of impulse.
He had come through from London by the afternoon service﻿—via Boulogne﻿—travelling light, with nothing but a brace of handbags and his life in his hands. Two coups to his credit since the previous midnight had made the shift advisable, though only one of them, the later, rendered it urgent.
Scotland Yard would, he reckoned, require at least twenty-four hours to unlimber for action on the Omber affair; but the other, the theft of the Huysman plans, though not consummated before noon, must have set the Chancelleries of at least three Powers by the ears before Lanyard was fairly entrained at Charing Cross.
Now his opinion of Scotland Yard was low; its emissaries must operate gingerly to keep within the laws they serve. But the agents of the various Continental secret services have a way of making their own laws as they go along: and for these Lanyard entertained a respect little short of profound.

===Chinese===
重返特鲁瓦旅馆这件事，虽说兰亚德已筹谋多年——其实打从伯克去世以来便一直存着这心思——到头来却几乎全然成于一时的冲动。
他乘午后的班船从伦敦取道布洛涅（Boulogne）一路过来，轻装简行，除了两只手提包，就只剩一条性命捏在手心里。前夜零点以来他已干成两票，这一趟挪窝固然是明智之举，但真正叫它刻不容缓的，还得数后一票。
他估摸着，苏格兰场要缓过劲来对欧姆贝案着手行动，至少得要二十四小时；可另一票——盗窃于伊斯曼图纸——虽说正午之前才得手，只怕不等兰亚德在查令十字（Charing Cross）车站安稳坐进车厢，至少三大强国的内阁就已经为之吵翻了天。
话说回来，他对苏格兰场的本事素来估价不高；那班人手下的密探行事必须战战兢兢，不敢越出其所奉法律半步。可欧陆各国特工机构的鹰犬却自有门道，走到哪儿就把法律改到哪儿：对这路人，兰亚德所怀的敬意几近于五体投地。

===Original===
He would not have been surprised had he ran foul of trouble on the pier at Folkestone. Boulogne, as well, figured in his imagination as a crucial point: its harbour lights, heaving up over the grim grey waste, peered through the deepening violet dusk to find him on the packet’s deck, responding to their curious stare with one no less insistently inquiring.﻿ ﻿… But it wasn’t until in the gauntlet of the Gare du Nord itself that he found anything to shy at.
Dropping from train to platform, he surrendered his luggage to a ready facteur, and followed the man through the crush, elbowed and shouldered, offended by the pervasive reek of chilled steam and coal-gas, and dazzled by the brilliant glare of the overhanging electric arcs.
Almost the first face he saw turned his way was that of Roddy.
The man from Scotland Yard was stationed at one side of the platform gates. Opposite him stood another known by sight to Lanyard﻿—a highly decorative official from the *Préfecture de Police*. Both were scanning narrowly every face in the tide that churned between them.
Wondering if through some fatal freak of fortuity these were acting under late telegraphic advice from London, Lanyard held himself well in hand: the first sign of intent to hinder him would prove the signal for a spectacular demonstration of the ungentle art of not getting caught with the goods on. And for twenty seconds, while the crowd milled slowly through the narrow exit, he was as near to betraying himself as he had ever been﻿—nearer, for he had marked down the point on Roddy’s jaw where his first blow would fall, and just where to plant a *coup-de-savate* most surely to incapacitate the minion of the Préfecture; and all the while was looking the two over with a manner of the most calm and impersonal curiosity.

===Chinese===
倘或在福克斯通（Folkestone）码头上撞出乱子，他绝不会感到意外。布洛涅在他想象中也算得一处要冲：海港的灯火在阴沉沉的灰色荒水上方翻涌而起，透过渐浓的紫暮窥探渡轮甲板上的他，而他对那好奇的凝望回敬以同样咄咄逼人的一瞥。……
跳下车厢踏上月台，他把行李交给一个殷勤候着的搬运工（facteur），随那人挤过人丛，一路挨胳膊肘、吃肩膀，让四下弥漫的冷蒸汽与煤气臭味熏得皱鼻，又叫头顶电弧灯刺目的强光照得眼花。
他抬眼望去，最先转向他的面孔之一，便是罗迪那张脸。
那名苏格兰场来客把守在月台栅门的一侧。他对面还立着一个兰亚德眼熟的人物——巴黎警察总局（*Préfecture de Police*）派来的一位仪表煌煌的官员。两人都在细细扫视当中翻腾而过的人潮里的每一张面孔。
他心里犯嘀咕：这两位莫非是奉了伦敦刚拍来的电令行事，命运营偏在此时开了个要命的玩笑？——但兰亚德把自己拿捏得牢牢的：只要对方稍露拦阻之意，便是信号，让他当场献演一场好戏，示范示范「人赃俱获之前如何脱身」这门不甚温柔的艺术。足足二十秒钟，趁着人潮在狭窄出口缓缓磨蹭的当口，他离自曝身份从未如此之近——比以往任何时候都更近，因为他已经在罗迪的下巴上标定了头一拳的落点，也盘算好了往哪儿一记法国式踢击（*coup-de-savate*）准能把警察总局那条爪牙踢趴下；而自始至终，他打量那两人的神色，始终是那种最平静、最不带私情的好奇。

===Original===
But beyond an almost imperceptible narrowing of Roddy’s eyes when they met his own, as if the Englishman were struggling with a faulty memory, neither police agent betrayed the least recognition.
And then Lanyard was outside the station, his facteur introducing him to a ramshackle taxicab.
No need to speculate whether or not Roddy were gazing after him; in the ragged animal who held the door while Lanyard fumbled for his facteur’s tip, he recognized a runner for the Préfecture; and beyond question there were many such about. If any lingering doubt should trouble Roddy’s mind he need only ask, “Such-and-such an one took what cab and for what destination?” to be instantly and accurately informed.
In such case to go directly to his apartment, that handy little *rez-de-chaussée* near the Trocadéro, was obviously inadvisable. Without apparent hesitation Lanyard directed the driver to the Hotel Lutetia, tossed the ragged spy a sou, and was off to the tune of a slammed door and a motor that sorely needed overhauling.﻿ ﻿…
The rain, which had welcomed the train a few miles from Paris, was in the city torrential. Few wayfarers braved the swimming sidewalks, and the little clusters of chairs and tables beneath permanent café awnings were one and all neglected. But in the roadways an amazing concourse of vehicles, mostly motor-driven, skimmed, skidded, and shot over burnished asphaltum; all, of course, at top-speed﻿—else this were not Paris. Lanyard thought of insects on the surface of some dark forest pool.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
然而，除了罗迪的目光与他相接时几乎不可察觉地眯了一眯——仿佛那英国人正跟失灵的记忆苦苦搏斗——两名警探谁都没有露出些微认出他的神气。
转眼兰亚德已出了车站，那搬运工把他引荐给一辆摇摇欲散架的出租车。
罗迪有没有在背后目送他，大可不必费神揣测；趁兰亚德摸摸索索给搬运工赏钱的工夫，那个衣衫褴褛、替他拉车门的家伙，他一眼便认出是警察总局的眼线；而且毫无疑问，此等角色四下里还多的是。罗迪心里若还有什么疑团化解不开，只消问一句「某位先生坐了哪辆车、去了哪个去处」，立刻便能得到分毫不差的回话。
这情形之下，径直回自己的住所——特罗卡德罗附近那套方便的底层小公寓（*rez-de-chaussée*）——显然是不智之举。兰亚德不见半分踌躇，吩咐司机开去吕特斯亚旅馆，丢给那褴褛密探一枚苏（sou），随即便在一声摔车门和一台亟须大修的马达轰响里绝尘而去。……
离巴黎还剩几英里便迎上火车的那场雨，进了城已下得瓢泼一般。行人少有敢蹚水过街的，咖啡馆常设凉棚底下一小簇一小簇的桌椅也一概无人问津。可大路上却涌动着惊人的一群车辆，大半是机动车，在油亮的柏油路面上掠过、打滑、飞窜；当然，一律全速——否则这就不是巴黎了。兰亚德想到了某个幽暗林间水塘水面上的虫豸。……

===Original===
The roof of the cab rang like a drumhead; the driver blinked through the back-splatter from his rubber apron; now and again the tyres lost grip on the treacherous going and provided instants of lively suspense. Lanyard lowered a window to release the musty odour peculiar to French taxis, got well peppered with moisture, and promptly put it up again. Then insensibly he relaxed, in the toils of memories roused by the reflection that this night fairly duplicated that which had welcomed him to Paris, twenty years ago.
It was then that, for the first time in several months, he thought definitely of Troyon’s.
And it was then that Chance ordained that his taxicab should skid. On the point of leaving the Ile de la Cité by way of the Pont St. Michel, it suddenly (one might pardonably have believed) went mad, darting crabwise from the middle of the road to the right-hand footway with evident design to climb the rail and make an end to everything in the Seine. The driver regained control barely in time to avert a tragedy, and had no more than accomplished this much when a bit of broken glass gutted one of the rear tyres, which promptly gave up the ghost with a roar like that of a lusty young cannon.
At this the driver (apparently a person of religious bias) said something heartfelt about the sacred name of his pipe and, crawling from under the apron, turned aft to assess damages.
On his own part Lanyard swore in sound Saxon, opened the door, and delivered himself to the pelting shower.

===Chinese===
车顶敲得像面鼓；司机隔着橡胶挡布回溅的水花直眨眼；轮胎不时在刁滑的路面上抓不住地，送来几阵惊心动魄的刺激。兰亚德摇落一扇车窗，放走法国出租车特有的霉味，却叫雨水兜头扑了个正着，赶紧又摇了上去。随后他不知不觉松弛下来，缠陷在回忆的罗网里——只因想到，今夜这一场，与二十年前迎接他来巴黎的那一夜竟如出一辙。
正是在那时，几个月来他头一回清清楚楚地想起了特鲁瓦旅馆。
也正是在那时，机缘注定他的出租车要打滑。车子正要取道圣米歇尔桥驶出西岱岛（Ile de la Cité），忽然——你完全可以情有可原地当它疯了——横着从路当中窜向右手边的人行道，摆明了要攀过栏杆，连人带车去塞纳河里一了百了。司机好不容易赶在惨剧酿成之前重新把住了车；这一步方才做完，一小片碎玻璃便豁开了后轮，那轮胎当即轰然断气，一声吼赛过一门血气方刚的小炮。
见此情形，司机（瞧那样子是个颇有宗教立场的人）就他那烟斗的神圣名号念叨了几句掏心窝子的话，随后从挡布底下爬出来，绕到车后估量损失。
兰亚德这边则用地道的撒克逊腔粗口咒骂了一声，推开车门，把自己交付给了倾盆骤雨。

===Original===
“Well?” he enquired after watching the driver muzzle the eviscerated tyre for some eloquent moments.
Turning up a distorted face, the other gesticulated with profane abandon, by way of good measure interpolating a few disconnected words and phrases. Lanyard gathered that this was the second accident of the same nature since noon, that the cab consequently lacked a spare tyre, and that short of a trip to the garage the accident was irremediable. So he said (intelligently) it couldn’t be helped, paid the man and over tipped precisely as though their journey had been successfully consummated, and standing over his luggage watched the maimed vehicle limp miserably off through the teeming mists.
Now in normal course his plight should have been relieved within two minutes. But it wasn’t. For some time all such taxis as did pass displayed scornfully inverted flags. Also, their drivers jeered in their pleasing Parisian way at the lonely outlander occupying a position of such uncommon distinction in the heart of the storm and the precise middle of the Pont St. Michel.
Over to the left, on the Quai de Marché Neuf, the façade of the Préfecture frowned portentously﻿—La Tour Pointue, as the Parisian loves to term it. Lanyard forgot his annoyance long enough to salute that grim pile with a mocking bow, thinking of the men therein who would give half their possessions to lay hands on him who was only a few hundred yards distant, marooned in the rain!﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
他瞧着司机与那条开膛破肚的轮胎纠缠了好几个意味深长的时刻，方才问道：「怎么样？」
那司机扭起一张走了形的脸，旁若无人地打着手势亵渎神明，还夹七夹八插上几个不成句的字眼凑趣。兰亚德大致听明白了：这是正午以来第二起同样性质的事故，车里因此没了备用轮胎，除非跑一趟修车行，这桩祸事便无从收拾。于是他（心领神会地）说了声没法子，照付车钱之外还多给了赏钱，俨然这趟行程已经功德圆满；随即站在行李旁，目送那辆伤残的车子凄凄惶惶、一步一拐地没入滂沱雨雾之中。
按说常理之下，他的窘境不出两分钟便该得到解救。可偏偏没有。有好一阵子，凡是路过的出租车，个个轻蔑地翻倒空车旗拒载。它们的司机还以巴黎人那讨喜的腔调，讥笑这个孤零零的外乡人：瞧他占着何等不同凡响的位置——暴风雨的正中心，圣米歇尔桥的正当中。
左手边，新市集滨河道上，警察总局的立面正不祥地沉着脸——巴黎人爱管它叫「尖塔」（La Tour Pointue）。兰亚德一时忘了恼怒，朝那座阴森的大楼行了个揶揄的鞠躬，心里想着楼中那些人：只要能伸手擒住他这个近在几百码外、困陷雨中的人，他们肯舍出一半家私！……

===Original===
In its own good time a night-prowling fiacre ambled up and veered over to his hail. He viewed this stroke of good-fortune with intense disgust: the shambling, weather-beaten animal between the shafts promised a long, damp crawl to the Lutetia.
And on this reflection he yielded to impulse.
Heaving in his luggage﻿—“Troyon’s!” he told the cocher.﻿ ﻿…
The fiacre lumbered off into that dark maze of streets, narrow and tortuous, which backs up from the Seine to the Luxembourg, while its fare reflected that Fate had not served him so hardly after all: if Roddy had really been watching for him at the Gare du Nord, with a mind to follow and wait for his prey to make some incriminating move, this chance-contrived change of vehicles and destination would throw the detective off the scent and gain the adventurer, at worst, several hours’ leeway.
When at length his conveyance drew up at the historic corner, Lanyard alighting could have rubbed his eyes to see the windows of Troyon’s all bright with electric light.

===Chinese===
也不知磨到什么时候，一辆夜游的出租马车（fiacre）蹓跶过来，应着他的招呼拐到跟前。他望着这份好运却满心嫌恶：车辕之间那匹步履蹒跚、饱经风霜的牲口，预示着去吕特斯亚旅馆将是一段漫长而湿漉漉的爬行。
而恰恰是出于这番思量，他向冲动缴了械。
他把行李搬上车——「去特鲁瓦旅馆！」他吩咐车夫（cocher）。……
马车吱吱嘎嘎驶进那片黑黢黢的街巷迷宫——又窄又绕，从塞纳河一路铺排到卢森堡公园——车中人则在心里盘算：命运待他终究不算太苛。倘若罗迪当真在北站守着他，存心缀在后面、单等猎物露出什么自陷罪证的动静，那么这番因偶然而生的换车改道，就会甩脱那侦探的鼻子，退一万步讲，也能给这位冒险家挣来几个钟头的回旋余地。
待车子终于停在那个有来历的街角，兰亚德下车一看，险些要揉眼睛：特鲁瓦旅馆的一扇扇窗竟全都亮着电灯。

===Original===
Somehow, and most unreasonably, he had always believed the place would go to the hands of the house-wrecker unchanged.
A smart portier ducked out, seized his luggage, and offered an umbrella. Lanyard composed his features to immobility as he entered the hotel, of no mind to let the least flicker of recognition be detected in his eyes when they should re-encounter familiar faces.
And this was quite as well: for﻿—again﻿—the first he saw was Roddy.

===Chinese===
不知怎么，他又毫无道理地一向认定，这地方会原封不动地一直落到拆房队手里。
一个利落的门房（portier）钻出来，一把抢过行李，还奉上一把伞。兰亚德走进旅馆时把一脸神色摆得纹丝不动，打定主意：待会儿再照见那些熟面孔时，绝不让眼里闪出半点认出来的微光。
而他这般谨慎也确属应当：因为——又是这样——他头一眼望见的，正是罗迪。

## XIV Rive Droit / 十四、右岸

===Original===
Falling without presage upon the slumberous hush enveloping the little house marooned in that dead backwater of Paris, the shock of that alarm drove the girl back from the table to the nearest wall, and for a moment held her there, transfixed in panic.
To the wide, staring eyes that questioned his so urgently, Lanyard promptly nodded grave reassurance. He hadn’t stirred since his first, involuntary and almost imperceptible start, and before the last fragment of splintered glass had tinkled on the floor above, he was calming her in the most matter-of-fact manner.
“Don’t be alarmed,” he said. “It’s nothing﻿—merely Solon’s skylight gone smash!”
“You call that nothing!” she cried gustily. “What caused it, then?”
“My negligence,” he admitted gloomily. “I might have known that wide spread of glass with the studio electrics on, full-blaze, would give the show away completely. The house is known to be unoccupied; and it wasn’t to be expected that both the police and Popinot’s crew would overlook so shining a mark.﻿ ﻿… And it’s all my fault, my oversight: I should have thought of it before.﻿ ﻿… High time I was quitting a game I’ve no longer the wit to play by the rules!”
“But the police would never﻿ ﻿… !”

===Chinese===
那阵声响毫无先兆，劈落在沉沉欲睡般笼罩着小宅的寂静上——这座小屋孤零零陷在巴黎那片死水般的僻壤里；这一惊把姑娘从桌边直逼到最近的墙跟前，有一忽儿将她钉在原地，惶惶然不能自已。
对那双睁得溜圆、急切地质问着他的眼睛，兰亚德当即点头，郑重地递去安神的保证。打从最初那一记不由自主、几乎无从察觉的惊跳起，他就再没有动过；而楼上最后一片迸碎的玻璃还未叮的一声落地，他已经在用最寻常不过的口气安抚她了。
「别惊慌，」他说，「没事——不过是梭伦家的天窗砸了个粉碎！」
「这还叫没事！」她一口气嚷道，「那是什么弄出来的？」
「怪我疏忽，」他阴沉沉地认了，「我本该想到，画室的电灯大放光明，配着那一大片玻璃，非把这出戏彻底抖搂出去不可。谁都知道这宅子没住人；警察和波皮诺那一伙，谁都不可能放过这么一块亮晃晃的靶子。……全是我之过，是我失察：早该想到这一层。……也真到了我该退场的时候——这场游戏，我已没了那份按规矩玩下去的头脑了！」
「可警察总决不会……！」

===Original===
“Certainly not. This is Popinot’s gentle method of letting us know he’s on the job. But I’ll just have a look, to make sure.﻿ ﻿… No: stop where you are, please. I’d rather go alone.”
He swung alertly through to the hall window, pausing there only long enough for an instantaneous glance through the draperies﻿—a fugitive survey that discovered the impasse Stanislas no more abandoned to the wind and rain, but tenanted visibly by one at least who lounged beneath the lonely lamppost, a shoulder against it: a featureless civilian silhouette with attention fixed to the little house.
But Lanyard didn’t doubt this one had a dozen fellows stationed within call.﻿ ﻿…
Springing up the stairs, he paused prudently at the topmost step, one quick glance showing him the huge rent gaping black in the skylight, the second the missile of destruction lying amid a litter of broken glass﻿—a brick wrapped in newspaper, by the look of it.
Swooping forward, he retrieved this, darted back from the exposed space beneath the shattered skylight, and had no more than cleared the threshold than a second something fell through the gap and buried itself in the parquetry. This was a bullet fired from the roof of one of the adjoining buildings: confirming his prior reasoning that the first missile must have fallen from a height, rather than have been thrown up from the street, to have wrought such destruction with those tough, thick panes of clouded glass.﻿ ﻿…
Swearing softly to himself, he descended to the kitchen.

===Chinese===
「当然决不会。这是波皮诺的温文手腕，好教我们知道他已经上工了。不过我还是要去看一眼，看个确实。……不：请你待在原地别动。我宁可一个人去。」
他一个利落的转身穿到门厅窗前，只停留了够看一眼的工夫，透过帘幔电光石火地一瞥——这一瞥逃犯式的侦察发现，斯坦尼斯拉斯死巷已不再任凭风雨独占，分明住进了至少一个人：那人闲散地倚在那盏孤零零的街灯柱下，一肩抵着灯柱——一个眉目莫辨的便装人影，全部注意力钉在这座小宅上。
不过兰亚德毫不怀疑，这一个身后还埋伏着一打同伙，个个驻在呼之即应的距离之内。……
他几步蹿上楼梯，在顶上一级台阶前审慎地收住脚：一眼望去，天窗上豁开一道黑洞洞的大口子；第二眼便看见那件肇祸的飞弹卧在一摊碎玻璃中间——看那模样，是一块裹着报纸的砖头。
他抢步上前将它拾起，闪身退出破天窗底下那片暴露的地界；前脚刚跨出门槛，第二件什么东西便穿过豁口坠了下来，一头扎进拼花地板。那是一颗子弹，从邻接的哪幢楼房屋顶上射下来的：这也坐实了他先前的推想——头一件飞弹必定从高处坠落，而非自街头抛上，否则那些又韧又厚的毛玻璃板，断砸不出这等残破。……
他低低咒骂了自己一句，下楼去了厨房。

===Original===
“As I thought,” he said coolly, exhibiting his find. “They’re on the roof of the next house﻿—though they’ve posted a sentry in the street, of course.”
“But that second thump﻿—?” the girl demanded.
“A bullet,” he said, placing the bundle on the table and cutting the string that bound it: “they were on the quivive and fired when I showed myself beneath the skylight.”
“But I heard no report,” she objected.
“A Maxim silencer on the gun, I fancy,” he explained, unwrapping the brick and smoothing out the newspaper.﻿ ﻿… “Glad you thought to put on your hat before you came down,” he added, with an approving glance for the girl; “it won’t be safe to go up to the studio again﻿—of course.”
His nonchalance was far less real than it seemed, but helped to steady one who was holding herself together with a struggle, on the verge of nervous collapse.

===Chinese===
「不出我所料，」他把捡来的东西一亮，口吻凉凉地说，「他们上了隔壁房子的屋顶——当然，街上也照例放了个哨。」
「可那第二记闷响是——？」姑娘追问。
「一颗子弹，」他说着把那卷东西放到桌上，割断捆着它的麻绳，「他们正提着神戒备，我一在天窗底下露面就开了枪。」
「可我没听见枪响啊，」她不服道。
「枪上想必装了马克西姆消音器（Maxim silencer），」他解释着，一面解开砖上的报纸，把它抚平。……「幸亏你想得周到，下来之前戴上了帽子，」他朝姑娘投去赞许的一瞥，又添上一句，「再要上画室去，可就不安全了——自然。」
他这份满不在乎，远不像表面上那么真切；可是对一个正苦苦撑持自己、站在神经崩溃边缘的人，它终究是一剂镇定的良药。

===Original===
“But what are we to do now?” she stammered. “If they’ve surrounded the house﻿—!”
“Don’t worry: there’s more than one way out,” he responded, frowning at the newspaper; “I wouldn’t have picked this place out, otherwise. Nor would Solon have rented it in the first instance had it lacked an emergency exit, in event of creditors.﻿ ﻿… Ah﻿—thought so!”
“What﻿—?”
“Troyon’s is gone,” he said, without looking up. “This is tonight’s *Presse*.﻿ ﻿… ‘*Totally destroyed by a fire which started at six-thirty this morning and in less than half an hour had reduced the ancient structure to a heap of smoking ashes*’!﻿ ﻿…” He ran his eye quickly down the column, selecting salient phrases: “ ‘*Believed to have been of incendiary origin though the premises were uninsured*’﻿—that’s an intelligent guess!﻿ ﻿… ‘*Narrow escape of guests in their*’ whatyemaycallems.﻿ ﻿… ‘*Three lives believed to have been lost﻿ ﻿… one body recovered charred almost beyond recognition*’﻿—but later identified as Roddy﻿—poor devil!﻿ ﻿… ‘*Two guests missing, Monsieur Lanyard, the well-known connoisseur of art, who occupied the room adjoining that of the unfortunate detective, and Mademoiselle Bannon, daughter of the American millionaire, who himself escaped only by a miracle with his secretary Monsieur Greggs, the latter being overcome by fumes*’﻿—what a shame!﻿ ﻿… ‘*Police and firemen searching the ruins*’﻿—hm-hm﻿—‘*extraordinary interest manifested by the Préfecture indicates a suspicion that the building may have been fired to conceal some crime of a political nature*.’ ”
Crushing the newspaper between his hands, he tossed it into a corner. “That’s all of importance. Thoughtful of Popinot to let me know, this way! The Préfecture, of course, is humming like a wasp’s-nest with the mystery of that telegram, signed with Roddy’s name and handed in at the Bourse an hour or so before he was ‘burned to death.’ Too bad I didn’t know then what I do now; if I’d even remotely suspected Greggs’ association with the Pack was via Bannon.﻿ ﻿… But what’s the use? I did my possible, knowing the odds were heavy against success.”
“What was written on the paper?” the girl demanded obliquely.

===Chinese===
「可我们现在怎么办？」她结结巴巴地问，「要是他们已经把宅子围死了——！」
「别担心：出去的路不止一条，」他皱着眉望着报纸答道，「要不然，当初我也不会挑中这地方。再说，这宅子若少了一条应急的退路，以备债主上门，梭伦当初也根本不会租它。……啊——果然如此！」
「什么——？」
「特鲁瓦旅馆没了，」他说，头也没抬。「这是今晚的《新闻报》（*Presse*）。……『*今晨六时三十分起火，不出半小时，已将这座古老建筑夷为一堆余烟未熄的灰烬，全然焚毁*』！……」他的眼睛顺着那一栏飞快扫下去，专拣扎眼的字句：「『*虽则店屋并未投保，仍疑纵火*』——这一猜倒聪明！……『*房客们穿着他们的那个——*』什么玩艺儿来着。……『*据信三人罹难……寻获焦尸一具，炭毁得几难辨认*』——不过事后认出是罗迪——可怜的家伙！……『*两名房客失踪：兰亚德先生，知名艺术鉴赏家，其客房毗邻那位不幸侦探的下榻处；及班农小姐，美国百万富翁之女；那位百万富翁本人偕秘书格雷格斯（Greggs）先生奇迹般脱身，后者被烟气熏倒*』——真是遗憾之至！……『*警察与消防员正在废墟中搜寻*』——嗯哼——『*警察总局对此表现出异乎寻常的关注，可见怀疑此楼纵火，或系为掩盖某种政治性罪行之举*』。」
他两手把报纸一攥，攥作一团，扔进了墙角。「要紧的就这么多。波皮诺这样递消息，可谓体贴入微！警察总局这会儿，自然正为那封电报之谜嗡嗡然如一窝被搅的马蜂——电报署着罗迪的名字，在他『葬身火海』之前约莫一个钟头，从交易所交进。可惜我当时不知道如今知道的这些；哪怕我稍稍疑心到一点，格雷格斯与群狼的勾结，竟是打班农这条线走的。……可是说它何用？我尽了力，也早知道胜算寥寥。」
「那纸上写的是什么？」姑娘拐着弯子追问。

===Original===
He made his eyes blank: “Written on the paper﻿—?”
“I saw something in red ink at the head of the column. You tried to hide it from me, but I saw.﻿ ﻿… What was it?”
“Oh﻿—that!” he laughed contemptuously: “just Popinot’s impudence﻿—an invitation to come out and be a good target.”
She shook her head impatiently: “You’re not telling me the truth. It was something else, or you wouldn’t have been so anxious to hide it.”
“Oh, but I assure you﻿—!”
“You can’t. Be honest with me, Mr. Lanyard. It was an offer to let you off if you’d give me up to Bannon﻿—wasn’t it?”

===Chinese===
他把眼睛装成一派茫然：「纸上写的——？」
「我看见那一栏顶上有红墨水写的字。你想瞒我，可我看见了。……写的是什么？」
「噢——那个呀！」他不屑地一笑，「不过是波皮诺的放肆——一封请帖，请我出去做一个好靶子。」
她不耐烦地摇头：「你没对我说实话。那是别的什么，不然你也不至于那样急着遮掩。」
「噢，可我向你保证——！」
「你保证不了。对我坦白吧，兰亚德先生。那是个条件——只要你把我交给班农，就放你走——对不对？」

===Original===
“Something like that,” he assented sheepishly﻿—“too absurd for consideration.﻿ ﻿… But now we’re due to clear out of this before they find a way in. Not that they’re likely to risk a raid until they’ve tried starving us out; but it would be as well to put a good distance between us before they find out we’ve decamped.”
He shrugged into his borrowed raincoat, buttoned it to his chin, and turned down the brim of his felt hat; but when he looked up at the girl again, he found she hadn’t moved; rather, she remained as one spellbound, staring less at than through him, her expression inscrutable.
“Well,” he ventured﻿—“if you’re quite ready, Miss Shannon﻿—?”
“Mr. Lanyard,” she demanded almost sharply﻿—“what was the full wording of that message?”
“If you must know﻿—”
“I must!”

===Chinese===
「差不多是那么回事，」他讪讪地应承，「荒唐得不值一驳。……不过趁他们还没摸着进来的门道，我们该趁早撤离此地了。倒不是说他们多半会冒险来硬攻——总得先试过把我们困死饿降；可在他们发觉我们已经遁走之前，还是先同他们拉开一段可观的路程为妙。」
他把借来的雨衣往肩上一耸披上，扣子一路扣到下巴，又把毡帽帽檐往下一捺；可待他再抬眼望那姑娘时，却发现她分毫未动，反倒像中了定身咒，与其说是在看他，不如说是望穿了他，一脸高深莫测。
「怎么样，」他试探道，「你要是全准备好了，香农小姐——？」
「兰亚德先生，」她几乎是疾言厉色地质问，「那封信的全文究竟是什么？」
「你一定要知道的话——」
「一定要！」

===Original===
He lifted a depreciative shoulder. “If you like, I’ll read it to you﻿—or, rather, translate it from the thieves’ argot Popinot complimented me by using.”
“Not necessary,” she said tersely. “I’ll take your word for it.﻿ ﻿… But you must tell me the truth.”
“As you will.﻿ ﻿… Popinot delicately suggested that if I leave you here, to be reunited to your alleged parent﻿—if I’ll trust to his word of honour, that is, and walk out of the house alone, he’ll give me twenty-four hours in which to leave Paris.”
“Then only I stand between you and﻿—”
“My dear young woman!” he protested hastily. “Please don’t run away with any absurd notion like that. Do you imagine I’d consent to treat with such canaille under any circumstances?”
“All the same,” she continued stubbornly, “I’m the stumbling-block. You’re risking your life for me﻿—”

===Chinese===
他耸起一边不以为然的肩膀。「你愿意的话，我念给你听——或者说，把它从波皮诺抬举我所用的贼帮切口里翻译出来。」
「不必了，」她言简意赅地说，「你说什么，我信什么。……但你必须对我说真话。」
「随你的便。……波皮诺婉转地提议：倘若我把你留在此地，让你与你那位所谓的尊亲团聚——也就是说，只要我信得过他的人格担保，独自一人走出这所宅子——他就宽限我二十四小时，容我离开巴黎。」
「那么横在你和——之间的，就只有我一个人——」
「我亲爱的姑娘！」他慌忙抗辩，「千万别让那种荒唐念头在心里生根。你难道以为，无论何种境况之下，我都肯同这路 *canaille*（法语：无赖贱种）讲条件吗？」
「尽管如此，」她兀自执拗地说下去，「我终归是那块绊脚石。你是在为我冒性命之险——」

===Original===
“I’m not,” he insisted almost angrily.
“You are,” she returned with quiet conviction.
“Well!” he laughed﻿—“have it your own way!﻿ ﻿… But it’s *my* life, isn’t it? I really don’t see how you’re going to prevent my risking it for anything that may seem to me worth the risk!”
But she wouldn’t laugh; only her countenance, suddenly bereft of its mutinous expression, softened winningly﻿—and her eyes grew very kind to him.
“As long as it’s understood I understand﻿—very well,” she said quietly; “I’ll do as you wish, Mr. Lanyard.”
“Good!” he cried cheerfully. “I wish, by your leave, to take you out to dinner.﻿ ﻿… This way, please!”

===Chinese===
「我没有，」他几乎是愠怒地坚称。
「你有的，」她回敬道，语气安静而笃定。
「好吧！」他笑道，「就照你的说法！……可这是*我的*性命，对不对？凡是我觉得值得一冒的险，我真看不出你有什么法子拦住我拿它去冒！」
可她没有笑；只是那张脸上桀骜不驯的神色蓦然褪尽，变得温婉动人——她那双眼睛望向他，盛满了说不出的和善。
「只要说定了我心里有数——那就很好，」她轻声说，「你怎么安排，我就怎么做，兰亚德先生。」
「好！」他欣然叫道，「恳请你赏光，容我带你出去用一顿晚餐。……这边请！」

===Original===
Leading through the scullery, he unbarred a low, arched door in one of the walls, discovering the black mouth of a narrow and tunnel-like passageway.
With a word of caution, flash-lamp in his left hand, pistol in right, Lanyard stepped out into the darkness.
In two minutes he was back, with a look of relief.
“All clear,” he reported; “I felt pretty sure Popinot knew nothing of this way out﻿—else we’d have entertained uninvited guests long since. Now, half a minute.﻿ ﻿…”
The electric meter occupied a place on the wall of the scullery not far from the door. Prying open its cover, he unscrewed and removed the fuse plug, plunging the entire house in complete darkness.
“That’ll keep ’em guessing a while!” he explained with a chuckle. “They’ll hesitate a long time before rushing a dark house infested by a desperate armed man﻿—if I know anything about that mongrel lot!﻿ ﻿… Besides, when they do get their courage up, the lack of light will stave off discovery of this way of escape.﻿ ﻿… And now, one word more.”

===Chinese===
他领路穿过洗涤间，抽开一面墙上一道低矮拱门上的门闩，露出一条窄如隧道的暗道，黑洞洞地张着口。
叮嘱了一声小心，兰亚德左手电筒、右手手枪，举步跨入黑暗。
不到两分钟他就回来了，一脸如释重负。
「一路无阻，」他报告道，「我原本就十拿九稳，波皮诺对这条出路一无所知——不然的话，不请自来的客人早该光临了。好，稍待半分钟。……」
电表嵌在洗涤间的墙上，离那道门不远。他撬开表盖，旋下保险丝插头取了出来，整座宅子顿时坠入全然的黑暗。
「这够他们捉摸一阵子了！」他呵呵一笑，解释道，「要强攻一座黑黢黢的宅子，里头还窝着一个亡命的持枪汉子，他们可得踌躇半天——那帮杂种货色，这点我还识得！……再说，等他们真鼓起胆来，没了灯光，也能叫这条逃路迟些败露。……好了，还有一句话。」

===Original===
A flash of the lamp located her hand. Calmly he possessed himself of it, if without opposition.
“I’ve brought you into trouble enough, as it is, through my stupidity,” he said; “but for that, this place should have been a refuge to us until we were quite ready to leave Paris. So now we mustn’t forget, before we go out to run God-only-knows-what gauntlet, to fix a rendezvous in event of separation.﻿ ﻿… Popinot, for instance, may have drawn a cordon around the block; we can’t tell until we’re in the street; if he has, you must leave me to entertain them until you’re safe beyond their reach.﻿ ﻿… Oh, don’t worry: I’m perfectly well able to take care of myself.﻿ ﻿… But afterwards, we must know where to find each other. Hotels, cafés and restaurants are out of the question: in the first place, we’ve barely money enough for our dinner; besides, they’ll be watched closely; as for our embassies and consulates, they aren’t open at all hours, and will likewise be watched. There remain﻿—unless you can suggest something﻿—only the churches; and I can think of none better suited to our purposes than the Sacré-Cœur.”
Her fingers tightened gently upon his.
“I understand,” she said quietly; “if we’re obliged to separate, I’m to go direct to the Sacré-Cœur and await you there.”
“Right!﻿ ﻿… But let’s hope there’ll be no such necessity.”
Hand-in-hand like frightened children, these two stole down the tunnel-like passageway, through a forlorn little court cramped between two tall old tenements, and so came out into the gloomy, sinuous and silent rue d’Assas.

===Chinese===
手电一闪，照见了她的手。他从容地把它握进掌心——她并未抗拒。
「全凭我一时愚蠢，已经连累你吃了这许多苦头，」他说，「若不是这样，这地方本该做我们的庇护所，直到我们从容准备停当、离开巴黎为止。所以如今，趁还没出门去闯天知道什么样的刀山剑树，我们千万不可忘了先约定一个走散之后的碰头之处。……比如波皮诺，说不定已沿着这一带街区拉起了一道警戒线；不走到街上，我们无从知晓；万一真有，你必须把我留下，让我去应酬他们，直到你安然脱出他们的爪牙之外。……哦，别担心：我照管自己，绰绰有余。……可是过后，我们总得知道到哪里去找彼此。旅馆、咖啡馆、饭馆，一概休想：一来，我们的钱紧巴巴的，只够这一顿晚饭；二来，那些地方必有人严盯死守；至于两国的大使馆和领事馆，又不是任何时辰都开门，同样会有人盯梢。剩下的——除非你能想出什么——就只有教堂了；而我想不出有哪一座，比圣心堂（Sacré-Cœur）更合我们的用途。」
她的手指在他手上轻轻地收紧了。
「我明白了，」她轻声说，「倘若我们被迫分开，我就径直去圣心堂，在那里等你。」
「正是！……不过但愿不至于此。」
这两人手搀着手，活像一对受惊的孩子，蹑手蹑脚地溜下那条隧道般的暗道，穿过夹在两幢高老大宅之间一方促狭而凄清的小院，就这样走进了幽暗、曲折而阒寂无声的阿萨斯街（rue d'Assas）。

===Original===
Here they encountered few wayfarers; and to these, preoccupied with anxiety to gain shelter from the inclement night, they seemed, no doubt, some student of the Quarter with his sweetheart﻿—Lanyard in his shabby raincoat, striding rapidly, head and shoulders bowed against the driving mist, the girl in her trim Burberry clinging to his arm.﻿ ﻿…
Avoiding the nearer stations as dangerous, Lanyard steered a roundabout course through byways to the rue de Sèvres station of the Nord-Sud subway; from which in due course they came to the surface again at the place de la Concorde, walked several blocks, took a taxicab, and in less than half an hour after leaving the impasse Stanislas were comfortably ensconced in a cabinet particulier of a little restaurant of modest pretensions just north of Les Halles.
They feasted famously: the cuisine, if bourgeois, was admirable and, better still, well within the resources of Lanyard’s emaciated purse. Nor did he fret with consciousness that, when the bill had been paid and the essential tips bestowed, there would remain in his pocket hardly more than cab fare. Supremely self-confident, he harboured no doubts of a smiling future﻿—now that the dark pages in his record had been turned and sealed by a resolution he held irrevocable.
His spirits had mounted to a high pitch, thanks to their successful evasion. He was young, he was in love, he was hungry, he was﻿—in short﻿—very much alive. And the consciousness of common peril knitted an enchanting intimacy into their communications. For the first time in his history Lanyard found himself in the company of a woman with whom he dared﻿—and cared﻿—to speak without reserve: a circumstance intrinsically intoxicating. And stimulated by her unquestionable interest and sympathy, he did talk without reserve of old Troyon’s and its drudge, Marcel; of Bourke and his wanderings; of the education of the Lone Wolf and his career, less in pride than in relief that it was ended; of the future he must achieve for himself.
And sitting with chin cradled on the backs of her interlaced fingers, the girl listened with such indulgence as women find always for their lovers. Of herself she had little to say: Lanyard filled in to his taste the outlines of the simple history of a young woman of good family obliged to become self-supporting.
And if at times her grave eyes clouded and her attention wandered, it was less in ennui than because of occult trains of thought set astir by some chance word or phrase of Lanyard’s.

===Chinese===
这一路上他们少有遇见行人；而在那寥寥几个行人的眼里——他们正一心急着寻个遮蔽，好躲开这风雨凄凄的寒夜——这一对八成就是拉丁区的某个学生携着他的心上人：兰亚德裹着他那件寒酸的雨衣，头与肩膀迎着扑面的雨雾低俯着，健步如飞；姑娘穿着她那件剪裁利落的巴宝莉雨衣，紧紧挽住他的手臂。……
兰亚德嫌近处的几座车站凶险，专拣僻径兜了个大圈子，来到塞夫勒街（rue de Sèvres）的北南线地铁（Nord-Sud）车站；两人适时在协和广场（place de la Concorde）重回地面，步行了好几个街区，雇了一辆出租汽车，离开斯坦尼斯拉斯死巷不到半小时，已经舒舒服服地安顿进中央市场（Les Halles）正北一家排场朴素的小餐馆的专用雅室（*cabinet particulier*）里了。
这一顿他们吃得风光十足：这家的烹调虽属市民家常一路，却无可挑剔；更妙的是，价钱也全在兰亚德那只干瘪的钱包所能支应的范围之内。他也不为这个发愁：想到账单付讫、必不可少的赏钱打发过之后，他口袋里所剩的将不过勉够车钱而已。他自信十足，对未来的一团笑意不存半点疑虑——既然他那本履历里的黑暗几页，已让一个他视为不可逆转的决心翻了过去、加了封。
多亏这一场顺利的脱身，他的兴致高涨到了极点。他年轻，他恋爱着，他饿着肚子，他——总而言之——活力勃发。而同陷险境的自觉，又给两人的倾谈织入一层醉人的亲昵。有生以来头一次，兰亚德发觉自己相伴的这位女士，是他既敢、也愿对她倾谈无忌的人：这桩事本身就自带醉意。受了那份显而易见的关切与同情的鼓动，他当真倾谈无忌：谈老特鲁瓦旅馆和馆里那个小苦工马塞尔；谈伯克和他的萍踪浪迹；谈独狼的受业与出道，与其说是志得意满，不如说是庆幸它已成过去；也谈他必须亲手挣得的前程。
姑娘坐着，下巴托在交扣的手指背上，以女人们一贯给予恋人的那份纵容听着。关于她自己，她无甚可说：一位良家姑娘被迫自食其力的简单履历，那几笔淡淡的轮廓，全由兰亚德照自己的口味填补成图。
而纵使她那双庄重的眼睛时时浮起云翳、心神间或游移，那也与其说是倦怠无聊（*ennui*），不如说是被兰亚德无意间漏出的某个字眼、某句话，搅起了几缕幽秘的心思。

===Original===
“I’m boring you,” he surmised once with quick contrition, waking up to the fact that he had monopolized the conversation for many minutes on end.
She shook a pensive head. “No, again.﻿ ﻿… But I wonder, do you appreciate the magnitude of the task you’ve undertaken?”
“Possibly not,” he conceded arrogantly; “but it doesn’t matter. The heavier the odds, the greater the incentive to win.”
“But,” she objected, “you’ve told me a curious story of one who never had a chance or incentive to ‘go straight’﻿—as you put it. And yet you seem to think that an overnight resolution to reform is all that’s needed to change all the habits of a lifetime. You persuade me of your sincerity of today; but how will it be with you tomorrow﻿—and not so much tomorrow as six months from tomorrow, when you’ve found the going rough and know you’ve only to take one step aside to gain a smooth and easy way?”
“If I fail, then, it will be because I’m unfit﻿—and I’ll go under, and never be heard of again.﻿ ﻿… But I shan’t fail. It seems to me the very fact that I want to go straight is proof enough that I’ve something inherently decent in me to build on.”
“I do believe that, and yet﻿ ﻿…” She lowered her head and began to trace a meaningless pattern on the cloth before she resumed. “You’ve given me to understand I’m responsible for your sudden awakening, that it’s because of a regard conceived for me you’re so anxious to become an honest man. Suppose﻿ ﻿… suppose you were to find out﻿ ﻿… you’d been mistaken in me?”

===Chinese===
「我把你讲闷了吧，」有一回他遽生愧意，如此揣度——他这才醒悟，自己已接连好几分钟独占了话头。
她若有所思地摇了摇头。「没有，还是没有。……只是我不知道，你可曾掂量过你肩起的这副担子的分量？」
「兴许不曾，」他傲然认了，「但那无关紧要。赌得越重，赢头越大。」
「可是，」她提出异议，「你给我讲过一个奇怪的故事——一个人，从没得到过机会、也没得到过动力去『改邪归正』——这是你自己的说法。可你如今似乎又认为，一夜之间立下自新的决心，便足以扭转一生养成的全部积习。你今日的真诚，我信；可到明天你又将如何——与其说明天，不如说明天的六个月之后，当你发觉路途崎岖，又明知只消往旁边跨出一步，便是一条平顺省力的道路，到那时你又将如何？」
「那么倘若我败了，只会是因为我不成器——我就此沉沦，再无声息。……可我不会败。在我看来，单凭我一心想改邪归正这一事实，便足以证明我骨子里尚有几分成善的根基，可以凭藉。」
「这一点我相信，然而……」她低下头去，在桌布上描起一个没有意义的花样，停了停才续道：「你让我明白了，你那场突如其来的醒悟，责任在我；说是因对我生了情意，你才这般急切地要做个诚实的人。倘若……倘若你竟发现……自己看错了『我』这个人呢？」

===Original===
“That isn’t possible,” he objected promptly.
She smiled upon him wistfully﻿—and leniently from her remote coign of superior intuitive knowledge of human nature.
“But if it were﻿—?”
“Then﻿—I think,” he said soberly﻿—“I think I’d feel as though there were nothing but emptiness beneath my feet!”
“And you’d backslide﻿—?”
“How can I tell?” he expostulated. “It’s not a fair question. I don’t know what I’d do, but I do know it would need something damnable to shake my faith in you!”

===Chinese===
「那不可能，」他即刻驳道。
她怅然朝他一笑——又是宽厚的一笑，立在她那凭直觉参透人性、高人一等的遥远高处。
「可倘若真是呢——？」
「那么——我想，」他正色道，「我想我会觉得，脚底下除了空空洞洞，什么也没有了！」
「于是你就会故态复萌——？」
「这叫我怎么说？」他抗辩道，「这不是个公道的问题。我不知道我会做什么，可我的确知道：要动摇我对你的信心，非得有什么伤天害理的事不可！」

===Original===
“You think so now,” she said tolerantly. “But if appearances were against me﻿—”
“They’d have to be black!”
“If you found I had deceived you﻿—?”
“Miss Shannon!” He threw an arm across the table and suddenly imprisoned her hand. “There’s no use beating about the bush. You’ve got to know﻿—”
She drew back suddenly with a frightened look and a monosyllable of sharp protest: “No!”
“But you must listen to me. I want you to understand.﻿ ﻿… Bourke used to say to me: ‘The man who lets love into his life opens a door no mortal hand can close﻿—and God only knows what will follow in!’ And Bourke was right.﻿ ﻿… Now that door is open in my heart, and I think that whatever follows in won’t be evil or degrading.﻿ ﻿… Oh, I’ve said it a dozen different ways of indirection, but I may as well say it squarely now: I love you; it’s love of you makes me want to go straight﻿—the hope that when I’ve proved myself you’ll maybe let me ask you to marry me.﻿ ﻿… Perhaps you’re in love with a better man today; I’m willing to chance that; a year brings many changes. Perhaps there’s something I don’t fathom in your doubting my strength and constancy. Only the outcome can declare that. But please understand this: if I fail to make good, it will be no fault of yours; it will be because I’m unfit and have proved it.﻿ ﻿… All I ask is what you’ve generously promised me: opportunity to come to you at the end of the year and make my report.﻿ ﻿… And then, if you will, you can say no to the question I’ll ask you and I shan’t resent it, and it won’t ruin me; for if a man can stick to a purpose for a year, he can stick to it forever, with or without the love of the woman he loves.”

===Chinese===
「你如今是这么想，」她宽厚地说，「可万一种种表象都于我不利——」
「那可得黑得透透的才行！」
「万一你发现我骗了你——？」
「香农小姐！」他一条手臂横过桌面，猛地扣住了她的手。「再旁敲侧击没有用处。你必须知道——」
她猛然缩回手，面带惊惶，吐出一个单音节的锐声抗议：「不！」
「可你必须听我说完。我要你明白。……伯克从前常对我说：『把爱情放进生命里的人，就打开了一扇凡人的手再也关不上的门——而只有天知道，随后会跟进些什么来！』伯克说得对。……如今那扇门已在我心里敞开，而我想，跟进来的一切，不会是邪恶，也不会是堕落。……哦，这话我已经用十来种转弯抹角的说法说过了，可如今不妨说得直截了当：我爱你；正是对你的爱，叫我想要改邪归正——盼着有朝一日我证明了自己，你或许肯让我向你求婚。……也许你今天爱上的是一个更好的人；这个险我甘愿冒；一年之间，变迁多矣。也许你怀疑我的坚忍与恒久，里头有我参不透的缘故。那只有结局才能判定。可请你明白这一点：倘若我终归不成器，那决不是你的过错；那将因为我不成器，并且自己证实了不成器。……我所请求的，无非是你已经慨然应许我的那一件：一年期满时，容我到你面前交一份成绩单。……到那时，你若愿意，尽可以对我将问你的那个问题说不，我不会怀怨，它也毁不了我；因为一个人若能守着一个宗旨熬过一年，他就能守着它熬到永远——不管他爱的那个女人，爱不爱他。」

===Original===
She heard him out without attempt at interruption, but her answer was prefaced by a sad little shake of her head.
“That’s what makes it so hard, so terribly hard,” she said.﻿ ﻿… “Of course I’ve understood you. All that you’ve said by indirection, and much besides, has had its meaning to me. And I’m glad and proud of the honour you offer me. But I can’t accept it; I can never accept it﻿—not now nor a year from now. It wouldn’t be fair to let you go on hoping I might some time consent to marry you.﻿ ﻿… For that’s impossible.”
“You﻿—forgive me﻿—you’re not already married?”
“No.﻿ ﻿…”
“Or promised?”

===Chinese===
她听他讲完，一次也没想打断；只是她的回答之前，先来了一阵小小的、伤感的摇头。
「这正是叫人为难之处，为难得很呐，」她说。……「你说的我当然都懂得。你那些旁敲侧击的话，以及话外的许许多多，于我都自有其意义。你赐我的这份荣光，我高兴，也引以为荣。可我不能接受；我永远不能接受——如今不能，一年之后也不能。让你一味指望我或有一日肯嫁给你，那是不公道的。……因为那是办不到的。」
「你——恕我唐突——你不会是已经嫁过人了吧？」
「没有。……」
「也没有许过人家？」

===Original===
“No.﻿ ﻿…”
“Or in love with someone else?”
Again she told him, gently, “No.”
His face cleared. He squared his shoulders. He even mustered up a smile.
“Then it isn’t impossible. No human obstacle exists that time can’t overthrow. In spite of all you say, I shall go on hoping with all my heart and soul and strength.”

===Chinese===
「没有。……」
「也没有心上另有他人？」
她又温柔地告诉他：「没有。」
他的脸色豁然开朗。他挺起肩膀。他甚至还挤出了一抹微笑。
「那么就不是办不到。人间的障碍，没有一样是时间所推翻不了的。任凭你怎么说，我也要用我的全副心灵与气力，继续指望下去。」

===Original===
“But you don’t understand﻿—”
“Can you tell me﻿—make me understand?”
After a long pause, she told him once more, and very sadly: “No.”

===Chinese===
「可你不明白——」
「你能告诉我吗——让我明白？」
良久的一顿之后，她再一次告诉他，说得凄婉之至：「不能。」

## XV Sheer Impudence / 十五、胆大包天

===Original===
Though it had been nearly eight when they entered the restaurant, it was something after eleven before Lanyard called for his bill.
“We’ve plenty of time,” he had explained; “it’ll be midnight before we can move. The gentle art of housebreaking has its technique, you know, its professional ethics: we can’t well violate the privacy of Madame Omber’s strongbox before the caretakers on the premises are sound asleep. It isn’t *done*, you know, it isn’t class, to go burglarizing when decent, law-abiding folk are wide-awake.﻿ ﻿… Meantime we’re better off here than trapezing the streets.﻿ ﻿…”
It’s a silent web of side ways and a gloomy one by night that backs up north of Les Halles: old Paris, taciturn and sombre, steeped in its memories of grim romance. But for infrequent, flickering, corner lamps, the street that welcomed them from the doors of the warm and cosy restaurant was as dismal as an alley in some city of the dead. Its houses with their mansard roofs and boarded windows bent their heads together like mutes at a wake, black-cloaked and hooded; seldom one showed a light; never one betrayed by any sound the life that lurked behind its jealous blinds.

===Chinese===
他们进餐馆时将近八点，等兰亚德开口叫账单，已是十一点过响了。
「我们时间充裕得很，」他早先解释过；「非到午夜，咱们动不了手。入室行窃这门优雅艺术自有其技法，你也知道，自有其行内的道德：宅子里的看守人没睡熟之前，咱们不便去惊扰欧姆贝夫人保险箱的隐私。正经守法的好人都还大睁着眼的时候跑出去撬门，你知道，这种事是不兴（*done*）的，有失身份。……再说，与其在街面上荡来荡去，咱们还不如待在这儿。……」
中央市场（Les Halles）以北铺开一片纵横交错的小巷，入夜后更见阴沉：这是老巴黎，缄默而晦暗，浸透着阴郁传奇的记忆。若不是街角零零星星几盏摇曳的路灯，从温暖舒适的餐馆门口迎来他们的这条街，简直同死城里的小巷一般凄清。两旁复折式屋顶、窗板紧闭的房屋彼此俯首凑近，宛如守灵时一袭黑袍兜帽的哑仆；难得有一扇窗透出灯火；也从来没有一栋屋子发出半点声响，泄露严严实实的百叶窗后潜藏的人烟。

===Original===
Now again the rain had ceased and, though the sky remained overcast, the atmosphere was clear and brisk with a touch of frost, in grateful contrast to the dull and muggy airs that had obtained for the last twenty-four hours.
“We’ll walk,” Lanyard suggested﻿—“if you don’t mind﻿—part of the way at least; it’ll eat up time, and a bit of exercise will do us both good.”
The girl assented quietly.﻿ ﻿…
The drum of their heels on fast-drying sidewalks struck sharp echoes from the silence of that drowsy quarter, a lonely clamour that rendered it impossible to ignore their apparent solitude﻿—as impossible as it was for Lanyard to ignore the fact that they were followed.

===Chinese===
这时雨又停了，天空虽仍阴沉，空气却清冽爽利，带着一缕霜意，较之前二十四小时那沉闷湿黏的空气，令人为之一爽。
「咱们走路去吧，」兰亚德提议——「你要是不介意——至少走上一段；好消磨时光，活动活动筋骨，对咱们俩都有益处。」
姑娘静静地应允了。……
两人的鞋跟叩在渐干的人行道上，从这半睡街区的一片岑寂里敲出清脆的回声——这一孤零零的响动，叫人无法不理会他们眼下的形单影只——正如兰亚德无法不理会那个事实：他们被人跟上了。

===Original===
The shadow dogging them on the far side of the street, some fifty yards behind, was as noiseless as any cat; but for this circumstance﻿—had it moved boldly with unmuffled footsteps﻿—Lanyard would have been slow to believe it concerned with him, so confident had he felt, till that moment, of having given the Pack the slip.
And from this he diagnosed still another symptom of the Pack’s incurable stupidity!
Supremely on the alert, he had discovered the pursuit before they left the block of the restaurant. Dissembling, partly to avoid alarming the girl, partly to trick the spy, he turned this way and that round several corners, until quite convinced that the shadow was dedicated to himself exclusively, then promptly revised his first purpose and, instead of sticking to darker back ways, struck out directly for the broad, well-lighted and lively boulevard de Sébastopol.

===Chinese===
街对面那道人影缀着他们，落后五十来码，脚底下轻得像猫；若非这一层——倘或它大模大样、步声不掩地跟来——兰亚德还不会轻易相信这影子是冲他来的，因为直到那一刻，他一直自信早已把群狼甩脱了。
由此他又诊断出群狼那无可救药的愚蠢的又一症状！
他警觉到了极处，还没走出餐馆那一带就已发现了这趟跟踪。他不动声色——一半为免惊扰姑娘，一半为愚弄奸细——绕着好几个街角东转西拐，直到确信那道影子专门冲他一人而来；随即当机立断改了主意，不再拣昏暗的背街小巷走，径直奔向宽阔明亮、人烟稠密的塞瓦斯托波尔大道（boulevard de Sébastopol）。

===Original===
Crossing this without a backward glance, he turned north, seeking some café whose arrangements suited his designs; and, presently, though not before their tramp had brought them almost to the Grand Boulevards, found one to his taste, a cheerful and well-lighted establishment occupying a corner, with entrances from both streets. A hedge of forlorn fir-trees knee-deep in wooden tubs guarded its terrasse of round metal tables and spindle-shanked chairs; of which few were occupied. Inside, visible through the wide plate-glass windows, perhaps a dozen patrons sat round half as many tables﻿—no more﻿—idling over dominoes and gossip: steady-paced burghers with their wives, men in small ways of business of the neighbourhood.
Entering to this company, Lanyard selected a square marble-topped table against the back wall, entrenched himself with the girl upon the seat behind it, ordered coffee and writing materials, and proceeded to light a cigarette with the nonchalance of one to whom time is of no consequence.
“What is it?” the girl asked guardedly as the waiter scurried off to execute his commands. “You’ve not stopped in here for nothing!”
“True﻿—but lower, please!” he begged. “If we speak English loud enough to be heard it will attract attention.﻿ ﻿… The trouble is, we’re followed. But as yet our faithful shadow doesn’t know we know it﻿—unless he’s more intelligent than he seems. Consequently, if I don’t misjudge him, he’ll take a table outside, the better to keep an eye on us, as soon as he sees we’re apparently settled for some time. More than that, I’ve got a note to write﻿—and not merely as a subterfuge. This fellow must be shaken off, and as long as we stick together, that can’t well be done.”

===Chinese===
他头也不回地横穿过去，折向北行，寻一家布局合乎他算计的咖啡馆；不多时——两人这一程已差不多走到大林荫大道（the Grand Boulevards）——他寻着一家中意的：一爿占着街角、两面临街开门的铺面，灯明火亮，暖意融融。一排蔫头耷脑的冷杉栽在齐膝深的木桶里，围着露天座（terrasse）的圆铁桌和细腿椅；桌边少有人坐。店堂里，透过宽大的平板玻璃窗望进去，十来个客人围着五六张桌子——再多没有了——慢悠悠地下骨牌、闲磕牙：是不慌不忙的小市民偕着太太，还有本街做小本生意的买卖人。
走进这一屋子人中，兰亚德挑了张靠后墙的大理石面方桌，同姑娘在桌后长椅上坐定扎营，点了咖啡和纸笔，然后点上一支烟，那份从容，仿佛时间于他全然不算回事。
「怎么回事？」侍者小跑着去照吩咐张罗时，姑娘带着戒意问道。「你在这儿停下总不是白停的！」
「不错——不过请放低声音！」他央告道。「咱们说英语要是响得叫人听见，会惹人注目。……麻烦在于：咱们被跟上了。不过咱们那位忠心耿耿的影子，眼下还不知道咱们已经察觉——除非他比外表看上去更聪明。所以，要是我没看错他，等他见咱们显然要安坐好一阵，就会到外头占张桌子，好就近盯着咱们。再者，我还有封信要写——倒也不只是做个幌子。这家伙必须甩掉，可只要咱们俩还拴在一处，这事就断难办到。」

===Original===
He interrupted himself while the waiter served them, then added sugar to his coffee, arranged the ink bottle and paper to his satisfaction, and bent over his pen.
“Come closer,” he requested﻿—“as if you were interested in what I’m writing﻿—and amused; if you can laugh a bit at nothing, so much the better. But keep a sharp eye on the windows. You can do that more readily than I, more naturally from under the brim of your hat.﻿ ﻿… And tell me what you see.﻿ ﻿…”
He had no more than settled into the swing of composition, than the girl﻿—apparently following his pen with closest attention﻿—giggled coquettishly and nudged his elbow.
“The window to the right of the door we came in,” she said, smiling delightedly; “he’s standing behind the fir-trees, staring in.”

===Chinese===
侍者端上东西来时他住了口，随后往咖啡里加糖，把墨水瓶和纸摆弄到称心如意，俯身执笔。
「再凑近些，」他要求道——「就当你在留意我写的东西——而且看得有趣；你要是能没来由地轻笑几声，那就更好。可要盯紧窗子。这个你比我便当，从你帽檐底下望出去也更自然。……看见什么，就告诉我。……」
他刚刚进入运笔如飞的境界，那姑娘——分明在全神贯注地追随他的笔尖——便娇滴滴地嗤笑一声，用胳膊肘碰了碰他。
「咱们进来的那扇门，右手边那扇窗，」她喜滋滋地含笑说道；「他站在冷杉后头，正往里张望呢。」

===Original===
“Can you make out who he is?” Lanyard asked without moving his lips.
“Nothing more than that he’s tall,” she said with every indication of enjoying a tremendous joke. “His face is all in shadow.﻿ ﻿…”
“Patience!” counselled the adventurer. “He’ll take heart of courage when convinced of our innocence.”
He poised his pen, examined the ceiling for inspiration, and permitted a slow smile to lighten his countenance.
“You’ll take this note, if you please,” he said cheerfully, “to the address on the envelope, by taxi: it’s some distance, near the Étoile.﻿ ﻿… A long chance, but one we must risk; give me half an hour alone and I’ll guarantee to discourage this animal one way or another. You understand?”

===Chinese===
「看得出他是谁吗？」兰亚德嘴唇不动地问。
「只看得出他个头高，」她说，那副神气，活像正享用着一个天大的笑话。「他的脸整个儿在暗处。……」
「耐心点！」这位冒险家进言道。「等他确信了咱们的清白无辜，自会鼓起勇气来。」
他悬笔半空，端详天花板寻灵感，让一抹徐徐的笑意慢慢照亮了面容。
「有劳你把这封信送去，」他高高兴兴地说，「送到信封上的地址，坐出租车去：路不近，在星形广场（the Étoile）附近。……胜算渺茫，可这险非冒不可；给我半个钟头单枪匹马，我担保有法子叫这头畜生丧气而归，不管用什么法子。你明白？」

===Original===
“Perfectly,” she laughed archly.
He bent and for a few moments wrote busily.
“Now he’s walking slowly round the corner, never taking his eyes from you,” the girl reported, shoulder to his shoulder and head distractingly near his head.
“Good. Can you see him any better?”
“Not yet.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「再明白不过，」她乖觉地笑了。
他俯下身去，奋笔疾书了一阵。
「这会儿他正绕着街角慢慢踱，眼睛一刻没离开过你，」姑娘报告道，肩贴着他的肩，那颗头凑得撩人心神。
「好。这回能看清他些了吗？」
「还看不清。……」

===Original===
“This note,” he said, without stopping his pen or appearing to say anything “is for the concierge of a building where I rent stabling for a little motorcar. I’m supposed there to be a chauffeur in the employ of a crazy Englishman, who keeps me constantly travelling with him back and forth between Paris and London. That’s to account for the irregularity with which I use the car. They know me, monsieur and madame of the conciergerie, as Pierre Lamier; and I *think* they’re safe﻿—not only trustworthy and of friendly disposition, but quite simple-minded; I don’t believe they gossip much. So the chances are De Morbihan and his gang know nothing of the arrangement. But that’s all speculation﻿—a forlorn hope!”
“I understand,” the girl observed. “He’s still prowling up and down outside the hedge.”
“We’re not going to need that car tonight; but the hotel of Madame Omber is close by; and I’ll follow and join you there within an hour at most. Meantime, this note will introduce you to the concierge and his wife﻿—I hope you won’t mind﻿—as my fiancée. I’m telling them we became engaged in England, and I’ve brought you to Paris to visit my mother in Montrouge; but am detained by my employer’s business; and will they please give you shelter for an hour.”
“He’s coming in,” the girl announced quietly.

===Chinese===
「这封信，」他说，笔不停挥，口中也仿佛什么都不曾说，「是给一栋房子里的门房（concierge）的，我在那儿租了个棚位存放一辆小汽车。在那边，人们只当我是给一个疯疯癫癫的英国人当差的司机（chauffeur），他拖着我成天在巴黎和伦敦之间来来回回。这就算交代了我用车为何没个定数。门房小屋（conciergerie）里的先生和太太都认得我，管我叫皮埃尔·拉米耶（Pierre Lamier）；我*想*他们靠得住——不光诚实可靠、心性和善，人还相当憨实；我看他们不大嚼舌根。所以德·莫尔比昂和他那一伙多半不知道这层安排。不过这全是臆测——一线渺茫的希望罢了！」
「我明白了，」姑娘说。「他还在那排树篱外头来回逡巡。」
「咱们今晚用不着那辆车；可欧姆贝夫人的宅子就在左近；我随后就跟去，至多一个钟头内与你碰头。眼下，这封信会把你引荐给门房两口子——望你别介意——就说你是我的未婚妻。我在信里说，咱们在英格兰订的婚，我把你带到巴黎来探望我母亲，她住在蒙鲁日（Montrouge）；偏巧我被东家的事绊住了脚；就劳驾他们收留你一个钟头。」
「他进来了，」姑娘悄声通报。

===Original===
“In here?”
“No﻿—merely inside the row of little trees.”
“Which entrance?”
“The boulevard side. He’s taken the corner table. Now a waiter’s going out to him.”
“You can see his face now?” Lanyard asked, sealing the note.

===Chinese===
「进这儿来了？」
「没有——只是进了那排小树里头。」
「打哪边的门？」
「大道那头。他占了角上那张桌子。这会儿正有个侍者往外给他张罗去。」
「这下能看见他的脸了吧？」兰亚德一边封信口一边问。

===Original===
“Not well.﻿ ﻿…”
“Nothing you recognize about him, eh?”
“Nothing.﻿ ﻿…”
“You know Popinot and Wertheimer by sight?”

===Chinese===
「看不大真。……」
「他身上就没有你认得出的地方，嗯？」
「没有。……」
「波皮诺和维特海默的长相，你认得吗？」

===Original===
“No; they’re only names to me; De Morbihan and Mr. Bannon mentioned them last night.”
“It won’t be Popinot,” Lanyard reflected, addressing the envelope; “he’s tubby.”
“This man is tall and slender.”
“Wertheimer, possibly. Does he suggest an Englishman, anyway?”
“Not in the least. He wears a moustache﻿—blond﻿—twisted up like the Kaiser’s.”

===Chinese===
「不认得；他们于我只是两个名字；德·莫尔比昂和班农先生昨晚提起过。」
「那不会是波皮诺，」兰亚德一边往信封上写地址一边寻思；「他是矮胖子。」
「这人又高又瘦。」
「那可能是维特海默。他究竟像不像个英国人？」
「一点也不像。他留着两撇小胡子——金黄色的——两梢翘起，跟德皇的一个样。」

===Original===
Lanyard made no reply; but his heart sank, and he shivered imperceptibly with foreboding. He entertained no doubt but that the worst had happened, that to the number of his enemies in Paris was added Ekstrom.
One furtive glance confirmed this inference. He swore bitterly, if privately and with a countenance of childlike blandness, as he sipped the coffee and finished his cigarette.
“Who is it, then?” she asked. “Do you know him?”

===Chinese===
兰亚德没有作声；可他心往下一沉，凭着不祥的预感，不觉微微打了个寒噤。他毫不怀疑，最糟的已成事实——他在巴黎的仇敌名册上，又添了一个埃克斯特罗姆。
偷偷一瞥，证实了这个推断。他狠狠咒骂了一句——只是闷在心里，脸上仍是一派孩童般的天真平和——一面呷着咖啡，把烟抽完。
「那么，那是谁？」她问。「你认得他？」

===Original===
He reckoned swiftly against distressing her, recalling his mention of the fact that Ekstrom was credited with the Huysman murder.
“Merely a hanger-on of De Morbihan’s,” he told her lightly; “a spineless animal﻿—no trouble about scaring him off.﻿ ﻿… Now take this note, please, and we’ll go. But as we reach the door, turn back﻿—and go out the other. You’ll find a taxi without trouble. And stop for nothing!”
He had shown foresight in paying when served, and was consequently able to leave abruptly, without giving Ekstrom time to shy. Rising smartly, he pushed the table aside. The girl was no less quick, and little less sensitive to the strain of the moment; but as she passed him her lashes lifted and her eyes were all his for the instant.

===Chinese===
他飞快权衡了一下，还是别让她担惊受怕——想起自己曾提过，于伊斯曼那桩凶案正算在埃克斯特罗姆头上。
「不过是德·莫尔比昂手下一名跟班，」他轻描淡写地告诉她；「一头没脊梁的畜生——吓退他费不了事。……好了，请拿上这封信，咱们走。只是到了门口，你就折回去——从另一边出去。出租车一招就有。路上什么都别耽搁！」
他早有先见之明，东西一上来就付了账，这时才能说走就走，不给埃克斯特罗姆缩头闪避的工夫。他利落地起身，把桌子推开。姑娘也不慢，对这千钧一发的紧张也不比他少敏感到哪儿去；只是打他身边经过时，她睫毛一抬，那一瞬，一双眼睛整个儿都是他的。

===Original===
“Good night,” she breathed﻿—“good night﻿ ﻿… my dear!”
She could have guessed no more shrewdly what he needed to nerve him against the impending clash. He hadn’t hesitated as to his only course, but till then he’d been horribly afraid, knowing too well the desperate cast of the outlawed German’s nature. But now he couldn’t fail.
He strode briskly toward the door to the boulevard, out of the corner of his eye aware that Ekstrom, taken by surprise, half-started from his chair, then sank back.

===Chinese===
「晚安，」她低声说——「晚安……亲爱的！」
她再也猜不出比这更熨帖的话，来给他壮胆，去迎接迫在眉睫的冲突了。他对自己唯一的这条路从没犹豫过，可直到那一刻，他心里一直怕得厉害——他太清楚那个不容于法的德国人是何等亡命的天性。而此刻，他不会失手了。
他大步流星朝通大道的那扇门走去，眼角余光瞥见埃克斯特罗姆猝不及防，从椅子上半撑起身，又坐了回去。

===Original===
Two paces from the entrance the girl checked, murmured in French, “Oh, my handkerchief!” and turned briskly back.
Without pause, as though he hadn’t heard, Lanyard threw the door wide and swung out, turning directly to the spy. At the same time he dropped a hand into the pocket where nestled his automatic.
Fortunately Ekstrom had chosen a table in a corner well removed from any in use. Lanyard could speak without fear of being overheard.
But for a moment he refrained. Nor did Ekstrom speak or stir; sitting sideways at his table, negligently, with knees crossed, the German likewise kept a hand buried in the pocket of his heavy, dark ulster. Thus neither doubted the other’s ill-will or preparedness. And through thirty seconds of silence they remained at pause, each striving with all his might to read the other’s purpose in his eyes. But there was this distinction to be drawn between their attitudes, that whereas Lanyard’s gaze challenged, the German’s was sullenly defiant. And presently Lanyard felt his heart stir with relief: the spy’s glance had winced.

===Chinese===
离门口还有两步，姑娘顿住脚，用法语低声说了句「哎呀，我的手帕！」，随即利落地转身折回。
兰亚德毫不停顿，仿佛压根不曾听见，一把推开门跨了出去，径直转向那奸细。与此同时，他一只手垂进衣袋，袋里正安卧着他的自动手枪。
幸而埃克斯特罗姆挑的桌子在一个角落里，离任何有人的桌子都远。兰亚德说话不怕叫人听去。
可他一时没有开口。埃克斯特罗姆也不言语、不动弹；他侧身坐在桌前，懒懒地架着二郎腿，那只手同样深埋在厚重黑大衣的口袋里。于是两人对彼此的敌意与防备都不存疑。整整三十秒的沉默里，他们僵持不动，各自竭尽全力，想从对方眼睛里读出对方的心思。只是两人的姿态有这样一个分别：兰亚德的目光是挑战，德国人的目光却是阴郁的顽抗。不多时，兰亚德觉出心头一动，松了口气：那奸细的眼神先怯了。

===Original===
“Ekstrom,” the adventurer said quietly, “if you fire, I’ll get you before I fall. That’s a simple statement of fact.”
The German hesitated, moistened the corners of his lips with a nervous tongue, but contented himself with a nod of acknowledgement.
“Take your hand off that gun,” Lanyard ordered. “Remember﻿—I’ve only to cry your name aloud to have you torn to pieces by these people. Your life’s not worth a moment’s purchase in Paris﻿—as you should know.”
The German hesitated, but in his heart knew that Lanyard didn’t exaggerate. The murder of the inventor had exasperated all France; and though tonight’s weather kept a third of Paris within doors, there was still a tide of pedestrians fluent on the sidewalk, beyond the flimsy barrier of firs, that would thicken to a ravening mob upon the least excuse.

===Chinese===
「埃克斯特罗姆，」冒险家平静地说，「你若开枪，我倒下之前也准能撂倒你。这不过是陈述一桩简单的事实。」
德国人迟疑了一下，紧张地用舌尖舔了舔嘴角，到底只点头认了。
「把那只手从枪上拿开，」兰亚德命令道。「记住——我只消高声喊出你的名字，这些人就会把你撕成碎片。在巴黎，你这条命连片刻也保不住——这个你该知道。」
德国人又迟疑了，可他心里明白兰亚德并非夸大其词。那位发明家遇害一案早已激怒了全法国；虽说今夜这天气把三分之一的巴黎人关在了屋里，人行道上——就在那道单薄的冷杉屏障之外——仍有川流不息的行人，稍有个由头，便会稠成一头噬人的暴徒。

===Original===
He had mistaken his man; he had thought that Lanyard, even if aware of his pursuit, would seek to shake it off in flight rather than turn and fight﻿—and fight here, of all places!
“Do you hear me?” Lanyard continued in the same level and unyielding tone. “Bring both hands in sight﻿—upon the table!”
There was no more hesitation: Ekstrom obeyed, if with the sullen grace of a wild beast that would and could slay its trainer with one sweep of its paw﻿—if only it dared.
For the first time since leaving the girl Lanyard relaxed his vigilant watch over the man long enough for one swift glance through the window at his side. But she was already vanished from the café.

===Chinese===
他看错了他的对手；他原以为兰亚德即使察觉了跟踪，也只会夺路而逃，绝不会回身应战——更不会偏偏选在这种地方开战！
「听见没有？」兰亚德继续用那种平稳而不容置喙的声调说。「把两只手都放到看得见的地方——放在桌上！」
这回不再迟疑：埃克斯特罗姆照办了，只是带着一头猛兽那阴沉的体面——它想、也能够一爪扫毙它的驯兽师——只要它敢。
自打离开姑娘，兰亚德头一回把紧盯此人的目光松开须臾，透过身旁的窗子飞快扫了一眼。可她早已从咖啡馆里消失了。

===Original===
He breathed more freely now.
“Come!” he said peremptorily. “Get up. We’ve got to talk, I presume﻿—thrash this matter out﻿—and we’ll come to no decision here.”
“Where do we go, then?” the German demanded suspiciously.
“We can walk.”

===Chinese===
至此他才觉得呼吸畅快了些。
「来吧！」他斩钉截铁地说。「起来。我想，咱们得谈一谈——把这桩事抖落个明白——在这儿可拿不出个决断来。」
「那么去哪儿？」德国人狐疑地质问。
「走着谈就是。」

===Original===
Irresolutely the spy uncrossed his knees, but didn’t rise.
“Walk?” he repeated, “walk where?”
“Up the boulevard, if you like﻿—where the lights are brightest.”
“Ah!”﻿—with a malignant flash of teeth﻿—“but I don’t trust you.”
Lanyard laughed: “You wear only one shoe of that pair, my dear captain! We’re a distrustful flock, we birds of prey. Come along! Why sit there sulking, like a spoiled child? You’ve made an ass of yourself, following me to Paris; sadly though you bungled that job in London, I gave you credit for more wit than to poke your head into the lion’s mouth here. But﻿—admitting that﻿—why not be graceful about it? Here am I, amiably treating you like an equal: you might at least show gratitude enough to accept my invitation to flâner yourself!”

===Chinese===
奸细迟疑不决地放下翘起的二郎腿，人却没有起身。
「走？」他重复道，「走去哪儿？」
「沿大道走，要是你乐意——哪边灯最亮走哪边。」
「啊！」——牙关一闪凶光——「可我信不过你。」
兰亚德哈哈一笑：「这双鞋咱们俩只合穿一只，我亲爱的上尉！咱们猛禽这一族，个个天生多疑。走吧！干吗坐在那儿怄气，像个惯坏的孩子？跟着我到巴黎来，你已把自己作践成一头蠢驴；你在伦敦那趟差事办得固然凄惨，可我还当你多少有点脑子，不至于跑到这儿来把脑袋往狮子口里送。不过——就算如此——何不识趣点，姿态放漂亮些？你看我，和和气气拿你当个平辈：你总该领这点情，接受我的邀请，去 flâner（闲逛）上一番吧！」

===Original===
With a grunt the spy got upon his feet, while Lanyard stood back, against the window, and made him free of the narrow path between the tree-tubs and the tables.
“After you, my dear Adolph﻿ ﻿… !”
The German paused, half turned towards him, choking with rage, his suffused face darkly relieving its white scars won at Heidelberg. At this, with a nod of unmistakable meaning, Lanyard advanced the muzzle of his pocketed weapon; and with an ugly growl the German moved on and out to the sidewalk, Lanyard respectfully an inch or two behind his elbow.

===Chinese===
奸细咕哝一声，站起身来；兰亚德退后靠窗，给他让出树桶与桌子之间那条窄道。
「您先请，我亲爱的阿道夫……！」
德国人顿住脚，半转过身来，气得直噎，涨红的脸上，那几道海德堡（Heidelberg）决斗挣来的白疤映着暗色，格外分明。见状，兰亚德意味再明白不过地点了点头，衣袋里的枪口往前一顶；德国人恶吼一声，接着往前走去，出了人行道，兰亚德毕恭毕敬地缀在他臂肘后头一两英寸的地方。

===Original===
“To your right,” he requested pleasantly﻿—“if it’s all the same to you: I’ve business on the Boulevards﻿ ﻿…”
Ekstrom said nothing for the moment, but sullenly yielded to the suggestion.
“By the way,” the adventurer presently pursued, “you might be good enough to inform me how you knew where we were dining﻿—eh?”
“If it interests you﻿—”

===Chinese===
「往你右手边，」他和颜悦色地关照——「要是你不介意的话：我在林荫大道那儿还有点事儿……」
埃克斯特罗姆一时没言语，只是悻悻地依了这番指点。
「对了，」冒险家随即又续上，「可否劳驾告诉我，你是怎么知道我们在哪儿用晚餐的——嗯？」
「你要是当真感兴趣——」

===Original===
“I own it does﻿—tremendously!”
“Pure accident: I happened to be sitting in the café, and caught a glimpse of you through the door as you went upstairs. Therefore I waited till the waiter asked for your bill at the caisse, then stationed myself outside.”
“But why? Can you tell me what you thought to accomplish?”
“You know well,” Ekstrom muttered. “After what happened in London﻿ ﻿… it’s your life or mine!”

===Chinese===
「我承认当真感兴趣——而且兴趣大得很！」
「纯属偶然：我碰巧坐在那家馆子里，你们上楼时，我打门口瞥见了你。于是我等到侍者上账台（caisse）去结你们的账，便到外头占好了位置。」
「可这是为什么？能否告诉我，你指望捞到什么？」
「你清楚得很，」埃克斯特罗姆咕哝道。「伦敦出过那档子事之后……不是你的命，就是我的命！」

===Original===
“Spoken like a true villain! But it seems to me you overlooked a conspicuous chance to accomplish your hellish design, back there in the side streets.”
“Would I be such a fool as to shoot you down before finding out what you’ve done with those plans?”
“You might as well have,” Lanyard informed him lightly﻿ ﻿… “For you won’t know otherwise.”
With an infuriated oath the German stopped short: but he dared not ignore the readiness with which his tormentor imitated the manoeuvre and kept the pistol trained through the fabric of his raincoat.

===Chinese===
「好一副地道反派的口吻！不过依我看，方才在那些背街小巷里，你可是白白放过了一个大好机会，没干成你那地狱里的勾当。」
「没弄清你把那些图纸弄到了何处之前，我会蠢到一枪把你撂倒吗？」
「撂倒不撂倒都一样，」兰亚德轻飘飘地告诉他……「横竖你别想知道了。」
德国人气得一声怒骂，猛地站住：可他的对头当即如法炮制、亦步亦趋，隔着雨衣的布面拿枪指牢了他，他不敢装看不见。

===Original===
“Yes﻿—?” the adventurer enquired with an exasperating accent of surprise.
“Understand me,” Ekstrom muttered vindictively: “next time I’ll show you no mercy﻿—”
“But if there *is* no next time? We’re not apt to meet again, you know.”
“That’s something beyond your knowledge﻿—”

===Chinese===
「怎么啦——？」冒险家用一种叫人冒火的惊讶腔调探问。
「你给我记住，」埃克斯特罗姆恨恨地低声说：「下回我可不再留情——」
「可要是根本没*有*下回呢？你知道，咱们多半不会再见面了。」
「这可不是你能知道的事——」

===Original===
“You think so?﻿ ﻿… But shan’t we resume our stroll? People might notice us standing here﻿—you with your teeth bared like an ill-tempered dog.﻿ ﻿… Oh, thank you!” And as they moved on, Lanyard continued: “Shall I explain why we’re not apt to meet again?”
“If it amuses you.”
“Thanks once more!﻿ ﻿… For the simple reason that Paris satisfies me; so here I stop.”
“Well?” the spy asked with a blank sidelong look.
“Whereas you are leaving Paris tonight.”

===Chinese===
「你这么想？……咱们还是接着遛弯吧？站在这儿，人家该起疑了——你龇着牙，活像条恼火的狗。……哦，多谢！」待两人重新迈步，兰亚德续道：「要不要我解释解释，咱们为什么多半不会再见面了？」
「只要你觉得开心。」
「那就再谢一回！……理由再简单不过：巴黎叫我称心如意；所以我就在这儿落脚不走了。」
「哦？」奸细斜睨他一眼，一脸茫然地问。
「而你呢，今晚就要离开巴黎。」

===Original===
“What makes you think that?”
“Because you value your thick hide too highly to remain, my dear captain.” Having gained the corner of the boulevard St. Denis, Lanyard pulled up. “One moment, by your leave. You see yonder the entrance to the Métro﻿—don’t you? And here, a dozen feet away, a perfectly able-bodied *sergent de ville*? Let this fateful conjunction impress you properly: for five minutes after you have descended to the Métro﻿—or as soon as the noise of a train advises me you’ve had one chance to get away﻿—I shall mention casually to the sergo﻿—that I have seen Captain Ek﻿—”
“Hush!” the German protested in a hiss of fright.
“But certainly: I’ve no desire to embarrass you: publicity must be terribly distasteful to one of your sensitive and retiring disposition.﻿ ﻿… But I trust you understand me? On the one hand, there’s the Métro; on the other, there’s the flic; while here, you must admit, am I, as large as life and very much on the job!﻿ ﻿… And inasmuch as I shall certainly mention my suspicions to the minion of the law﻿—as aforesaid﻿—I’d advise you to be well out of Paris before dawn!”

===Chinese===
「你凭什么这么想？」
「因为你太爱惜你那身厚皮，舍不得留下，我亲爱的上尉。」走到圣德尼大道（boulevard St. Denis）街角，兰亚德收住脚步。「劳驾，稍候片刻。你瞧见那边地铁（Métro）的入口了吧？还有这儿，十来英尺开外，一名体格健壮的*sergent de ville*（巡警）？让这要命的巧合好生印进你脑子：你下地铁五分钟之后——或者说，等一列火车的轰响告诉我，你已经有了一次脱身的机会——我就随口跟这位条子（sergo）提一句，说我瞧见了埃克上——」
「嘘！」德国人惊惶地嘶声抗议。
「那是自然：我绝无让你难堪之意：以你这样敏感内向的性子，抛头露面想必是万分难堪的。……不过，相信你听懂了我的意思？一头是地铁，一头是条子（flic），而在这儿，你不得不承认，还有我本人，如假包换、寸步不离！……再说，如前所言，我准会把我的疑心透露给那位执法老爷——所以我奉劝你，天亮之前，滚出巴黎越远越好！」

===Original===
There was murder in the eyes of the spy as he lingered, truculently glowering at the smiling adventurer; and for an instant Lanyard was well-persuaded he had gone too far, that even there, even on that busy junction of two crowded thoroughfares, Ekstrom would let his temper get the better of his judgment and risk everything in an attempt upon the life of his despoiler.
But he was mistaken.

===Chinese===
奸细踟蹰不去，眼里透着杀机，凶霸霸地瞪着满面含笑的冒险家；有一瞬间，兰亚德深信自己做得过了头——哪怕在那里，哪怕在两条通衢大道人潮交汇的路口，埃克斯特罗姆也会任由火气压倒理智，孤注一掷，豁出一切去取他劫夺者的性命。
可他看错了。

===Original===
With a surly shrug the spy swung about and marched straight to the kiosk of the underground railway, into which, without one backward glance, he disappeared.
Two minutes later the earth beneath Lanyard’s feet quaked with the crash and rumble of a northbound train.
He waited three minutes longer; but Ekstrom didn’t reappear; and at length convinced that his warning had proved effectual, Lanyard turned and made off.

===Chinese===
奸细悻悻地耸了耸肩，霍地转身，径直走向地下铁道的小亭入口，头也不回地钻了进去。
两分钟后，一列北去的火车轰隆隆碾过，兰亚德脚下的地面都震颤起来。
他又多等了三分钟；埃克斯特罗姆没有再露面；他终于确信自己的警告已然奏效，便转身走掉了。

## XXVI The Flying Death / 二十六、飞来的死神

===Original===
Steadying himself with a splendid display of self-control and sheer courage, Captain Vauquelin concentrated upon the management of the biplane.
The drone of its motor thickened again, its speed became greater, and the machine began to rise still higher, tracing a long, graceful curve.
Lanyard glanced apprehensively toward the girl, but apparently she remained unconscious of anything out of the ordinary. Her face was still turned forward, and still the wind-veil trembled against her glowing cheeks.
Thanks to the racket of the motor, no audible reports had accompanied the sharpshooting of the man in the monoplane; while Lanyard’s cry of horror and dismay had been audible to himself exclusively. Hearing nothing, Lucy suspected nothing.
Again Lanyard looked back.
Now the *Valkyr* seemed to have crept up to within the quarter of a mile of the biplane, and was boring on at a tremendous pace, its single spread of wings on an approximate level with that of the lower plane of the *Parrott*.

===Chinese===
沃克兰上尉拿出十足的自制与胆气，稳住心神，专心致志地操纵起双翼机来。
发动机的嗡鸣重新沉厚起来，机速愈增，机身划出一道悠长而优美的弧线，继续向更高处攀升。
兰亚德忧心忡忡地瞥了姑娘一眼，可她显然并未察觉任何异样。她的脸依旧朝前望着，防风面纱仍贴着她红扑扑的双颊颤动。
多亏发动机的轰响，单翼机上那人打出的冷枪没有半点枪声可闻；而兰亚德那声惊骇绝望的喊叫，也只有他一个人听得见。什么也没听见，露西便什么也没有起疑。
兰亚德再度回头望去。
这时*瓦尔基里*（*Valkyr*）号似乎已悄然逼近到双翼机四分之一英里之内，正以惊人的速度一味猛钻，那一整片单翼已大致与*帕罗特*（*Parrott*）的下层翼面齐平。

===Original===
But this last was rising steadily.﻿ ﻿…
The driver’s seat of the *Valkyr* held a muffled, burly figure that might be anybody﻿—De Morbihan, Ekstrom, or any other homicidal maniac. At the distance its actions were as illegible as their results were unquestionable: Lanyard saw a little tongue of flame lick out from a point close beside the head of the figure﻿—he couldn’t distinguish the firearm itself﻿—and, like Vauquelin, quite without premeditation, he ducked.
At the same time there sounded a harsh, ripping noise immediately above his head; and he found himself staring up at a long ragged tear in the canvas, caused by the bullet striking it aslant.
“What’s to be done?” he screamed passionately at Vauquelin.
The aviator shook his head impatiently; and they continued to ascend; already the web of gold that cloaked earth and sea seemed thrice as far beneath their feet as it had when Vauquelin made the appalling discovery of his bullet-punctured sleeve.

===Chinese===
可这架*帕罗特*仍在稳步爬升。……
*瓦尔基里*号的驾驶座里裹着一个裹束严实的魁梧身影，是谁都有可能——德·莫尔比昂、埃克斯特罗姆，或是随便哪个杀人狂。隔着这样的距离，那人的动作无从辨认，动作的结果却无可置疑：兰亚德看见一小条火舌自那人头侧不远处舔出——枪本身他分辨不出——于是便像沃克兰方才那样，全然未加思索，一缩脖颈。
与此同时，他头顶正上方响起一声刺耳的撕裂之音；他抬头望去，只见机翼蒙皮上豁开一道歪斜的长口子，正是子弹斜穿而过的手笔。
「这可怎么办？」他情绪激越地朝沃克兰尖叫。
飞行员不耐烦地摇了摇头；他们继续上升；那罩着大地与海洋的金色罗网，此刻已在脚下远出三倍——比起沃克兰骇然发现自己袖上弹孔的当口。

===Original===
But the monoplane was doggedly following suit; as the *Parrott* rose, so did the *Valkyr*, if a trace more slowly and less flexibly.
Lanyard had read somewhere, or heard it said, that monoplanes were poor machines for climbing. He told himself that, if this were true, Vauquelin knew his business; and from this reflection drew what comfort he might.
And he was glad, very glad of the dark wind-veil that shrouded his face, which he believed to be nothing less than a mask of panic terror.
He was, in fact, quite rigid with fright and horror. It were idle to argue that only unlikely chance would wing one of the bullets from the *Valkyr* to a vital point: there was the torn canvas overhead, there was that hole through Vauquelin’s sleeve.﻿ ﻿…
And then the barograph on the strut beside Lanyard disappeared as if by magic. He was aware of a slight jar; the framework of the biplane quivered as from a heavy blow; something that resembled a handful of black crumbs sprayed out into the air ahead and vanished: and where the instrument had been, nothing remained but an iron clamp gripping the strut.

===Chinese===
可单翼机偏不死心，步步效尤；*帕罗特*升多高，*瓦尔基里*也跟多高，只是略嫌迟缓僵硬了些。
兰亚德从前在哪里读到过，或者听人说起过：单翼机不擅爬升。他暗自宽解：此话若当真，沃克兰便是行家；他从这层思量里挤出一点聊以自慰的东西来。
而且他庆幸，深深庆幸脸上罩着这方深色的防风面纱——他相信自己那张脸，此刻已俨然是一副惊惶失措的面具了。
事实上，他早已吓得不折不扣地僵在了那里。若还要强辩，说什么*瓦尔基里*的子弹须靠万难有一的侥幸才能凑巧命中要害，那是徒费唇舌：头顶那道撕裂的蒙皮便是明证，沃克兰袖上的弹孔也是明证。……
紧接着，兰亚德手边撑杆上的自记气压计（barograph）竟像变戏法似的没了踪影。他觉察到轻轻一震；双翼机的骨架猛地一颤，像挨了重重一击；一把仿佛黑面包屑似的东西喷洒在前方的空中，倏忽消散：仪器原先所在之处，只剩下一只咬定撑杆的铁夹子。

===Original===
And even as any one of these bullets might have proved fatal, their first successor might disable the aviator if it did not slay him outright; in either case, the inevitable result would be death following a fall from a height, as recorded on the barograph dial an instant before its destruction, of more than four thousand feet.
They were still climbing.﻿ ﻿…
Now the pursuer was losing some of the advantage of his superior speed; the *Parrott* was perceptibly higher; the *Valkyr* must needs mount in a more sweeping curve.
None the less, Lanyard, peering down, saw still another tongue of flame spit out at him; and two bullet-holes appeared in the port-side wings of the biplane, one in the lower, one in the upper spread of canvas.
White-lipped and trembling, the adventurer began to work at the fastenings of his surtout. After a moment he plucked off one of his gloves and cast it impatiently from him. A-sprawl, it sailed down the wind like a wounded sparrow. He caught Vauquelin’s eye upon him, quick with a curiosity which changed to a sudden gleam of comprehension as Lanyard, thrusting his hand under the leather coat, groped for his pocket and produced an automatic pistol which Ducroy had pressed upon his acceptance.

===Chinese===
而这些子弹，随便哪一颗都足以致命，下一颗更可能叫飞行员伤重失能——纵不当场毙命；无论哪种情形，结局都逃不开自高空坠跌而死——那高度，仪器毁灭前的一瞬还记在自记气压计的刻度盘上：四千余英尺。
他们还在爬升。……
这时追击者凭速度占先的优势已折损了几分；*帕罗特*明显高出一截；*瓦尔基里*只得兜一个更宽的弧圈往上攀升。
尽管如此，兰亚德俯身下望，仍看见又一束火舌朝他啐来；双翼机左舷机翼上随即现出两个弹孔，一个在下层翼面，一个在上层翼面。
这位冒险家嘴唇刷白、浑身发颤，动手去解皮质外套（surtout）的纽襻。片刻之后，他索性褪下一只手套，不耐烦地甩了出去。手套四仰八叉地随风飘坠，活像一只受伤的麻雀。他察觉沃克兰正拿眼睛盯着他，那目光先是一团急切的好奇，待兰亚德把手伸进这件皮外套、摸向口袋、掏出迪克罗硬塞给他的一支自动手枪时，那好奇蓦地化作一道恍然的神光。

===Original===
They were now perhaps a hundred feet higher than the *Valkyr*, which was soaring a quarter of a mile off to starboard. Under the guidance of the Frenchman, the *Parrott* swooped round in a narrow circle until it hung almost immediately above the other﻿—a manoeuvre requiring, first and last, something more than five minutes to effect.
Meanwhile, Lanyard rebuttoned his surtout and clutched the pistol, trying hard not to think. But already his imagination was sick with the thought of what would ensue when the time came for him to carry out his purpose.
Vauquelin touched his arm with urgent pressure; but Lanyard only shook his head, gulped, and without looking surrendered the weapon to the aviator.﻿ ﻿…
Bearing heavily against the chest-band, he commanded the broad white spread of the *Valkyr*’s back and wings. Invisible beneath these hung the motor and driver’s seat.
An instant more, and he was aware that Vauquelin was leaning forward and looking down.

===Chinese===
这时他们已比*瓦尔基里*高出约莫一百英尺，那架单翼机正在右舷四分之一英里外翱翔。在这位法国人的操纵之下，*帕罗特*兜出一个狭窄的圈子，直转到几乎正悬在对方头顶——这一番机动，前后足足费了五分多钟。
这工夫，兰亚德重新扣好外套，攥紧手枪，拼命不叫自己去多想。可他的想象力早已恶心欲呕地预演起来：待到动手实行他那个打算的时刻一到，将会是怎样的光景。
沃克兰急切地按了按他的手臂；兰亚德却只是摇头，咽了一口唾沫，看也不看，把手枪让给了飞行员。……
他沉沉地压着胸前的保险背带，*瓦尔基里*号宽阔的白色脊背与双翼尽收眼底。那下面悬着的发动机与驾驶座，则一概隐没不见。
又过一瞬，他察觉沃克兰正俯身向下张望。

===Original===
Aiming with what deliberation was possible, the aviator emptied the clip of its eight cartridges in less than a minute.
The vicious reports rang out against the drum of the motor like the cracking of a blacksnake-whip.
Momentarily, Lanyard doubted if any one bullet had taken effect. He could not, with his swimming vision, detect sign of damage in the canvas of the *Valkyr*.
He saw the empty automatic slip from Vauquelin’s numb and nerveless fingers. It vanished.﻿ ﻿…
A frightful fascination kept his gaze constant to the soaring *Valkyr*.

===Chinese===
飞行员竭尽所能地从容瞄准，不到一分钟，便把弹匣里的八发子弹打了精光。
一声声狠辣的枪响撞在发动机的轰鸣鼓面上，宛如黑蛇长鞭炸响。
一时间，兰亚德疑心竟没有一颗子弹奏效。他两眼发花，实在看不出*瓦尔基里*的蒙皮上有半点损伤的迹象。
他看见那支打空了的自动手枪从沃克兰麻木无力的指间滑落。它消失了。……
一种骇人的魅惑攫住了他，教他目不转睛地盯住那架翱翔的*瓦尔基里*号。

===Original===
Beyond it, down, deep down a mile of emptiness, was that golden floor of tumbled cloud, waiting﻿ ﻿…
He saw the monoplane check abruptly in its strong onward surge﻿—as if it had run, full-tilt, head-on, against an invisible obstacle﻿—and for what seemed a round minute it hung so, veering and wobbling, nuzzling the wind. Then like a sounding whale it turned and dived headlong, propeller spinning like a top.
Down through the eighth of a mile of space it plunged plummet-like; then, perhaps caught in a flaw of wind, it turned sideways and began to revolve, at first slowly, but with increasing rapidity in its fatally swift descent.
Toward the beginning of its revolutions, something was thrown off, something small, dark and sprawling﻿ ﻿… like that glove which Lanyard had discarded. But this object dropped with a speed even greater than that of the *Valkyr*, in a brace of seconds had diminished to the proportions of a gnat, in another was engulfed in that vast sea of golden vapour.
Even so the monoplane itself, scarcely less precipitate, spun down through the abyss and plunged to oblivion in the fog-rack.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
在它之外，向下，向下一英里的空落深处，便是那片乱云堆叠的金色地板，正静候着……
他看见那架单翼机在强劲的前冲之势里猛地一顿——仿佛全速迎头撞上了一堵看不见的障碍物——就那么悬了仿佛整整一分钟，摇摇晃晃，左右打摆，用鼻子蹭着风。随后，像一头深潜入水的鲸，它翻身直坠，螺旋桨陀螺似的狂转。
它铅锤似的直插而下，穿落八分之一英里的空域；随即许是叫一阵骤风兜住，它侧过身来，旋转起来，起初尚慢，在那致命的疾坠中越转越急。
将转未转之际，有什么东西被甩了出来，一样小小的、黑黑的、四肢摊开的东西……很像兰亚德丢开的那只手套。只是这东西下坠得比*瓦尔基里*号还急，转眼两秒之间便缩成了蚊蚋的一点，再一转眼，已叫那片浩瀚的金色云海吞没。
单翼机自己也相差无几，旋着圈子坠下深渊，一头扎进云雾，从此湮没无闻。……

===Original===
And Lanyard was still hanging against the chest-band, limp and spent and trying not to vomit, when, of a sudden and without any warning whatever, the stentorian chant of the motor ceased and was blotted up by that immense silence, by the terrible silence of those vast solitudes of the upper air, where never a sound is heard save the voices of the elements at war among themselves: a silence that rang with an accent as dreadful as the crack of Doom in the ears of those three suspended there, in the heart of that unimaginably pellucid and immaculate radiance, in the vast hollow of the heavens, midway between the deep blue of the eternal dome and the rose and golden welter of the fog﻿—that fog which, cloaking earth and sea, hid as well every vestige of the tragedy they had wrought, every sign of the murder that they had done that they themselves might not be murdered and cast down to destruction.
And, its propeller no longer gripping the air, the aeroplane drifted on at ever-lessening speed, until it had no way whatever and rested without motion of any sort; as it might have been in the cup of some mighty and invisible hand, held up to that stark and merciless light, under the passionless eye of the Infinite, to await a Judgment.﻿ ﻿…
Then, with a little shudder of hesitation, the planes dipped, inclined slightly earthwards, and began slowly and as if reluctantly to slip down the long and empty channels of the air.

===Chinese===
兰亚德还倚在胸带上，浑身瘫软，精疲力竭，强忍着不呕出来；就在这时，毫无半点征兆，发动机那洪钟般的吟唱戛然而止，被一片浩大的寂静吞没了——被上层空气那片浩渺旷野的可怕寂静吞没了；在那里，除了互相角斗的天籁之声，什么音响也听不见：那寂静在悬吊半空的三人耳中嗡然作响，其可怖不亚于末日审判的霹雳；他们悬在那团澄澈得不可思议、纤尘不染的光焰中心，悬在穹苍的巨大空腔里，上不沾永恒穹顶的深蓝，下不沾云雾翻腾的玫瑰金——那片雾罩着大地与海洋，也一并掩去了他们一手酿成的惨剧的每一丝痕迹，掩去了他们行凶杀人的每一个征状——他们杀人，为的正是自己不遭人毒手，不被人打下去落个粉身碎骨。
螺旋桨不再咬住空气，飞机便越飘越慢，直到彻底失了速，再无一丝去势，纹丝不动地悬在那里；仿佛托在某只伟力无穷的无形巨掌的手窝里，被举向那凛冽无情的天光，处在「无限」漠然的注视之下，静候一场审判。……
随后，机翼微微一颤，像踌躇了一下，朝地面略一侧倾，缓缓地、仿佛老大不情愿似的，顺着空气那长长的空落落的航道滑降下去。

===Original===
At this, rousing, Lanyard became aware of his own voice yammering wildly at Vauquelin:
“Good God, man! Why did you do that?”
Vauquelin answered only with a pale grimace and a barely perceptible shrug.
Momentarily gathering momentum, the biplane sped downward with a resistless rush, with the speed of a great wind﻿—a speed so great that when Lanyard again attempted speech, the breath was whipped from his lips and he could utter no sound.
Thus from that awful height, from the still heart of that immeasurable void, they swept down and ever down, in a long series of sickening swoops, broken only by negligible pauses. And though they approached it on a long slant, the floor of vapour rose to meet them like a mighty rushing wave: in a trice the biplane was hovering instantaneously before plunging on down into that cold, grey world of fog.

===Chinese===
这一下惊醒过来，兰亚德才发觉自己的嗓门正对着沃克兰声嘶力竭地嚷嚷：
「天哪，老兄！你干吗要那么干？」
沃克兰只回他一个发白的苦脸和一丝几乎觉察不出的耸肩。
双翼机越冲越快，其势沛然莫御，挟着大风般的速度直坠而下——那速度疾到这般地步：兰亚德再想开口时，气息立时从唇边被抽走，一个字也吐不出来。
就这样，从那骇人的高度，从那不可测度的虚空的静默中心，他们一浪又一浪地俯冲而下，每一浪都令人作呕，中间只隔着些微不足道的间歇。虽然他们是循着长长的斜线接近它，那片雾的地板却像一堵奔涌而至的巨浪迎面掀起：转眼之间，双翼机倏然悬停了一瞬，随即一头再扎进那寒冷灰白的雾之世界。

===Original===
In that moment of hesitation, while still the adventurer gasped for breath and pawed at his streaming eyes with an aching hand, pierced through and through with cold, the fog showed itself as something less substantial than it had seemed; blurs of colour glowed through its folds of gauze, and with these the rounded summit of a brownish knoll.
Then they plunged on, down out of the bleak, bright sunshine into cool twilight depths of clinging vapours; and the good green earth lifted its warm bosom to receive them.
Tilting its nose a trifle, fluttering as though undecided, the *Parrott* settled gracefully, with scarcely a jar, upon a wide sweep of untilled land covered with short coarse grass.
For some time the three remained in their perches like petrified things, quite moveless and﻿—with the possible exception of the aviator﻿—hardly conscious.
But presently Lanyard became aware that he was regularly filling his lungs with air sweet, damp, wholesome, and by comparison warm, and that the blood was tingling painfully in his half-frozen hands and feet.

===Chinese===
就在那悬停的一瞬，冒险家还在喘息，用一只冻得生疼的手抹着泪流不止的眼睛，寒气已把他浸得透透的；这时，雾露出了原形，远不似方才看上去那么厚实：一团团模糊的色块透过它的纱褶隐隐发亮，与这些色块一道显现的，还有一座黄褐色圆丘浑圆的顶。
随后他们继续下扎，脱出那片荒冷明亮的日光，扎进缠人的雾气的清凉幽深处；而美好的绿色大地挺起温暖的胸膛，来承接他们。
机头微微一扬，颤颤地像是拿不定主意，*帕罗特*随即姿态优雅地落了下来，几乎不带一丝震动，停在一片开阔的未垦地上，地上覆着短短的粗草。
好一阵子，三个人都像石化了一般呆坐在各自的座架上，纹丝不动——飞行员或许除外——几乎失了知觉。
可是不多时，兰亚德便觉出，自己正一口又一口地把甘甜、湿润、洁净而相比之下尚称温暖的空气吸进肺里；血脉在他半僵的手脚里奔涌，胀得生疼。

===Original===
He sighed as one waking from a strange dream.
At the same time the aviator bestirred himself, and began a bit stiffly to climb down.
Feeling the earth beneath his feet, he took a step or two away from the machine, reeling and stumbling like a drunken man, then turned back.
“Come, my friend!” he urged Lanyard in a voice of strangely normal intonation﻿—“look alive﻿—if you’re able﻿—and lend me a hand with mademoiselle. I’m afraid she has fainted.”
The girl was reclining inertly in the bands of webbing, her eyes closed, her lips ajar, her limbs slackened.

===Chinese===
他叹了一口气，像一个从奇梦中醒来的人。
与此同时，飞行员也振作起来，颇有些僵硬地开始往下爬。
双脚一踏着实地，他便踉踉跄跄、跌跌撞撞，像个醉汉似的走出机身一两步，又回转身来。
「来呀，我的朋友！」他用一种出奇如常的声调催促兰亚德——「打起精神来——要是还撑得住——帮我搭把手，照应一下小姐。我怕她是晕过去了。」
姑娘软软地瘫倚在织带之间，双目阖着，嘴唇微启，四肢松垂。

===Original===
“Small blame to her!” Lanyard commented, fumbling clumsily with the chest-band. “That dive was enough to drive a body mad!”
“But I had to do it!” the aviator protested earnestly. “I dared not remain longer up there. I have never before been afraid in the air, but after *that* I was terribly afraid. I could feel myself going﻿—taking leave of my senses﻿—and I knew I must act if we were not to follow that other﻿ ﻿… God! what a death!”
He paused, shuddered, and drew the back of his hand across his eyes before continuing: “So I cut off the ignition and volplaned. Here﻿—my hand. So-o! All right, eh?”
“Oh, I’m all right,” Lanyard insisted confidently.
But his confidence was belied by a look of daze; for the earth was billowing and reeling round him as though bewitched; and before he knew what had happened he sat down hard and stared foolishly up at the aviator.

===Chinese===
「这也怨不得她！」兰亚德一面笨手笨脚地解胸带，一面议论道。「方才那一俯冲，够把大活人逼疯！」
「可我不得不那么做呀！」飞行员恳切地分辩。「我不敢再在上头多待一刻。我在空中从来不曾害怕，可经过*那*一场，我怕得要死。我觉出自己在垮——神志在一寸寸离开我——我明白非行动不可，不然我们就得跟着那一架去……天哪！那是怎样一种死法！」
他顿了顿，打了个寒噤，用手背抹了抹眼睛，方才接着说：「于是我切断了点火，一路滑翔（volplane）下来。来——拉住我的手。好——嘞！没事了吧，嗯？」
「噢，我好着呢。」兰亚德煞有介事地一口咬定。
可他一脸茫然，把这番自信当场拆穿；大地在他四周翻腾打转，像中了邪似的；还没弄明白出了什么事，他已重重坐倒在地，傻呵呵地仰望着飞行员。

===Original===
“Here!” said the latter courteously, his wind-mask hiding a smile﻿—“my hand again, monsieur. You’ve endured more than you know. And now for mademoiselle.”
But when they approached the girl, she surprised both by shivering, sitting up, and obviously pulling herself together.
“You feel better now, mademoiselle?” Vauquelin enquired, hastening to loosen her fastenings.
“I’m better﻿—yes, thank you,” she admitted in a small, broken voice﻿—“but not yet quite myself.”

===Chinese===
「来！」后者客气地说，防风面罩底下掩着一丝笑意——「再拉一把我的手，先生。您受的罪，比您自己知道的还多。这就去照应小姐吧。」
可待他们走到姑娘跟前，她却打个寒颤坐起身来，分明在强打精神，倒叫两人都吃了一惊。
「您这会儿好些了么，小姐？」沃克兰一边问，一边赶紧上前替她松解带扣。
「好些了——是的，谢谢您，」她声音细弱、断断续续地认了——「只是还不全是我自己。」

===Original===
She gave a hand to the aviator, the other to Lanyard, and as they helped her to the ground, Lanyard, warned by his experience, stood by with a ready arm.
She needed that support, and for a few minutes didn’t seem even conscious of it. Then gently disengaging, she moved a foot or two away.
“Where are we﻿—do you know?”
“On the South Downs, somewhere?” Lanyard suggested, consulting Vauquelin.
“That is probable,” this last affirmed﻿—“at all events, judging from the course I steered. Somewhere well in from the coast, at a venture; I don’t hear the sea.”
“Near Lewes, perhaps?”

===Chinese===
她一只手交予飞行员，另一只交予兰亚德；两人扶她下地时，兰亚德吃一堑长一智，早备好一条胳膊在旁伺候着。
她当真少不了那一点搀扶，有几分钟，连自己被搀着似乎都不曾觉察。随后她轻轻抽身，挪开一两步远。
「我们这是在哪儿——你们知道么？」
「多半在南丘原（South Downs）的什么地方吧？」兰亚德建议道，一面征询地望着沃克兰。
「想来是这么回事，」后者予以坐实——「至少，照我掌的航向判断。瞎猜的话，此地离海岸已颇有些纵深；我听不见海声。」
「兴许在刘易斯（Lewes）附近？」

===Original===
“I have no reason to doubt that.”
