A constrained pause ensued. The girl looked from the aviator to Lanyard, then turned away from both and, trembling with fatigue and enforcing self-control by clenching her hands, stared aimlessly off into the mist.
Painfully, Lanyard set himself to consider their position.
The *Parrott* had come to rest in what seemed to be a wide, shallow, saucer-like depression, whose irregular bounds were cloaked in fog. In this space no living thing stirred save themselves; and the waste was crossed by not so much as a sheep track. In brief, they were lost. There might be a road running past the saucer ten yards from its brim in any quarter. There might not. Possibly there was a town or village immediately adjacent. Quite as possibly the Downs billowed away for desolate miles on either hand.
“Well﻿—what do we do now?” the girl demanded suddenly, in a nervous voice, sharp and jarring.

===Chinese===
「我没有理由怀疑这个。」
接着是一阵局促的沉默。姑娘的目光从飞行员移到兰亚德身上，随后干脆从两人身上都移开，累得发着抖，攥紧双手强自镇定，茫然望进雾里去。
兰亚德打起精神，苦苦掂量起他们的处境来。
*帕罗特*降落之处，像是一口宽而浅的碟形洼地，四缘参差，尽数裹在雾里。这片天地间，除他们自己外没有一个活物走动；这片荒地上，连一条羊肠小径也不曾划过。简而言之，他们迷路了。也许在洼地边沿随便哪个方位的十码开外，便有一条大路经过。也许没有。也许紧挨着便有一座城镇或村庄。也一样可能，丘原在左右两侧荒荒凉凉地绵延出去好几英里。
「那么——眼下我们怎么办？」姑娘突然发问，嗓音紧张，又尖利又刺耳。

===Original===
“Oh, we’ll find a way out of this somehow,” Vauquelin asserted confidently. “England isn’t big enough for anybody to remain lost in it﻿—not for long, at all events. I’m sorry only on Miss Shannon’s account.”
“We’ll manage, somehow,” Lanyard affirmed stoutly.
The aviator smiled curiously. “To begin with,” he advanced, “I daresay we might as well get rid of these awkward costumes. They’ll hamper walking﻿—rather.”
In spite of his fatigue Lanyard was so struck by the circumstances that he couldn’t help remarking it as he tore off his wind-veil.
“Your English is remarkably good, Captain Vauquelin,” he observed.

===Chinese===
「噢，我们总归能想出法子脱身，」沃克兰满怀把握地断言。「英格兰还不够大，容不得一个人在里面迷路——至少，迷不了多久。我只是替香农小姐过意不去。」
「我们总会有办法的。」兰亚德斩钉截铁地说。
飞行员露出一丝古怪的笑。「首先，」他提议，「我看咱们不妨先把这些累赘的行头卸了。穿着它们赶路——可够碍事的。」
兰亚德尽管疲惫不堪，心头却为此中情由所动，扯下防风面纱时，忍不住说了出来。
「您的英语说得真不错，沃克兰上尉。」他说。

===Original===
The other laughed shortly.
“Why not?” said he, removing his mask.
Lanyard looked up into his face, stared, and fell back a pace.
“Wertheimer!” he gasped.

===Chinese===
对方短促地笑了一声。
「有什么不可以？」他说着，摘下了面罩。
兰亚德抬眼望进那张脸，怔住了，倒退了一步。
「维特海默！」他倒抽一口冷气。

## XVII The Forlorn Hope / 十七、孤注一掷

===Original===
Established behind his splendid mahogany desk in his office at the Ministère de la Guerre, or moving majestically abroad attired in frock coat and glossy topper, or lending the dignity of his presence to some formal ceremony in that beautiful uniform which appertained unto his office, Monsieur Hector Ducroy cut an imposing figure.
Abed﻿ ﻿… it was sadly otherwise.
Lanyard switched on the bedside light, turning it so that it struck full upon the face of the sleeper; and as he sat down, smiled.
The Minister of War lay upon his back, his distinguished corpulence severely dislocating the chaste simplicity of the bed-clothing. Athwart his shelving chest, fat hands were folded in a gesture affectingly naive. His face was red, a noble highlight shone upon the promontory of his bald pate, his mouth was open. To the best of his unconscious ability he was giving a protracted imitation of a dogfight; and he was really exhibiting sublime virtuosity: one readily distinguished individual howls, growls, yelps, against an undertone of blended voices of excited noncombatants﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
埃克托·迪克罗先生（Monsieur Hector Ducroy）端坐在陆军部（Ministère de la Guerre）办公室那张气派的红木办公桌后，或身着长礼服、头戴锃亮的大礼帽，威严地出外公干，或穿着与其官职相称的那身漂亮制服，莅临某场隆重典礼为仪式增光添彩——无论哪种场合，他都称得上仪表堂堂。
卧床之际……那可就惨不忍睹了。
兰亚德拧亮床头灯，把灯罩拨得灯光直射熟睡者的脸；他一面坐下，一面微笑。
陆军部长仰面朝天躺着，那副尊贵的臃肿身躯把素净的被褥挤得不成体统。在他那面缓缓斜降的胸膛上，两只胖手交叠着，姿态天真得感人。他满脸通红，秃脑袋那片海岬上高悬着一道堂皇的亮光，嘴巴张着。他正凭着昏睡中的全副本领，长篇累牍地模拟一场狗子打架；而且当真展现了出神入化的技艺：在一群激动的非战斗人员混杂声浪的底噪之上，一声声各自的嗥叫、咆哮、哀吠，皆清晰可辨……

===Original===
As suddenly as though someone, wearying of the entertainment, had lifted the needle from that record, it was discontinued. The Minister of War stirred uneasily in his sleep, muttered a naughty word, opened one eye, scowled, opened the other.
He blinked furiously, half-blinded but still able to make out the disconcerting silhouette of a man seated just beyond the glare: a quiet presence that moved not but eyed him steadfastly; an apparition the more arresting because of its very immobility.
Rapidly the face of the Minister of War lost several shades of purple. He moistened his lips nervously with a thick, dry tongue, and convulsively he clutched the bed-clothing high and tight about his neck, as though labouring under the erroneous impression that the sanctity of his person was threatened.
“What do you want, monsieur?” he stuttered in a still, small voice which he would have been the last to acknowledge his own.

===Chinese===
这演奏戛然而止，仿佛有谁听腻了这出余兴，把唱针从那张唱片上提了起来。陆军部长在睡梦中不安地动了动，咕哝出一句不干不净的字眼，睁开一只眼，皱起眉头，又睁开了另一只。
他拼命眨眼，被灯光晃得半瞎，却仍辨得出刺目强光之外端坐着一个令人心慌的人影：那是一团安安静静的存在，纹丝不动，只把一双眼睛牢牢盯住他；恰恰因为这份纹丝不动，这幽灵才愈发慑人。
陆军部长的脸色飞快地褪去了好几层紫。他用肥厚干燥的舌头紧张地舔了舔嘴唇，又抽搐似的把被褥高高拽起，紧紧勒住脖颈，仿佛误以为他这具肉身的圣洁正遭威胁。
「您、您想要什么，先生？」他结结巴巴地问，声音细若蚊蝇——打死他也不会承认那是自己的嗓音。

===Original===
“I desire to discuss a matter of business with monsieur,” replied the intruder after a small pause. “If you will be good enough to calm yourself﻿—”
“I am perfectly calm﻿—”
But here the Minister of War verified with one swift glance an earlier impression, to the effect that the trespasser was holding something that shone with metallic lustre; and his soul began to curl up round the edges.
“There are eighteen hundred francs in my pocketbook﻿—about,” he managed to articulate. “My watch is on the stand here. You will find the family plate in the dining-room safe, behind the buffet﻿—the key is on my ring﻿—and the jewels of madame my wife are in a small strongbox beneath the head of her bed. The combination﻿—”

===Chinese===
「在下想与先生谈一桩买卖，」不速之客略停了停，答道。「倘蒙先生俯允，先定一定神——」
「我镇定得很——」
可就在这时，陆军部长眼光疾扫，证实了先前的一个印象：那闯入者手里果真握着一件泛出金属光泽的东西；于是他的灵魂开始沿着四边卷了起来。
「我皮夹子里有一千八百法郎——大约，」他好容易才把字吐清楚。「我的表就搁在这小几上。家里的银餐具在饭厅的保险柜里，在碗柜后头——钥匙串在我的钥匙环上——我夫人的首饰在她床头底下的一只小铁箱里。那开箱的暗码是——」

===Original===
“Pardon: monsieur labours under a misapprehension,” the housebreaker interposed drily. “Had one desired these valuables, one would readily have taken them without going to the trouble of disturbing the repose of monsieur.﻿ ﻿… I have, however, already mentioned the nature of my errand.”
“Eh?” demanded the Minister of War. “What is that? But give me of your mercy one chance to explain! I have never wittingly harmed you, monsieur, and if I have done so without my knowledge, rest assured you have but to petition me through the proper channels and I will be only too glad to make amends!”
“*Still* you do not listen!” the other insisted. “Come, Monsieur Ducroy﻿—calm yourself. I have not robbed you, because I have no wish to rob you. I have not harmed you, for I have no wish to harm you. Nor have I any wish other than to lay before you, as representing Government, a certain matter of State business.”

===Chinese===
「得罪：先生误会了，」这位入室之客干巴巴地插话。「在下若存心要这些东西，顺手拿了就是，犯不上费事搅扰先生的清梦。……不过在下方才已经说明过，此行所为何来。」
「啊？」陆军部长追问道。「此话怎讲？只求您发发慈悲，容我分辩一句！我从不曾有意加害先生；倘若无心开罪了您，您只管循正当途径向我陈情，我必欣然补过，绝无二话！」
「您*还是*没听进去！」对方坚持道。「得了，迪克罗先生——定定神。我没有抢您，因为我无意抢您；我没有伤害您，因为我无意伤害您。我别无他想，只想把一桩国务摆到您面前——您乃政府的代表。」

===Original===
There was silence while the Minister of War permitted this exhortation to sink in. Then, apparently reassured, he sat up in bed and eyed his untimely visitor with a glare little short of truculent.
“Eh? What’s that?” he demanded. “Business? What sort of business? If you wish to submit to my consideration any matter of business, how is it you break into my home at dead of night and rouse me in this brutal fashion”﻿—here his voice faltered﻿—“with a lethal weapon pointed at my head?”
“Monsieur will admit he speaks under an error,” returned the burglar. “I have yet to point this pistol at him. I should be very sorry to feel obliged to do so. I display it, in fact, simply that monsieur may not forget himself and attempt to summon servants in his resentment of this (I admit) unusual method of introducing one’s self to his attention. When we understand each other better there will be no need for such precautions, and then I shall put my pistol away, so that the sight of it may no longer annoy monsieur.”
“It is true, I do not understand you,” grumbled the Minister of War. “Why﻿—if your errand be peaceable﻿—break into my house?”

===Chinese===
一时寂然，陆军部长由着这番劝导慢慢渗入心田。随后，他显然放了心，在床上坐起身来，拿一双眼色不逊的目光上下打量这位来得不是时候的访客。
「啊？什么？」他质问。「买卖？什么买卖？你若有什么买卖要呈交本席斟酌，何苦半夜三更闯进我的宅邸，用这等粗野的手段把我从睡梦里惊起来」——说到这里，他的嗓音虚了——「还拿一柄致命的凶器指着我的脑袋？」
「先生得承认，这话您说岔了，」窃贼答道。「我还不曾把这把手枪对准过先生。倘若逼得我非那么做不可，我会很过意不去的。我把它亮出来，无非是防着先生一时意气、忘乎所以，去唤仆人前来——毕竟（我承认）这种向先生自荐的方式是有些异乎寻常。等我们彼此多些了解，这等防备便属多余，那时我自会把手枪收起，免得它再碍先生的眼。」
「这话不假，我确实不明白你，」陆军部长嘟囔。「既然——你此来并无恶意——又何苦破门进我家？」

===Original===
“Because it was urgently necessary to see monsieur instantly. Monsieur will reflect upon the reception one would receive did one ring the front doorbell and demand audience at three o’clock in the morning!”
“Well﻿ ﻿…” Monsieur Ducroy conceded dubiously. Then, on reflection, he iterated the monosyllable testily: “Well! What is it you want, then?”
“I can best explain by asking monsieur to examine﻿—what I have to show him.”

===Chinese===
「因为有十万火急之事，必须立时面见先生。先生不妨想想：倘或有人按响正门门铃，赶凌晨三点钟求见，那会落得个什么接待！」
「唔……」迪克罗先生将信将疑地让了这一步。随即，再一寻思，又不耐烦地把这个单音节字重了一遍：「唔！那么你究竟要什么？」
「请先生检视一样东西——看看我要给先生看的是什么，这比任何解释都明白。」

===Original===
With this Lanyard dropped the pistol into his coat-pocket, from another produced a gold cigarette-case, and from the store of this last with meticulous care selected a single cigarette.
Regarding the Minister of War in a mystifying manner, he began to roll the cigarette briskly between his palms. A small shower of tobacco sifted to the floor: the rice-paper cracked and came away; and with the bland smile and gesture of a professional conjurer, Lanyard exhibited a small cylinder of stiff paper between his thumb and index-finger.
Goggling resentfully, Monsieur Ducroy spluttered:
“Eh﻿—what impudence is this?”

===Chinese===
说话间，兰亚德把手枪丢进外套口袋，又从另一只口袋里摸出一只金烟盒，从盒中之烟里以近乎苛刻的精心拣出一支香烟。
他神情高深莫测地望着陆军部长，把那支烟在两掌之间利落地搓动。一阵细细的烟丝簌簌筛落在地板上：烟纸绽裂，剥落开来；兰亚德带着职业魔术师那副和蔼的微笑与手势，在拇指与食指之间拈出一小卷硬纸。
迪克罗先生怒目圆睁，气急败坏地嚷道：
「啊——这算什么意思？」

===Original===
His smile unchanged, Lanyard bent forward and silently dropped the cylinder into the Frenchman’s hand. At the same time he offered him a pocket magnifying-glass.
“What is this?” Ducroy persisted stupidly. “What﻿—what﻿—!”
“If monsieur will be good enough to unroll the papers and examine them with the aid of this glass﻿—”
With a wondering grunt, the other complied, unrolling several small sheets of photographer’s printing-out paper, to which several extraordinarily complicated and minute designs had been transferred﻿—strongly resembling laborious efforts to conventionalize a spider’s web.

===Chinese===
兰亚德笑容不改，俯身把那纸卷无声地放进这位法国人的手心，同时递上一只袖珍放大镜。
「这是什么玩意儿？」迪克罗执拗地问，一脸的懵懂。「什么——什么——！」
「倘蒙先生把这卷纸展开，借这放大镜一观——」
对方惊奇地哼了一声，依言照办，展开几张摄影印相纸小片，上面转印着若干繁复精微得异乎寻常的图样——极像一番苦功，要把蛛网纳入图案的规范。

===Original===
But no sooner had Monsieur Ducroy viewed these through the glass, than he started violently, uttered an excited exclamation, and subjected them to an examination both prolonged and exacting.
“Monsieur is, no doubt, now satisfied?” Lanyard enquired when his patience would endure no longer.
“These are genuine?” the Minister of War demanded sharply, without looking up.
“Monsieur can readily discern notations made upon the drawings by the inventor, Georges Huysman, in his own hand. Furthermore, each plan has been marked in the lower left-hand corner with the word ‘*accepted*’ followed by the initials of the German Minister of War. I think this establishes beyond dispute the authenticity of these photographs of the plan for Huysman’s invention.”

===Chinese===
可迪克罗先生才透过放大镜看了一眼，便猛地一震，激动地失声叫喊，随即对那几张纸展开了一番既漫长又严苛的检视。
「先生如今，想必心满意足了？」兰亚德捱到耐心告罄，方才问道。
「这些东西是真的？」陆军部长厉声追问，头也不抬。
「先生不难辨认图上批注，皆出自发明人乔治·于伊斯曼（Georges Huysman）亲笔。再者，每张图纸左下角都标注『*验收合格*』（*accepted*）字样，其后缀有德国陆军部长姓名的首字母。我想，这几张于伊斯曼发明设计图照片的真伪，至此已无可置辩。」

===Original===
“Yes,” the Minister of War agreed breathlessly. “You have the negatives from which these prints were made?”
“Here,” Lanyard said, indicating a second cigarette.
And then, with a movement so leisurely and careless that his purpose was accomplished before the other in his preoccupation was aware of it, the adventurer leaned forward and swept up the prints from the counterpane in front of Monsieur Ducroy.
“Here!” the Frenchman exclaimed. “Why do you do that?”

===Chinese===
「不错，」陆军部长屏着气应道。「印出这些照片的底片也在你手里？」
「在这儿，」兰亚德说着，指了指第二支香烟。
接着，冒险家俯身向前，那动作从容随便之至，待对方从凝神中察觉时，大事已然办毕——迪克罗先生面前床单上的那几张照片，已被他一扫而回。
「哎！」法国人叫起来。「你这是干什么？」

===Original===
“Monsieur no longer questions their authenticity?”
“I grant you that.”
“Then I return to myself these prints, pending negotiations for their transfer to France.”
“How did you come by them?” demanded Monsieur Ducroy, after a moment’s thought.
“Need monsieur ask? Is France so ill-served by her spies that you do not already know of the misfortune one Captain Ekstrom recently suffered in London?”

===Chinese===
「先生不再置疑它们的真伪了？」
「这一点我认了。」
「那么，在商谈将它们移交法兰西的期间，恕我把这几张照片收回己有。」
「它们是怎么落到你手里的？」迪克罗先生寻思片刻，问道。
「这还用先生问？莫非法国的密探这般不济事，埃克斯特罗姆上尉新近在伦敦吃的亏，你们竟还一无所知？」

===Original===
Ducroy shook his head. Lanyard received this indication with impatience. It seemed hardly possible that the French Minister of War could be either so stupid or so ignorant.﻿ ﻿…
But with a patient shrug, he proceeded to elucidate.
“Captain Ekstrom,” he said, “but recently succeeded in photographing these plans and took them to London to sell to the English. Unfortunately for himself﻿—unhappily for perfidious Albion!﻿—Captain Ekstrom fell in with me and mistook me for Downing Street’s representative. And here are the plans.”

===Chinese===
迪克罗摇头。见了这等表示，兰亚德好不耐烦。这位法国陆军部长竟会这般愚蠢、这般闭目塞听，简直教人难以置信。……
但他耐心地耸了耸肩，径自解说下去。
「埃克斯特罗姆上尉，」他说，「新近才把这些图纸拍摄到手，带去伦敦预备售与英国人。于他自己，这是不幸——于背信弃义的阿尔比恩（perfidious Albion），也未必是福！——埃克斯特罗姆上尉中途遇上我，把我错认作唐宁街的代表。图纸于是便到了此地。」

===Original===
“You are﻿—the Lone Wolf﻿—then?”
“I am, as far as concerns you, monsieur, merely the person in possession of these plans, who offers them through you, to France, for a price.”
“But why introduce yourself to me in this extraordinary fashion, for a transaction for which the customary channels﻿—with which you must be familiar﻿—are entirely adequate?”
“Simply because Ekstrom has followed me to Paris,” Lanyard explained indulgently. “Did I venture to approach you in the usual way, my chances of rounding out a useful life thereafter would be practically nil. Furthermore, my circumstances are such that it has become necessary for me to leave France immediately﻿—without an hour’s delay﻿—also secretly; else I might as well remain here to be butchered.﻿ ﻿… Now you command the only means I know of, to accomplish my purpose. And that is the price, the only price, you will have to pay me for these plans.”

===Chinese===
「那么你就是——独狼？」
「就与先生相干的部分而言，我不过是持有这些图纸之人，愿通过先生之手，作价让与法兰西——如此而已。」
「可是这样一桩交易，循惯常的门路——这想必你是熟门熟路——尽可从容办妥，你何必用这种离奇的方式向我自荐？」
「不为别的，只因埃克斯特罗姆已把我一路跟到了巴黎，」兰亚德宽厚地解释道。「我若斗胆按寻常路数来接近先生，往后想安安稳稳了此余生的指望，便几乎等于零。再者，我眼下的处境，已到了必须即刻离开法国的地步——一个钟头也耽搁不得——还须悄悄地走；不然，倒不如干脆留下等着挨刀。……而今，据我所知，能成全我这桩心愿的门路，唯先生执掌在握。这便是价钱，先生为这几张图纸须付给我的唯一的价钱。」

===Original===
“I don’t understand you.”
“It is on schedule, is it not, that Captain Vauquelin of the Aviation Corps is to attempt a nonstop flight from Paris to London this morning, with two passengers, in a new *Parrott* biplane?”
“That is so.﻿ ﻿… Well?”
“I must be one of those passengers; and I have a companion, a young lady, who will take the place of the other.”
“It isn’t possible, monsieur. Those arrangements are already fixed.”
“You will countermand them.”

===Chinese===
「我不明白你的意思。」
「按日程，航空队的沃克兰上尉（Captain Vauquelin）今晨不是要驾驶一架新式*帕罗特*（*Parrott*）双翼机，搭载两名乘客，尝试自巴黎直飞伦敦不着陆么？」
「是有这么回事。……怎么样？」
「我必须是那两名乘客之一；我还有一位同伴，一位年轻小姐，将顶替另一个人的位子。」
「这不可能，先生。那些安排早已定死了。」
「先生把它们撤销便是。」

===Original===
“There is no time﻿—”
“You can get into telephonic communication with Port Aviation in two minutes.”
“But the passengers have been promised﻿—”
“You will disappoint them.”
“The start is to be made in the first flush of daylight. How could you reach Port Aviation in time?”
“In your motorcar, monsieur.”

===Chinese===
「来不及了——」
「两分钟之内，先生就能与航空港（Port Aviation）通上电话。」
「可那两个乘客是早已应承给人的——」
「先生教他们失望便是。」
「起飞定在天光初亮之时。你怎么来得及赶到航空港？」
「坐先生您的汽车去。」

===Original===
“It cannot be done.”
“It must! If the start must be delayed till we arrive, you will give orders that it shall be so delayed.”
For a minute the Minister of War hesitated; then he shook his head definitely.
“The difficulties are insuperable﻿—”
“There is no such thing, monsieur.”

===Chinese===
「这办不到。」
「非办到不可！倘若起飞非推迟到我们抵达不可，先生便下一道命令，教它推迟好了。」
陆军部长迟疑了一分钟，随即断然摇头。
「困难重重，无从克服——」
「世上没有『无从克服』这回事，先生。」

===Original===
“I am sorry: it can’t be done.”
“That is your answer?”
“It is regrettable, monsieur﻿ ﻿…”
“Very well!” Lanyard bent forward again, took a match from the stand on the bedside table, and struck it. Very calmly he advanced the flame toward the cigarette containing the roll of inflammable films.

===Chinese===
「恕难从命：办不到。」
「先生就这么答复我？」
「实为遗憾，先生……」
「很好！」兰亚德又俯身向前，从床头小几的搁架上取过一根火柴，划着了。他神色从容之极，把火苗慢慢凑向那支裹着易燃胶片卷的香烟。

===Original===
“Monsieur!” Ducroy cried in horror. “What are you doing?”
Lanyard favoured him with a look of surprise.
“I am about to destroy these films and prints.”
“You must never do that!”

===Chinese===
「先生！」迪克罗惊骇万状地喊道。「你要干什么？」
兰亚德回敬他一记诧异的目光。
「我正要销毁这些底片和照片。」
「那是万万使不得的！」

===Original===
“Why not? They are mine, to do with as I like. If I cannot dispose of them at my price, I shall destroy them!”
“But﻿—my God!﻿—what you demand is impossible! Stay, monsieur! Think what your action means to France!”
“I have already thought of that. Now I must think of myself.”
“But﻿—one moment!”

===Chinese===
「为何使不得？它们是我的东西，我爱怎么处置便怎么处置。倘若不能照我的价钱脱手，我就烧了它们！」
「可是——老天爷！——你所求的事根本办不到！慢着，先生！想一想你这举动对法兰西意味着什么！」
「这层我早已想过了。如今，我不得不想想我自己。」
「可是——且慢！」

===Original===
Ducroy sat up in bed and dangled hairy fat legs over the side.
“But one moment only, monsieur. Don’t make me waste your matches!”
“Monsieur, it shall be as you desire, if it lies in my power to accomplish it.”
With this the Minister of War stood up and made for the telephone, in his agitation forgetful of dressing-gown and slippers.

===Chinese===
迪克罗在床上坐起，两条毛茸茸的胖腿垂到床沿外。
「只许且慢片刻，先生。可别叫我白糟蹋了您的火柴！」
「先生，但凡力所能及，必当如你所愿。」
说罢，陆军部长起身径奔电话而去，慌乱之中竟忘了披晨衣、趿拖鞋。

===Original===
“You *must* accomplish it, Monsieur Ducroy,” Lanyard advised him gravely, puffing out the flame; “for if you fail, you make yourself the instrument of my death. Here are the plans.”
“You trust them to me?” Ducroy asked in astonishment.
“But naturally: that makes it an affair of your honour,” Lanyard explained suavely.
With a gesture of graceful capitulation the Frenchman accepted the little roll of film.

===Chinese===
「您*务必*办到，迪克罗先生，」兰亚德吹灭火苗，正色告诫道；「您若办砸，便等于亲手把自己变成送我上死路的凶器。图纸在这儿。」
「你就这样托付给我了？」迪克罗愕然问道。
「这是自然：如此一来，此事便成了关乎先生荣誉的事，」兰亚德温文地解释道。
法国人摆出一副优雅认输的手势，接过那一小卷胶片。

===Original===
“Permit me,” he said, “to acknowledge the honour of monsieur’s confidence!”
Lanyard bowed low: “One knows with whom one deals, monsieur!﻿ ﻿… And now, if you will be good enough to excuse me.﻿ ﻿…”
He turned to the door.
“But﻿—eh﻿—where are you going?” Ducroy demanded.

===Chinese===
「容在下，」他说，「对先生垂赐信任之荣，谨表谢忱！」
兰亚德深深一躬：「跟什么人打交道，在下心中有数，先生！……那么，倘蒙见谅，就此告退。……」
他转身向门口走去。
「可是——啊——您这是去哪儿？」迪克罗问。

===Original===
“Mademoiselle,” Lanyard said, pausing on the threshold﻿—“that is, the young lady who is to accompany me﻿—is waiting anxiously in the garden, out yonder. I go to find and reassure her and﻿—with your permission﻿—to bring her in to the library, where we will await monsieur when he has finished telephoning and﻿—ah﻿—repaired the deficiencies in his attire; which one trusts he will forgive one’s mentioning!”
He bowed again, impudently, gaily, and﻿—when the Minister of War looked up again sheepishly from contemplation of his naked shanks﻿—had vanished.
In high feather Lanyard made his way to a door at the rear of the house which gave upon the garden﻿—in his new social status of Governmental protégé disdaining any such a commonplace avenue as that conservatory window whose fastenings he had forced on entering. And boldly unbolting the door, he ran out into the night, to rejoin his beloved, like a man waking to new life.
But she was no more there: the bench was vacant, the garden deserted, the gateway yawning on the street.

===Chinese===
「小姐，」兰亚德在门槛上顿住脚——「也就是要陪我同行的那位年轻小姐——正在外头花园里焦急等候。我去寻她、安她的心，并——征得您的恩准——把她带进书房，在那儿恭候先生打完电话，并——啊——补缀一下衣装上的缺欠；此事冒昧提起，尚祈包涵！」
他又施了一礼，放肆而欢快；待陆军部长望着自己两条光腿、讪讪地再抬起头来时，人已杳然。
兰亚德兴高采烈，径自寻到宅子后部一扇通花园的门——如今他既新膺政府座上客的身份，便不屑再走那等俗气的路径，比如他进来时撬开窗闩的那扇温室花窗。他大大方方拉开门闩，奔跑着投入夜色，去与心上人重聚，恍如一个人一觉醒来重获新生。
可她已不在了：长凳空空如也，园中杳无人迹，院门朝街上洞开着。

===Original===
With a low, stifled cry, Lanyard turned from the bench and stumbled out to the junction of the cross-street. But nowhere in their several perspectives could he see anything that moved.
After some time he returned to the garden and quartered it with the thoroughness of a pointer beating a covert. But he did this hopelessly, bitterly aware that the outcome would be precisely what it eventually was, that is to say, nothing.﻿ ﻿…
He was kneeling beside the bench﻿—scrutinizing the turf with microscopic attention by aid of his flash-lamp, seeking some sign of struggle to prove she had not left him willingly, and finding none﻿—when a voice brought him momentarily out of his distraction.
He looked up wildly, to discover Ducroy standing over him, his stout person chastely swathed in a quilted dressing-gown and trousers, his expression one of stupefaction.

===Chinese===
兰亚德低低地、压抑地喊了一声，转身离开长凳，踉踉跄跄奔到横街交口。可无论顺着哪条街的纵深望去，都不见一个移动的人影。
半晌之后，他回到花园，像猎犬排搜树丛那样，把园子来回篦了个遍。但他做这一切时全然无望，心里悲苦地明白，结局必与最终的所得分毫不差——也就是说，一无所有。……
他正跪在长凳旁——借着袖珍手电，以显微镜般的专注细察草皮，想寻出一点挣扎的痕迹，好证明她并非自愿离他而去，却一无所获——一个声音蓦地将他从失魂落魄中唤起一瞬。
他狂乱地抬头，只见迪克罗立在跟前，肥硕的身躯端严地裹在绗缝晨衣与长裤里，满脸的目瞪口呆。

===Original===
“Well, monsieur﻿—well?” the Minister of War demanded irritably. “What﻿—I repeat﻿—what are you doing there?”
Lanyard essayed response, choked up, and gulped. He rose and stood swaying, showing a stricken face.
“Eh?” Ducroy insisted with an accent of exasperation. “Why do you stand glaring at me like that﻿—eh? Come, monsieur: what ails you? I have arranged everything, I say. Where is mademoiselle?”
Lanyard made a broken gesture.

===Chinese===
「怎么样了，先生——怎么样？」陆军部长焦躁地质问。「你——我再说一遍——你在那儿干什么？」
兰亚德想要答话，喉咙哽住，咽了一口。他起身立定，身形摇晃，一张脸如遭雷殛。
「啊？」迪克罗带着愠怒的口吻追问。「你干么这般瞪着我——啊？得了，先生：你这是怎么了？一切都安排妥当了，我说。小姐人在哪儿？」
兰亚德做了个残缺不全的手势。

===Original===
“Gone!” he muttered forlornly.
Instantly the countenance of the stout Frenchman was lightened with a gleam of eager interest﻿—inveterate romantic that he was!﻿—and he stepped nearer, peering closely into the face of the adventurer.
“Gone?” he echoed. “Mademoiselle? Your sweetheart, eh?”
Lanyard assented with a disconsolate nod and sigh. Impatiently Ducroy caught him by the sleeve.
“Come!” he insisted, tugging﻿—“but come at once into the house. Now, monsieur﻿—now at length you enlist all one’s sympathies! Come, I say! Is it your desire that I catch my death of cold?”
Indifferently Lanyard suffered himself to be led away. He was, indeed, barely conscious of what was happening. All his being was possessed by the thought that she had forsaken him. And he could well guess why: impossible for such an one as she to contemplate without a shudder association with the man who had been what he had been! Infatuate!﻿—to have dreamed that she would tolerate the devotion of a criminal, that she could ever forget his identity with the Lone Wolf. Inevitably﻿—soon or late﻿—she must have fled that ignominious thought in dread and horror, daring whatever consequences to escape and forget both it and him. And better now, perhaps, than later.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
「走了！」他凄然喃喃。
霎时间，这位胖法国人的脸上亮起一抹热切的兴味——他骨子里原是个积习已深的浪漫种子！——他趋步上前，凑近端详冒险家的脸。
「走了？」他应声道。「小姐？你的心上人，啊？」
兰亚德郁郁地点头长叹，权作应承。迪克罗不耐烦地一把攥住他的衣袖。
「来！」他拽着人不放——「这就随我进屋里去。到这地步，先生——你总算把人的满腔同情都勾起来了！来，我说！莫非你要存心叫我冻出人命不成？」
兰亚德浑浑噩噩地任人把自己领走。说实在的，他对眼前的一切几乎没了知觉。他整个身心只被一个念头牢牢盘踞：她弃他而去了。缘由他也猜得到：似她这等人，一想到与一个有着他那样过去的男人为伴，断无不寒而栗的道理！痴心妄想！——竟梦想她会容忍一个罪犯的痴恋，梦想她有朝一日能忘掉他就是独狼。或早或迟，她必然要在惊惧恐怖之中逃离那个可耻的念头，不计一切后果，只求躲开它、忘掉他。而眼下就走，或许还胜过日后再走。……

## VIII The High Hand / 八、强横手腕

===Original===
Evidently his first move toward departure was signalled; for as he passed out through L’Abbaye’s doors the carriage-porter darted forward and saluted.
“Monsieur Lanyarr’?”
“Yes?”
“Monsieur’s car is waiting.”
“Indeed?” Lanyard surveyed briefly a handsome black limousine that, at pause beside the curb, was champing its bits in the most spirited fashion. Then he smiled appreciatively. “All the same, I thank you for the compliment,” he said, and forthwith tipped the porter.
But before entrusting himself to this gratuitous conveyance, he put himself to the trouble of inspecting the chauffeur﻿—a capable-looking mechanic togged out in a rich black livery which, though relieved by a vast amount of silk braiding, was like the car guiltless of any sort of insignia.

===Chinese===
显然，他头一步告辞的动静早有人递了讯号出去；因为方才他一脚踏出「修道院街」（L'Abbaye）的大门，那停车侍者便一个箭步抢上前，恭恭敬敬行了个礼。
「Monsieur Lanyarr’？（兰亚尔先生？）」
「什么事？」
「先生的车驾已在恭候。」
「是吗？」兰亚德朝一辆气派的黑色豪华轿车略一打量——那车正停在路缘，发动机突突喷吐，活像一匹烈马咬着嚼子、跃跃欲试。随即他会心一笑。「不过这份恭维我领情，多谢了。」说罢便赏了那侍者。
不过，在把自己托付给这辆白来的代步之前，他还是不惮其烦，把司机端详了一番——一个看着干练的机工，一身华贵的黑号衣，虽说缀了大量丝绦聊作调剂，却同那辆车一样，清清白白，不沾半点徽记。

===Original===
“I presume you know where I wish to go, my man?”
The chauffeur touched his cap: “But naturally, monsieur.”
“Then take me there, the quickest way you know.”
Nodding acknowledgement of the porter’s salute, Lanyard sank gratefully back upon uncommonly luxurious upholstery. The fatigue of the last thirty-six hours was beginning to tell on him a bit, though his youth was still so vital, so instinct with strength and vigour, that he could go as long again without sleep if need be.
None the less he was glad of this opportunity to snatch a few minutes’ rest by way of preparation against the occult culmination of this adventure. No telling what might ensue of this violation of all those principles which had hitherto conserved his welfare! And he entertained a gloomy suspicion that he would be inclined to name another ass, who proposed as he did to beard this Pack in its den with nothing more than his wits and an automatic pistol to protect ten thousand-francs, the jewels of Madame Omber, the Huysman plans, and (possibly) his life.

===Chinese===
「我料想，你知道我想去哪儿吧，朋友？」
司机碰了碰帽檐：「那是自然，先生。」
「那就劳驾带我去，拣你知道的最近的路。」
兰亚德点头还了停车侍者的礼，心怀感慰地往后一靠，陷进异常奢华的坐垫里。过去三十六个钟头的疲惫已开始在他身上作祟，虽说他的青春依旧元气淋漓、浑身蓄满劲力，真有必要，再熬上同样久的时辰不睡也顶得住。
尽管如此，能抓住这个机会偷得几分钟的休憩，以备战这场奇遇玄奥的压轴戏，他还是乐得领受。他把迄今护佑自己平安的那些信条一概践踏无遗，往后会招出什么祸事，天晓得！何况他心里还憋着一句丧气话：换个人，像他这般做派——仅凭一副头脑、一支自动手枪，便要去群狼的窝里捋虎须，保住一万法郎、欧姆贝夫人的珠宝、于伊斯曼图纸，外带（说不定）自己一条性命——只怕他自己也要骂一声蠢驴。

===Original===
However, he stood committed to his folly, if folly it were: he would play the game as it lay.
As for curiosity concerning his immediate destination, there was little enough of that in his temper; a single glance round on leaving the car would fix his whereabouts beyond dispute, so thorough was his knowledge of Paris.
He contemplated briefly, with admiration, the simplicity with which that affair at L’Abbaye had been managed, finding no just cause to suspect anyone there of criminal complicity in the plans of the Pack: a forged order for a table to the maitre-d’hôtel, ten francs to the carriage-porter and twenty more to the dancing woman to play parts in a putative practical joke﻿—and the thing had been arranged without implicating a soul!﻿ ﻿…
Of a sudden, ending a ride much shorter than Lanyard would have liked, the limousine swung in toward a curb.

===Chinese===
话虽如此，这桩荒唐事——姑且算它荒唐——他既已应承在前，便无从反悔：牌是怎么摆下的，他就怎么打。
至于对眼下目的地的好奇，他性子里本就少得可怜；何况他对巴黎熟到家了，下车时只消四下一扫，身在何处便断得毫不含糊。
他略一回味，倒不由得佩服「修道院街」那件事办得何等简洁利落，实在找不出正当理由，怀疑那店里哪一位与群狼的图谋有犯罪的勾连：给餐厅总管递一张伪造的订座单，塞给停车侍者十法郎、再塞给那跳舞的女子二十法郎，请他们在一出想当然的恶作剧里各扮一角——
冷不防地，轿车一个转向，靠向了路缘——这趟车程，可比兰亚德巴望的短得多。

===Original===
Bending forward, he unlatched the door and, glancing through the window, uttered a grunt of profound disgust.
If this were the best that Pack could do﻿ ﻿… !
He had hoped for something a trifle more original from men with wit and imagination enough to plot the earlier phases of this intrigue.
The car had pulled up in front of an institution which he knew well﻿—far too well, indeed, for his own good.
None the less, he consented to get out.

===Chinese===
他俯身拨开门闩，往窗外一瞥，鼻子里哼出一声深恶痛绝。
这若是群狼拿得出的看家本事……！
布置这桩阴谋前几步的人，既有那份才情与想象力，他本指望他们能玩出点稍见新意的花样。
车子停在一家所在门前——那地方他熟得很；老实说，熟得太过了，于他自己绝无好处。
话虽如此，他还是屈尊下了车。

===Original===
“Sure you’ve come to the right place?” he asked the chauffeur.
Two fingers touching the visor of his cap: “But certainly, monsieur!”
“Oh, all right!” Lanyard grumbled resignedly; and tossing the man a five-franc piece, applied his knuckles to the door of an outwardly commonplace hôtel particulier in the rue Chaptal between the impasse of the Grand Guignol and the rue Pigalle.
Now the neophyte needs the introduction of a trusted sponsor before he can win admission to the clubhouse of the exclusive Circle of Friends of Humanity; but Lanyard’s knock secured him prompt and unquestioned right of way. The unfortunate fact is, he was a member in the best of standing; for this society of pseudo-altruistic aims was nothing more nor less than one of those several private gambling clubs of Paris which the French Government tolerates more or less openly, despite adequate restrictive legislation; and gambling was Lanyard’s ruling passion﻿—a legacy from Bourke no less than the rest of his professional equipment.

===Chinese===
「你拿得准没走错地方？」他问司机。
那人两指碰了碰帽舌：「千真万确，先生！」
「哦，行吧！」兰亚德认命地嘟囔一句，抛给那人一枚五法郎银币，随即抬起指节，敲响一宅外表平平无奇的私邸（hôtel particulier）大门——它坐落在沙普塔尔街上，正卡在大木偶剧场（Grand Guignol）那条死巷（impasse）与皮加勒街之间。
寻常新客要想获准入内，踏进这家入会苛严的「人类之友会」（Circle of Friends of Humanity）俱乐部，总得先有一位信得过的会友出面引荐；而兰亚德只消叩一叩门，便立时取得了不容盘问的通行权。煞风景的事实是：他本人正是会籍极佳的会员。原来这个标榜伪利他宗旨的社团，说穿了无非是巴黎那几家私立赌俱乐部之一——尽管限制性的立法一应俱全，法国政府对它们多少是半公开地容让着；而赌博正是兰亚德凌驾一切的嗜好——同他那一身本事的其余装备一样，也是伯克留下的一份遗产。

===Original===
To every man his vice (the argument is Bourke’s, in defence of his failing). And perhaps the least mischievous vice a professional cracksman can indulge is that of gambling, since it can hardly drive him to lengths more desperate than those whereby he gains a livelihood.
In the esteem of Paris, Count Remy de Morbihan himself was scarcely a more lighthearted plunger than Monsieur Lanyard.
Naturally, with this reputation, he was always free of the handsome salons wherein the Friends of Humanity devoted themselves to roulette, auction bridge, baccarat and chemin-de-fer: and of this freedom he now proceeded to avail himself, with his hat just a shade aslant on his head, his hands in his pockets, a suspicion of a smile on his lips and a glint of the devil in his eyes﻿—in all an expression accurately reflecting the latest phase of his humour, which was become largely one of contemptuous toleration, thanks to what he chose to consider an exhibition of insipid stupidity on the part of the Pack.
Nor was this humour in any way modified when, in due course, he confirmed anticipation by discovering Monsieur le Comte Remy de Morbihan lounging beside one of the roulette tables, watching the play, and now and again risking a maximum on his own account.

===Chinese===
人各有一癖（这话是伯克替自家毛病辩护时说的）。而一个职业撬保险柜高手纵容自己沾染的恶癖里，赌博兴许还算为害最轻的一种，毕竟它很难驱他走到比讨生活更不顾死活的地步去。
在巴黎一城的口碑里，论起兴冲冲一掷千金的豪客做派，只怕雷米·德·莫尔比昂伯爵本人也不见得胜过兰亚德先生几分。
既有这般名声作保，那几间漂亮厅堂对他自然永远敞开、分文不取——「人类之友」们的功夫，便都下在轮盘赌、竞叫桥牌、巴卡拉纸牌和铁路牌（chemin-de-fer）上。眼下他就要来行使这份特权：帽子微微斜扣，两手插在兜里，唇边挂一丝若有似无的笑意，眼底闪一星魔鬼的光——总而言之，这副神情不偏不倚，正照出他心境的最新阶段：那心境如今大半已化作一种居高临下的宽宥，全拜群狼那一场——他一厢情愿认定的——乏味蠢行所赐。
而当他不旋踵便坐实了所料不差——雷米·德·莫尔比昂伯爵先生（Monsieur le Comte）果然懒洋洋倚着一张轮盘赌台观局，时不时也替自己押上一注顶格的——他这份心境也丝毫未见好转。

===Original===
A flash of animation crossed the unlovely mask of the Count when he saw Lanyard approaching, and he greeted the adventurer with a gay little flirt of his pudgy dark hand.
“Ah, my friend!” he cried. “It is you, then, who have changed your mind! But this is delightful!”
“And what has become of your American friend?” asked the adventurer.
“He tired quickly, that one, and packed himself off to Troyon’s. Be sure I didn’t press him to continue the grand tour!”

===Chinese===
伯爵那张其貌不扬的假脸上掠过一丝活气——望见兰亚德走近，他便用胖乎乎的黑手轻俏地一挥，向这位冒险家打招呼。
「啊，我的朋友！」他嚷道。「这么说，回心转意的是您！这可太叫人开心了！」
「您那位美国朋友后来怎么了？」冒险家问。
「那人兴致短得很，自顾自打包回特鲁瓦旅馆去了。放心，我可没有死缠烂打，求他继续这趟壮游！」

===Original===
“Then you really did wish to see me tonight?” Lanyard enquired innocently.
“Always﻿—always, my dear Lanyard!” the Count declared, jumping up. “But come,” he insisted: “I’ve a word for your private ear, if these gentlemen will excuse us.”
“Do!” Lanyard addressed in a confidential manner those he knew at the table, before turning away to the tug of the Count’s hand on his arm﻿—“I think he means to pay up twenty pounds he owes me!”
Some derisive laughter greeted this sally.
“I mean that, however,” Lanyard informed the other cheerfully as they moved away to a corner where conversation without an audience was possible﻿—“you ruined that Bank of England note, you know.”

===Chinese===
「那么今晚，您当真有话要见我？」兰亚德一脸无辜地问道。
「随时——随时，我亲爱的兰亚德！」伯爵一跃而起，朗声宣告。「不过来吧，」他执意道，「我有句话要单对您一人讲，只看这几位先生肯不肯包涵。」
「快请吧！」兰亚德先朝桌边几位相识的故作机密地招呼了一声，才由着伯爵扯住自己胳膊拽开去——「我看，他是要清那二十镑的欠账了！」
这句俏皮话博得一片哄笑。
「不过，那话可是当真的，」两人挪到一处说私话不致有听众的角落，兰亚德快快活活地告诉他，「您把那张英格兰银行（Bank of England）的钞票给糟蹋了，这您是知道的。」

===Original===
“Cheap at the price!” the Count protested, producing his billfold. “Five hundred francs for an introduction to Monsieur the Lone Wolf!”
“Are you joking?” Lanyard asked blankly﻿—and with a magnificent gesture abolished the proffered banknote.
“Joking? I! But surely you don’t mean to deny﻿—”
“My friend,” Lanyard interrupted, “before we assert or deny anything, let us gather the rest of the players round the table and deal from a sealed deck. Meantime, let us rest on the understanding that I have found, at one end, a message scrawled on a banknote hidden in a secret place, at the other end, yourself, Monsieur le Comte. Between and beyond these points exists a mystery, of which one anticipates elucidation.”
“You shall have it,” De Morbihan promised. “But first, we must go to those others who await us.”

===Chinese===
「这价钱再便宜不过！」伯爵提出抗议，一面掏出皮夹。「五百法郎，买一个独狼先生的引见！」
「您是开玩笑？」兰亚德木然发问——随即手笔豪阔地一挥，把递来的钞票勾销了。
「开玩笑？我！可您总不至于是要否认——」
「我的朋友，」兰亚德打断他，「咱们且什么也不认定、什么也不否认，先把其余的玩家都请到桌边，从一副未启封的牌里发牌再作道理。这中间，不妨先依一个共识行事：一头，是我在一处秘藏的所在、一张钞票上发现的涂写便条；另一头，是您本人，伯爵先生。这两点之间、这两点之外，横着一桩谜案，静候水落石出。」
「自当奉告。」德·莫尔比昂许诺道。「不过得先走一趟，去见那几位正等着我们的人。」

===Original===
“Not so fast!” Lanyard interposed. “What am I to understand? That you wish me to accompany you to the﻿—ah﻿—den of the Pack?”
“Where else?” De Morbihan grinned.
“But where is that?”
“I am not permitted to say﻿—”
“Still, one has one’s eyes. Why not satisfy me here?”
“Your eyes, by your leave, monsieur, will be blindfolded.”

===Chinese===
「且慢！」兰亚德插话。「我该怎么领会？您是要我陪您去——啊——群狼的巢穴？」
「不去那儿去哪儿？」德·莫尔比昂咧嘴一笑。
「可那巢穴在哪儿？」
「这个，我无权奉告——」
「话虽如此，人还长着一双眼睛。何不就在这儿，让我称心如意？」
「请您恕罪，先生，您那双眼睛是要蒙起来的。」

===Original===
“Impossible.”
“Pardon﻿—it is an essential﻿—”
“Come, come, my friend: we are not in the Middle Ages!”
“I have no discretion, monsieur. My confrères﻿—”
“I insist: there will be trust on both sides or no negotiations.”

===Chinese===
「办不到。」
「恕罪——这是断不可少的一环——」
「得了，得了，我的朋友：如今可不是中世纪！」
「我做不得主，先生。我那些 confrères（同道）——」
「我把话放在这儿：要么两边互相信任，要么一拍两散。」

===Original===
“But I assure you, my dear friend﻿—”
“My dear Count, it is useless: I am determined. Blindfold? I should say not! This is not﻿—need I remind you again?﻿—the Paris of Balzac and that wonderful Dumas of yours!”
“What do you propose, then?” De Morbihan enquired, worrying his moustache.
“What better place for the proposed conference than here?”
“But not here!”
“Why not? Everybody comes here: it will cause no gossip. I am here﻿—I have come halfway; your friends must do as much on their part.”

===Chinese===
「可我向您担保，亲爱的朋友——」
「亲爱的伯爵，白费唇舌：我意已决。蒙眼罩？想也别想！这里可不是——还要我再提醒您一回么？——巴尔扎克（Balzac）的巴黎，也不是您那位神乎其神的大仲马（Dumas）的巴黎！」
「那么您有何高见？」德·莫尔比昂捻着小胡子问道。
「拟议中的这场会谈，还有比这儿更好的去处么？」
「偏偏不能在这儿！」
「为什么？谁都上这儿来：断乎惹不出一句闲话。我已经到了这儿——路走了一半；您那些朋友那一头，也得照样走上这么远。」

===Original===
“It is not possible.﻿ ﻿…”
“Then, I beg you, tender them my regrets.”
“Would you give us away?”
“Never that: one makes gifts to one’s friends only. But my interest in yours is depreciating so rapidly that, should you delay much longer, it will be on sale for the sum of two sous.”
“O﻿—damn!” the Count complained peevishly.

===Chinese===
「这不可能。……」
「那么，拜托您代我向他们致以歉意。」
「您要出卖我们不成？」
「决无此事：礼物嘛，只送朋友。不过我对您那几位朋友的兴趣正行情看跌，跌得飞快；您要是再耽搁些时，它就得挂出两苏（sou）的价钱贱卖了。」
「哦——该死！」伯爵气呼呼地抱怨。

===Original===
“With all the pleasure in life.﻿ ﻿… But now,” Lanyard went on, rising to end the interview, “you must forgive me for reminding you that the morning wanes apace. I shall be going home in another hour.”
De Morbihan shrugged. “Out of my great affection for you,” he purred venomously, “I will do my possible. But I promise nothing.”
“I have every confidence in your powers of moral suasion, monsieur,” Lanyard assured him cheerfully. “*Au revoir!*”
And with this, not at all ill-pleased with himself, he strutted off to a table at which a high-strung session of chemin-de-fer was in process, possessed himself of a vacant chair, and in two minutes was so engrossed in the game that the Pack was quite forgotten.
In fifteen minutes he had won thrice as many thousands of francs.

===Chinese===
「那真是人生至乐，乐意之至。……不过言归正传，」兰亚德起身作收场，「还得恕我提醒一句：晨光苦短，过得飞快。再过一个钟头，我就要回家了。」
德·莫尔比昂耸耸肩。「瞧在鄙人对您的一片深情厚谊上，」他柔声柔气，字字带毒，「我自会尽力而为。但许诺是概不作出的。」
「我对您道德劝化的功力信心十足，先生。」兰亚德快快活活地向他打包票，「*Au revoir!*（后会有期！）」
说罢这一句，他对自己着实受用，大摇大摆踱向一张正杀得难分难解的铁路牌赌桌，占下一把空椅，不出两分钟便沉进牌局里，把群狼忘了个一干二净。
一刻钟的工夫，他赢进腰包的法郎便以千计，数目恰是分钟数的三倍。

===Original===
Twenty minutes or half an hour later, a hand on his shoulder broke the grip of his besetting passion.
“Our table is made up, my friend,” De Morbihan announced with his inextinguishable grin. “We’re waiting for you.”
“Quite at your service.”
Settling his score and finding himself considerably better off than he had imagined, he resigned his place gracefully, and suffered the Count to link arms and drag him away up the main staircase to the second storey, where smaller rooms were reserved for parties who preferred to gamble privately.

===Chinese===
又过了二十分钟还是半个钟头，一只手落上他的肩头，才把这缠人不散的嗜好从他身上掰了开来。
「我们那桌牌齐了，我的朋友，」德·莫尔比昂带着他那副扑不灭的咧嘴笑宣布道，「就等您大驾。」
「悉听尊便。」
结清账目，发觉自己竟比设想的还阔绰不少，他从容让出座位，由得伯爵挽住胳膊，一路拽上大楼梯，来到二层——几间小些的房间，专留给喜欢关起门来私下聚赌的宾客。

===Original===
“So it appears you succeeded!” he chaffed his conductor good-humouredly.
“I have brought you the mountain,” De Morbihan assented.
“One is grateful for small miracles.﻿ ﻿…”
But De Morbihan wouldn’t laugh at his own expense; for a moment, indeed, he seemed inclined to take umbrage at Lanyard’s levity. But the sudden squaring of his broad shoulders and the hardening of his features was quickly modified by an uneasy sidelong glance at his companion. And then they were at the door of the cabinet particulier.

===Chinese===
「看来您倒是马到功成了！」他好脾气地打趣这位领路人。
「我把大山请到您跟前来了。」德·莫尔比昂应道。
「小小的灵迹，也已足感念的了。……」
只是德·莫尔比昂断不肯拿自己当笑料使；有那么一瞬，他当真像是要为兰亚德的轻狂动气。可他宽阔的肩膀猛然一端、面皮陡然一硬的那股气势，很快便被朝同伴斜瞟过去的不安一瞥冲淡了。说话间，两人已到那间专用雅室（cabinet particulier）门前。

===Original===
De Morbihan rapped, turned the knob, and stood aside, bowing politely.
With a nod acknowledging the courtesy, Lanyard consented to precede him, and entered a room of intimate proportions, furnished chiefly with a green-covered card-table and five easy-chairs, of which three were occupied﻿—two by men in evening dress, the third by one in a well-tailored lounge suit of dark grey.
Now all three men wore visors of black velvet.
Lanyard looked from one to the other and chuckled quietly.

===Chinese===
德·莫尔比昂叩了叩门，拧开门把，侧身闪开，彬彬有礼打一躬。
兰亚德点头谢过这一礼数，屈尊先行一步，走进一间便于密谈的小屋：陈设以一张绿呢牌桌为主，外加五把安乐椅，其中三把有人落座——两把坐着晚礼服的男子，第三把上那位，一身剪裁考究的深灰便装西装。
而这三位，一概罩着黑丝绒的假面。
兰亚德把他们挨个儿瞧过来，肚里暗暗好笑。

===Original===
With an aggrieved air De Morbihan launched into introductions:
“Messieurs, I have the honour to present to you our confrère, Monsieur Lanyard, best known as ‘The Lone Wolf.’ Monsieur Lanyard﻿—the Council of our Association, known to you as ‘The Pack.’ ”
The three rose and bowed ceremoniously, Lanyard returned a cool, good-natured nod. Then he laughed again and more openly:
“A pack of knaves!”
“Monsieur doubtless feels at ease?” one retorted acidly.

===Chinese===
德·莫尔比昂一脸委屈，张口引见：
「Messieurs（诸位先生），鄙人有幸向各位引见我们的同道兰亚德先生——江湖人称『独狼』，如雷贯耳。兰亚德先生——本协会的理事会，也就是您知道的『群狼』。」
三人起身，郑重其事地鞠躬；兰亚德回敬一个冷淡而好性子的颔首。接着他又笑了，这回笑得更不遮不掩：
「好一窝无赖！」
「先生这会儿想必泰然自若？」有人尖酸地回敬。

===Original===
“In your company, Popinot? But hardly!” Lanyard returned in light contempt.
The fellow thus indicated, a burly rogue of a Frenchman in rusty and baggy evening clothes, started and flushed scarlet beneath his mask; but the man next him dropped a restraining hand upon his arm, and Popinot, with a shrug, sank back into his chair.
“Upon my word!” Lanyard declared gracelessly, “it’s as good as a play! Are you sure, Monsieur le Comte, there’s no mistake﻿—that these gay masqueraders haven’t lost their way to the stage of the Grand Guignol?”
“Damn!” muttered the Count. “Take care, my friend! You go too far!”

===Chinese===
「同波皮诺您作伴？只怕未必！」兰亚德回上一句，轻蔑里带着谐谑。
被点名的这位是个五大三粗的法国流氓，一身晚礼服颜色晦暗、松松垮垮，闻言一激灵，假面底下涨得通红；但他旁边那人把一只镇场的手按上他的胳膊，波皮诺也就耸耸肩，缩回椅子里坐下了。
「我说！」兰亚德毫不客气地宣布，「这简直比看戏还过瘾！伯爵先生，您拿得准没出岔子——这几位兴致勃勃的化装客，不是走岔了道，误了上大木偶剧场戏台的时辰么？」
「该死！」伯爵低声骂道。「小心，我的朋友！您玩笑过头了！」

===Original===
“You really think so? But you amaze me! You can’t in reason expect me to take you seriously, gentlemen!”
“If you don’t, it will prove serious business for you!” growled the one he had called Popinot.
“You mean that? But you are magnificent, all of you! We lack only the solitary illumination of a candle-end﻿—a grinning skull﻿—a cup of blood upon the table﻿—to make the farce complete! But as it is.﻿ ﻿… Messieurs, you must be rarely uncomfortable, and feeling as foolish as you look, into the bargain! Moreover, I’m no child.﻿ ﻿… Popinot, why not disembarrass your amiable features? And you, Mr. Wertheimer, I’m sure, will feel more at ease with an open countenance﻿—as the saying runs,” he said, nodding to the man beside Popinot. “As for this gentleman,” he concluded, eyeing the third, “I haven’t the pleasure of his acquaintance.”
With a short laugh, Wertheimer unmasked and exposed a face of decidedly English type, fair and well-modelled, betraying only the faintest traces of Semitic cast to account for his surname. And with this example, Popinot snatched off his own black visor﻿—and glared at Lanyard: in his shabby dress, the incarnate essence of bourgeoisie outraged. But the third, he of the grey lounge suit, remained motionless; only his eyes clashed coldly with the adventurer’s.

===Chinese===
「您当真这样想？您可真叫我吃惊！按情理，各位总不能指望我正儿八经把你们当回事吧，先生们！」
「您要不当真，回头有您受的！」被唤作波皮诺的那位咆哮道。
「此话当真？可诸位实在气派极了！如今只差一支蜡烛头孤零零照着——桌上再摆一颗龇牙咧嘴的骷髅、一杯鲜血——这出闹剧便十全十美了！可惜照眼下这样。……Messieurs，几位这会儿想必浑身不自在得紧，而且面上瞧着有多蠢，心里便觉得有多蠢，一并奉送！再者，我又不是三岁孩子。……波皮诺，何不解放一下您那副和蔼可亲的眉眼？还有您，维特海默先生，敞开脸面定然自在得多——俗话就是这么说的，」他朝波皮诺身旁那人颔首致意。「至于这一位，」他端详着第三人，收了尾，「我尚无结识的荣幸。」
维特海默短促一笑，摘下面罩，露出一张十足英国型的脸，白净周正，只余一丝极淡的闪米特血韵，恰够交代他那姓氏。有此一例，波皮诺也一把扯下自己的黑面罩——瞪住兰亚德：配上那身寒酸行头，活脱脱是市侩体面惨遭冒犯的化身。唯独第三位，灰便装的那位，纹丝不动；只有他的目光，同冒险家的目光冷冷相击。

===Original===
He seemed a man little if at all Lanyard’s senior, and built upon much the same lines. A close-clipped black moustache ornamented his upper lip. His chin was square and strong with character. The cut of his clothing was conspicuously neither English nor Continental.
“I don’t know you, sir,” Lanyard continued slowly, puzzled to account for a feeling of familiarity with this person, whom he could have sworn he had never met before. “But you won’t let your friends here outdo you in civility, I trust?”
“If you mean you want me to unmask, I won’t,” the other returned brusquely, in fair French but with a decided transatlantic intonation.
“American, eh?”

===Chinese===
此人年纪同兰亚德不相上下，至多略长，身量骨架也大体一路：上唇一撮修剪贴伏的黑髭，下巴方正有骨，见出性格。一身衣裳的剪裁，明眼一瞧便知既非英式，也非欧陆货色。
「我不认识您，先生，」兰亚德慢慢说道，一面纳罕于心，何以对这人竟有几分面熟——他敢起誓从未谋面。「不过，想必您不至于让这几位朋友在礼数上抢了先？」
「您要是想叫我摘面罩，我不摘。」对方莽声莽气地回话，法语说得周正，一口大西洋彼岸的腔调却分明不过。
「美国人，嗯？」

===Original===
“Native-born, if it interests you.”
“Have I ever met you before?”
“You have not.”
“My dear Count,” Lanyard said, turning to De Morbihan, “do me the favour to introduce this gentleman.”
“Your dear Count will do nothing like that, Mr. Lanyard. If you need a name to call me by, Smith’s good enough.”

===Chinese===
「土生土长，您若有兴致知道。」
「我从前可曾见过您？」
「没有见过。」
「亲爱的伯爵，」兰亚德转向德·莫尔比昂，「赏个脸，把这位先生引见引见。」
「您那位亲爱的伯爵偏不干这个，兰亚德先生。您要是非得有个称呼不可，『史密斯』就尽够用了。」

===Original===
The incisive force of his enunciation assorted consistently with the general habit of the man. Lanyard recognized a nature no more pliable than his own. Idle to waste time bickering with this one.﻿ ﻿…
“It doesn’t matter,” he said shortly; and drawing back a chair, sat down. “If it did, I should insist﻿—or else decline the honour of receiving the addresses of this cosmopolitan committee. Truly, messieurs, you flatter me. Here we have Mr. Wertheimer, representing the swell-mobsmen across Channel; Monsieur le Comte standing for the gratin of Paris; Popinot, spokesman for our friends the Apaches; and the well-known Mr. Goodenough Smith, ambassador of the gunmen of New York﻿—no doubt. I presume one is to understand you wait upon me as representing the fine flower of the European underworld?”
“You’re to understand that I, for one, don’t relish your impudence,” the stout Popinot snapped.
“Sorry.﻿ ﻿… But I have already indicated my inability to take you seriously.”
“Why not?” the American demanded ominously. “You’d be sore enough if we took you as a joke, wouldn’t you?”

===Chinese===
他吐字斩钉截铁，与这人的通身做派一脉相承。兰亚德认出这是一副同他自己一样折不动弯的性子。同这号人磨嘴皮子，纯属虚耗光阴。……
「无所谓。」他三言两语说完，拖过一把椅子坐下。「若真有所谓，我便要坚持——再不然，就敬谢这番殊荣，不劳你们这个万国委员会的『垂询』了。说实在的，Messieurs，各位太抬举我了。瞧这一桌：维特海默先生，代表海峡对岸的时髦窃帮（swell-mobsmen）；伯爵先生，充作巴黎上流精华（gratin）的门面；波皮诺，我们阿帕奇朋友们的发言人；还有那位大名鼎鼎的『尽够用』史密斯先生（Goodenough Smith），纽约枪手们的大使——想必错不了。我是否应当领会：各位联袂枉顾，是代表欧洲下流社会的精华中之精华？」
「你该领会的是，单说鄙人一个，可爱不了你这副轻薄。」胖大的波皮诺厉声道。
「抱歉。……不过我早已表明，没法把各位当真。」
「为什么不能？」美国人不祥地逼问。「要是我们把你看成一个笑话，你有得恼的，不是么？」

===Original===
“You misapprehend, Mr.﻿—ah﻿—Smith: it is my first aim and wish that you do not take me in any manner, shape or form. It is you, remember, who requested this interview and﻿—er﻿—dressed your parts so strikingly!”
“What are we to understand by that?” De Morbihan interposed.
“This, messieurs﻿—if you must know.” Lanyard dropped for the moment his tone of raillery and bent forward, emphasizing his points by tapping the table with a forefinger. “Through some oversight of mine or cleverness of yours﻿—I can’t say which﻿—perhaps both﻿—you have succeeded in penetrating my secret. What then? You become envious of my success. In short, I stand in your light: I’m always getting away with something you might have lifted if you’d only had wit enough to think of it first. As your American accomplice, Mr. Mysterious Smith, would say, I ‘cramp your style.’ ”
“You learned that on Broadway,” the American commented shrewdly.

===Chinese===
「您误会了，那位——啊——史密斯先生：我头一桩心愿，便是请各位无论如何、以任何方式、在任何形态下，都别把我当一回事。要知道，是你们求的这次会面，还——呃——把行头装扮得这般惹眼！」
「这话从何说起？」德·莫尔比昂插进来。
「这样讲，Messieurs——非要知道的话。」兰亚德一时收起插科打诨的腔调，俯身向前，拿食指叩着桌面，字字加重。「托我的疏忽，或是你们的精明——我说不上是哪样——兴许两样都有——你们总算刺穿了我的秘密。然后呢？你们眼红我的成功了。一句话，我挡了各位的光：我顺手牵走的东西回回得手，你们但凡脑子够用、想在前头，原也轮得着你们。用你们那位美国同伙、神秘史密斯先生的话说，我『碍着你们的手脚』（cramp your style）。」
「这话你是在百老汇（Broadway）学的。」美国人伶俐地点破。

===Original===
“Possibly.﻿ ﻿… To continue: so you get together, and bite your nails until you concoct a plan to frighten me into sharing my profits. I’ve no doubt you’re prepared to allow me to retain one-half the proceeds of my operations, should I elect to ally myself with you?”
“That’s the suggestion we are empowered to make,” De Morbihan admitted.
“In other words, you need me. You say to yourselves: ‘We’ll pretend to be the head of a criminal syndicate, such as the silly novelists are forever writing about, and we’ll threaten to put him out of business unless he comes to our terms.’ But you overlook one important fact: that you are not mentally equipped to get away with this amusing impersonation! What! Do you expect me to accept you as leading spirits of a gigantic criminal system﻿—you, Popinot, who live by standing between the police and your murderous rats of Belleville, or you, Wertheimer, sneak-thief and blackmailer of timid women, or you, De Morbihan, because you eke out your income by showing a handful of second-storey men where to seek plunder in the homes of your friends!”
He made a gesture of impatience, and lounged back to wait the answer to this indictment. His gaze, ranging the four faces, encountered but one that was not darkly flushed with resentment; and this was the American’s.

===Chinese===
「也许。……接着说：于是你们聚作一堆，咬着指甲熬啊熬，熬出一条妙计，要吓得我乖乖分利。倘若我肯同你们结盟，你们想必已备好恩典，许我留下一半的营生进项——我没猜错吧？」
「我们受权提出的，正是这个建议。」德·莫尔比昂认了。
「换句话说，你们缺不得我。你们心里盘算：『咱们就装成一个犯罪辛迪加的首脑，蹩脚小说家们写个没完的那种，再吓唬他：不受咱们的条件，就砸他的饭碗。』可你们漏算了一件要紧事：凭各位的脑力，还演不像这出逗笑的假戏！什么！你们指望我把你们认作一个庞大犯罪体系的主脑——你，波皮诺，靠在警察和你那群贝尔维尔（Belleville）的杀人鼠辈之间居中渔利；你，维特海默，一个专向怯懦妇人下手的鼠窃兼勒索犯；还有你，德·莫尔比昂，靠指点一小撮翻窗贼（second-storey men），去你朋友们府上何处觅宝，贴补用度！」
他做个不耐烦的手势，懒懒靠回椅背，静候这篇控词的回音。目光逡巡过那四张脸，只有一张没憋出阴沉沉的怒红——那便是美国人的。

===Original===
“Aren’t you overlooking me?” this last suggested gently.
“On the contrary: I refuse to recognize you as long as you lack courage to show your face.”
“As you will, my friend,” the American chuckled. “Make your profit out of that any way you like.”
Lanyard sat up again: “Well, I’ve stated your case, messieurs. It amounts to simple, clumsy blackmail. I’m to split my earnings with you, or you’ll denounce me to the police. That’s about it, isn’t it?”

===Chinese===
「您不觉得把鄙人漏了么？」这末了一位温文地提点。
「恰恰相反：您几时肯拿出露脸的胆量来，我几时才认您这个人。」
「悉随尊便，我的朋友。」美国人呵呵一笑。「这句话，您爱怎么生利就怎么生利。」
兰亚德重新坐直身子：「好了，各位的诉状我陈述完了，Messieurs。归结起来，就是一桩直截了当、笨手笨脚的敲诈：我须同你们分赃，不然你们就去向警察告发我。差不离就这些，对吧？」

===Original===
“Not of necessity,” De Morbihan softly purred, twisting his moustache.
“For my part,” Popinot declared hotly, “I engage that Monsieur of the High Hand, here, will either work with us or conduct no more operations in Paris.”
“Or in New York,” the American amended.
“England is yet to be heard from,” Lanyard suggested mockingly.

===Chinese===
「未必尽然。」德·莫尔比昂捻着髭须，柔声咕噜。
「要依我说，」波皮诺勃然作色，「我担保这位『强横手腕』先生，要么同我们合股，要么就休想在巴黎再干一票。」
「纽约也一并算上。」美国人补了一句。
「英国那头还没听见信儿呢。」兰亚德讥诮地提点。

===Original===
To this Wertheimer replied, almost with diffidence: “If you ask me, I don’t think you’d find it so jolly pleasant over there, if you mean to cut up nasty at this end.”
“Then what am I to infer? If you’re afraid to lay an information against me﻿—and it wouldn’t be wise, I admit﻿—you’ll merely cause me to be assassinated, eh?”
“Not of necessity,” the Count murmured in the same thoughtful tone and manner﻿—as one holding a hidden trump.
“There are so many ways of arranging these matters,” Wertheimer ventured.

===Chinese===
这话头维特海默接了过去，几乎带着几分赧然：「要问我的话，您在这一头要是存心撒泼，我以为您到了那一头，怕也就不那么逍遥自在了。」
「那我该作何推想？你们若不敢去向官府首告我——我承认，那确非上策——那就干脆打发人把我暗算了结，嗯？」
「未必尽然。」伯爵喃喃，还是那副深思的声口与做派——好比手里扣着一张暗牌。
「料理这类事情，路数多得是。」维特海默小心搭腔。

===Original===
“None the less, if I refuse, you declare war?”
“Something like that,” the American admitted.
“In that case﻿—I am now able to state my position definitely.” Lanyard got up and grinned provokingly down at the group. “You can﻿—all four of you﻿—go plumb to hell!”
“My dear friend!” the Count cried, shocked﻿—“you forget﻿—”

===Chinese===
「尽管如此，我若是回绝，你们便宣战？」
「差不多是这个意思。」美国人认账。
「既然如此——现在，我可以把立场一锤定音了。」兰亚德起身，挑衅地俯瞰这一伙，咧嘴而笑。「你们四位——一个不落——统统给我见鬼去吧！」
「我亲爱的朋友！」伯爵骇然叫出声——「您忘了——」

===Original===
“I forget nothing!” Lanyard cut in coldly﻿—“and my decision is final. Consider yourselves at liberty to go ahead and do your damnedest! But don’t forget that it is you who are the aggressors. Already you’ve had the insolence to interfere with my arrangements: you began offensive operations before you declared war. So now if you’re hit beneath the belt, you mustn’t complain: you’ve asked for it!”
“Now just what *do* you mean by that?” the American drawled ironically.
“I leave you to figure it out for yourselves. But I will say this: I confidently expect you to decide to live and let live, and shall be sorry, as you’ll certainly be sorry, if you force my hand.”
He opened the door, turned, and saluted them with sarcastic punctilio.
“I have the honour to bid adieu to Messieurs the Council of﻿—‘The Pack’!”

===Chinese===
「我什么也没忘！」兰亚德冷然截住——「我的决定便是终局。各位只管放开手脚，把最毒的那一着使出来！只是别忘了，先启衅端的是你们。你们已然放肆搅扰过我的部署：宣战之前，先动了刀兵。往后若是挨着了腰带底下的拳，可休要喊冤：这是你们自己讨来的！」
「那么您这话*究竟*是什么意思？」美国人拖着长腔，语带讥诮。
「留给各位自家参详去吧。不过有一句话我仍要说：我笃信各位终会肯相安无事、互留活路；真要逼我摊牌，我固然要遗憾，而各位，必定更遗憾。」
他推开门，回身站定，以一丝不苟的讥讽礼数向他们致意。
「谨此有幸，向『群狼』理事会的诸位先生，道一声 adieu（告辞）！」

## VI The Pack Gives Tongue / 六、群犬齐吠

===Original===
Lanyard’s first destination was that convenient little *rez-de-chaussée* apartment near the Trocadéro, at the junction of the rue Roget and the avenue de l’Alma; but his way thither was so roundabout that the best part of an hour was required for what might have been less than a twenty-minute taxicab course direct from Troyon’s. It was past one when he arrived, afoot, at the corner.

Not that he grudged the time; for in Lanyard’s esteem Bourke’s epigram had come to have the weight and force of an axiom: “The more trouble you make for yourself, the less the good public will make for you.”

Paradoxically, he hadn’t the least intention of attempting to deceive anybody as to his permanent address in Paris, where Michael Lanyard, connoisseur of fine paintings, was a figure too conspicuous to permit his making a secret of his residence. De Morbihan, moreover, through recognizing him at Troyon’s, had rendered it impossible for Lanyard to adopt a nom-de-guerre there, even had he thought that ruse advisable.

But he had certain businesses to attend to before dawn, affairs demanding privacy; and while by no means sure he was followed, one can seldom be sure of anything, especially in Paris, where nothing is impossible; and it were as well to lose a spy first as last. And his mind could not be at ease with respect to Roddy, thanks to De Morbihan’s gasconade in the presence of the detective and also to that hint which the Count had dropped concerning some fatal blunder in the course of Lanyard’s British campaign.

===Chinese===
兰亚德的第一站，是特罗卡德罗附近那套称手的小公寓——一套底层（*rez-de-chaussée*）寓所，坐落在罗热街（rue Roget）与阿尔玛大道（avenue de l'Alma）交汇的路口；只是他绕道绕得太迂回，从特鲁瓦旅馆直接雇一辆出租汽车本用不了二十分钟的路程，竟耗去了将近一个钟头。待他步行抵达街角时，已是凌晨一点过后。

这倒不是他舍不得这点工夫；在兰亚德的心目中，伯克那句妙语早已取得公理般的分量与效力：「你给自己找的麻烦越多，善良的公众替你找的麻烦就越少。」

说来矛盾，他压根无意向任何人隐瞒自己在巴黎的常住地址——在巴黎，名画鉴赏家迈克尔·兰亚德是个过于显眼的人物，想把自己的住处瞒成一桩秘密也办不到。何况德·莫尔比昂既已在特鲁瓦旅馆认出了他，兰亚德在那里便再无可能改用化名（nom-de-guerre），纵使他当真认为这条诡计可取。

但天亮之前他还得料理几桩事务，都是需要避人耳目的勾当；虽说拿不准身后有没有盯梢的，可人对什么事都难得拿得准，在巴黎尤其如此——这座城市里没有一桩事不可能；而要甩脱密探，晚甩不如早甩。再者，一想到罗迪，他的心便不得安生：多亏德·莫尔比昂当着那侦探的面夸下的那番海口，外加伯爵顺口漏出的那一点暗示——说兰亚德在不列颠战役中铸下了某个致命的大错。

===Original===
The adventurer could recall leaving no step uncovered. Indeed, he had prided himself on conducting his operations with a degree of circumspection unusually thoroughgoing, even for him. Yet he was unable to rid himself of those misgivings roused by De Morbihan’s declaration that the theft of the Omber jewels had been accomplished only at cost of a clue to the thief’s identity.

Now the Count’s positive information concerning the robbery proved that the news thereof had anticipated the arrival of its perpetrator in Paris; yet Roddy unquestionably had known nothing of it prior to its mention in his presence, after dinner. Or else the detective was a finer actor than Lanyard credited.

But how could De Morbihan have come by his news?

Lanyard was really and deeply perturbed.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
这位冒险家自问，没有一步棋不曾遮掩妥当。的确，他素来引以为傲的，便是行事那份异乎寻常的周密，即便以他自己的标准衡量也格外彻底。然而德·莫尔比昂断言，欧姆贝珍珠到手是以留下一条指向窃贼身份的线索为代价的——这话激起的疑虑，他怎么也摆脱不掉。

而伯爵对这桩劫案言之凿凿，足证消息比作案人本人更早一步到了巴黎；可是罗迪分明对此一无所知，直到晚餐之后，那案子才当着他的面初次被人提起。要不然，便是这位侦探的演技比兰亚德所估量的还要高明。

可是，德·莫尔比昂的消息究竟从何而来？

兰亚德这回是当真深深不安了。……

===Original===
Pestered to distraction by such thoughts, he fitted key to latch and quietly let himself into his flat by a private street-entrance which, in addition to the usual door opening on the court and under the eye of the concierge, distinguished this from the ordinary Parisian apartment and rendered it doubly suited to the adventurer’s uses.

Then he turned on the lights and moved quickly from room to room of the three comprising his quarters, with comprehensive glances reviewing their condition.

But, indeed, he hadn’t left the reception-hall for the salon without recognizing that things were in no respect as they ought to be: a hat he had left on the hall rack had been moved to another peg; a chair had been shifted six inches from its ordained position; and the door of a clothespress, which he had locked on leaving, now stood ajar.

Furthermore, the state of the salon, which he had furnished as a lounge and study, and of the tiny dining-room and the bedchamber adjoining, bore out these testimonies to the fact that alien hands had thoroughly ransacked the apartment, leaving no square inch unscrutinized.

Yet the proprietor missed nothing. His rooms were a private gallery of valuable paintings and antique furniture to poison with envy the mind of any collector, and housed into the bargain a small museum of rare books, manuscripts, and articles of exquisite workmanship whose individuality, aside from intrinsic worth, rendered them priceless. A burglar of discrimination might have carried off in one coat-pocket loot enough to foot the bill for a twelvemonth of profligate existence. But nothing had been removed, nothing at least that was apparent in the first tour of inspection; which, if sweeping, was by no means superficial.

===Chinese===
他被这些念头搅扰得心烦意乱，遂将钥匙凑上锁眼，从一处临街的私家入口悄悄进入自己的公寓——除了那扇开向院子、处在门房（concierge）眼皮底下的寻常大门之外，还独有这样一道私用入口，正是它令这套寓所有别于普通的巴黎公寓，加倍合乎冒险家的用途。

随即他拧亮电灯，在他那三间一套的居所里快步穿行，逐屋以总揽全局的目光检视各处的情形。

不过说实话，他人还没从前厅走进客厅，便已察觉情形处处不对：他留在前厅衣帽架上的一顶帽子，被挪到了另一个挂钩上；一把椅子从它原定的位置挪开了六英寸；还有一扇衣柜的门，出门时他分明锁得好好的，如今却虚掩着一条缝。

再者，那间被他布置成休憩兼书房两用的客厅，连同紧挨着的小饭厅与卧室，都印证了这些迹象：外人的手已把整套寓所彻底翻检了一遍，没让一寸地方逃过细看。

然而主人却什么也不曾短少。他这几间屋子堪称一座私人画廊，藏画与古董家具足以叫任何收藏家妒意攻心，此外还容着一座小型博物馆：珍本书、手稿，以及工艺精绝的物件——撇开内在价值不谈，单凭各自的独特品性，便已是无价之宝。一个有眼力的窃贼，单凭一件上衣的口袋，便足可带走够他挥霍一整年的赃物。可什么都没有被拿走，至少初步巡看之下未见短少；而这一番巡看虽是扫荡式的，却绝非浮皮潦草。

===Original===
Before checking off more elaborately his mental inventory, Lanyard turned attention to the protective device, a simple but exhaustive system of burglar-alarm wiring so contrived that any attempt to enter the apartment save by means of a key which fitted both doors and of which no duplicate existed would alarm both the concierge and the burglar protective society. Though it seemed to have been in no way tampered with, to test the apparatus he opened a window on the court.

The lodge of the concierge was within earshot. If the alarm had been in good order, Lanyard could have heard the bell from his window. He heard nothing.

With a shrug, he shut the window. He knew well﻿—none better﻿—how such protection could be rendered valueless by a thoughtful and forehanded housebreaker.

Returning to the salon, where the main body of his collection was assembled, he moved slowly from object to object, ticking off items and noting their condition; with the sole result of justifying his first conclusion, that whereas nothing had escaped handling, nothing had been removed.

===Chinese===
在更精细地核对心中那份藏品清单之前，兰亚德先去查验防盗装置——一套简单而穷尽一切的防盗警铃线路，设计得如此周密：除非用一把两门通用、世上再无第二把的钥匙进门，任何进入公寓的尝试都会同时惊动门房和防盗护卫社。线路看上去未被动过手脚，为验证起见，他还是打开了一扇临院的窗子。

门房小屋近在耳力所及之处。警铃若还完好，兰亚德在自家窗前本可听见铃响。他什么也没听见。

他耸了耸肩，关上窗子。这样的防护如何会被一个心思周密、先事筹备的梁上君子弄得一文不值，他再清楚不过——没有人比他更清楚。

他回到客厅——藏品的大宗都汇聚于此——从一件物件缓步移向另一件，逐项清点，留意它们的状态；唯一的结果，是坐实了他最初的结论：样样东西都被人经手，却没有一件被带走。

===Original===
By way of a final test, he opened his desk (of which the lock had been deftly picked) and went through its pigeonholes.

His scanty correspondence, composed chiefly of letters exchanged with art dealers, had been scrutinized and replaced carelessly, in disorder: and here again he missed nothing; but in the end, removing a small drawer and inserting a hand in its socket, he dislodged a rack of pigeonholes and exposed the secret cabinet that is almost inevitably an attribute of such pieces of period furniture.

A shallow box, this secret space contained one thing only, but that one of considerable value, being the leather billfold in which the adventurer kept a store of ready money against emergencies.

It was mostly for this, indeed, that he had come to his apartment; his London campaign having demanded an expenditure far beyond his calculations, so that he had landed in Paris with less than one hundred francs in pocket. And Lanyard, for all his pride of spirit, acknowledged one haunting fear, that of finding himself strapped in the face of emergency.

===Chinese===
作为最后一道检验，他打开书桌（桌锁已被人灵巧地撬开），把那一格一格的信格都检视了一遍。

他本来寥寥的信件，主要是与画商往来的书函，已被人检视过，又被漫不经心地胡乱塞回，凌乱不堪：这里他同样不缺什么；但到末了，他抽出一只小抽屉，把手探进抽屉的槽窝，卸下整架的信格，露出那口暗柜——这类古式家具几乎必然配有这么一处机关。

这暗格是只浅浅的匣子，里面只盛着一样东西，却是相当值钱的家当：一只皮夹子，冒险家在里头存着一笔现钞，以备不时之需。

说实在的，他回公寓主要就是为了它：伦敦一役的开销远远超出他的盘算，以致他抵达巴黎时，口袋里所剩不足一百法郎。而兰亚德尽管心气高傲，也自认有一桩挥之不去的惧怕——怕的是大难临头之际，发现自己囊空如洗。

===Original===
The fold yielded up its hoard to a sou: Lanyard counted out five notes of one thousand francs and ten of twenty pounds: their sum, upwards of two thousand dollars.

But if nothing had been abstracted, something had been added: the back of one of the Bank of England notes had been used as a blank for memorandum.

Lanyard spread it out and studied it attentively.

The handwriting had been traced with no discernible attempt at disguise, but was quite strange to him. The pen employed had been one of those needle-pointed nibs so popular in France; the hand was that of an educated Frenchman. The import of the memorandum translated substantially as follows:

===Chinese===
皮夹子如数交出了它的窖藏，一个苏也不差：兰亚德点出五张一千法郎、十张二十英镑：合计两千多美元。

不过，若说什么也不曾短少，倒添了一样东西：其中一张英格兰银行钞票的背面，被人拿来当了便笺纸。

兰亚德把它摊平，凝神细看。

那笔迹看不出任何刻意伪装的痕迹，对他却全然陌生。所用的是法国风行的那种针尖般的笔尖；执笔的是一位受过良好教育的法国人。这纸便笺的大意译出如下：

===Original===
> To the Lone Wolf﻿— 

 The Pack sends Greetings 

 and extends its invitation 

 to participate in the benefits 

 of its Fraternity. 

 One awaits him always at 

 L’Abbaye Thêléme.

A date was added, the date of that very day﻿ ﻿…

Deliberately, having conned this communication, Lanyard produced his cigarette-case, selected a cigarette, found his briquet, struck a light, twisted the note of twenty pounds into a rude spill, set it afire, lighted his cigarette there from and, rising, conveyed the burning paper to a cold and empty fireplace wherein he permitted it to burn to a crisp black ash.

===Chinese===
> 致独狼——

 群狼谨致问候，

 并诚伸邀约，

 邀君共享

 友爱会之惠益。

 敝处有人长候于

 泰莱姆修道院（L'Abbaye Thêléme）。

末尾还添了一个日期——正是当天的日期……

把这封来函细细读过，兰亚德不动声色地取出烟盒，拣定一支香烟，摸出打火机（briquet），打火，将那张二十英镑的钞票拧成一枝粗陋的纸捻，点着，就着它的火苗燃起香烟，随后站起身来，把燃烧的纸送到冷而空的壁炉里，任它烧成一片焦脆的黑灰。

===Original===
When this was done, his smile broke through his clouding scowl.

“Well, my friend!” he apostrophized the author of that document which now could never prove incriminating﻿—“at all events, I have you to thank for a new sensation. It has long been my ambition to feel warranted in lighting a cigarette with a twenty-pound note, if the whim should ever seize me!”

His smile faded slowly; the frown replaced it: something far more valuable to him than a hundred dollars had just gone up in smoke﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
此事一毕，他的笑容便破开满面渐聚的阴云，绽了出来。

「好啊，我的朋友！」他向那封信的作者呼道——那文件如今再也无从构成罪证——「不管怎么说，我得谢谢你让我开了番眼界。多年以来，我一直怀着一个抱负：倘若哪天心血来潮，要拿一张二十英镑的钞票点支烟，也要点得心安理得！」

他的笑容缓缓隐去；眉头重新锁了上来：方才化作青烟的，是一件对他而言远比一百美元金贵的东西……

## XXII Trapped / 二十二、瓮中之鳖

===Original===
It was a sound so slight, so very small and still, that only a super-subtle sense of hearing could have discriminated it from the confused multiplicity of almost inaudible, interwoven, interdependent sounds that make up the slumberous quiet of every human habitation, by night.
Lanyard, whose training had taught him how to listen, had learned that the nocturnal hush of each and every house has its singular cadence, its own gentle movement of muted but harmonious sound in which the introduction of an alien sound produces immediate discord, and to which, while at his work, he need attend only subconsciously since the least variation from the norm would give him warning.
Now, in the silence of this old mansion, he detected a faint flutter of discordance that sounded a note of stealth; such a note as no move of his since entering had evoked.
He was no longer alone, but shared the empty magnificence of those vast salons with one whose purpose was as furtive, as secret, as wary as his own; no servant or watchman roused by an intuition of evil, but one who had no more than he any lawful business there.
And while he stood at alert attention the sound was repeated from a point less distant, indicating that the second intruder was moving toward the library.
In two swift strides Lanyard left the shelter of the screen and took to cover in the recess of one of the tall windows, behind its heavy velvet hangings: an action that could have been timed no more precisely had it been rehearsed; he was barely in hiding when a shape of shadow slipped into the library, paused beside the massive desk, and raked the room with the light of a powerful flash-lamp.

===Chinese===
那声响轻极了，细极了，静极了，唯有超凡入微的听觉，方能把它从那一团纷乱的、几不可闻而又相互交织、彼此依存的细响中分辨出来——正是这无数细响，构成了任何一处人居宅邸夜间的酣沉寂静。
兰亚德所受的训练教会了他如何谛听；他早已懂得，每一座宅子的夜静都自有一种独特的节律，自有一脉轻柔的声息之流——喑哑，却和谐——其中一旦掺入异响，立时便起不谐之音。干活的当儿，他只须下意识地留意它便是，因为哪怕只偏离常态一丝一毫，也自会向他示警。
此刻，在这座古老宅邸的岑寂之中，他辨出一缕隐隐的不谐之颤，音色里透着鬼祟；这等音色，是他入宅以来自己的一举一动都不曾引发的。
他不再是独自一人了；那几间大厅空落落的富丽里，如今另有一个人同他分享，而那人来意的鬼祟、隐秘与警觉，半点不输于他——那不是被凶兆直觉惊醒的仆役或更夫，而是同他一样，在此地没有任何正经营生的人。
他屏息凝神、警觉地立着，那声响又起了一次，发自身形更近之处——可见那第二个闯入者正在朝书房过来。
兰亚德疾跨两步，撤出屏风的荫蔽，闪身躲进一扇高窗的窗龛，藏到厚重的丝绒帷幔后面：这套动作，纵使预先排演过，节拍也未必掐得这般毫厘不爽；他前脚刚刚藏定，一团影子的形体后脚便溜进了书房，在那张厚重的大书桌旁略停一停，随即用一支强力手电的光把满屋扫了一遍。

===Original===
Its initial glare struck squarely into Lanyard’s eyes, dazzling them, as he peered through a narrow opening in the portières; and though the light was instantly shifted, for several moments a blur of peacock colour, blending, ebbing, hung like a curtain in the darkness, and he could see nothing distinctly﻿—only the trail traced by that dancing spotlight over walls and furnishings.
When at length his vision cleared, the newcomer was kneeling in turn before the safe; but more light was needed, and this one, lacking Lanyard’s patience and studious caution, turned back to the desk, and, taking the reading-lamp, transferred it to the floor behind the screen.
But even before the flood of light followed the dull click of the switch, Lanyard had recognized the woman.
For an instant he felt dazed, half-stunned, suffocating, much as he had felt with Greggs’ fingers tightening on his windpipe, that week-old night at Troyon’s; he experienced real difficulty about breathing, and was conscious of a sickish throbbing in his temples and a pounding in his bosom like the tolling of a great bell. He stared, swaying.﻿ ﻿…
The light, gushing from the opaque hood, made the safe door a glare, and was thrown back into her intent, masked face, throwing out in sharp silhouette her lithe, sweet body, indisputably identified by the individual poise of her head and shoulders and the gracious contours of her tailored coat.

===Chinese===
手电乍亮的光柱不偏不倚，正打在兰亚德的眼睛上，刺得他一时目眩——他正隔着帷幔（portières）的一道窄缝向外窥探。那道光虽当即移开，有好几瞬，一团孔雀色的晕彩仍在交融、退漾，像一幅帷幕垂悬在黑暗中，教他什么都看不真切——唯有那跳动的光斑游过墙壁与陈设时拖出的轨迹。
待他的视力终于恢复，那新来的人也已跪到保险柜跟前；只是还嫌光不够，而这一位缺少兰亚德那份耐心与步步为营的谨慎，竟折回书桌那边，拿起台灯，把它挪到了屏风后头的地板上。
然而不等开关一声闷响、灯光倾泻而下，兰亚德已认出了那个女人。
一时间他只觉目眩神迷、半僵半麻、透不过气来，滋味同那一周之前特鲁瓦旅馆的夜里、格雷格斯的手指掐紧他咽喉时相去无几；他连呼吸都实实在在犯了难，只觉太阳穴里一阵阵发虚地悸痛，胸中如巨钟轰鸣，一声沉似一声。他瞪望着，身子摇摇欲坠。……
灯光从那不透光的灯罩里迸涌而出，把保险柜的柜门照得一片灼亮，又反照到她那张专注而蒙着面罩的脸上，把她窈窕动人的身段衬成一道分明的剪影——那头颅与肩背独具一格的仪态，那身定做外套典雅合度的轮廓，教人一望便无可置疑地认出了她。

===Original===
She was all in black, even to her hands, no trace of white or any colour showing but the fair curve of the cheek below her mask and the red of her lips. And if more evidence were needed, the intelligence with which she attacked the combination, the confident, businesslike precision distinguishing her every action, proved her an apt pupil in that business.
His thoughts were all in a welter of miserable confusion. He knew that this explained many things he would have held questionable had not his infatuation forbidden him to consider them at all, lest he be disloyal to this woman whom he adored; but in the anguish of that moment he could entertain but one thought, and that possessed him altogether﻿—that she must somehow be saved from the evil she contemplated.﻿ ﻿…
But while he hesitated, she became sensitive to his presence; though he had made no sound since her entrance, though he had not even stirred, somehow she divined that he﻿—someone﻿—was there in the recess of the window, watching her.
In the act of opening the safe﻿—using the memorandum of its combination which he had jotted down in her presence﻿—he saw her pause, freeze to a pose of attention, then turn to stare directly at the portière that hid him. And for an eternal second she remained kneeling there, so still that she seemed not even to breathe, her gaze fixed and level, waiting for some sound, some sign, some tremor of the curtain’s folds, to confirm her suspicion.
When at length she rose it was in one swift, alert movement. And as she paused with her slight shoulders squared and her head thrown back defiantly, challengingly, as one without will of his own but drawn irresistibly by her gaze, he stepped out into the room.
And since he was no more the Lone Wolf, but now a simple man in agony, with no thought for their circumstances﻿—for the fact that they were both housebreakers and that the slightest sound might raise a hue-and-cry upon them﻿—he took one faltering step toward her, stopped, lifted a hand in a gesture of appeal, and stammered:

===Chinese===
她通身上下一色玄黑，连一双手也不例外；除却面罩下脸颊白皙的曲线和唇上的那点猩红，再无一丝白色或别的颜色显露。倘若还需更多的凭据，那么她对付那把密码锁时的那份伶俐机敏，她一举一动间那股自信、干练、有条不紊的利落，足证她在此道中是个高足。
他的思绪乱作一团，凄惶地搅缠不清。他知道，这一下许多事情便都解释得通了——若非痴恋之心禁止他去推究，他本该早存疑问的，只因生怕对这位他倾心爱慕的女人存半分不忠；可是在那一刻的痛楚中，他心里容得下的只有一个念头，那念头把他整个儿攫住了——她所图谋的那桩祸事，无论如何也得把她救出来。……
可就在他迟疑不决的当口，她已察觉出他的存在了；自她进屋以来，他不曾弄出半点声响，甚至连动也未曾动过，可她偏偏就猜到了——他——有什么人——正待在那窗龛里，注视着她。
她正开着手中的保险柜——用的正是他当着她面匆匆记下的那串密码备忘——他看见她蓦地停住，凝成一尊倾听的姿势，随即转过身来，直直瞪住遮着他的那幅帷幔。有一个漫长得近乎永恒的瞬间，她就那样跪着，静得仿佛连气息都断了，目光平直地凝定，只等一点声响、一点迹象、帘褶间一丝一毫的颤动，来坐实心头的疑窦。
待她终于站起身来，那动作迅捷而机警，一气呵成。她纤瘦的双肩一端，头颅倔强地向后一仰，站定在那里，一脸决绝、一脸挑战；而他仿佛失了自家主意、被她的目光不由分说地牵引着一般，抬脚跨进了房中。
此刻他已不再是独狼，只是一个痛彻心肺的寻常男人，全忘了他们眼下的处境——忘了两人同为入室行窃的贼，忘了哪怕最轻微的一点声响都可能引来一片捉贼的喧噪——他朝她趔趄着迈出一步，停住，抬起一只手做出恳求的姿势，讷讷地唤道：

===Original===
“Lucy﻿—you﻿—”
His voice broke and failed.
She didn’t answer, more than by recoiling as though he had offered to strike her, until the table stopped her, and she leaned back as if glad of its support.
“Oh!” she cried, trembling﻿—“why﻿—*why* did you do it?”
He might have answered her in kind, but self-justification passed his power. He couldn’t say, “Because this evening you made me lose faith in everything, and I thought to forget you by going to the devil the quickest way I knew﻿—this way!”﻿—though that was true. He couldn’t say: “Because, a thief from boyhood, habit proved too strong for me, and I couldn’t withstand temptation!”﻿—for that was untrue. He could only hang his head and mumble the wretched confession: “I don’t know.”
As if he hadn’t spoken, she cried again: “Why﻿—*why* did you do it? I was so proud of you, so sure of you, the man who had turned straight because of me!﻿ ﻿… It compensated﻿ ﻿… But now﻿ ﻿… !”

===Chinese===
「露西——是你——」
他的嗓音哽住，散了。
她没有回答——只是直往后缩，仿佛他要挥手打她一般，直缩到桌子拦住去路，才倚在桌上，倒像庆幸有它撑住似的。
「哦！」她颤声喊道——「为什么——你*为什么*要这么做？」
他本可以拿同样的话回敬她，只是自辩一事已非他力所能及。他没法说：「因为今晚你教我对一切都失了信念，我便想，要忘掉你，就走一条我所知道的最快的堕落路子——这条！」——尽管这是实情。他也没法说：「因为我自幼为贼，积习难改，抵不住诱惑！」——因为这不是实情。他只能垂下头，讷讷吐出那句可怜的自供：「我不知道。」
只当他的话不曾说过一般，她又喊了起来：「为什么——你*为什么*要这么做？我曾那样为你骄傲，那样信得过你——那个为了我才改邪归正的人！……这也算值了……可如今……！」

===Original===
Her voice broke in a short, dry sob.
“Compensated?” he repeated stupidly.
“Yes, compensated!” She lifted her head with a gesture of impatience: “For this﻿—don’t you understand?﻿—for this that I’m doing! You don’t imagine I’m here of my own will?﻿—that I went back to Bannon for any reason but to try to save you from him? I knew something of his power, and you didn’t; I knew if I went away with you he’d never rest until he had you murdered. And I thought if I could mislead him by lies for a little time﻿—long enough to give you a chance to escape﻿—I thought﻿—perhaps﻿—I might be able to communicate with the police, denounce him﻿—”
She hesitated, breathless and appealing.
At her first words he had drawn close to her; and all their talk was murmurings. But this was quite instinctive; for both were beyond considerations of prudence, the one coherent thought of each being that now, once and forever, all misunderstanding must be done away with.
Now, as naturally as though they had been lovers always, Lanyard took her hand, and clasped it between his own.

===Chinese===
她的声音断了，化作一声短促而干涩的呜咽。
「值了？」他木然跟着重复。
「是的，值了！」她抬起头来，做了个不耐烦的手势：「为的是这个——你还不明白么？——为的是我眼下干的这桩事！你总不至于以为，我是自己愿意到这儿来的吧？——总不至于以为，我回到班农身边，除了设法救你脱开他的手，还能有别的缘故？他的势力我有几分见识，你没有；我知道，我若随你走掉，他不到买凶害了你的性命决不肯甘休。我原想，若能拿谎话蒙他一蒙——哪怕短短一时，够你挣得一个脱身的机会——我想——也许——我还能递话给警察，告发他——」
她迟疑着，气息急促，眼里满是恳求。
她一开口，他便凑近了她；两人这一番话，句句都是低低的絮语。但这全然出于本能；因为两个都已把谨慎抛在了脑后，各人心头只剩一个清明的念头：就是现在，一劳永逸，把所有的误会统统涤荡干净。
此刻，自然而然，仿佛他们从来就是一对恋人似的，兰亚德握住她的手，把它拢进自己双手中间。

===Original===
“You cared as much as that!”
“I love you,” she told him﻿—“I love you so much I am ready to sacrifice everything for you﻿—life, liberty, honour﻿—”
“Hush, dearest, hush!” he begged, half distracted.
“I mean it: if honour could hold me back, do you think I would have broken in here tonight to steal for Bannon?”
“He sent you, eh?” Lanyard commented in a dangerous voice.
“He was too cunning for me﻿ ﻿… I was afraid to tell you﻿ ﻿… I meant to tell﻿—to warn you, this evening in the cab. But then I thought perhaps if I said nothing and sent you away believing the worst of me﻿—perhaps you would save yourself and forget me﻿—”

===Chinese===
「你对我的情意，竟有这么深！」
「我爱你，」她对他说——「我爱你爱到甘愿为你牺牲一切——生命、自由、名誉——」
「别说了，最亲爱的，别说了！」他央求道，心神都要乱了。
「我说的是真心话：倘若名誉还拘得住我，你想我今夜还会闯进这儿来，替班农做贼么？」
「是他打发你来的，嗯？」兰亚德接口道，嗓音里透着一股危险。
「他太狡诈，我斗不过……我不敢告诉你……我原打算说的——想警告你，就在今晚，在马车里。可转念一想，我若索性什么都不说，让你带着对我的最坏看法走开——说不定你倒能保全自己，把我忘掉——」

===Original===
“But never!”
“I tried my best to deceive him, but couldn’t. They got the truth from me by threats﻿—”
“They wouldn’t dare﻿—”
“They dare anything, I tell you! They knew enough of what had happened, through their spies, to go on, and they tormented and bullied me until I broke down and told them everything﻿ ﻿… And when they learned you had brought the jewels back here, Bannon told me I must bring them to him﻿—that, if I refused, he’d have you killed. I held out until tonight; then just as I was about to go to bed he received a telephone message, and told me you were driving a taxi and followed by Apaches and wouldn’t live till daylight if I persisted in refusing.”

===Chinese===
「可我永远也忘不掉！」
「我拼尽全力想骗过他，可是骗不过。他们用威胁逼我，逼出了真相——」
「他们不敢吧——」
「他们什么都敢，我告诉你！他们那些坐探早把内情摸了个八九不离十，尽够他们施展了；他们折磨我、威逼我，直到我支撑不住，把一切都倒了出来……后来他们得知你把珠宝送回了这宅子里，班农便对我说，我必须把珠宝给他取去——我若不肯，他就结果你的性命。我一直硬顶到今晚；谁知我刚要上床，他接了一个电话，便对我说，你正开着一辆出租车，叫一群阿帕奇追着，我若执意再拒，你便活不到天亮。」

===Original===
“You came alone?”
“No. Three men brought me to the gate. They’re waiting outside, in the park.”
“Apaches?”
“Two of them. The other is Captain Ekstrom.”
“Ekstrom!” Lanyard cried in despair. “Is he﻿—”
The dull, heavy, crashing slam of the great front doors silenced him.

===Chinese===
「你是一个人来的？」
「不是。三个人把我送到大门口。他们就在外头等着，在园子里。」
「是阿帕奇？」
「两个是。另一个是埃克斯特罗姆上尉。」
「埃克斯特罗姆！」兰亚德绝望地喊出声来。「他是不是——」
前宅大门闷沉沉、重甸甸、轰隆一声砸上，把他后半句话生生堵了回去。

## I Troyon’s / 一、特鲁瓦旅馆

===Original===
It must have been Bourke who first said that even if you knew your way about Paris you had to lose it in order to find it to Troyon’s. But then Bourke was proud to be Irish.

Troyon’s occupied a corner in a jungle of side-streets, well withdrawn from the bustle of the adjacent boulevards of St. Germain and St. Michel, and in its day was a restaurant famous with a fame jealously guarded by a select circle of patrons. Its cooking was the best in Paris, its cellar second to none, its rates ridiculously reasonable; yet Baedeker knew it not. And in the wisdom of the cognoscenti this was well: it had been a pity to loose upon so excellent an establishment the swarms of tourists that profaned every temple of gastronomy on the Rive Droit.

The building was of three storeys, painted a dingy drab and trimmed with dull green shutters. The restaurant occupied almost all of the street front of the ground floor, a blank, noncommittal double doorway at one extreme of its plate-glass windows was seldom open and even more seldom noticed.

This doorway was squat and broad and closed the mouth of a wide, stone-walled passageway. In one of its two substantial wings of oak a smaller door had been cut for the convenience of Troyon’s guests, who by this route gained the courtyard, a semi-roofed and shadowy place, cool on the hottest day. From the court a staircase, with an air of leading nowhere in particular, climbed lazily to the second storey and thereby justified its modest pretensions; for the two upper floors of Troyon’s might have been plotted by a nightmare-ridden architect after witnessing one of the first of the Palais Royal farces.

Above stairs, a medieval maze of corridors long and short, complicated by many unexpected steps and staircases and turns and enigmatic doors, ran every-which-way and as a rule landed one in the wrong room, linking together, in all, some twoscore bedchambers. There were no salons or reception-rooms, there was never a bathroom, there wasn’t even running water aside from two hallway taps, one to each storey. The honoured guest and the exacting went to bed by lamplight: others put up with candlesticks: gas burned only in the corridors and the restaurant﻿—asthmatic jets that, spluttering blue within globes obese, semi-opaque, and yellowish, went well with furnishings and decorations of the Second Empire to which years had lent a mellow and somehow rakish dinginess; since nothing was ever refurbished.

===Chinese===
说出这话的，想必是伯克——在巴黎，你纵然路路皆熟，也得先迷失了路，才寻得见特鲁瓦旅馆。不过呢，伯克向来以身为爱尔兰人自豪。

特鲁瓦旅馆蜗居在一片乱麻也似的僻街陋巷的角落里，远远退避开邻近圣日耳曼大道与圣米歇尔大道的喧嚣；盛时它是一家名餐馆，名声由一小圈子精选的老主顾妒意十足地呵护着。它的烹调冠绝巴黎，酒窖无出其右，价钱低得近乎荒唐；然而贝德克尔（Baedeker）旅行指南却对它一无所知。在行家们的智慧看来，这样正好：倘若放任那蜂拥的游客扑向如此一家上等好店——正是这路人，玷污了右岸（Rive Droit）的每一座美食圣殿——那才叫可惜呢。

楼共三层，漆成灰扑扑的土褐，缀着暗绿的百叶窗。餐馆几乎占尽底层的临街一面，其橱窗玻璃的尽头有一扇双开门，平板呆滞、莫测高深，难得开启，更难得受人留意。

这扇门矮而宽，把住一条宽敞石砌通道的口子。在两扇厚实橡木门扇的一扇上，又为特鲁瓦旅馆客人的方便开了扇小门，循此可达天井——半有顶棚、荫翳沉沉的去处，最热的天里也自清凉。从天井有道楼梯，一副不知通向何方的懒散做派，慢吞吞爬上二层，倒也不辱没它那份谦抑的矜持：须知特鲁瓦旅馆上面两层的设计图样，简直像出自哪位被噩梦魇住的建筑师之手——在看完帕拉丁皇家剧院（Palais Royal）早年一出闹剧之后。

楼上是一片中世纪式的迷魂阵，长廊短廊七拐八绕，又添上许许多多出其不意的台阶、楼梯、拐角和来路不明的门，横七竖八四处乱窜，十之八九把人送错房间，总共串起约莫四十来间卧房。没有客厅，没有会客室，绝没有浴室，甚至没有自来水——每层楼只在过道里装一对龙头。尊贵的主顾与讲究的主顾点着油灯就寝：旁人将就烛台：煤气只在走廊和餐馆里燃点——那一只只气喘吁吁的喷嘴，在肥胖、半透光、发黄的玻璃球罩里溅着蓝火苗，同第二帝国（Second Empire）风格的陈设装演正相得益彰；岁月又给这一切镀上层醇厚中透着几分放浪的旧暗，因为这里从不翻修任何东西。

===Original===
With such accommodations the guests of Troyon’s were well content. They were not many, to begin with, and they were almost all middle-aged bourgeois, a caste that resents innovations. They took Troyon’s as they found it: the rooms suited them admirably, and the tariff was modest. Why do anything to disturb the perennial peace of so discreet and confidential an establishment? One did much as one pleased there, providing one’s bill was paid with tolerable regularity and the hand kept supple that operated the cordon in the small hours of the night. Papa Troyon came from a tribe of innkeepers and was liberal-minded; while as for Madame his wife, she cared for nothing but pieces of gold.﻿ ﻿…

To Troyon’s on a wet winter night in the year 1893 came the child who as a man was to call himself Michael Lanyard.

He must have been four or five years old at that time: an age at which consciousness is just beginning to recognize its individuality and memory registers with capricious irregularity.
