「若真是走风，那条线早该把警察引到我身上了——当初你们费了多大的周章，要把罪名栽到我头上。这桩事里头的名堂，恐怕不止是简简单单的背信，维特海默先生。」
「也许你说得对，」对方若有所思地说。

===Original===
“And it doesn’t speak well for the discipline of your precious organization﻿—granting, for the sake of the argument, the possibility of such nonsense.”
“Well, well, have your own way about that. I don’t insist, so long as you agree to join forces with me.”
“Oh, it’s with you alone, now﻿—is it? Not with that insane fiction, the International Underworld Unlimited?”
“With me alone. I offer you a clear field. Go where you like, do what you will﻿—I wouldn’t have the cheek to attempt to guide or influence you.”
Lanyard kept himself in hand with considerable difficulty.

===Chinese===
「再者，这也够叫你们那个宝贝组织的纪律脸上一红——姑且为了抬杠起见，就算这派胡言真有其事。」
「罢了罢了，这一节随你怎么说。我不较真——只要你肯同我合兵一处。」
「哦，如今是同你一个——是吧？不同那家痴人说梦的『国际黑社会无限公司』了？」
「同我一个。我奉送你一片清静天地。想去哪去哪，想干什么干什么——我可没那份脸皮，妄想指点你或摆布你。」
兰亚德好不为难才按捺住了自己。

===Original===
“But you?” he asked. “Where do you come in?”
Wertheimer lounged back in his chair and laughed quietly. “Need you ask? Must I recall to you the foundations of my prosperity? You had the name of it glib enough on your tongue the other night in the rue Chaptal.﻿ ﻿… When you’ve done your work, you’ll come to me and split the proceeds fairly﻿—and as long as you do that, never a word will pass my lips!”
“Blackmail﻿ ﻿… !”
“Oh, if you insist! Odd, how I dislike that word!”
Abruptly the adventurer got to his feet. “By God!” he cried, “I’d better get out of this before I do you an injury!”
The door slammed behind him on a room ringing with Wertheimer’s unaffected laughter.

===Chinese===
「那你呢？」他问。「你的好处落在哪儿？」
维特海默往椅背上一仰，无声地笑了。「还用问？还要我给你细数我这份富贵的根基么？那天夜里在沙普塔尔街，这个名目你舌头上滚得可溜呢。……等你做完你的活计，便来寻我，赃利对半、公平分账——只要你守着这一条，我的嘴便一日缝得严丝合缝！」
「敲诈……！」
「哦，你非要叫这个名目！也怪，这个词儿我怎么这样不爱听！」
冒险家霍地立起身。「上帝作证！」他喊道，「趁我还没伤着你，还是趁早离了这个门！」
他身后，门砰然合上，满屋回荡着维特海默毫无做作的笑声。

## XX War / 二十、战争

===Original===
But why?﻿—he asked himself as he swung his cab aimlessly away﻿—why that blind rage with which he had welcomed Wertheimer’s overtures?
Unquestionably the business of blackmailing was despicable enough; and as a master cracksman, of the highest caste of the criminal world, the Lone Wolf had warrantably treated with scorn and contempt the advances of a pariah like Wertheimer. But in no such spirit had he comprehended the Englishman’s meaning, when finally that one came to the point; no cool disdain had coloured his attitude, but in the beginning hot indignation, in the end insensate rage.﻿ ﻿…
He puzzled himself. That fit of passion had all the aspect of a psychical inconsistency impossible to reconcile with reason.
He recalled in perplexity how, toward the last, the face of the Englishman had swum in haze before his eyes; with what disfavour, approaching hatred, he had regarded its fixed, false smirk; with what loathing he had suffered the intimacy of Wertheimer’s tone; how he had been tempted to fly at the man’s throat and shake him senseless in reward of his effrontery: emotions that had suited better a man of unblemished honour and integrity subjected to the insolent addresses of a contemptible blackguard, emotions that might well have been expected of the man Lanyard had once dreamed to become.
But now, since he had resigned that infatuate ambition and turned apostate to all his vows, his part in character had been to laugh in Wertheimer’s face and bid him go to the devil ere a worse thing befall him. Instead of which, he had flown into fury. And as he sat brooding over the wheel, he knew that, were the circumstances to be duplicated, his demeanour would be the same.
Was it possible he had changed so absolutely in the course of that short-lived spasm of reform?

===Chinese===
可是，为什么？——他驾着出租汽车漫无目标地荡开去，一面暗自盘问——为什么维特海默那番前来攀话试探，迎上去的竟是自己一股失明的狂怒？
无可置疑，敲诈这门营生本就够卑鄙；独狼身为窃贼世界里最高种姓的撬保险柜宗师，尽可以名正言顺地拿轻蔑和不齿，打发维特海默这等下三滥的殷勤。可待那英国人终于把话挑明时，他领会其来意的心境却全然不是那么回事：给他态度着色的不是冷静的倨傲，而是起初一腔滚烫的义愤，到末了一阵丧心病狂的暴怒。……
他把自己也想糊涂了。方才那阵激情，十足是一桩心理上的自相抵牾，拿道理断然调停不下。
他困惑地回忆起来：到了后来，那英国人的脸如何在自己眼前晕成一团雾霭；自己怀着怎样近乎憎恨的嫌恶，盯着那张脸上凝固的、虚伪的假笑；怎样满心厌恶地忍受维特海默那一口亲热得发腻的腔调；有几回简直想扑上去扼住那人的咽喉，把他那副厚颜无耻摇个不省人事，权当酬谢：这般情绪，更配一个清白端方、洁身自好的君子——当他遭到卑劣恶棍的恶语相戏之时；也是兰亚德昔日梦想成为的那种人，理当生出的情绪。
可如今，他既已辞谢那桩痴心妄想的抱负、背弃了昔日的一切誓愿，那么合乎他今日身份的做派，本该是冲着维特海默的脸哈哈一笑，趁比这更糟的事还没落到他头上，先打发他滚去见鬼。然而非但如此，他反倒勃然大怒。此刻他伏在方向盘上闷想，心里明白：倘使旧景重演一遍，他的做派仍会一模一样。
难道说，就在那阵为期短促、幡然向善的痉挛里，他竟脱胎换骨到了这般田地？

===Original===
He cried no to that: knowing well what he contemplated, that all his plans were laid and serious mischance alone could prevent him from putting them into effect, feeling himself once more quick with the wanton, ruthless spirit of the Lone Wolf, invincibly self-sufficient, strong and cunning.
When at length he roused from his reverie, it was to discover that his haphazard course had taken him back toward the heart of Paris; and presently, weary with futile cruising and being in the neighbourhood of the Madeleine, he sought the cab-rank there, silenced his motor, and relapsed into morose reflections so profound that nothing objective had any place in his consciousness.

===Chinese===
对此他断然喊出「不」：他深知自己此刻打的什么主意，深知全盘计划业已铺排停当，若非飞来横祸，什么也拦不住他见诸实行；他自觉重新怀满了独狼那股恣肆无情的精魂——自负自足，无坚不摧，既雄强又狡黠。
待他终于从沉思里惊醒，才发现这一路信马由缰，竟又荡回了巴黎腹地；不多时，白绕了半天、倦意滋生，他正行到马德莱娜一带，便驶进那里的出租汽车候点，熄了引擎，重新沉入一泓阴郁的深思——沉得那样深，以致身外万物在他意识里再无一席之地。

===Original===
Thus it was that without his knowledge a brace of furtive thugs were able to slouch down the rank, scrutinizing it covertly but in detail, pause opposite Lanyard’s car under pretext of lighting cigarettes, identify him to their satisfaction, and hastily take themselves off.
Not until they were quite disappeared did the driver of the cab ahead dare warn him.
Lounging back, this last looked the adventurer over inquisitively.
“Is it, then,” he enquired civilly, when Lanyard at length looked round, “that you are in the bad books of the good General Popinot, my friend?”
“Eh﻿—what’s that you say?” Lanyard asked, with a stare of blank misapprehension.

===Chinese===
就这样，趁他一无所觉，两个行迹鬼祟的凶徒得以顺着候点一路溜达下来，暗地里却把一排车子挨个儿细看，又在兰亚德的汽车对面借口点烟站定，把他验看得心满意足，方才匆匆开溜。
直到那两人去得没了影，前一辆出租车的司机才敢向他示警。
那位往后一仰，把这冒险家好奇地打量了一遍。
「这么说，」待兰亚德终于回过头来，他客客气气地问道，「我的朋友，您是在好心的波皮诺将军（General Popinot）那儿记上黑名了？」
「呃——您说什么？」兰亚德问，睁着眼一派茫然的错愕。

===Original===
The man nodded wisely. “He who is at odds with Popinot,” he observed, sententious, “does well not to sleep in public. You did not see those two who passed just now and took your number﻿—rats of Montmartre, if I know my Paris! You were dreaming, my friend, and it is my impression that only the presence of those two flies over the way prevented your immediate assassination. If I were you, I should go away very quickly, and never stop till I had put stout walls between myself and Popinot.”
A chill of apprehension sent a shiver stealing down Lanyard’s spine.
“You’re sure?”
“But of a certainty, my old one!”
“A thousand thanks!”
Jumping down, the adventurer cranked the motor, sprang back to his seat, and was off like a hunted hare.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
那人不无精明地点了点头。「同波皮诺结怨的人，」他一副警句腔地发表高见，「千万莫在露天地里打瞌睡。方才过去的那两位、顺手记下您车号的朋友，您竟没瞧见——蒙马特的地老鼠，凭我对巴黎的那点学问，错不了！您方才正做梦呢，我的朋友，而且据我看，全亏对面那两只苍蝇杵着，您才没当场送命。我要是您，就赶紧走，走得越快越好，半路也别歇，直到在自己和波皮诺之间砌起几堵厚墙才算完。」
一阵忧惧的寒意顺着兰亚德的脊梁骨悄悄爬下去，激得他一哆嗦。
「您拿得准？」
「那还用说，我的老伙计！」
「千恩万谢！」
冒险家跳下车去，摇着曲柄点起引擎，纵身蹿回座上，像只被追猎的野兔一般窜了出去。……

===Original===
And when, more than an hour later, he brought his panting car to a pause in a quiet and empty back-street of the Auteuil quarter, after a course that had involved the better part of Paris, it was with the conviction that he had beyond question shaken off pursuit﻿—had there in fact been any attempt to follow him.
He took advantage of that secluded spot to substitute false numbers for those he was licensed to display; then at a more sedate pace followed the line of the fortifications northward as far as La Muette, where, branching off, he sought and made a circuit of two sides of the private park enclosing the hôtel of Madame Omber.
But the mansion showed no lights, and there was nothing in the aspect of the property to lead him to believe that the chatelaine had as yet returned to Paris.

===Chinese===
一个多钟头以后，在跑过大半个巴黎的路线之后，他才把那辆喘息不已的汽车，停进欧特伊（Auteuil）区一条清静无人的后街；其时他已抱定一个信念：追兵——倘若当真有人追的话——毫无疑问，早叫他甩了个干净。
他借那处僻静，把执照上准挂的车号换上了假牌；随后放缓车速，循着巴黎城墙的走势一路向北，直到拉米埃特才拐下来，寻着环抱欧姆贝夫人私邸的那座私家园林，绕着走了两边。
可宅子里不见一星灯火，那片产业四外的气象，也无从叫他相信女主人已经回到巴黎。

===Original===
Now the night was still young, but Lanyard had his cab to dispose of and not a few other essential details to arrange before he could take definite steps toward the reincarnation of the Lone Wolf.
Picking a most circumspect route across the river﻿—via the Pont Mirabeau﻿—to the all-night telegraph bureau in the rue de Grenelle he despatched a cryptic message to the Minister of War, then with the same pains to avoid notice made back toward the rue des Acacias. But it wasn’t possible to recross the Seine secretly﻿—in effect, at least﻿—without returning the way he had come﻿—a long detour that irked his impatient spirit to contemplate.
Unwisely he elected to cross by way of the Pont des Invalides﻿—how unwisely was borne in upon him almost as soon as he turned from the brilliant Quai de la Conférence into the darkling rue François Premier. He had won scarcely twenty yards from the corner when, with a rush, its motor purring like some great tiger-cat, a powerful touring-car swept up from behind, drew abreast, but instead of passing checked speed until its pace was even with his own.
Struck by the strangeness of this manoeuvre, he looked quickly round, to recognize the moon-like mask of De Morbihan grinning sardonically at him over the steering-wheel of the black car.

===Chinese===
话说夜色尚早，可兰亚德手头还有这辆出租车须得打发，还有不少别的要紧节目须得安排，然后才谈得上迈出让独狼还魂转世的确切步子。
他拣了条顶顶谨慎的路线过河——取道米拉波桥——到格勒纳勒街（rue de Grenelle）那家通宵电报局，给陆军部长拍了一封隐语天书般的电报，随后又拿出同等的细心避开耳目，折向阿卡西亚街。只是，若不原路折回，再想悄没声儿地重渡塞纳河——至少在实际上——是办不到的：一想到这番长途绕行，他那颗急性子的心便好生烦躁。
他失策地拣了荣军院桥（Pont des Invalides）过河——这一失策有多重，几乎打他刚驶出灯彩煌煌的会议滨河道（Quai de la Conférence）、拐入暮色四合的弗朗索瓦一世街（rue François Premier），便领教明白了。他驶离街角还不满二十码，一辆强劲的旅行轿车便挟着一阵疾风从背后赶了上来，与他并了肩，却又不超过去，只收住车速，把步子放得同他一般齐——那引擎像一头大山猫似的，低哼着。
这一着透着古怪，他心头一凛，急促回首，登时认出那辆黑车的方向盘后，德·莫尔比昂那张月亮似的假面，正冲着他讪笑。

===Original===
A second hasty glance discovered four men in the tonneau. Lacking time to identify them, Lanyard questioned their character as little as their malign intent: Belleville bullies, beyond doubt, drafted from Popinot’s batallions, with orders to bring in the Lone Wolf, dead or alive.
He had instant proof that his apprehensions were not exaggerated. Of a sudden De Morbihan cut out the muffler and turned loose, full strength, the electric horn. Between the harsh detonations of the exhaust and the mad, blatant shrieks of the warning, a hideous clamour echoed and reechoed in that quiet street﻿—a din in which the report of a revolver-shot was drowned out and went unnoticed. Lanyard himself might have been unaware of it, had he not caught out of the corner of his eye a flash that spat out at him like a fiery serpent’s tongue, and heard the crash of the window behind him as it fell inward, shattered.
That the shot had no immediate successor was due almost wholly to Lanyard’s instant and instinctive action.

===Chinese===
再匆促一瞥，又发现后座舱（tonneau）里坐着四条大汉。不及细认眉目，兰亚德对他们是什么出身，也同对他们的歹毒来意一样，毫不置疑：分明是贝尔维尔的凶汉，从波皮诺的营伍里拔派的，奉命捉拿独狼归案——死的活的都成。
他的忧惧绝非夸张，铁证立时应验。德·莫尔比昂蓦地敞开消声器，又把电喇叭放开全副气力。排气口粗粝的爆响，夹着那警号疯狂嚣刺的锐啸，一片可怖的喧聒在那条安静的街上荡过来又荡回去——左轮枪的一声轰响便淹没在这片噪声里，无声无息。连兰亚德自己也未必察觉，若不是他眼角里掠进一道火光，像火蛇吐信似的朝他啐来，又听得身后车窗哗啦啦向内坍碎。
那一枪之所以没有立刻续上第二发，几乎全亏兰亚德出于本能、当机立断的一着。

===Original===
Even before the clash of broken glass registered on his consciousness, he threw in the high-speed and shot away like a frightened greyhound.
So sudden was this move that it caught De Morbihan himself unprepared. In an instant Lanyard had ten yards’ lead. In another he was spinning on two wheels round an acute corner, into the rue Jean Goujon; and in a third, as he shot through that short block to the avenue d’Antin, had increased his lead to fifteen yards. But he could never hope to better that: rather, the contrary. The pursuit had the more powerful car, and it was captained by one said to be the most daring and skilful motorist in France.
The considerations that dictated Lanyard’s simple strategy were sound if unformulated: barring interference on the part of the police﻿—something he dared not count upon﻿—his sole hope lay in open flight and in keeping persistently to the better-lighted, main-travelled thoroughfares, where a repetition of the attempt would be inadvisable﻿—at least, less probable. There was always a bare chance of an accident﻿—that De Morbihan’s car would burst a tire or be pocketed by the traffic, enabling Lanyard to strike off into some maze of dark side-streets, abandon the cab, and take to cover in good earnest.

===Chinese===
不等碎玻璃那一声炸响在他意识里挂号，他已推进高速挡，像条受惊的灵缇般蹿了出去。
这一着来得如此突兀，连德·莫尔比昂本人也着了道儿。一转瞬，兰亚德已抢出十码的先手；再一转瞬，他已两个轮子着地打旋，掠过一个锐角街角，拐进让·古容街（rue Jean Goujon）；第三转瞬，当他射过短短一个街区、直扑安坦大道（avenue d'Antin）时，已把先手扩到了十五码。但再想扩大，他是休想了：只会恰恰相反。追的一方马力更足，领队的又是号称全法兰西最胆大包天、手段最精的驾车人。
主宰着兰亚德这条简单方略的那些盘算，虽未成形，却条条在理：除非警方横插一杠——这他不敢指望——他唯一的活路便是明着奔逃，死死咬住灯火较亮、车马辐辏的通衢大道不撒嘴，在那等地方，故伎重演一遍未免不妥——至少，机会较小。天底下到底还有一线出岔子的微光——譬如德·莫尔比昂的车爆了胎，或教车流囿住，兰亚德便可趁隙扎进某片黑黢黢的陋巷迷宫，弃车而去，正儿八经寻个掩蔽。

===Original===
But that was a forlorn hope at best, and he knew it. Moreover, an accident was as apt to happen to him as to De Morbihan: given an unsound tire or a puncture, or let him be delayed two seconds by some traffic hindrance, and nothing short of a miracle could save him.﻿ ﻿…
As he swung from the avenue d’Antin into Rond Point des Champs Élysées, the nose of the pursuing car inched up on his right, effectually blocking any attempt to strike off toward the east, to the Boulevards and the centre of the city’s life by night. He had no choice but to fly westwards.
He cut an arc round the sexpartite circle of the Rond Point that lost no inch of advantage, and straightened out, *ventre-à-terre*, up the avenue for the place de l’Étoile, shooting madly in and out of the tide of more leisurely traffic﻿—and ever the motor of the touring-car purred contentedly just at his elbow.
If there were police about, Lanyard saw nothing of them: not that he would have dreamed of stopping or even of checking speed for anything less than an immovable obstacle.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
可这充其量不过是个渺茫的指望，他自己有数。况且，岔子落到他头上的概率，同落到德·莫尔比昂头上的一样现成：但教哪条轮胎不争气地爆了，或教什么交通障碍耽搁上两秒钟，那么除非天降奇迹，再没谁能救他。……
当他从安坦大道一拐，转入香榭丽舍圆形广场（Rond Point des Champs Élysées）时，追车的鼻子已一寸一寸蹭上他的右翼，把他向东脱身、奔大林荫大道、奔这座不夜城夜生活中心的一切念想，堵了个水泄不通。他别无选择，只好向西而遁。
他绕着那六岔圆场兜出一道弧线，分毫先机也不折损，随即扳正车身，*ventre-à-terre*（贴地疾驰），沿大道直取星形广场，在那股从容些的车流里疯了似的穿出穿进——而那辆旅行轿车的引擎，始终心满意足地低哼在他肘边。
那一带若有警察，兰亚德是半个也没瞧见：这倒不是说，换了什么事儿他都会异想天开地停车、甚至减速——除非撞上的是一座纹丝不动的障碍物。……

===Original===
But as minutes sped it became apparent that there was to be no renewed attempt upon his life for the time being. The pursuers could afford to wait. They could afford to ape the patience of Death itself.
And it came then to Lanyard that he drove no more alone: Death was his passenger.
Absorbed though he was with the control of his machine and the ever-shifting problems of the road, he still found time to think quite clearly of himself, to recognize the fact that he was very likely looking his last on Paris﻿ ﻿… on life.﻿ ﻿…
But a little longer, and the name of Michael Lanyard would be not even a memory to those whose lives composed the untiring life of this broad avenue.
Before him the Arc de Triomphe loomed ever larger and more darkly beautiful against the field of midnight stars. He wondered, would he reach it alive.﻿ ﻿…
He did: still the pursuit bided its time. But the hood of the touring-car nosed him inexorably round the arch, away from the avenue de la Grande Armée and into the avenue du Bois.

===Chinese===
然而随着分分秒秒飞逝，情形渐渐显豁：一时半会儿，他们不会再要他的命。追猎的一方等得起。他们大可以效法死神本人的耐心。
就在这时，兰亚德恍然彻悟：他驾的车里已不止他一人——死神，就坐在他的乘客席上。
他虽全神贯注于驾驭机器、应付路面上层出不穷的难题，却仍挤出闲工夫，把自己想了个通透，认清了这样一桩事实：他此刻十有八九，正在向巴黎投去最后一瞥……向人生投去最后一瞥。……
再过片刻，迈克尔·兰亚德这个名字，对于那些以各自的生活织成这条通衢大道不倦脉息的众生，便连一缕回忆也谈不上了。
在他前方，凯旋门（Arc de Triomphe）衬在子夜的星空上，身影愈来愈巍峨，愈来愈显出一种沉黑的美。他不知道，自己能否活着赶到它跟前。……
他赶到了：追猎的一方依旧在候着时机。只是那旅行轿车的车头，毫不容情地把他拱过凯旋门，逼离大军团大道，赶进了布瓦大道。

===Original===
Only when in full course for Porte Dauphine did he appreciate De Morbihan’s design. He was to be rushed out into the midnight solitudes of the Bois de Boulogne and there run down and slain.
But now he began to nurse a feeble thrill of hope.
Once inside the park enclosure, he reckoned vaguely on some opportunity to make sudden halt, abandon the car and, taking refuge in the friendly obscurity of trees and shrubbery, either make good his escape afoot or stand off the Apaches until police came to his aid. With night to cloak his movements and with a clump of trees to shelter in, he dared believe he would have a chance for his life﻿—whereas in naked streets any such attempt would prove simply suicidal.
Infrequent glances over-shoulder showed no change in the gap between his own and the car of the assassins. But his motor ran sweet and true: humouring it, coaxing it, he contrived a little longer to hold his own.

===Chinese===
直到一径向多芬门（Porte Dauphine）狂奔，他才咂摸出德·莫尔比昂的棋路：先把他一口气疾赶出城，逐入布洛涅林苑子夜阒寂的荒僻，再在那里碾倒他，结果他。
可到了这时，他心里反倒孵起了一星微弱而颤巍巍的希望。
只消进了园林的围墙，他模模糊糊地盘算着，总会有个空子让他骤然煞住，弃车而走，往树木灌丛那善意的昏暗里一藏，要么徒步脱身，要么顶住阿帕奇们的围攻，捱到警察赶来搭救。有夜色替他遮掩行藏，有一丛树木替他藏身，他竟敢相信自己会挣出一条活路——而在赤裸裸的街面上，这等尝试无异于白白送死。
隔一阵回一次头，只见他那辆车同刺客们的车之间的空当，分毫未变。但他的引擎运转得又甜又稳：他顺着它、哄着它，又勉力撑持了一程。

===Original===
Approaching the Porte Dauphine he became aware of two sergents de ville standing in the middle of the way and wildly brandishing their arms. He held on toward them relentlessly﻿—it was their lives or his﻿—and they leaped aside barely in time to save themselves.
And as he slipped into the park like a hunted shadow, he fancied that he heard a pistol-shot﻿—whether directed at himself by the Apaches, or fired by the police to emphasize their indignation, he couldn’t say. But he was grateful enough it was a taxicab he drove, not a touring-car: lacking the body of his vehicle to shield him, he little doubted that a bullet would long since have found him.
In that dead hour the drives of the Bois were almost deserted. Between the porte and the first carrefour he passed only one motorcar, a limousine whose driver shouted something inarticulate as Lanyard hummed past. The freedom from traffic dangers was a relief: but the pursuit was creeping up, inch by inch, as he swung down the roadway along the eastern border of the lake; and still he had found no opening, had recognized no invitation in the lay of the land to attempt his one plan; as matters stood, the Apaches would be upon him before he could jump from his seat.

===Chinese===
将近多芬门，他望见两名 sergent de ville（巡警）站在路心，没命地挥舞手臂。他毫不容情地朝他们直冲过去——不是他们的命，便是他的命——两人堪堪跳开，才算捡回性命。
当他像一道遭猎的影子溜进园林时，恍惚听得背后一声枪响——是阿帕奇们冲他放的，还是警察为壮义愤之色而鸣的，他说不上来。不过他已觉谢天谢地：幸而他开的是一辆带厢的出租车，不是敞篷的旅行车——若没有车身替他挡着，他毫不怀疑，一颗子弹早就寻着他了。
在那万籁俱寂的时辰，林苑里的几条车道几乎空无一人。从城门到头一个十字路口，他只交会过一辆汽车——一辆豪华轿车，兰亚德嗡地掠过时，那司机冲他嚷了句什么，字句含混。没了车马之险，倒是一桩宽慰：可追兵正一寸一寸地贴上来，这时他正循着湖东岸的行车路驶下坡去；而他至今不曾觅得空隙，不曾在这片地势里认出半点足以诱他一试那唯一良策的破绽；照眼前的局面，不等他跳离座位，阿帕奇们便会扑到他身上。

===Original===
Bending low over the wheel, searching with anxious eyes the shadowed reaches of that winding drive, he steered for a time with one hand, while the other tore open his ulster and brought his pistol into readiness.
Then, as he topped the brow of the incline, above the whine of his motor, the crackle of road-metal beneath the tires, and the boom of the rushing air in his ears, he heard the sharp clatter of hoofs, and surmised that the gendarmerie had given chase.
And then, on a slight downgrade, though he took it at perilous speed and seemed veritably to ride the wind, the following machine, aided by its greater weight, began to close in still more rapidly. Momentarily the hoarse snoring of its motor sounded more loud and menacing. It was now a mere question of seconds.﻿ ﻿…
Inspiration of despair came to him, as wild as any ever conceived by mind of man.

===Chinese===
他伏低在方向盘上，焦灼的目光搜索着那条蜿蜒车道阴影层叠的远近，有一阵只用一只手把舵，另一只手扯开长毛粗呢大衣（ulster），把手枪操到了待发的地步。
随后，正当他驶上坡顶，在引擎的嘶鸣之上，在路石于轮胎下炸响的噼啪之上，在灌满耳鼓的疾风轰鸣之上，他听见一阵尖利的马蹄嘚嘚，猜想必是宪兵队（gendarmerie）也出动了。
接着，在一道缓坡上，虽说他以骇人的车速冲了下去、俨然当真驾着风，后面那台机器却倚仗分量更重，开始愈加飞快地收拢距离。它引擎嘶哑的鼾声，一刻响似一刻，一刻狠似一刻。如今只剩几秒钟的事了。……
绝望催出的灵光，临到了他的头上——其狂想之烈，不亚于人类心智所曾孕育过的任何一桩。

===Original===
They approached a point where, on the left, a dense plantation walled the road. To the right a wide foot walk separated the drive from a gentle declivity sown with saplings, running down to the water.
Rising in his place, Lanyard slipped from under him the heavy waterproof cushion.
Then edging over to the left of the middle of the road, abruptly he shut off power and applied the brakes with all his might.
From its terrific speed the cab came to a stop within twice its length.
Lanyard was thrown forward against the wheel, but having braced in anticipation, escaped injury and effected instant recovery.

===Chinese===
他们行近一处地方：左手边，一片茂密的林子如高墙般夹着大路；右手边，一条宽阔的人行步道把车道同一道缓坡隔开，坡上遍植幼树，一路斜落到水边。
兰亚德在座位上直起身，把身底下那只厚实的防水坐垫悄悄抽了出来。
随后他把车向路中线偏左蹭去，蓦地切断动力，拼尽全身力气踩下刹车。
以那般骇人的速度，出租车在两倍于车身长短的距离内停了下来。
兰亚德被向前抛上方向盘，但他早有预料、绷紧了身架，因而毫发未伤，转瞬便稳住了阵脚。

===Original===
The car of the Apaches was upon him in a pulse-beat. With no least warning of his intention, De Morbihan had no time to employ brakes. Lanyard saw its dark shape flash past the windows of his cab and heard a shout of triumph. Then with all his might he flung the heavy cushion across that scant space, directly into the face of De Morbihan.
His aim was straight and true.
In alarm, unable to comprehend the nature of that large, dark, whirling mass, De Morbihan attempted to lift a warding elbow. He was too slow: the cushion caught him in the face, full-force, and before he could recover or guess what he was doing, he had twisted the wheel sharply to the right.
The car, running a little less than locomotive speed, shot across the strip of sidewalk, caught its right forewheel against a sapling, swung heavily broadside to the drive, and turned completely over as it shot down the slope to the lake.

===Chinese===
阿帕奇们的车一眨眼便扑到近前。兰亚德的意图没有半点先兆，德·莫尔比昂根本来不及踩刹车。兰亚德只见那团黑影从他车窗外一闪而过，听得一声得胜的欢呼。随即他抡足全力，把那只沉重的坐垫横掷过那一线空隙，端端正正掷进德·莫尔比昂的脸。
他这一掷，又端又正。
德·莫尔比昂吃了一惊，辨不清那又大又黑、旋转不止的一团究竟是什么物事，抬起一条胳膊肘想去招架。他慢了半拍：坐垫挟着全力砸在他脸上，不等他缓过神、也不等他明白自己在干什么，方向盘已被他狠狠向右拧死。
那辆车以仅比火车头略缓的速度，射过那窄窄一条人行道，右前轮在一株幼树上磕住，沉甸甸地横甩过车道，就势翻了个底朝天，一路射下坡去，直奔湖水。

===Original===
A terrific crash was followed by a hideous chorus of oaths, shrieks, cries and groans.
Promptly Lanyard started his motor anew and, trembling in every limb, ran on for several hundred yards. But time pressed, and the usefulness of his car was at an end, as far as he was concerned; there was no saying how many times its identity might not have been established by the police in the course of that wild chase through Paris, or how soon these last might contrive to overhaul and apprehend him; and as soon as a bend in the road shut off the scene of wreck, he stopped finally, jumped down, and plunged headlong into the dark midnight heart of the Bois, seeking its silences where trees stood thickest and lights were few.
Later, like some worried creature of the night, panting, dishevelled, his rough clothing stained and muddied, he slunk across an open space, a mile or so from his point of disappearance, dropped cautiously down into the dry bed of the moat, climbed as stealthily a slippery glacis of the fortifications, darted across the inner boulevard, and began to describe a wide arc toward his destination, the hôtel Omber.

===Chinese===
一声惊天动地的轰响过后，紧随而至的，是咒骂、尖嚎、呼号与呻吟交织的一场骇人大合唱。
兰亚德当即重新发动引擎，四肢兀自发颤，又朝前跑出几百码。但光阴紧迫，而就他而言，这辆车的用处已经到了头：在那场横穿巴黎的疯狂追逐里，警方谁知已几番验明它的身份；也不知他们几时便会追上来、把他拿下；于是一俟一个路弯把失事的现场遮断，他便最终停了车，跳下来，一头扎进林苑子夜漆黑的心脏，专拣树木最密、灯火最稀的静处觅路而行。
后来，活像一头惶惶不可终日的夜游生物，他气喘吁吁、蓬头散发，一身粗呢衣服又是污渍又是泥浆，鬼鬼祟祟溜过一片空地——离他销声匿迹之处约莫一英里——小心翼翼地溜下干涸的壕底，又蹑手蹑脚爬上城墙一道滑溜溜的斜堤（glacis），闪过内环林荫大道，开始朝他的目的地欧姆贝宅，兜出一道大大的弧线。

## XXV Wings of the Morning / 二十五、清晨之翼

===Original===
About half-past six Lanyard left the dressing-room assigned him in the barracks at Port Aviation and, waddling quaintly in the heavy wind-resisting garments supplied him at the instance of Ducroy, made his way between two hangars toward the practice field.
Now the eastern skies were pulsing with fitful promise of the dawn; but within the vast enclosure of the aerodrome the gloom of night lingered so stubbornly that two huge searchlights had been pressed into the service of those engaged in tuning up the motor of the *Parrott* biplane.
In the intense, white, concentrated glare﻿—that rippled oddly upon the wrinkled, oily garments of the dozen or so mechanics busy about the machine﻿—the under sides of those wide, motionless planes hung against the dark with an effect of impermanence: as though they were already afloat and needed but a breath to send them winging skyward.﻿ ﻿…
To one side a number of young and keen-faced Frenchmen, officers of the corps, were lounging and watching the preparations with alert and intelligent interest.
To the other, all the majesty of Mars was incarnate in the person of Monsieur Ducroy, posing valiantly in fur-lined coat and shining top-hat while he chatted with an officer whose trim, athletic figure was well set off by his aviating uniform.
As Lanyard drew near, this last brought his heels together smartly, saluted the Minister of War, and strode off toward the flying-machine.

===Chinese===
六点半光景，兰亚德离开了航空港营房里分派给他的更衣室。他裹着迪克罗吩咐人发给他的那身厚重防风装，一步一摆、模样颇为古怪，打两座机库中间穿行，朝练习场走去。
此刻东方天际正一阵一阵搏动着黎明的许诺；然而飞行场那一大片围场之内，夜色依旧恋栈不去、顽固得很，以致两座巨大的探照灯也被调来当差，供人调试*帕罗特*（*Parrott*）双翼机的发动机。
在那强白而凝聚的光柱里——光华在十来个围着机器忙碌的机械士那皱巴巴、油渍渍的工装上奇异地荡漾——那一双双宽阔静止的翼底衬着黑暗悬垂着，透出一股难以久驻之感：仿佛它们已然浮在空中，只消一口气，便能送它们振翅上天。……
一边，是一群年轻而面孔精悍的法国人——航空队里的军官们——闲闲散立，以警觉而聪慧的兴味，观看准备工作的进行。
另一边，战神玛尔斯的全部威仪都化身为迪克罗先生其人：他一身皮里大衣、头顶锃亮的大礼帽，英姿勃发地摆着架势，正与一位军官攀谈；那军官身姿利落矫健，穿上飞行制服愈发显得挺拔出挑。
兰亚德走近时，那军官咔地一声并拢脚跟，向陆军部长行了个礼，迈开大步朝飞行器走去。

===Original===
“Captain Vauquelin informs me he will be ready to start in five minutes, monsieur,” Ducroy announced. “You are in good time.”
“And mademoiselle?” the adventurer asked, peering anxiously round.
Almost immediately the girl came forward from the shadows, with a smile apologetic for the strangeness of her attire.
She had donned, over her street dress, an ample leather garment which enveloped her completely, buttoning tight at throat and wrists and ankles. Her small hat had been replaced by a leather helmet which left only her eyes, nose, mouth and chin exposed, and even these were soon to be hidden by a heavy veil for protection against spattering oil.
“Mademoiselle is not nervous?” Ducroy enquired politely.
Lucy smiled brightly.

===Chinese===
「沃克兰上尉告知我，五分钟后便可起飞，先生。」迪克罗宣布道，「二位来得正是时候。」
「那么小姐呢？」冒险家焦急地四下张望，问道。
几乎紧接着，姑娘便从暗影里走了出来，脸上带着笑，先为自家那身奇装异服告了个罪。
她在出门衣裙外头罩了一件宽大的皮衣，将全身裹得严严实实，颈口、手腕、脚踝处处扣紧。小帽换成了一顶皮盔，只露出眼睛、鼻子、嘴巴和下巴，而连这些地方，转眼也要罩进一层厚重的面纱，好抵挡喷溅的机油。
「小姐不紧张么？」迪克罗客气地探问。
露西灿然一笑。

===Original===
“I? Why should I be, monsieur?”
“I trust mademoiselle will permit me to commend her courage. But pardon! I have one last word for the ear of Captain Vauquelin.”
Lifting his hat, the Frenchman joined the group near the machine.
Lanyard stared unaffectedly at the girl, unable to disguise his wonder at the high spirits advertised by her rekindled colour and brilliant eyes.
“Well?” she demanded gaily. “Don’t tell me I don’t look like a fright! I know I do!”
“I daren’t tell you how you look to me,” Lanyard replied soberly. “But I will say this, that for sheer, down right pluck, you﻿—”

===Chinese===
「我？我干吗要紧张呀，先生？」
「还望小姐容我赞一句胆色。不过失陪！我还有最后一句话，要说与沃克兰上尉的耳朵。」
法国人举了举帽子，凑到机器旁那一群人里去了。
兰亚德毫不作假地盯着姑娘瞧：她那重燃的血色和晶亮的双眸，处处标榜着一股高涨的兴头，他满心的惊奇怎么也掩饰不住。
「怎么着？」她快活地发问，「可别说我这模样不像个丑八怪！我自己知道像极了！」
「你在我眼里是什么模样，我可不敢说。」兰亚德正色答道，「但有一句话我敢讲：单论这份彻头彻尾的胆气，你——」

===Original===
“Thank you, monsieur! And you?”
He glanced with a deprecatory smile at the flimsy-looking contrivance to which they were presently to entrust their lives.
“Somehow,” said he doubtfully, “I don’t feel in the least upset or exhilarated. It seems little out of the average run of life﻿—all in the day’s work!”
“I think,” she said, judgmatical, “that you’re very like the other lone wolf, the fictitious one﻿—Lupin, you know﻿—a bit of a blagueur. If you’re not nervous, why keep glancing over there?﻿—as if you were rather expecting somebody﻿—as if you wouldn’t be surprised to see Popinot or De Morbihan pop out of the ground﻿—or Ekstrom!”
“*Hum!*” he said gravely. “I don’t mind telling you now, that’s precisely what I am afraid of.”
“Nonsense!” the girl cried in open contempt. “What could they do?”

===Chinese===
「多谢夸奖，先生！那么您呢？」
他带着自谦的一笑，望了望那副瞧着单薄的家什——他们转眼就要把性命托付给它。
「不知怎么，」他将信将疑地说，「我心里既不着慌，也不亢奋。这档子事似乎没怎么出格——不过例行公事罢了！」
「我看呀，」她一副明断的口吻说，「你活脱脱像另一位独狼——那位纸面上的独狼——你知道的，亚森·罗平（Lupin）——多少是个 blagueur（爱说大话的行家）。你要是不紧张，干吗一个劲儿往那边瞟？——好像你巴望着谁来——好像瞧见波皮诺或者德·莫尔比昂从地底下蹦出来，你也不会吃惊——再不然，就是埃克斯特罗姆！」
「*嗯！*」他郑重其事地说，「如今我不妨告诉你：我怕的恰恰就是这个。」
「瞎说！」姑娘公然嗤之以鼻，「他们还能怎么样？」

===Original===
“Please don’t ask me,” Lanyard begged seriously. “I might try to tell you.”
“But don’t worry, my dear!” Fugitively her hand touched his. “We’re ready.”
It was true enough: Ducroy was moving impressively back toward them.
“All is prepared,” he announced in sonorous accents.
A bit sobered, in silence they approached the machine.
Vauquelin kept himself aloof while Lanyard and a young officer helped the girl to the seat to the right of the pilot, and strapped her in. When Lanyard had been similarly secured in the place on the left, the two sat, imprisoned, some six feet above the ground.

===Chinese===
「请别问我，」兰亚德一本正经地央告道，「我怕我会忍不住，试着一一道来。」
「可别犯愁，亲爱的！」她的手倏地碰了碰他的手。「我们准备好了。」
这话不假：迪克罗正迈着威严可观的步子，朝他们这边走回来。
「一切已准备停当。」他以洪亮的腔调宣布。
两人神色微敛，默默走近那架机器。
沃克兰矜持地立在一边，由兰亚德和一名年轻军官扶着姑娘坐上驾驶员右侧的座位，替她系牢皮带。待兰亚德也在左边那个位子上同样被缚停当，两人便这般受着拘囚，端坐在离地约六英尺的半空。

===Original===
Lanyard found his perch comfortable enough. A broad band of webbing furnished support for his back; another crossed his chest by way of provision against forward pitching; there were rests for his feet, and for his hands cloth-wound grips fixed to struts on either side.
He smiled at Lucy across the empty seat, and was surprised at the clearness with which her answering smile was visible. But he wasn’t to see it again for a long and weary time; almost immediately she began to adjust her veil.
The morning had grown much lighter within the last few minutes.
A long wait ensued, during which the swarm of mechanics, assistants and military aviators buzzed round their feet like bees.
The sky was now pale to the western horizon. A fleet of heavy clouds was drifting off into the south, leaving in their wake thin veils of mist that promised soon to disappear before the rays of the sun. The air seemed tolerably clear and not unseasonably cold.
The light grew stronger still: features of distant objects defined themselves; traces of colour warmed the winter landscape.

===Chinese===
兰亚德发觉这栖身之处倒还算舒服。一条宽阔的帆布带托住他的后背；另一条横过胸前，以防身子前冲；脚下有踏蹬，两手旁边各有缠布的把手，固定在两侧的支柱上。
他隔着当中那把空座朝露西微笑，惊觉她回眸一笑竟看得那样分明。可是此后一段漫长难熬的时光里，他再也无缘见到它了；几乎紧接着，她便动手放下面纱。
这几分钟里，天色已经亮了许多。
接着是久久的等候，其间那一大群机械士、帮手和军用飞行员围着他俩脚下嗡嗡营营，活像一窝蜜蜂。
天空此刻淡白到了西天的地平线。一大阵浓云正往南飘散，身后拖下一绺绺薄雾，瞧那情势，转眼便要在日头底下消散。空气还算清朗，冷得也不算反季。
光线愈发强了：远处景物的眉目自行显形；一痕一痕的色彩，给冬日的大地添了暖意。

===Original===
At length their pilot, wearing his wind-mask, appeared and began to climb to his perch. With a cool nod for Lanyard and a civil bow to his woman passenger, he settled himself, adjusted several levers, and flirted a gay hand to his brother-officers.
There was a warning cry. The crowd dropped back rapidly to either side. Ducroy lifted his hat in parting salute, cried “Bon voyage!” and scuttled clear like a startled rooster before a motorcar. And the motor and propeller broke loose with a mighty roar comparable only, in Lanyard’s fancy, to the chant of ten thousand rivetting locusts.
He felt momentarily as if his eardrums must burst with the incessant and tremendous concussions registered upon them; but presently this sensation passed, leaving him with that of permanent deafness.
Before he could recover and regain control of his startled wits the aviator had thrown down a lever, and the great fabric was in motion.
It swept down the field like a frightened swan; and the wheels of its chassis, registering every infinitesimal irregularity in the surface of the ground, magnified them all a hundredfold. It was like riding in a tumbril driven at top-speed over the Giant’s Causeway. Lanyard was shaken violently to the very marrow of his bones; he believed that even his eyes must be rattling in their sockets.﻿ ﻿…
Then the *Parrott* began to ascend. Singularly enough, this change was marked, at first, by no more than slight lessening of the vibration: still the machine seemed to be dashing over a cobbled thoroughfare at breakneck speed; and Lanyard found it difficult to appreciate that they were afloat, even when he looked down and discovered a hundred feet of space between himself and the practice-field.

===Chinese===
末了，他们的飞行员戴好防风面罩，现身出来，开始攀上自己的座位。他对兰亚德冷淡地一点头，对女乘客客气地一躬身，坐定下来，调了几根操纵杆，朝他的军官同僚们俏皮地一挥手。
一声示警的高呼。人群迅速向两旁退开。迪克罗举帽作别，喊了声「Bon voyage（一路平安）！」便像汽车跟前受了惊的公鸡似的窜开去。紧接着，发动机和螺旋桨轰然挣脱出来，那声势浩大的怒吼，在兰亚德的想象里，只有一万只铆钉鸣蝉的合唱差可比拟。
有那么一瞬，他觉得那接连不断、惊天动地的声浪捶在耳膜上，耳膜非给震破不可；可这感觉旋即过去，留给他的是一种从此永久失聪之感。
还不等他定下神来、收回受惊的魂魄，飞行员已扳下一根操纵杆，那庞大的家伙动起来了。
它像一只受惊的天鹅掠过场地；起落架的轮子把地面每一丝微末的凹凸都如实收录，又尽数放大百倍。这好比乘着一辆全速疾驰的死刑囚车，颠过巨人堤道（Giant’s Causeway）。兰亚德被震得连骨头缝里的骨髓都在晃；他相信连自己的眼珠都要在眼窝里咯咯作响了。……
随后，*帕罗特*开始爬升。说来奇怪，这一变化起初只不过表现为震动略略见轻：机器依旧像在卵石铺就的大道上不要命地飞奔；兰亚德很难领会他们已然腾空，乃至低头看见自身与练习场之间已隔着一百英尺的空当，也仍难相信。

===Original===
In another breath they were soaring over housetops.
Momentarily, now, the shocks became less frequent. And presently they ceased almost altogether, to be repeated only at rare intervals, when the drift of air opposing the planes developed irregularities in its velocity. There succeeded, in contrast, the sublimest peace; even the roaring of the propeller dwindled to a sustained drone; the biplane seemed to float without an effort upon a vast, still sea, flawed only occasionally by inconsiderable ripples.
Still rising, they surprised the earliest rays of the sun; and in their virgin light the aeroplane was transformed into a thing of gossamer gold.
Continually the air buffeted their faces like a flood of icy water.
Below, the scroll of the world unrolled like some vast and intricately illuminated missal, or like some strange mosaic, marvellously minute.﻿ ﻿…
Lanyard could see the dial of the compass, fixed to a strut on the pilot’s left. By that telltale their course lay nearly due northeast. Already the weltering roofs of Paris were in sight, to the right, the Eiffel Tower spearing up like a fairy pillar of gold lacework, the Seine looping the cluttered acres like a sleek brown serpent, the Sacré-Cœur a dream-palace of opalescent walls.

===Chinese===
再一转瞬，他们已翱翔在重重屋顶之上。
此刻，颠簸一阵稀似一阵。很快便几乎全然止歇，只在迎着翼面的气流生出速度不匀的时候，才偶尔卷土重来。继之而来的，是截然相反的、至极的安宁；连螺旋桨的怒吼也弱成一片绵长的嗡鸣；这架双翼机仿佛毫不费力地浮在一片浩瀚静穆的大海上，只偶尔被几丝微不足道的涟漪搅扰。
一路攀升，他们迎头截住了太阳最早的光线；在那处子般的光里，这架飞机化作了一件蛛丝织金的造物。
气流不住地扑打他们的脸，好比一股接一股的冰水兜头浇下。
脚下，世界的长卷徐徐展开，像一部浩大而纹饰繁复的彩绘弥撒书（missal），又像一幅奇异的镶嵌画，精微得不可思议。……
兰亚德看得见罗盘的表盘，装在飞行员左手边的一根支柱上。凭那出卖行藏的指针，他们的航向差不多是正东北。巴黎翻腾挤叠的屋顶已经在望，在右方：埃菲尔铁塔像一根金丝镂花的仙柱直刺上去，塞纳河像一条油光的褐色巨蟒，蜿蜒盘过那片鳞次栉比的屋海，圣心堂则是一座乳光石墙的梦中宫殿。

===Original===
Versailles broke the horizon to port and slipped astern. Paris closed up, telescoped its panorama, became a mere blur, a smoky smudge. But it was long before the distance eclipsed that admonitory finger of the Eiffel.
Vauquelin manipulating the levers, the plane tilted its nose and swam higher and yet higher. The song of the motor dropped an octave to a richer tone. The speed was sensibly increased.
Lanyard contemplated with untempered wonder the fact of his equanimity: there seemed nothing at all strange in this extraordinary experience; he was by no means excited, remained merely if deeply interested. And he could detect in his physical sensations no trace of that qualmish dread he always experienced in high places: the sense he had of security, of solidity, was and ever remained wholly unaccountable in his understanding.
Of a sudden, surprised by a touch on his arm, he turned to see through the mica windows of the wind-mask the eyes of the aviator informed with importunate doubt. Infinitely mystified and so an easy prey to sickening fear lest something were going wrong with the machine, Lanyard shook his head to indicate lack of comprehension. With an impatient gesture the aviator pointed downward. Appreciating the fact that speech was impossible, Lanyard clutched the struts and bent forward. But the pace was now so fast and their elevation so great that the landscape swimming beneath his vision was no more than a brownish plain fugitively maculated with blots of contrasting colour.
He looked up blankly, but only to be treated to the same gesture.
Piqued, he concentrated attention more closely upon the flat, streaming landscape. And suddenly he recognized something oddly familiar in an approaching bend of the Seine.

===Chinese===
凡尔赛在左舷撞破地平线，又滑到机尾后头去了。巴黎收拢身量，把全景缩成一卷，化作一团模糊的影、一抹烟痕。但隔了许久，那段距离才吞没埃菲尔铁塔那根警示的手指。
沃克兰拨弄着操纵杆，机身抬起机头，越游越高。发动机的歌唱低了八度，转为更醇厚的音色。速度分明加了上去。
兰亚德怀着不加稀释的惊奇，审视着自己心境平静这一事实：这段奇遇于他竟好像没有半分不寻常；他绝非亢奋，只是兴味盎然——而且这兴味委实不浅。再者，在他周身的感觉里，他寻不出半点他每逢登高必犯的那种呕心的恐惧：他心里这份安稳、这份扎实之感，当时如此，其后也始终如此，在他自己的理解里全然无从解释。
冷不防臂上一触，他吃了一惊，转过头，透过防风面罩的云母镜窗，看见飞行员的一双眼睛布满急切难安的疑虑。兰亚德丈二金刚摸不着头脑，于是轻而易举成了那令人作呕的恐惧的猎物——生怕机器出了什么岔子——他摇了摇头，表示不明其意。飞行员做个不耐烦的手势，朝下一指。兰亚德明白言语是无望的，便攥住支柱，俯身向前。可此刻速度太快、高度太高，在他目光下游动的大地，不过是一片褐色平原，倏忽间点缀着几块反差刺目的色斑。
他茫然抬眼，得到的却还是那同一个手势。
他着了恼，把注意力更紧地聚在那幅平坦流动的画卷上。蓦地，他在塞纳河一处迎面而来的河湾里，认出了一种说不出的眼熟。

===Original===
“St.-Germain-en-Laye!” he exclaimed with a start of alarm.
This was the danger point.﻿ ﻿…
“And over there,” he reminded himself﻿—“to the left﻿—that wide field with a queer white thing in the middle that looks like a winged grub﻿—that must be De Morbihan’s aerodrome and his *Valkyr* monoplane! Are they bringing it out? Is that what Vauquelin means? And if so﻿—what of it? I don’t see﻿ ﻿…”
Suddenly doubt and wonder chilled the adventurer.
Temporarily Vauquelin returned entire attention to the management of the biplane. The wind was now blowing more fitfully, creating pockets﻿—those holes in the air so dreaded by cloud pilots﻿—and in quest of more constant resistance the aviator was swinging his craft in a wide northerly curve, climbing ever higher and more high.
The earth soon lost all semblance of design; even the twisted silver wire of the Seine vanished, far over to the left; remained only the effect of firm suspension in that high blue vault, of a continuous low of iced water in the face, together with the tuneless chanting of the motor.

===Chinese===
「圣日耳曼昂莱（St.-Germain-en-Laye）！」他失声惊呼，心头一凛。
这里正是那处凶险之地。……
「还有那边，」他提醒自己——「在左手——那片开阔的场子，当中卧着个古怪的白家伙，活像一条长翅的蛴螬——那必是德·莫尔比昂的飞行场和他的*瓦尔基里*（*Valkyr*）号单翼机了！他们要把它弄出来？沃克兰指的是这个？若是这样——那又如何？我不明白……」
疑云与困惑骤然袭来，冒险家遍体生寒。
沃克兰暂且把全副心思收回，专顾驾驭这架双翼机。此刻风刮得愈发一阵一阵，激出一个个气穴——就是云端飞行员闻之色变的那种「空中窟窿」——为寻求更稳定的承托，飞行员正把他的机器荡上一个往北的大弧线，越爬越高，高之又高。
大地很快失尽了章法；连塞纳河那根拧曲的银线也无影无踪，远远落在左手边；剩下的只有稳稳悬于那高蓝穹窿之间的感觉，只有劈面而来、绵延不绝的冰水，外加发动机那不成腔调的吟唱。

===Original===
After some forty minutes of this﻿—it may have been an hour, for time was then an incalculable thing﻿—Lanyard, in a mood of abnormal sensitiveness, began to divine additional disquiet in the mind of the aviator, and stared until he caught his eye.
“What is it?” he screamed in futile effort to lift his voice above the din.
But the Frenchman understood, and responded with a sweep of his arm toward the horizon ahead. And seeing nothing but cloud in the quarter indicated, Lanyard grasped the nature of a phenomenon which, from the first, had been vaguely troubling him. The reason why he had been able to perceive no real rim to the world was that the earth was all a-steam from the recent heavy rains; all the more remote distances were veiled with rising vapour. And now they were approaching the coast, to which, it seemed, the mists clung closest; for all the world before them slept beneath a blanket of dull grey.
Nor was it difficult now to understand why the aviator was ill at ease facing the prospect of navigating a Channel fog.
Several minutes later, he startled Lanyard with another peremptory touch on his arm followed by a significant glance over his shoulder.
Lanyard turned quickly.

===Chinese===
这样过了约莫四十分钟——也可能是一个钟头，因为那时辰本是无可计较之物——兰亚德正处在一种异样敏感的心境里，开始揣摩出飞行员心头又添了一层不安，便盯住他瞧，直到勾住他的眼光。
「怎么回事？」他尖声喊道，妄图把嗓门抬过那片喧嚣——终归徒劳。
但那法国人懂了，抡起手臂朝前方地平线一挥。而在所指的那一方，除了云还是云；兰亚德这才悟透一桩打从头起便隐隐搅扰着他的景象的底细。他之所以始终望不见世界真正的边缘，是因为连日大雨之后，大地整片蒸腾着水汽；一切较远的远处都笼上了冉冉升起的雾瘴。而此刻他们正逼近海岸——雾气缠抱得最紧的，似乎正是那里；眼前整个世界，都睡在一床死灰色的毯子底下。
如今也不难明白，飞行员一想到要闯一场英吉利海峡的大雾，为何心神不宁了。
几分钟后，他又不容分说地在兰亚德臂上一拍，随即意味深长地朝肩后一瞥，惊得兰亚德一跳。
兰亚德霍地回头。

===Original===
Behind them, at a distance which he calculated roughly as two miles, the silhouette of a monoplane hung against the brilliant firmament, resembling, with its single spread of wings, more a solitary, soaring gull than any man-directed mechanism.
Only an infrequent and almost imperceptible shifting of the wings proved that it was moving.
He watched it for several seconds, in deepening perplexity and anxiety, finding it impossible to guess whether it were gaining or losing in that long chase, or who might be its pilot.
Yet he had little doubt but that the pursuing machine had risen from the aerodrome of Count Remy de Morbihan at St.-Germain-en-Laye; that it was nothing less, in fact, than De Morbihan’s *Valkyr*, reputed the fastest monoplane in Europe and winner of a dozen International events; and that it was guided, if not by De Morbihan himself, by one of the creatures of the Pack﻿—quite possibly, even more probably, by Ekstrom!
But﻿—assuming all this﻿—what evil could such pursuit portend? In what conceivable manner could the Pack reckon to further its ends by commissioning the monoplane to overtake or distance the *Parrott*? They could not hinder the escape of Lanyard and Lucy Shannon to England in any way, by any means reasonably to be imagined.
Was this simply one more move to keep the pair under espionage? But that might more readily have been accomplished by telegraphing or telephoning the Pack’s confrères, Wertheimer’s associates in England!

===Chinese===
在他们身后，距离约莫两英里——他粗粗估量——一架单翼机的侧影悬在灿亮的天穹上：凭那单独张开的一双翅膀，它与其说像什么人造的机器，倒更像一只孤身翱翔的海鸥。
只有那双翅膀不时一颤、颤得几乎无从察觉，才证明它在动。
他盯着它看了好几秒钟，困惑与焦灼愈积愈深：这场长追里它是逼近了还是落后了，掌舵的又是何方神圣，全都无从猜起。
然而他几乎毫不怀疑：那架追来的机器，正是从圣日耳曼昂莱雷米·德·莫尔比昂伯爵的飞行场上腾空的；说穿了，它不是别的，正是德·莫尔比昂的*瓦尔基里*号——号称全欧最快的单翼机、十二项国际赛事的赢家；而掌着它舵的，若非德·莫尔比昂本人，便是群狼的一名爪牙——很可能，甚至更可能，是埃克斯特罗姆！
可是——纵然这一切属实——这般追击又能预兆什么祸殃？群狼差遣这架单翼机去追上或者甩掉*帕罗特*，究竟想凭哪种想得出来的路数，成全他们的图谋？任凭什么想得出来的手段，他们也拦不住兰亚德和露西·香农逃往英国，这是明摆着的。
这莫非只是又一着棋，好教这一双男女仍处在监视之下？可要办成这个，打个电报、通个电话给群狼的同道（confrères）——英国那边维特海默的一伙——岂不更省事！

===Original===
Lanyard gave it up, admitting his inability to trump up any sane excuse for such conduct; but the riddle continued to fret his mind without respite.
From the first, from that moment when Lucy’s disappearance had required postponement of this flight, he had feared trouble; it hadn’t seemed reasonable to hope that the *Parrott* could be held in waiting on his convenience for many days without the secret leaking out; but it was trouble to develop before the start from Port Aviation that he had anticipated. The possibility that the Pack would be able to work any mischief to him, after that, had never entered his calculations. Even now he found it difficult to give it serious consideration.
Again he glanced back. Now, in his judgment, the monoplane loomed larger than before against the glowing sky, indicating that it was overtaking them.
Beneath his breath Lanyard swore from a brimming heart.
The *Parrott* was capable of a speed of eighty miles an hour; and unquestionably Vauquelin was wheedling every ounce of power out of its willing motor. Since drawing Lanyard’s attention to the pursuer he had brought about appreciable acceleration.
But would even that pace serve to hold the *Valkyr* if not to distance it?

===Chinese===
兰亚德只得作罢，自认编不出一个说得通的理由来解释这般行径；可这个谜照旧啮咬着他的心，无一刻消停。
打从一开始，打从露西失踪迫使这次飞行延期的那一刻起，他就怕出乱子；指望*帕罗特*一连多日悬着候他方便而风声不走漏，本就不像合乎情理的事；但他预想中的乱子，是从航空港起飞之前发作的那一种。至于在那之后群狼还能对他使出什么坏，这种可能他从未纳入盘算。即便此刻，他也难以郑重其事地掂量它。
他又回头望了一眼。此刻依他判断，那架单翼机衬着烧亮的天空，身影比先前更大了——这说明它正在追上来。
兰亚德压着嗓门咒骂了一句，胸中涨满。
*帕罗特*能跑出每小时八十英里的速度；而毫无疑问，沃克兰正连哄带劝，榨出那台心甘情愿的发动机的每一分力气。自打把追兵指给兰亚德看，他又添了可观的速。
可这样的速度，纵使甩不开那架*瓦尔基里*号，好歹也咬得住它么？

===Original===
His next backward look reckoned the monoplane no nearer.
And another thirty minutes or so elapsed without the relative positions of the two flying machines undergoing any perceptible change.
In the course of this period the *Parrott* rose to an altitude, indicated by the barograph at Lanyard’s elbow, of more than half a mile. Below, the Channel fog spread itself out like a sea of milk, slowly churning.
Staring down in fascination, Lanyard told himself gravely:
“Blue water below that, my friend!”
It seemed difficult to credit the fact that they had made the flight from Paris in so short a time.

===Chinese===
他下一次回头，估量那架单翼机并未更近。
又过了约莫半小时，两架飞行器相互的位置没有发生任何可辨的变动。
在这段工夫里，*帕罗特*爬上了一个高度——兰亚德肘边的自记气压计（barograph）作证——半英里还多。脚下，海峡的浓雾铺展开来，像一片牛乳的海，缓缓翻搅。
兰亚德目眩神迷地俯视着，郑重地告诉自己：
「那底下，可就是蓝汪汪的海水了，我的朋友！」
这么短的工夫就从巴黎飞了过来——这事实竟叫人难以置信。

===Original===
By his reckoning﻿—a very rough one﻿—the *Parrott* was then somewhere off Dieppe: it ought to pick up England, in such case, not far from Brighton. If only one could see﻿ ﻿… !
By bending forward a little and staring past the aviator Lanyard could catch a glimpse of Lucy Shannon.
Though all her beauty and grace of person were lost in the clumsy swaddling of her makeshift costume, she seemed to be comfortable enough; and the rushing air, keen with the chill of that great altitude, moulded her wind-veil precisely to the exquisite contours of her face and stung her firm cheeks until they glowed with a rare fire that even that thick dark mesh could not wholly quench.
The sun crept above the floor of mist, played upon it with iridescent rays, shot it through and through with a warm, pulsating glow like that of a fire opal, and suddenly turned it to a tumbled sea of gold which, apparently boundless, baffled every effort to surmise their position, whether they were above land or sea.
None the less Lanyard’s rough and rapid calculations persuaded him that they were then about Mid-Channel.
He had no more than arrived at this conclusion when a sharp, startled movement, that rocked the planes, drew his attention to the man at his side.

===Chinese===
照他的推算——粗之又粗的一种——*帕罗特*此刻正在迪耶普（Dieppe）外海某处：如此说来，该在布莱顿（Brighton）左近望见英国海岸了。只消能看得见……！
把身子略略前倾、目光越过飞行员，兰亚德便能瞥见露西·香农。
虽说她一身的美貌风韵，尽数埋在那套临时行头笨拙的裹束里，她瞧着却够自在的了；扑面的疾风带着那高空的凛冽，把她的防风面纱严丝合缝地贴上她脸庞秀致的轮廓，又刺得她紧致的双颊烧起一片罕有的光焰，连那层厚厚的黑纱也遮掩不尽。
太阳悄悄爬上雾海的地板，把虹彩的光线泼洒上去，又用一种温热搏动的光焰把它里里外外射透，如同火欧泊的辉光，蓦地把它变成一片翻腾的金海——那金色一望无际，任你如何揣度，也弄不清自家的方位，究竟是身在陆上还是海上。
尽管如此，兰亚德那套粗粗的速算还是说服了他：他们此刻约莫正在海峡正中。
这个结论才一落定，一个猛烈的惊跳——震得机翼都晃了晃——便把他的注意力引到身旁那人身上。

===Original===
Glancing in alarm at the aviator’s face, he saw it as white as marble﻿—what little of it was visible beyond and beneath the wind-mask.
Vauquelin was holding out an arm, and staring at it incredulously; Lanyard’s gaze was drawn to the same spot﻿—a ragged perforation in the sleeve of the pilot’s leather surtout, just above the elbow.
“What is it?” he enquired stupidly, again forgetting that he could not be heard.
The eyes of the aviator, lifting from the perforation to meet Lanyard’s stare, were clouded with consternation.
Then Vauquelin turned quickly and looked back. Simultaneously he ducked his head and something slipped whining past Lanyard’s cheek, touching his flesh with a touch more chill than that of the icy air itself.
“Damnation!” he shrieked, almost hysterically. “That madman in the *Valkyr* is firing at us!”

===Chinese===
他惊慌地瞥向飞行员的脸，只见那脸白得像大理石——防风面罩遮不住、罩底下露出的那一小片，无不如是。
沃克兰正伸出一条手臂，不敢相信似的瞪着它；兰亚德的目光也被牵到同一处——飞行员那件皮质外套（surtout）的袖子上，肘弯正上方，一个边缘毛糙的孔洞。
「怎么回事？」他呆头呆脑地问，又忘了人家压根听不见。
飞行员的眼光从那个孔洞上抬起来，迎上兰亚德的凝视，里头蒙着一层惊惶。
随即沃克兰霍地扭头回望。同时他猛一缩脖，一个东西啸着擦过兰亚德的面颊，那一点肌肤上的触碰，竟比冰冷的高天大气本身还要寒。
「该死！」他几乎歇斯底里地尖声喊道，「*瓦尔基里*号上那个疯子在朝我们开枪！」
